Every module, by layer. Pipeline view

TIA code walkthrough

How every module in tia/ works, written from a full read of the source. Modules are grouped by layer, from the processes that start everything down to the shared building blocks. Requests enter at the top; a module uses only its own layer and the layers below it.

Each module has the same four parts: what it is for, how it works, its key functions with line numbers, and its edge cases. For the order things happen in at run time, read the pipeline view first.

Line numbers are as of 2026-11-16 and will drift as the code changes.

Entry points

These four modules are the commands a person or an orchestrator actually runs: python -m tia.server (the HTTP API), python -m tia.worker (the background job runner), python -m tia.cli (one-off analyses with no server) and python -m tia.migrate (database schema set-up). Each one only reads configuration from environment variables or arguments, builds the objects the deeper layers need, and hands control over; none of them contains analysis logic.

server.py 105 lines

Starts the web server. It reads the environment, builds the FastAPI application through create_app in api.py, and runs it under uvicorn.

How it works

Half of the file (lines 1-51) is a docstring that serves as the reference list of environment variables. The code is two functions, build() and main().

main() (line 89) runs in this order:

  1. obs.setup_logging() and tracing.configure_from_env(...) set up structured logs and trace export.
  2. The bind address is read from TIA_HOST (default 127.0.0.1).
  3. A safety check (line 93): if authentication is off (AuthSettings.from_env().required is false), the host is not a loopback address, and TIA_INSECURE_NO_AUTH is not 1/true/yes, the process exits with a message. The reason is stated in the message: without authentication every caller is an administrator.
  4. build() and tls_options() are called inside a try; a SecretError (a secret reference that cannot be resolved) becomes sys.exit("configuration error: ...").
  5. uvicorn.run is called with port=int(TIA_PORT or 8080), log_config=None and access_log=False (TIA writes its own request log line in the observe middleware), and the TLS options spread in as keyword arguments.

build() (line 70) turns environment variables into create_app arguments:

VariableBecomesNotes
TIA_DATABASE_URLDatabase(check_db_tls(dsn))Read with env_secret, so it may be a literal or a secret reference. Required. check_db_tls is the TLS check on the connection string.
TIA_TENANT_IDtenant_idRequired. The default tenant: used when authentication is off and for unauthenticated routes.
TIA_WORKSPACEworkspaceDefault .tia-data/repos; where repositories are cloned.
TIA_ALLOW_LOCAL_PATHSallow_localTrue for 1, true, yes.
TIA_ALLOWED_GIT_HOSTSallowed_hostsComma-separated; an empty list becomes None, meaning any host.
TIA_AUDIT_KEYaudit_key (bytes)Key for the HMAC audit chain; None if unset.
master key settingssecrets=SecretStore(db, tenant, wrapper)Only when keywrapper_from_env() returns a wrapper; otherwise None and there is no encrypted secret store.
TIA_HSTS or TLS fileshstsTrue when the process terminates TLS itself (tls_options() non-empty) or TIA_HSTS is truthy.
TIA_TENANT_STATE_TTLgate_ttlDefault 5 seconds: how long a tenant's status and quota are cached.
TIA_DEFAULT_STORAGE_QUOTA_BYTESdefault_quotaNone (unlimited) when unset.

Four more configuration objects are built by their own from_env() methods and passed through: ScmConfig (GitHub connector), UploadConfig, AuthSettings and AdoConfig (Azure DevOps).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
build()70Reads the environment and returns the FastAPI app from create_app.
main()89Logging, the no-auth bind check, build(), then uvicorn.run.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Missing TIA_DATABASE_URL or TIA_TENANT_ID: sys.exit with a one-line message (line 73).
  • AuthSettings.from_env() is called twice, once in main for the bind check and once in build; both read the same variables.
  • A non-numeric TIA_PORT, TIA_TENANT_STATE_TTL or TIA_DEFAULT_STORAGE_QUOTA_BYTES raises an uncaught ValueError; only SecretError is turned into a clean exit.
  • The SecretStore built here is constructed for the default tenant, but create_app only uses its .wrapper attribute and builds a per-tenant store itself (see api.py line 212).

worker.py 113 lines

The background worker process. It repeatedly claims one job from the database job queue, runs the handler registered for that job's kind, and records success, retry or permanent failure.

How it works

A Worker holds a JobQueue, a dictionary handlers mapping a job kind (a string such as impact_analysis) to a function that takes a Job and returns a dictionary, and four settings with these defaults: lease_s=300 (how long a claimed job is reserved for this worker), per_tenant_cap=4 (passed to queue.claim; the cap is enforced inside the queue), base_delay_s=5 (the base of the retry backoff, also applied inside the queue) and a worker_id that defaults to "<pid>-<6 hex chars>".

run_once() (line 36) processes at most one job:

  1. Sets the WORKER_POLL metric to the current time. The worker's own /readyz (in health.py) uses this to tell whether the loop is still turning.
  2. At most once every 10 seconds, calls queue.beat(worker_id) to record a heartbeat. A failure here is logged as a warning and ignored.
  3. Calls queue.reap_expired() so jobs whose lease ran out (a worker died) are returned to the queue.
  4. Calls queue.claim(worker_id, lease_s, cap, kinds=list(handlers)). Only kinds this worker has a handler for are claimed. If nothing is returned, the method returns False.
  5. Reads _traceparent from the job payload. The API puts the request's W3C trace context there when it enqueues, so the span opened here (job.run) continues the trace of the HTTP request that queued the job.
  6. Runs the handler and records the outcome (table below).
  7. Increments JOB_RUNS (labelled by kind and outcome) and observes JOB_DURATION, then returns True.
What the handler doesQueue callOutcome label
Returns normallyqueue.succeed(job, result); a non-dictionary result is wrapped as {"result": result}succeeded
Raises PermanentErrorqueue.fail(job, str(e), permanent=True)failed
Raises anything elsequeue.fail(job, "Type: message", base_delay_s=...)retried if job.attempts < job.max_attempts, otherwise failed

run_forever(stop, poll_s=1.0) (line 68) calls run_once() until the threading.Event is set. When a job was processed it loops again immediately; when the queue was empty it waits poll_s seconds.

main() (line 79) is the process entry point. It requires TIA_WORKER_DATABASE_URL (a separate database role from the API's), builds the handler table with analysis_service.make_handlers(...) from the same workspace, host allow-list, audit key and SCM settings the server uses, registers the scrape-time metric collectors, and, if TIA_WORKER_METRICS_PORT is set, starts the small health/metrics HTTP server from health.serve_worker_endpoints on TIA_WORKER_METRICS_HOST (default 127.0.0.1). Then it runs Worker(JobQueue(db), handlers).run_forever(stop).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
PermanentError24Exception a handler raises to say "never retry this job".
Worker.__init__29Stores queue, handlers and the lease/cap/backoff settings.
Worker.run_once36Heartbeat, reap, claim one job, run it, record the result and metrics.
Worker.run_forever68The polling loop.
main79Environment to handlers, optional metrics server, then the loop.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Defect: run_forever catches every exception from run_once with the stated intent of surviving a database hiccup, and prints traceback.format_exc() (line 73). The module never imports traceback, so that line raises NameError and the loop ends instead of continuing.
  • A heartbeat failure never stops work (line 43), but reap_expired and claim are not wrapped: an error there propagates to run_forever.
  • KeyboardInterrupt in main sets the stop event and returns.
  • Configuration problems (SecretError) exit with configuration error: ....

cli.py 197 lines

The tia command line. It runs an analysis or one of the read-only views directly against local repository checkouts, with no server and (optionally) no database.

How it works

main(argv) builds an argparse parser with eight sub-commands, parses, and dispatches on args.cmd. Every sub-command takes repeatable --repo NAME=PATH arguments; _repos() (line 15) splits each on the first = and exits with a message if there is none.

All sub-commands except analyze start the same way: each repository is extracted at HEAD with impact._extract(name, path, "HEAD", None, ()) (no store, no strip prefixes), the results are collected in a dictionary ex keyed by repository name, and most then call build_graph(ex). Output is written to sys.stdout.buffer as UTF-8 bytes, which avoids encoding errors on Windows consoles.

Sub-commandMain optionsWhat it doesLines
analyze--target, --base, --head (all required), --pr, --change-record, --db-url, --tenant-id, --format json|comment|both (default comment), --out, --readable, --max-depth (8), --max-results (200), --github-repoBuilds an AnalysisRequest and calls impact.analyze(req, store). The store exists only when both a DSN and a tenant id are given; otherwise nothing is persisted. The report is redacted to --readable and printed. With --github-repo the comment is also posted with github.upsert_comment.179-193
shared-components--min-entry-points (2), --limit (50)Prints shared_components and rule_gaps from testscope as JSON. The minimum is forced to at least 2.169-178
automation--uiAPI inventory plus summary, or with --ui the page/element inventory.148-158
coverage--test-case FILEThe coverage report, or an assessment of one test case loaded with yaml.safe_load (which also reads JSON).159-167
neo4j-sync--tenant-id (default local), --kf-links, --kf-label, --kf-key, --kf-rel (default IMPLEMENTS)Connects with NEO4J_URI/NEO4J_USER/NEO4J_PASSWORD, projects the graph and syncs it; optionally reads a CSV with columns tia and kf and creates ontology links.121-147
flows--endpoint, --format text|jsonExecution flows of entry points; text output renders each flow, JSON adds the service design.99-106
script--app, --from, --to (required), --base-url (default http://localhost:3000), --outGenerates a Playwright test for a screen-to-screen flow; each unknown is printed to stderr as a TODO line.107-120

_emit() (line 25) formats the analyze output: it first applies redact(report, readable), then picks JSON, the Markdown comment, or for both the comment followed by a fenced JSON block. It writes to --out if given, else to stdout.

The automation and coverage outputs pass through scrub_json(out)[0] before printing, the same secret-scrubbing step the API applies to those views.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_repos15Parses NAME=PATH items into a dictionary.
_emit25Redacts and prints or writes the analysis report.
main36Defines the parser and runs the chosen sub-command.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • analyze returns exit code 2 when the report's status is failed, otherwise 0 (line 193), so a CI job can fail on it.
  • --github-repo without both --pr and $GITHUB_TOKEN exits with a message, but only after the analysis has run and been printed (lines 187-191).
  • neo4j-sync refuses --kf-links without --kf-label and --kf-key: the message says TIA does not guess the Knowledge Fabric schema. The driver is always closed in a finally block. The CSV is opened with utf-8-sig so a byte-order mark from Excel is tolerated.
  • flows --endpoint X with no match and script with an unknown screen both exit with a message that tells the user the expected form or lists the screens known.
  • coverage --test-case exits if the file does not parse to a mapping.

migrate.py 37 lines

Applies pending database migrations with administrator credentials, sets the passwords of the two runtime database roles, and can make sure a named tenant exists.

How it works

  1. Parses one optional argument, --tenant NAME.
  2. Reads TIA_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL and TIA_APP_PASSWORD through env_secret; both are required. The admin DSN is described in the docstring as "never given to the running server": the server and worker connect as the restricted roles instead.
  3. Calls db.migrate(check_db_tls(admin), pw, env_secret("TIA_WORKER_PASSWORD")). The third argument is the worker role's password; the docstring says it defaults to the app password when unset (that default is applied inside db.migrate, not here).
  4. Prints the list of migrations applied, or nothing (up to date), and the two role names APP_ROLE and WORKER_ROLE.
  5. With --tenant, calls TenantAdmin(admin).ensure(name) and prints the returned tenant id. That id is what goes into TIA_TENANT_ID.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
main18The whole command: read secrets, migrate, optionally ensure a tenant.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Missing variables exit with TIA_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL and TIA_APP_PASSWORD are required; an unresolvable secret reference exits with configuration error: ....
  • TenantAdmin(admin) on line 33 receives the raw DSN, not the result of check_db_tls(admin) that migrate was given. Whether that matters depends on what check_db_tls returns, which is outside this module.

HTTP API, web UI and agent surfaces

This layer is everything reachable over HTTP. api.py builds the FastAPI application: it wires a chain of middleware (logging, transport security, authentication, tenant status, rate limiting) around roughly one hundred routes that call into the analysis, graph, evidence and repository services. The other modules are the parts that chain is made of (authapi.py with oidc.py and saml.py, ratelimit.py, gate.py, health.py) and three output adapters that present a finished report to different readers: reportpage.py for people, mcp.py for coding assistants, and kfadapter.py for Knowledge Fabric agents.

api.py 2062 lines

Defines the request body models and one function, create_app, which builds the whole FastAPI application: per-tenant service objects, the middleware chain, shared helper functions, and every route. All routes are closures inside create_app, so they share its local variables.

How it works

Request models (lines 84-182). Fifteen Pydantic models describe JSON bodies. The ones with validation worth knowing:

ModelLineFields and constraints
RepoIn84url, path, name, token, branch; all optional.
DiscoverIn92provider (github, ado or github-app), org, project, token, api_base, ingest (default true), names (limit ingestion to these).
PublishIn106repository, pr, optional head_sha, comment and check_run (both default true).
InstallationIn114installation_id and code, GitHub's user-authorisation code.
VerdictIn137finding_id must match ^F\d{1,6}$; verdict is confirmed, false_positive or irrelevant; comment up to 1000 characters.
AckIn, ApprovalIn143, 148A team or owner name of 1-120 characters; decision is approve or reject.
TestCaseIn154title (500), steps (20000), expected (5000), entry_point (500), optional id (200).
ScriptIn162repo, start and end with JSON aliases from and to, base_url.
AnalysisIn172target (required), then head or pr, base, token, repos (scope), revisions (other repositories' refs), change_record, max_depth, max_results.

One module constant sits among the imports: EVIDENCE_MAX = 25 * 1024 * 1024 (line 58), the largest evidence body accepted.

Set-up at the top of create_app (lines 185-246).

  1. If authentication is required, the app is created with docs_url, redoc_url and openapi_url all None. The comment explains: interactive documentation would be an unauthenticated map of the API.
  2. Process-wide objects are created once: jobs = JobQueue(db), tokens = TokenSource(scm, db) (GitHub credentials), and upload_root = <workspace>/.uploads.
  3. The class Ctx (line 202) bundles one tenant's services: store, svc (a RepoService), gate (a TenantGate), access (an AccessStore), plan (a TenantPlan) and secrets. When authentication is required the tenant's workspace is <workspace>/<tenant id>, so tenants never share a clone directory; in development it is the workspace itself. The secret store is wrapped in a TenantKeyWrapper that looks up the tenant's own key reference (plan.settings()["key_ref"]) each time, falling back to the platform wrapper.
  4. ctx_for(t) (line 218) returns the cached Ctx for a tenant id, creating it under a lock with a double check. If the cache already holds 512 entries it is cleared first; the objects are cheap and are rebuilt on demand.
  5. tid() (line 229) returns the current request's tenant: the context variable authapi.current_tenant if set, otherwise the configured default tenant.
  6. _Current (line 232) is a small proxy. store, svc and gate are instances of it, so that store.audit(...) written anywhere in a handler resolves to ctx_for(tid()).store.audit(...) at call time: 
    def __getattr__(self, attr):
    return getattr(getattr(ctx_for(tid()), self._name), attr)

store, svc, gate = _Current("store"), _Current("svc"), _Current("gate")
    This is why handlers can be written as if there were one tenant.
  7. authn = authapi.Auth(...) is the authenticator, and limiter = ratelimit.RateLimiter(Limits(default_on=auth.required)) the rate limiter: limiting is on by default exactly when authentication is on.

Middleware order. Starlette runs the middleware that was registered last first. The code registers them in the order rate limit, tenant lifecycle, authentication, failed-authentication limit, transport security, observe; a request therefore passes through them in the reverse order:

Order on the way inMiddlewareLineWhat it does
1 (outermost)observe301Creates or accepts a request id (X-Request-ID), opens a trace span continuing an incoming traceparent, counts in-flight requests, and in a finally block records the request counter, the duration histogram and one log line. The route label is the matched route template (for example /api/v1/repositories/{name}) or unmatched. Routes in quiet_routes (health, readiness, metrics) log at debug level. Adds X-Request-ID and X-Trace-Id to the response.
2transport_security291If hsts is on and the request is https (directly, or X-Forwarded-Proto: https), adds Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains.
3failed_auth_limit277Before authentication: if this client address has used up its failed-authentication budget and the path is not exempt, answers 429. After the inner layers respond, a 401 charges the address one token.
4authenticate (installed by authapi.install, line 274)authapi 277Resolves the caller to a principal, sets the tenant, checks CSRF and the route's permission. See authapi.py.
5tenant_lifecycle261For paths starting /api/ or /analyses/, except /api/v1/health: runs gate.check_active and plan.check_region() in a thread. A GateError or PlanError becomes a JSON error with the exception's status (403 for suspended, 410 for offboarded) and an X-TIA-Code header.
6 (innermost)rate_limit249Identifies the caller as token_id, user_id or actor of the principal (none for a missing or system principal) and asks limiter.check. A non-zero wait becomes 429 with Retry-After rounded up to at least 1 second.

The order matters: authentication runs before the lifecycle check so the right tenant's status is checked, and observe is outermost so that refused requests are logged too.

Shared helpers (lines 335-441). These closures are used by many handlers:

HelperLineWhat it does
github_token335The GitHub credential to use: an explicit token if given, otherwise tokens.token_for(...) (an installation token when the GitHub App is configured, falling back to the stored or environment token). An AppError maps to 503 (not_configured, bad_key), 502 (unreachable) or 424.
scm_now345The connector configuration with the tenant's stored token filled in when the environment has none.
within_plan349Calls plan.check(what, adding); on PlanError it counts a gate denial, audits plan_limit_reached and raises the HTTP error.
room358Calls gate.check_room(adding); a GateError (storage quota exceeded) becomes an HTTP error.
declared366The request's Content-Length as an integer, or 0.
readable378The set of repository names a principal may read, or None meaning all. None is returned for no principal and for a system principal; otherwise access.readable(p, svc.list()) decides.
audience370Combines readable with the X-TIA-Readable-Repos header. The header can only narrow: if the principal is unrestricted the header list is used as is; otherwise the result is the intersection.
visible, require_repo384, 392visible(name) is true when the caller may read the repository. require_repo raises 404 repository '...' is not registered otherwise, so an unreadable repository is indistinguishable from a missing one.
public_if_visible388Returns a repository's full details only to someone who may read it; others get its name, provider and a note.
wrap397Calls a function and converts IngestError to HTTP 400.
landscape405The extracts (parsed facts) of the registered repositories, via landscape_extracts.
current_graph408Gets or builds the persisted graph for a set of extracts. If the extracts cover every registered repository, it tries to advance the landscape "head" to this graph version; when the head actually moves it audits graph_version_advanced and, if TIA_NEO4J_AUTO_SYNC is truthy, mirrors the graph to Neo4j.
mirror_to_neo4j419Projects and syncs a graph into Neo4j. Every failure is audited as neo4j_sync_failed. With raise_errors=False (the automatic path) the error is returned in the result, never raised; with True (the explicit route) it becomes 503 (not configured, air-gapped) or 502.
actor_of470Who to record as the actor: the authenticated principal's actor; only in development mode is the X-TIA-Actor header trusted, and then only if it matches [A-Za-z0-9@._ -]{1,64}, else api.

Every route. Paths are shown in full. "Readable-repos" means the route takes the X-TIA-Readable-Repos header and filters by audience. The authentication and administration routes are registered by authapi.install and are listed in the authapi.py section.

Agent surfaces and webhooks

MethodPathWhat it doesLine
POST/mcpModel Context Protocol endpoint for coding assistants (read-only tools).1613

Creating an analysis (create, line 1670).

  1. within_plan("analyses"), require_repo(body.target) and room() run first: plan limit, visibility of the target, and storage quota.
  2. Synchronous path: resolve(svc, params, True, config) turns the request into concrete commit ids (the third argument differs between the two paths; it is False for queued requests), then execute(svc, store, concrete, secrets) runs the analysis. The usage cache of the gate is invalidated because the store grew. The response is {"analysis_id", "status", "report"} with the report passed through redact(rep, audience(header)).
  3. Queued path (?async=true): a request carrying a token is refused with 400, because nothing secret is stored in the queue. The resolved parameters are enqueued as an impact_analysis job, with the current trace context added under _traceparent and two keys from job_key(concrete) and group_key(concrete); the queue uses the first to deduplicate identical requests. The route audits analysis_queued (including whether it was deduplicated) and answers 202 with the job plus target, base and head.

Reading a report (get, line 1947). Almost every per-analysis route goes through this helper or its twin readable_report (line 1538). Both load the stored report and raise 404 unless the caller may read the repository the change is in. get also applies redact, which removes findings in repositories outside the audience; readable_report returns the unredacted report for handlers that need to compute on the whole thing (policy, coordination, verdicts) and redact afterwards.

The report page (analysis_page, line 1962). Renders the redacted report with reportpage.render_page. Both the page and the not-found page are sent with PAGE_HEADERS (line 1957): a Content-Security-Policy of default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none', plus nosniff, no-referrer and Cache-Control: private, no-cache. The policy allows inline styles and nothing else, so no script can run even if repository content reached the page.

Registering and syncing repositories. add_repo (line 556) checks room() and within_plan("repositories"); if no token was supplied, the GitHub App is in use and the URL is a GitHub one, it fetches an installation token; it calls svc.register, then refresh_landscape(svc, store) (re-extract and update cross-repository relationships) and gate.invalidate(). sync (line 621) is notable for its failure path: on IngestError it still calls refresh_landscape before raising 400, so that the failed scan is recorded and relationships touching that repository become stale.

Uploads (lines 642-766). A staged upload is a Session object under <workspace>/.uploads, tied to the tenant. The flow for a browser is: start a session, send files in batches or one zip, commit. The supporting helpers:

  • session(uid) (line 643) opens a session, giving 403 if uploads are disabled and 404 if the id is unknown for this tenant.
  • upload_target(name) (line 705) cleans the name, requires access if a repository of that name already exists, and refuses (400) to upload over a repository that was registered from a provider rather than by upload.
  • receive_archive (line 670) checks the declared Content-Length against ucfg.max_bytes, then streams the body to archive.zip chunk by chunk, counting bytes and raising UploadTooLarge as soon as the limit is passed, so a lying Content-Length does not help. The zip is unpacked in a thread by s.add_zip and always deleted afterwards.
  • reject (line 651) is the single failure path: it discards the whole staged upload ("fail closed"), audits upload_rejected, and raises 413 for size errors or 400 otherwise. guarded and archive_into route every IngestError to it and always call s.flush().
  • finish_upload (line 696) calls svc.commit_upload, discards the staging area, refreshes the landscape and returns the repository's public details plus revision, files, bytes, skipped_files and unchanged (true when the content was identical to the previous revision).

The multipart route limits the form to max_files=2000 and max_fields=4000 per batch. Quota is checked with the declared body size before receiving, and again with the actual staged bytes before committing, because the committed copy also lives in the workspace.

Linking a GitHub App installation (installations_link, line 836). Knowing an installation id is not enough to claim it. The handler asks GitHub for the installation's account, then calls tokens.app.user_sees_installation(code, installation_id, account, account_type) with the user-authorisation code from GitHub's redirect. If the authorising GitHub user does not own the account, it audits installation_link_refused and answers 403. Otherwise it runs the SQL function tia_map_installation('github', id, account) in the tenant's transaction; any result other than mapped is 409 (already linked to an organisation).

Tester views and tester_scope (line 984). All tester routes start with this helper. It computes the audience, parses the optional repos list (each named repository must be readable), and takes only the extracts of readable repositories. If no explicit list was given and the caller can read every registered repository, it uses the persisted landscape graph (current_graph); otherwise it builds a graph from just the permitted extracts with build_graph. The effect, as the docstring says, is that nothing in an unreadable repository is matched, counted or named. Results pass through scrub_json(...)[0] before being returned.

Evidence ingestion (lines 1082-1199). The five POST /api/v1/evidence/... routes share a shape:

  1. require_registered(repo) where a repository is named: it must be visible and registered.
  2. evidence_body (line 1091) reads the raw body with authapi.read_capped(request, EVIDENCE_MAX); over 25 MB is 413, non-UTF-8 is 400.
  3. An inner work() function parses the text with the matching parser from evidence.py (a parse error is 400) and stores it through ev_put. It runs in a thread pool so parsing does not block the event loop.
  4. ev_put (line 1105) checks within_plan("evidence"), writes with EvidenceStore.put(kind, source, data, actor=...) and audits evidence_ingested.

Revisions are lower-cased before storing. For test results, the handler also computes counts per result value and uses ci:<build_id or 'unknown'> as the source. The same ev_put mechanism stores non-ingested record kinds too: release, incident, incident_link, verdict, acknowledgement, approval and itsm_attachment. The evidence store is in effect the append-only record for everything people or pipelines tell TIA.

Releases (lines 1249-1328). release_create validates the name against [A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:/-]{0,79} and the revisions map (1-500 entries), resolves every ref to a commit with svc.resolve, answers 409 if a release of that name already exists with different revisions, then release_graph extracts each repository at exactly its commit and gets or builds that graph. The release record stores the resolved revisions, what was requested, and the graph_id. release_compare rebuilds (or reloads from cache) both graphs and calls temporal.compare. release_record (line 1249) calls require_repo on every repository in the release, so a release that includes one unreadable repository is 404 for that caller.

Policy, approvals and coordination (lines 1525-1563). report_config(rep) loads the configuration the analysis ran under from its evidence snapshot, falling back to the defaults, so a later re-evaluation applies the same rules. policy_now(rep) evaluates the release policy against the stored report with the approvals recorded so far and the current coordination status; the stored report is never modified. analysis_approval accepts an approval only from an owner in required_owners(...), otherwise 400 listing who is needed. analysis_acknowledge does the same check against the affected teams.

The MCP endpoint (mcp_endpoint, line 1614). The body is capped at 1 MB and parsed as JSON; failure returns the protocol's invalid-request error with HTTP 400. The handler then builds a dictionary tools from tool name to a function of the arguments. Four are written here (tool_report, tool_find, tool_scope, tool_policy); the seven tester tools call the corresponding REST route function directly through via (line 1647), which passes the caller's X-TIA-Readable-Repos header and converts an HTTPException into an mcp.ToolError. Because the tools are the route functions, an assistant sees exactly what the same caller would see over REST. A JSON array body is treated as a batch; each message goes to mcp.handle in a thread. If every message was a notification the answer is an empty 202; otherwise a single response or an array, matching the shape of the request.

The Knowledge Fabric door (kf_query, line 1732). The request must have intent == "software_tia". The analysis must be named, either by analysis_id or by repository plus pull_request/revision (the newest match is used); the question text is never parsed. Every "cannot answer" case returns a well-formed empty response from kfadapter.empty with a message in metadata.errors, not an HTTP error. A found report is redacted, optionally accompanied by its tester pack (skipped for a failed analysis), and projected by kfadapter.to_query_response.

The GitHub webhook (github_webhook, line 1757).

  1. 503 if TIA_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET is not configured.
  2. The body is read with a 26 MB cap (GitHub's own cap is 25 MB), so nothing unbounded is read before the signature is checked.
  3. verify_signature(secret, raw, X-Hub-Signature-256); failure is 401 and deliberately not audited, so unauthenticated callers cannot fill the audit log.
  4. A ping event is answered {"ok": true}; any event other than pull_request is answered 202 "ignored".
  5. parse_pull_request returns None for actions that need no analysis (202 "ignored").
  6. Tenant routing: when the GitHub App is in use, installation_tenant(db, installation_id) finds the tenant that owns the installation and authapi.current_tenant.set(owner) switches the rest of the handler to that tenant; an unlinked installation is ignored with 202. Without the App, a GitHub token must be configured (503 otherwise) and the default tenant is used.
  7. room() checks the storage quota of that tenant.
  8. match_repo(svc.list(), clone_url) finds the registered repository; if none, webhook_ignored is audited and the answer is 202.
  9. A github_pull_request job is enqueued with the payload (repository, PR number, head sha, base ref, installation id, trace context) and the keys from pr_event_job_key and pr_event_group_key. The handler audits webhook_accepted and returns 202 with the job.

scm_host_webhook (line 1813, shared by GitLab and Bitbucket) and ado_webhook (line 1886) follow the same pattern with a 5 MB cap and their own verification. Their tenant routing has one extra rule: the event's namespace (or Azure DevOps collection id) is looked up; if it maps to a tenant, that tenant is used; if it does not but some mapping exists for the provider (provider_mapped), the event is ignored with 202, so an unmapped namespace can never fall into the default tenant once mappings are in use. The Azure DevOps handler additionally refuses (400) an event whose organisation URL is not on an allowed host.

Publishing (publish, line 1989). After the usual visibility checks on both the analysis and the named repository, the handler dispatches on the repository's provider: Azure DevOps remotes go to publish_ado (line 2021), GitLab and Bitbucket to publish_host (line 1864), everything else must be a GitHub remote. For GitHub, the analysis must be of that repository, head_sha (default: the analysed revision) must be a full 40-character hex sha, and the comment is scoped by comment_scope(access, svc, scm, repo, auth.required) so it only names repositories the pull request's readers may see. For the non-GitHub hosts the audience is the target repository only when authentication is required. Every successful publication is recorded with audit_publication.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An analysis, repository or job the caller may not read always produces 404, never 403, so its existence is not revealed (require_repo, get, job_status at line 1931).
  • X-TIA-Readable-Repos can only narrow access: audience intersects it with what the principal may read (line 376).
  • Webhook signature failures are not audited by design (comments at lines 1767, 1822 and 1895).
  • The tenant-lifecycle middleware runs before the route handler. For webhooks the handler switches to the owning tenant afterwards (line 1786, 1834, 1907), so the active/suspended check that ran in the middleware was for the default tenant; inside the handler only room() (quota) is applied to the owning tenant.
  • discover for the token-based providers (lines 578-591) registers repositories but, unlike add_repo and discover_app, does not call within_plan, refresh_landscape or gate.invalidate, and returns the full public details without public_if_visible.
  • upload_commit checks within_plan("repositories") for a new repository name (line 745); the one-call route upload_zip_once (line 758) does not.
  • evidence_list filters with r["repo"] in ok (line 1220). For a caller with restricted access, records that have no repository (for example runtime edges, which are stored by service) are therefore always dropped.
  • snapshot (line 2047) redacts the snapshot for the audience but, unlike the per-analysis routes, has no check that the caller may read the analysed repository.
  • readable_report accepts a header argument it never uses (line 1538); redaction is left to each caller.
  • In the MCP tool table, int(a.get("limit") or ...) (lines 1660-1661) and TestCaseIn(**...) (line 1659) are evaluated outside via's try. mcp.handle checks argument names but not types, so a non-numeric limit or a non-string title raises an exception that neither layer catches.
  • current_graph only advances the head when len(ex) == len(svc.list()); a partial view never moves it.
  • incident_investigation rejects a window outside 1 to 2160 hours (90 days); the default comes from the tenant configuration.
  • installations_unlink and incident_link validate identifiers before use: a commit must match [0-9a-f]{7,40}.
  • publish maps a PublishError to 503 when its message mentions TIA_GITHUB_TOKEN and to 502 otherwise (line 2017); this depends on the wording of the error message.

authapi.py 618 lines

Authentication and authorisation for every request, plus the sign-in, sign-out and administration routes. It resolves each request to a Principal (who is calling, in which tenant, with which roles) and refuses requests the principal's roles do not permit.

How it works

Three kinds of credential are accepted by Auth.authenticate (line 126), checked in this order:

CredentialHow it is recognisedHow it is verified
Service-account tokenAuthorization: Bearer tia_...parse_service_token splits it into tenant, token id and secret; IdentityStore.token_principal checks it in that tenant's store.
OpenID Connect tokenAuthorization: Bearer <JWT> not starting with tia_See the routing steps below.
Browser sessionCookie tia_sessionparse_session_cookie gives tenant and session secret; IdentityStore.session_principal checks it.

An Authorization header with any scheme other than Bearer is rejected with 401 rather than falling through to the cookie. No credential at all is authentication_required (401).

Routing a JWT to a tenant (from_token, line 142). A bearer token does not say which tenant it belongs to, so:

  1. jwtverify.peek reads the claims without verifying the signature.
  2. The issuer (iss) must be a string and there must be at least one string audience (aud).
  3. For up to the first five audiences, route_issuer(db, iss, aud) looks up which tenant has an identity provider configured with that issuer and audience. Results, including misses, are cached for 30 seconds in self._route.
  4. If no tenant trusts the pair: 401. Otherwise the identity provider row is loaded (cached 30 seconds); it must be enabled and of protocol oidc.
  5. Only now is the token fully verified by that provider's Provider.verify (signature, issuer, audience, expiry).
  6. principal_from_claims maps the verified claims to a user.

From claims to a principal (principal_from_claims, line 176). identity_from_claims extracts subject, name, email and groups according to the provider's configuration. The user row is looked up (cached 30 seconds except during a login). A person who has no existing row and whose groups map to no role is refused with no_role (403) before any row is created, so "someone who gets nothing" leaves no record. A disabled user is refused; a user whose effective roles are empty is refused. The result is a Principal of kind user carrying tenant, subject, roles, name, email, user id and source-host logins.

The middleware (resolve, line 289) is what runs on every request:

  1. If authentication is not required: set the development principal (an administrator on the default tenant) and continue.
  2. route_of(app, request) (line 222) finds the route template the request will hit by asking each route whether it matches, and whether it is a static mount.
  3. rbac.classify(method, template) returns the permission the route needs. Static mounts, unmatched paths, routes classified PUBLIC, and templates in rbac.UNLISTED_OK pass through with the default tenant and no principal.
  4. A route with no classification is refused: 403 route is not classified; refused. A new route that nobody listed in rbac is therefore closed by default.
  5. auth.authenticate runs in a thread. An AuthError goes to fail; a netsafe.FetchError (identity provider unreachable) becomes 503 auth_unavailable; any other exception is re-raised.
  6. The tenant and principal are set in context variables, which is how api.py's tid() and current_principal.get() see them.
  7. check_csrf, then the role check: unless the permission is AUTHENTICATED (any signed-in caller), rbac.allows(p.roles, perm) must be true, else 403 forbidden.

The wrapper authenticate (line 278) then adds browser-hardening headers to every response when authentication is required, using setdefault so a handler's stricter value wins: X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, X-Frame-Options: DENY, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none', and Cache-Control: no-store for paths under /api/, /auth/ and /analyses/.

CSRF (check_csrf, line 196). Cross-site request forgery protection applies only to cookie sessions, because only a cookie is attached by the browser on its own. A principal from a session has a csrf value; for methods in UNSAFE (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) the X-TIA-CSRF header must equal it (constant-time comparison). In addition, if the browser sent an Origin header, its host must equal the request's Host header or the origin must equal TIA_PUBLIC_URL.

Browser sign-in with OpenID Connect.

  1. GET /auth/options?org=NAME lists the sign-in methods of an organisation. An invalid, unknown, inactive or offboarded organisation all return the same empty list.
  2. GET /auth/login?org=...&idp=...&return_to=... (line 337) requires TIA_PUBLIC_URL, picks the provider, and generates a random browser value. For OIDC it creates a PKCE verifier/challenge pair and a nonce, stores them with save_login_state (which returns the state string), and redirects to the provider's authorization URL. The browser value is set as the cookie tia_login (10 minutes, HTTP-only, path /auth).
  3. GET /auth/callback (line 385) parses state as <32 hex tenant id>.<token>, then calls take_login_state(token, login cookie). That call consumes the state; it returns nothing for an unknown, expired, replayed state or one started by a different browser.
  4. The authorization code is exchanged at the token endpoint with the PKCE verifier, the ID token is verified including the nonce, and principal_from_claims(..., login=True) builds the principal.
  5. start_session (line 374) creates the session row, audits login_succeeded, redirects to the safe return path, sets tia_session (HTTP-only, SameSite=Lax, lifetime session_ttl) and deletes the login cookie.

SAML sign-in uses the same state mechanism. /auth/login delegates to saml.start_login, and sets the login cookie with SameSite=None because the identity provider returns by a cross-site POST on which a Lax cookie would not be sent. POST /auth/saml/acs (line 414) reads at most 1.5 MB, parses the form with at most 10 fields, and runs saml_login in a thread: take the login state from RelayState, validate the response against the stored request id, record the assertion id with saml_assertion_once (valid until the assertion's expiry plus 5 minutes) to refuse replays, convert attributes to claims, and start the session.

Routes registered by install:

MethodPathWhat it doesLine

save_idp (line 502) handles a write-only client_secret: it is stored in the tenant's encrypted secret store under idp/<name lower-cased, other characters replaced by ->/client-secret, and only that reference is saved with the provider. In users_update, the roles field distinguishes "absent" from "present and null" with model_fields_set: null clears the manual assignment so roles follow the identity provider's groups again.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
AuthSettings45required, public_url, session_ttl (default 8 hours), session_idle (default 30 minutes); secure_cookies is true when the public URL is https.
_TTL63A small thread-safe cache with a time-to-live; cleared entirely when it passes 5000 entries.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Error responses carry a fixed public message from _PUBLIC_MESSAGE (line 606) and a machine code in X-TIA-Code; the detailed reason goes only to the audit log, and only for the codes forbidden, csrf, user_disabled and no_role (line 265). A plain invalid token is not audited.
  • A 401 on a browser GET of /analyses/... that accepts HTML is turned into a redirect to /?return_to=... so a shared report link leads to the sign-in screen (line 273).
  • Login failures are audited only after the login state was confirmed to belong to a real tenant (confirmed flag, lines 387 and 428), so forged callbacks cannot write to a tenant's audit log.
  • read_capped raises AuthError("login_failed", 413, ...) whatever the caller; api.py catches it and substitutes its own message.
  • Auth.forget(tenant) (line 102) ignores its argument and clears the caches of every tenant.
  • idps_list (line 500) rebuilds each row with "client_secret_ref" set to the value it already has; the expression changes nothing.
  • /auth/saml/metadata calls saml.metadata(public_url, {}) with an empty configuration (line 457), so the published entity id is always the default one, even for an identity provider configured with its own sp_entity_id, which saml.validate does honour.
  • Auth.ids clears its cache above 512 tenants so a flood of forged tenant ids cannot grow memory.

oidc.py 133 lines

The OpenID Connect client: one Provider object per configured identity provider, able to build the sign-in URL, exchange an authorization code for an ID token, and verify tokens.

How it works

PKCE. pkce_pair() (line 26) returns a random verifier (secrets.token_urlsafe(48)) and its challenge, the URL-safe base64 of the verifier's SHA-256 with padding removed. The challenge goes to the provider at sign-in; the verifier is sent later with the code, proving that the same party started and finished the flow.

Discovery (Provider.endpoint, line 55). Endpoint URLs are looked up by name (authorization_endpoint, token_endpoint, jwks_uri):

  1. If the configuration has discovery: false, the URL is read directly from the configuration.
  2. Otherwise, under a lock: if discovery failed less than 30 seconds ago, the same error is raised again without a new network call.
  3. If there is no cached document or it is older than DISCOVERY_TTL (3600 seconds), <issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration is fetched. The document's issuer must equal the configured issuer exactly; the comment explains that a mismatch would let another provider vouch for this one.
  4. The named value must be a string, else idp_misconfigured (502).

Verification (verify, line 80). If the configuration lists allowed_algs, the token header's algorithm must be in it. Then jwtverify.verify checks signature (keys from self.jwks, which fetches the jwks_uri lazily), issuer, audience, optional nonce and optional max_token_age. A TokenError becomes invalid_token (401); a network failure becomes idp_unreachable (502).

Sign-in URL and code exchange. authorization_url (line 93) adds response_type=code, client_id (the configured audience), redirect_uri, scope (default openid email profile), state, nonce, code_challenge and code_challenge_method=S256. exchange (line 99) posts the form to the token endpoint, including client_secret only when the provider is a confidential client, and returns the id_token; anything but status 200 with a string token is login_failed (401).

ProviderCache (line 114) keeps providers so their key sets and discovery documents are reused. The cache key is the provider id, a SHA-256 of its sorted configuration, and its client-secret reference; any configuration change therefore produces a new Provider, and older entries for the same id are removed. A provider older than MAX_AGE (900 seconds) is rebuilt, which picks up a rotated client secret.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
OidcError22Subclass of AuthError for provider problems.
pkce_pair26Verifier and S256 challenge.
Provider.endpoint55Endpoint URL from configuration or cached discovery.
Provider.verify80Full token verification, returning the claims.
Provider.authorization_url93The redirect target for sign-in.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • All HTTP goes through netsafe.get_json and netsafe.post_form unless test doubles are injected (http_get, http_post). The module docstring states what netsafe enforces: https, public addresses, no redirects, a size cap.
  • Both kinds of discovery failure (unreachable, issuer mismatch) are remembered for 30 seconds so a failing provider is not retried on every request.
  • With discovery: false and a missing endpoint key, self.cfg[name] raises a plain KeyError, not an OidcError.

saml.py 216 lines

A SAML 2.0 service provider: it builds the sign-in request sent to the identity provider and strictly validates the signed response that comes back.

How it works

Starting sign-in (start_login, line 67). SAML sign-in requires an https public URL, because the browser must send the login cookie on a cross-site POST; otherwise idp_misconfigured (503). A request id "_" + 32 hex characters is generated and stored as the "nonce" of the login state (the PKCE verifier slot holds the literal "saml"). authn_request (line 52) builds the AuthnRequest XML by string formatting with XML-escaped values, and redirect_binding (line 61) compresses it with raw DEFLATE (zlib.compress(...)[2:-4] strips the zlib header and checksum), base64-encodes it and appends it to the provider's sso_url as SAMLRequest, with the login state as RelayState.

Parsing (parse_response, line 80). The base64 text may be at most about 1.33 MB and the decoded bytes at most MAX_RESPONSE (1,000,000). Any occurrence of <!doctype or <!entity (case-insensitive) is refused before parsing. The parser itself is configured with resolve_entities=False, no_network=True, load_dtd=False, huge_tree=False and remove_comments=True (line 31).

Validation (validate, line 110) runs these checks in order; each failure raises AuthError("login_failed", ...):

  1. The root element is a samlp:Response.
  2. There is no EncryptedAssertion and exactly one Assertion.
  3. Signature. If the assertion has its own signature, a detached copy of it is verified and from then on only the element the verifier returns is read. Otherwise, if the response is signed, the response is verified and the assertion must be a direct child of the signed element. If neither is signed: refused. This is the defence against signature-wrapping attacks, where an attacker adds an unsigned assertion next to a signed one.
  4. The status code is exactly urn:oasis:names:tc:SAML:2.0:status:Success.
  5. InResponseTo on the response equals the stored request id, so unsolicited (provider-initiated) responses are refused.
  6. Destination equals this server's assertion consumer URL, <public_url>/auth/saml/acs.
  7. Every Issuer on the response and the assertion equals the configured entity_id, and the assertion has one.
  8. The assertion has an ID and exactly one Conditions element with a NotOnOrAfter. With a clock skew allowance (clock_skew, default 60 seconds) the current time must be within NotBefore and NotOnOrAfter.
  9. The audience list contains this service provider's entity id.
  10. At least one SubjectConfirmation with the bearer method whose data has Recipient equal to the consumer URL, InResponseTo equal to the request id, and an unexpired NotOnOrAfter.

The result is a dictionary with name_id, attributes (each attribute's values indexed under both its Name and its FriendlyName), assertion_id and not_after. Because steps 5 and 6 read attributes of the response element, which is unsigned when only the assertion is signed, step 10 repeats the recipient and request-id checks on data inside the signed assertion.

Signature verification (_verify_signed, line 97) tries each configured certificate in turn (two during a certificate rotation) with signxml.XMLVerifier().verify(element, x509_cert=pem, expect_references=1) and returns .signed_xml. The docstring notes that signxml's defaults refuse SHA-1.

Mapping to the OIDC shape. claims_from (line 186) turns attributes into a claims dictionary (sub, email, name, groups, and scm_<host> logins) using the attribute names from the configuration; the subject falls back to the NameID. claim_config (line 212) produces the matching claim-name configuration, so the same identity_from_claims and role mapping code serves both protocols.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
sp_entity_id34Configured sp_entity_id or <public_url>/auth/saml/metadata.
acs_url38<public_url>/auth/saml/acs.
authn_request52The request XML.
redirect_binding61DEFLATE, base64 and URL-encode onto the SSO URL.

_verify_signed97Verifies a signature against the configured certificates.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Replay protection is not in this module: authapi.saml_login records the returned assertion_id once.
  • An unparseable timestamp is a login failure (_utc, line 42); a timestamp without a zone is treated as UTC.
  • The docstring of validate (line 111) says the result has a 'subject' key; the code returns 'name_id' (line 183), which is what claims_from reads.
  • metadata inserts the entity id and URL into XML attributes without escaping; both come from server configuration, not from the request.

ratelimit.py 112 lines

In-process request rate limiting with token buckets, keyed by authenticated principal, by tenant, and by client address for unauthenticated callers.

How it works

A token bucket is a counter that refills at a steady rate up to a maximum; each request removes tokens, and a request that finds too few is refused. Here every bucket's capacity equals its per-minute rate, so a caller may burst one minute's allowance and then continues at the steady rate.

Bucket keyApplies toSettingDefault per minute
p:<tenant>:<principal>One authenticated person or service tokenTIA_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE600
t:<tenant>A whole tenantTIA_RATE_LIMIT_TENANT_PER_MINUTE6000
a:<address>An unauthenticated callerTIA_RATE_LIMIT_ANON_PER_MINUTE120
f:<address>Failed authentications from an addressthe anonymous rate120

All three rates are divided by TIA_RATE_LIMIT_REPLICAS (default 1), because each API process keeps its own buckets. Limiting is enabled by the default_on argument unless TIA_RATE_LIMIT is set, in which case any value other than 0, false, no, off turns it on.

_take(key, rate, cost) (line 53) is the bucket arithmetic. Buckets live in an OrderedDict mapping key to [tokens, last refill time]:

b[0] = min(rate_per_min, b[0] + (now - b[1]) * rate_per_min / 60.0)
b[1] = now
if b[0] >= cost:
    b[0] -= cost
    return 0.0
return (cost - b[0]) * 60.0 / rate_per_min

A new key starts full. A used key is moved to the end of the dictionary; when there are more than MAX_KEYS (50,000) keys the least recently used one is dropped. The return value is 0 when allowed, otherwise the number of seconds until enough tokens will have refilled, which api.py sends as Retry-After.

check(path, method, tenant, principal, client) (line 71):

  1. Returns 0 if limiting is disabled or the path is in EXEMPT (/healthz, /readyz, /metrics, /api/v1/health).
  2. Decides the cost. A POST to exactly /api/v1/impact-analyses, to any path starting /api/v1/evidence/ or /api/v1/releases, or ending /reproduce costs ANALYSIS_COST (10) tokens; everything else costs 1. This stops a client from queuing unbounded heavy work.
  3. For an authenticated caller: take from the principal bucket; if that passes, take from the tenant bucket. For an anonymous caller: take from the address bucket.

Failed authentications. charge_failed_auth(client) takes one token from the address's f: bucket each time a request ends in 401. failed_auth_blocked(client) computes the bucket's current level without changing it and returns a wait time when fewer than one token remains. An address that has never failed has no bucket and is never blocked.

client_address(request) (line 106) returns the socket peer. Only when TIA_TRUST_PROXY is truthy does it use X-Forwarded-For, and then only the last entry, the hop added by the trusted proxy; earlier entries can be forged by the client.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
Limits35Reads the rates and the on/off flag.
RateLimiter._take53Refill, then take or compute the wait.
RateLimiter.check71The per-request decision.
RateLimiter.failed_auth_blocked87Read-only check of the failed-authentication budget.
RateLimiter.charge_failed_auth99Charges one token for a 401.
client_address106The address to attribute an unauthenticated request to.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • When the principal bucket passes but the tenant bucket refuses, the principal's tokens have already been taken (lines 80-83).
  • _env_int clamps values to at least 1 and falls back to the default on a non-numeric value.
  • Nothing is queued: a refused request is simply answered 429 by the caller in api.py.
  • The clock is injectable (clock=time.monotonic) so tests can advance time.

gate.py 92 lines

Answers two questions on the request path for one tenant: is this tenant allowed to act (active, suspended, offboarded), and is there storage room left under its quota?

How it works

A TenantGate is built per tenant with the database, the tenant id, the tenant's workspace directory, an optional default quota, and two cache lifetimes: ttl (default 5 seconds) for status and quota, usage_ttl (default 30 seconds) for storage usage.

Status. The runtime database role cannot read the tenant registry table. Instead state() (line 34) opens a transaction for the tenant with db.tx(tenant_id) and runs:

select status, storage_quota_bytes from tia_tenant_state()

The database function resolves the tenant from the transaction setting, so a process can only ever learn its own tenant's state. No row gives status unknown; a null quota is replaced by default_quota. The result is cached for ttl seconds, which is how long a suspension takes to bite.

Statuscheck_active() result
activeReturns normally.
offboarding, offboardedGateError(410, "tenant_offboarded", ...)
suspendedGateError(403, "tenant_suspended", ...)
anything elseGateError(403, "tenant_unknown", "this tenant is not provisioned")

Storage. usage() (line 57) sums two things. For every table in RUNTIME_TABLES it runs select count(*), coalesce(sum(pg_column_size(x.*)), 0)::bigint from "<table>" x inside the tenant's transaction (row-level security limits it to the tenant's rows); this counts row data but not indexes. To that it adds dir_size(workspace), the bytes on disk. The result is cached for 30 seconds unless fresh=True. The figure is approximate, and summary() says so with "approximate": True.

check_room(adding) (line 78) returns immediately when there is no quota; otherwise it raises GateError(403, "storage_quota_exceeded", ...) when used + max(adding, 0) > quota. invalidate() drops the usage cache; api.py calls it after operations that grow storage.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
GateError18Carries status_code, code and detail for the HTTP layer.
TenantGate.state34Cached status and quota from tia_tenant_state().
TenantGate.check_active46Raises unless the tenant is active.
TenantGate.usage57Database row bytes plus workspace bytes.
TenantGate.check_room78Raises when the quota would be exceeded.
TenantGate.summary87Status, quota, usage and used fraction for GET /api/v1/tenant; always computes fresh usage.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The lock is held only to read and write the cache, not during the database query, so two concurrent requests after expiry may both query; the last result wins.
  • Usage is up to 30 seconds stale between invalidations, so a quota can be overshot by concurrent writes within that window.
  • A quota of 0 is a real quota for check_room (everything is refused), but summary reports storage_used_fraction as None because it tests if q.

health.py 214 lines

Liveness and readiness probes, the metrics that are computed at scrape time, the rule for who may read /metrics, and a tiny HTTP server so the worker can expose the same endpoints.

How it works

Liveness (liveness(), line 33) returns {"status": "ok", "uptime_seconds": ...} and touches no dependency, so an orchestrator never restarts a healthy process because the database was briefly unreachable.

Readiness (readiness(), line 46) runs named checks. Each goes through _check (line 37), which times the function and converts any exception into {"ok": False, "detail": "<exception class name>"}; only the class name is reported, never a message that might contain a connection string.

CheckWhat it doesFailure makes the service
databaseTakes a pooled connection (2 second timeout) and runs select 1.not_ready (503)
schemaselect tia_schema_version() compared with the newest migration file name (expected_schema_version()). Older than the code is a failure; newer is accepted with a note. Skipped when the database is unreachable.not_ready
workspaceCreates the directory if needed and writes a temporary file in it. "not configured" passes.not_ready
queueCounts runnable queued jobs and their oldest age, and counts workers whose heartbeat is newer than WORKER_STALE_SECONDS (60).degraded only (still 200)

The queue check adds a degraded note when jobs are queued but no worker has been seen, or when the oldest queued job has waited more than QUEUE_STALE_SECONDS (300). The final status is not_ready if any non-queue check failed, degraded if there are notes or the queue check itself failed, else ready. Only not_ready gives HTTP 503. The body always includes a note that this is platform readiness only: a suspended tenant or an incomplete analysis does not make the platform unready.

Scrape-time metrics (register_collectors, line 116). Some gauges are cheaper to compute when Prometheus asks than to maintain continuously. Each collector is a generator yielding (name, type, help, samples) tuples, registered with metrics.REGISTRY.collector. The queries are wrapped in Cached (line 104), which reuses a result for ttl seconds so many scrapes in a short time cost one query.

CollectorMetricsSource
jobstia_jobs by status (six known statuses), tia_jobs_oldest_queued_seconds, tia_jobs_retryingThree SQL queries on jobs in one transaction.
pooltia_db_pool_size, tia_db_pool_available, tia_db_pool_requests_waiting, tia_db_pool_connection_errors_totaldb.pool.get_stats()
repositories (only when a store is given)tia_repositories by scan status, tia_repository_oldest_successful_scan_seconds, tia_relationships by statestore.list_repos() and store.list_relationships()

When a tenant id is given (the API process), the jobs query first runs select set_config('tia.tenant_id', %s, true); the third argument makes the setting local to the transaction so it cannot leak to the next user of the pooled connection. The worker passes no tenant and counts every tenant's jobs.

Who may scrape (metrics_allowed, line 172): if TIA_METRICS_TOKEN is set, a matching bearer token is required (constant-time comparison). Without a token, only loopback clients are allowed, unless TIA_METRICS_ALLOW_ANY is truthy.

Worker endpoints (serve_worker_endpoints, line 187) starts a ThreadingHTTPServer on a daemon thread serving /healthz, /readyz and /metrics. Its readiness runs the database and schema checks only (check_queue=False, no workspace) and adds a poll check: the worker is not ready if its loop last polled 60 or more seconds ago, read from the WORKER_POLL gauge.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
expected_schema_version29The largest migration file stem.
liveness33Process is up; uptime.
_check37Runs one check, timing it and catching exceptions.
readiness46Returns (http_status, body).
Cached104Callable that memoises a function's result for ttl seconds.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Schema versions are compared as strings (v < expected, line 60), which is correct as long as migration file names sort lexicographically in order.
  • A worker that has never polled reports age 0 and passes the poll check, because the expression on line 203 substitutes the current time for a missing value.
  • Cached treats a None result as "not cached" and holds its lock while the function runs, so concurrent scrapes wait for one query.
  • The worker server silences request logging (log_message does nothing) and answers unknown paths with 404 and {}.

reportpage.py 417 lines

Renders one analysis report as a self-contained HTML page: the page a pull-request comment or check run links to. It contains no JavaScript and loads no external resources.

How it works

The module is a set of functions that each turn one part of the report dictionary into an HTML string, plus render_page, which assembles them. Every value taken from the report passes through html.escape (imported as _e), because finding text ultimately comes from repository content. The page is built from the report the caller is allowed to see, so redaction has already happened before this module runs.

render_page(report, analysis_url) (line 359) produces the page in this order:

  1. Title line with the status as a badge; repository, the first 7 characters of the base and proposed revisions, the pull request number if any, the change type and the analysis id.
  2. A warning banner from STATUS_NOTE for the statuses complete_with_gaps, partial, running and failed; a failed analysis also shows its failure_reason.
  3. Summary cards: total findings, material findings, and a card per severity that has a non-zero count. If findings were withheld by redaction, a banner states how many are omitted.
  4. Links to the JSON report, the comment text, and the evidence (only when there is an evidence snapshot).
  5. "What to test" (_test_scope) and the evidence sections; both are skipped for a failed analysis.
  6. "Findings": one table row per finding (_finding_row).
  7. "Changes analysed" (_components), "Unknowns" (_unknowns), "Coverage" (_coverage), "Evidence" (the snapshot id and the reproduce call).
  8. A footer: "Advisory only", the generation time, the configuration version and the extractor versions.

A finding row (_finding_row, line 136) has five cells: severity with the rule that set it; component with its repository and a coloured type chip; evidence status with the resolution strategy; "Why"; and a graph. The "Why" cell is built by _why (line 94) as a definition list with labelled parts instead of one long sentence: "What changed", "Why this is affected", "Path from the change", "Evidence", "What to do" and "Limits". The first two come from _split_reason (line 88), which splits the report's reason text on the literal separator ". Change: ". Optional collapsible <details> blocks follow for data access, tests, required verification and runtime observations.

The path drawing (_path_svg, line 103) is inline SVG, since the page allows no script. Nodes are stacked top to bottom, 52 units apart, in a 250-unit-wide drawing. For each hop it draws a line and a triangle arrow head (a polygon, not an SVG marker, because marker ids would have to be unique across the page) with the relationship name beside it. The line colour comes from EDGE_COLOR by evidence status; an inferred link is dashed. The first node (what changed) has a red ring; the last node (what is affected) is larger and, when a severity is given, filled with the severity colour from SEV_FILL. Names longer than 34 characters are truncated with the full name in a <title> tooltip. A path with fewer than two nodes renders as a dash.

_short (line 69) produces the short name of a path hop: [hidden] for a redacted hop, call file:line for a call site, the part after :: for a qualified name, otherwise the part after the first :.

"What to test" (_test_scope, line 243) begins with _regression (line 279): the written test cases to run again, those that could not be mapped (in a banner asking for manual review), those not affected (collapsed), and those added by the change itself. Then a table with one row per entry point reached by the change: its label and route, service, the change effect (breaking, behaviour change, compatible change, no functional change, unknown (file-level), coloured through EFFECT_CLASS), the why-list with the tests found and their evidence states, and the path drawing. After the table come banners for rule gaps ("Possible rule gap", or "Rule moved into the shared service" when the change resolved it), consistency checks and configuration checks.

Evidence sections (_evidence_sections, line 184) render, when present: recommended tests with priority and last result; test gaps; deployments per repository and environment, marking in bold a deployed revision that is not the analysed one, with rollback and build notes from _dep_notes; builds per revision; and failure signatures.

Styling. One CSS string (lines 19-45) is embedded in the page. It defines colour variables with a dark-mode override, a print rule that expands all collapsed sections, and below 700 pixels a .stack rule that turns each table row into a card and shows each cell's data-label attribute as its caption, which is why every cell carries that attribute.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Most optional report sections are read with .get and render a muted placeholder when absent ("No findings.", "None recorded.").
  • Some lookups are direct: EFFECT_CLASS[e['change_effect']] and EFFECT_TEXT[...] (lines 255, 287, 310) raise KeyError for an effect value outside the five known ones, and a finding without id, severity, severity_rule, component or repository does the same in _finding_row.
  • Unknowns are sorted so those that affect confidence come first (line 327).
  • In _components, function-level changes without an effect and file-level changes are skipped; they are not listed as changes analysed.
  • The page declares <meta name="robots" content="noindex">; the strict Content-Security-Policy header is added by the route in api.py, not here.

mcp.py 119 lines

The protocol side of TIA's Model Context Protocol (MCP) server: the list of tools offered to coding assistants and a function that answers one JSON-RPC message. It contains no data access; the tool implementations are passed in by api.py.

How it works

MCP is a JSON-RPC 2.0 protocol through which an AI assistant discovers and calls tools. This module declares protocol version 2025-06-18 and answers each POST with a single JSON response.

The tool catalogue. TOOLS is a list of eleven descriptors. Each has a name, title, description, a JSON Schema for its arguments with additionalProperties: false, and annotations stating it is read-only, non-destructive and idempotent. There is deliberately no write tool: an assistant cannot record a verdict or change anything.

ToolArguments (required in bold)Returns
get_impact_reportanalysis_idThe full report as the caller may see it.
find_impact_reportrepository, pull_request, revisionThe newest report for that pull request or revision.
get_test_scopeanalysis_idTest scope, recommendations, gaps, and confidence-affecting unknowns.
get_policy_outcomeanalysis_idEach release-policy rule's outcome.
get_tester_packanalysis_idRetest list with coverage gate, locator pack, API matrix.
get_coveragerepositoriesTechnical coverage with no change involved.
get_api_inventoryrepositoriesEndpoints with inputs and test matrices.
get_ui_inventoryrepositoriesPages with elements and locators.
check_test_casetitle, entry_point, steps, expected, repositoriesWhat one test case covers and misses.
get_shared_componentslimitShared components and rule gaps.
query_graphquestion, component, repository, limitA direct graph answer with status ok, ambiguous or not_found.

INSTRUCTIONS (line 15) is text returned at initialisation that tells the assistant how to use the results: narrate only fields present in the report, make no blast-radius claim without a TIA analysis id, always mention unknowns and the analysis status, treat "covered" as "a test addresses the aspect" and not "it passes", and never guess when a graph answer is ambiguous.

handle(msg, tools, server_version) (line 90) processes one message:

  1. A message that is not an object, lacks "jsonrpc": "2.0" or has no string method gets error -32600 (invalid request).
  2. A message without an id is a notification (for example initialized): the function returns None and nothing is sent.
  3. initialize returns the protocol version, the capability {"tools": {"listChanged": false}}, server information and the instructions.
  4. ping returns an empty result; tools/list returns TOOLS.
  5. tools/call looks up the tool by name. Unknown names get -32602 with a message listing the available tools. The arguments must be an object with no key outside the schema's properties and with every required key present, else -32602.
  6. The tool function is called. A ToolError becomes a normal result with isError: true and the message as text, which is how MCP reports a tool-level failure to the model. A success returns the output twice: as JSON text in content and as structuredContent.
  7. Any other method gets -32601 (method not supported).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
TOOLS20, 51The eleven tool descriptors (four written out, seven built with _tool).
_tool46Builds a descriptor with the read-only annotations.
ToolError82Raised by a tool implementation for an expected failure.
_error86A JSON-RPC error object.
handle90One message in, one response (or None) out.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Argument validation checks key names and required keys only, not value types or the enum on question; type problems surface in the tool implementation.
  • Only ToolError is caught around the tool call (line 115). Any other exception from a tool propagates to the caller.
  • A tool must be both in TOOLS and in the tools dictionary passed in; a declared tool with no implementation is reported as unknown.
  • A malformed message that happens to have no id is answered with an error whose id is null, because the validity check runs before the notification check.

kfadapter.py 391 lines

Converts a finished impact report (and optionally its tester pack) into the QueryResponse format that Knowledge Fabric (KF) agents already consume, so those agents can read TIA's results without a new client.

How it works

Knowledge Fabric is a separate retrieval system whose answers consist of "chunks" (pieces of text with metadata) and "paths" (relationships between entities), each with a score. This adapter expresses a TIA report in those terms. The module docstring notes that KF's own source file was not available: the models follow an integration document field by field and "must be re-checked against retriever_api.py before production use".

Wire models (lines 35-109). All models inherit from _Strict, which sets extra="forbid", so an unexpected key is a validation error and the contract cannot drift silently. QueryRequest has KF's fields (intent, query, domain, top_k_semantic and top_k_graph, each 1-100, default 10) plus TIA's selectors: analysis_id, repository, pull_request, revision, and include_tester_pack (default true). QueryResponse holds chunks, reranked_chunks, paths, reranked_paths and metadata.

Scoring. A finding's score is its severity score multiplied by an evidence factor:

SeverityScoreEvidence statusFactor
critical1.0inferred0.5
high0.85stale0.5
medium0.6unknown0.3
low0.35anything else (confirmed)1.0
other0.2

So an inferred critical finding (0.5) ranks below a confirmed medium one (0.6). Evidence status is never upgraded; it is repeated in each chunk's extracted_fields and each path's source_evidence.

What becomes a chunk. Each chunk's block_id is <analysis id>#<suffix>; weaviate_uuid is always empty and page_number always null because TIA has no such concepts.

SourceBuilt bycategory / block_typeScoreSuffix
Each findingchunk_for (145)the finding's impact type / tia_findingscore_of#i
Each unknownunknown_chunk (171)unknown / tia_unknown0.0#unknown-j
Each entry point to testscope_chunks (190)test_scope / tia_test_targetEFFECT_SCORE times evidence factor#test-k
