Each selected test casescope_chunksregression_testEFFECT_SCORE#regression-k
Each unmapped test casescope_chunksregression_unmapped0.4#unmapped-k
Tester pack gate verdictpack_chunks (283)coverage_gateGATE_SCORE#gate
Each entry point not fully coveredpack_chunkscoverage_gapGATE_SCORE#gap-k
Each touched endpoint's test matrixpack_chunksapi_matrix0.5#api-k
Each page's locator packpack_chunksui_locators0.5#ui-k

EFFECT_SCORE is breaking 0.9, behaviour_change 0.6, unknown 0.4, compatible_change 0.3, no_functional_change 0.1. GATE_SCORE is gaps 0.9, not_evaluated 0.6, covered 0.3: the worse the coverage, the higher the rank.

A finding chunk's text is a sentence built from severity, impact type, component, status and reason; if the evidence is not one of source_confirmed, contract_confirmed or corroborated, the text gains the caveat "Evidence is X: treat this as a lead, not a fact." Structured data goes into extracted_fields as a JSON string (and, for larger payloads such as test cases and UI elements, into extracted_knowledge). To keep chunks bounded, an API matrix chunk carries at most MAX_CASES (60) cases and a UI chunk at most MAX_ELEMENTS (80) elements, with the true totals in the fields.

Paths. path_text (line 122) writes an impact path as a chain such as (function:"a")<-[:CALLS]-(function:"b"). TIA stores edges pointing from consumer to provider, while an impact path runs from the change outward, so most hops are drawn with a backward arrow; only relationships in FORWARD (HANDLES, PUBLISHES) are drawn forward. This keeps the relationship names and directions exactly as TIA stores them. path_for (line 157) builds a PathItem from a finding with at least two nodes and one edge; scope_paths (line 252) does the same for each entry point to test.

Assembly (to_query_response, line 343).

  1. Build finding chunks, unknown chunks and finding paths.
  2. Order findings by: material first, then higher score, then original position. reranked_chunks starts with the first top_k_semantic of these; reranked_paths follows the same order.
  3. Split the test-scope chunks into two groups and rank each on its own, each cut to top_k_semantic: the tester group (rule gaps, then entry points, then consistency checks) and the regression group (summary, selected test cases, configuration checks, unmapped cases). The comment explains the purpose: the regression answer never pushes the entry points to test out of the response.
  4. Append tester paths sorted by score, then cut the combined path list to top_k_graph.
  5. Append tester-pack chunks in the order of PACK_RANK (gate, gaps, API matrices, locators), each kind cut to top_k_semantic.
  6. Append every unknown chunk, uncut: unknowns "are never ranked away".
  7. Fill metadata.errors with everything that limits trust in the answer: repositories the tester pack could not read, a report status of partial, complete_with_gaps or failed, each unknown that affects confidence, the redaction note, and a truncated traversal.

The unranked chunks and paths lists contain everything; metadata.pattern is the analysis id, status and repository; max_depth comes from the report's traversal coverage.

empty(req, errors, t0) (line 336) returns a response with no chunks or paths and the given errors; api.py uses it for every case where no report can be returned.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
QueryRequest / QueryResponse39 / 101The request and response wire models.
ChunkItem / PathItem / RetrievalMetadata58 / 74 / 91The item and metadata models.
score_of113Severity score times evidence factor.
path_text122An impact path as a symbolic chain in TIA's stored directions.
chunk_for / path_for145 / 157One finding as a chunk / as a path.
unknown_chunk171One unknown as a chunk with score 0.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A redacted path node has no node value and is labelled [redacted] (_node_label, line 118).
  • A finding with fewer than two path nodes or no edges produces a chunk but no path.
  • Step 4 cuts finding paths and tester paths together to top_k_graph, with finding paths first; many findings can therefore leave no room for tester paths, whereas chunks of each group have their own allowance.
  • scope_chunks and pack_chunks index many keys directly (e["change_effect"], gp["rule"]["fields"], gate["outcome"]); a report or pack missing them, or an effect outside EFFECT_SCORE, raises KeyError.
  • The comment on FORWARD (line 31) speaks of "the one traversed relationship" but the set holds two names.
  • detected_domains simply echoes the request's domain with confidence 1.0, or is empty; TIA does no domain detection.

Orchestration, ingestion and jobs

This layer turns a request ("analyse this pull request") into finished work. repos.py and upload.py bring source code into git repositories that TIA owns, jobs.py is the PostgreSQL queue that holds the work, and analysis_service.py is the coordinator that resolves a request to exact commits, runs the analysis and contains the handlers the queue worker executes. relationships.py, incidents.py, temporal.py and calibration.py are pure calculation modules that the coordinator and the API call for relationship history, incident investigation, release comparison and rule precision.

analysis_service.py 479 lines

The coordinator shared by the synchronous API and the queue worker. It converts user-facing references (a branch name, a pull-request number) into a concrete request made of exact commit hashes, runs the analysis for that request, keeps the landscape graph and relationship history current, and defines the four job handlers the worker runs.

How it works

The module separates two phases. resolve() produces a concrete request: a plain dictionary in which every repository is named by a commit hash, so the same dictionary always describes the same analysis. execute() runs that dictionary. Because the concrete request is deterministic, job_key() can hash it to detect duplicate submissions.

resolve(svc, p, fetch_pr, cfg) (lines 36-73). svc is a RepoService; p is the caller's request; cfg is the tenant's configuration (DEFAULT when absent).

  1. svc.get(target) is called only for its side effect: it raises IngestError if the target repository is not registered (line 42).
  2. The head commit is found. If p["pr"] is set, svc.fetch_pull_request fetches the pull request and returns its commit; when fetch_pr is false this is refused with an error telling the caller to pass head. Otherwise p["head"] is resolved as a branch or hash. With neither, an IngestError is raised (lines 43-50).
  3. The base commit is the merge base (the newest commit common to both histories) of the resolved base reference and the head: svc.merge_base(target, svc.resolve(target, p.get("base")), head_sha). A missing base resolves to the default branch (line 51).
  4. All repository records are loaded with one svc.list() call into registry. The scope is p["repos"] or every registered repository; the target is added if missing, and the list is de-duplicated and sorted (lines 52-56).
  5. Every other repository in scope is resolved to a commit: the revision named in p["revisions"] for it, or its default branch. With more than four other repositories this runs in a ThreadPoolExecutor of at most 16 threads; each task runs inside contextvars.copy_context().run so the per-request tenant binding follows the call into the thread (lines 57-66).
  6. The result dictionary is built. max_depth and max_results are min(requested or tenant value, tenant value), so the tenant's limits are both the default and the ceiling. The tenant's strip_prefixes, exclude_patterns, test_naming, time_budget_seconds, full configuration (cfg.to_dict()), version and digest are copied in, so the job carries the configuration it must run under (lines 67-73).

Keys. job_key(c) (line 76) is the SHA-256 of the JSON of target, base, head, scope, revisions, max_depth, max_results plus the configuration identity (TenantConfig...ident(CONFIG_VERSION), or just CONFIG_VERSION when the request has no configuration). group_key(c) (line 82) is pr:<target>:<pr> for a pull request, otherwise head:<target>:<head_ref or head>; jobs with the same group key supersede each other in the queue. pr_event_job_key (line 301) hashes [provider, repo, pr, head_sha, base_ref] with the configuration identity, and pr_event_group_key (line 306) returns the same pr:<repo>:<pr> form, so a webhook job and a direct analysis of the same pull request are in one group.

execute(svc, store, c, secrets) (lines 150-172).

  1. Loads all repository records once and builds paths, a map from repository name to the directory git reads from.
  2. Selects stale relationships whose stale_reason is in UNVERIFIABLE (scan_failed, parse_failed) and whose consumer and provider are both in scope. These are passed to the analysis so it can report them as possibly still real.
  3. Rebuilds the TenantConfig from the request (TenantConfig.from_stored), or uses DEFAULT.
  4. Calls analyze(AnalysisRequest(...), store) with the paths, commits, limits, the scan freshness of each repository (freshness_of) and an evidence view bound to the target and head (EvidenceStore(...).for_analysis).
  5. If the report's status is not failed, pin_analysis writes git references that keep the analysed commits from being garbage-collected. If the tenant has coordination.auto_notify set and there are findings, notify_owners runs; any exception from it is caught and recorded as a team_notification_failed audit event, so a notification problem never fails the analysis.

notify_owners (lines 101-135) sends one notification per owning team. The ownership map is the report's owners overlaid with the configuration recorded in the analysis's evidence snapshot (_snapshot_config), while delivery channels come from the tenant's current configuration. It reads earlier notification evidence records for this analysis and skips any team that already has a record with status == "sent", so only failed deliveries are retried. For each remaining team it picks a channel with channel_for; no channel gives a failed outcome with an explanatory detail. A ServiceNow client is created lazily on the first servicenow_task channel and, if that creation raises ItsmError, the error text is reused as the failure for every later ServiceNow team. Each attempt is stored as a notification evidence record and a team_notified audit event. The function returns coordination_status, which gathers the notification and acknowledgement records of the analysis and passes them to coordination.status.

reproduce (lines 187-220) re-runs a stored analysis from its evidence snapshot. It refuses a missing or failed analysis and an analysis without a snapshot. It checks that every commit named in the snapshot (plus the target's base and head) still resolves in the clone, raising "cannot reproduce: revision ... is no longer available" otherwise. It then calls analyze with every parameter taken from the snapshot, the snapshot's own configuration, evidence pinned to the recorded ids (.pinned(snap["evidence_ids"])) and persist=False. The result is compared with the original by report.differences; versions_match says whether the configuration version and extractor versions are the ones the snapshot recorded. An analysis_reproduced audit event is written.

Landscape maintenance. The landscape is the graph of all registered repositories at their default branches.

  • landscape_extracts (line 229) extracts each repository at its default-branch commit using the tenant's strip_prefixes. A repository that raises is skipped: a graph_extract_failed audit event and svc.record_scan(name, error=...) record the failure. Success calls svc.record_scan with the commit and the count of unparseable files.
  • refresh_landscape (line 248) calls landscape_extracts, then get_or_build to obtain the graph for those extracts, moves the LANDSCAPE head pointer with store.set_head (auditing graph_version_advanced when it moved), and finally calls relationships.reconcile. With no extracts it reconciles against an empty Graph() and a graph id of None.
  • rescan_landscape (line 270) is the integrity check. For each repository it obtains the incremental result (the cached extract, or a fresh incremental extraction) and a forced full extraction (force=True), compares their canonical JSON (_norm sorts keys and turns tuples into lists so a database round trip is not counted as a difference), stores the full extract, and records drift. It then rebuilds the graph with rebuild=True, compares it with the previously stored graph of the same id (graph_drift), moves the head and audits graph_rescanned.

make_handlers (lines 314-479) returns a dictionary from job kind to handler function. Shared setup: one JobQueue, the base ScmConfig, one GitHubApp object from from_scm (so installation tokens are cached across jobs) and an AdoConfig from the environment. The inner helper cancelled(job) re-reads the job row and returns its cancel_requested flag. A handler signals a failure that must not be retried by raising PermanentError; any other exception is left to the worker's retry logic. _transient(msg) (line 310) treats an IngestError as retryable only when its text contains "Cannot reach" or "timed out".

Handler impact_analysis (lines 372-382) runs an already-resolved request submitted through the API.

  1. Creates a tenant-bound Store and reads the job state; if cancellation was requested it returns {"skipped": "superseded by a newer submission"} without doing any work.
  2. Creates a RepoService and calls execute with job.payload (the concrete request) and a SecretStore when a key wrapper is configured.
  3. An IngestError becomes a PermanentError. The result is {"analysis_id", "status"}.

Handler github_pull_request (lines 329-370) processes a GitHub pull_request webhook.

  1. Returns {"skipped": "superseded by a newer push"} if the job is already cancelled.
  2. Chooses a token. with_stored_token fills in the tenant's stored github/token when the environment has none. If a GitHub App is configured (tokens.uses_app), TokenSource.token_for obtains an installation token using the payload's full_name and installation_id as a hint. An AppError with code not_installed, installation_gone, no_access, not_configured or bad_key becomes a PermanentError; any other code (unreachable) is re-raised so the job is retried. No token at all is a PermanentError.
  3. Inside one try: svc.sync fetches the repository, refresh_landscape updates the graph, the tenant configuration is read, resolve is called with pr and base_ref (which fetches the pull-request ref), and execute runs the analysis.
  4. On IngestError, refresh_landscape runs again so the failed fetch marks the repository's relationships stale; then the error is re-raised if transient, or converted to PermanentError.
  5. Cancellation is checked a second time. If a newer push arrived during the analysis the handler returns the analysis_id with skipped_publish and does not write to the pull request.
  6. publish_to_github posts the comment and check run. The allowed set of repositories is computed by comment_scope with an AccessStore; the flag TIA_AUTH_REQUIRED is read from the environment here. A PublishError with permanent set becomes PermanentError; otherwise it is re-raised for retry.
  7. audit_publication records what was published, and the handler returns the analysis id, status, comment action and check-run result.

Handler ado_pull_request (lines 384-428) processes an Azure DevOps service hook.

  1. Cancellation check, as above.
  2. Token: ado_cfg.token or the tenant secret ado/token; none is a PermanentError. The organisation URL from the event (p["ado"]["base_url"]) must pass ado.allowed_host, otherwise PermanentError.
  3. svc.sync, then svc.fetch_pull_request, whose result is kept as merged. The head is the event's head_sha if gitutil.resolve_rev finds that commit in the clone, otherwise merged.
  4. refresh_landscape, then resolve with head (not pr, so nothing is fetched again), then concrete["pr"] is set by hand, then execute.
  5. The same IngestError handling and second cancellation check as the GitHub handler.
  6. The comment view is built by audience_view; when TIA_AUTH_REQUIRED is on, the allowed list is just the pull request's own repository. ado.publish writes the thread and status; ado.AdoError is mapped by its permanent flag.
  7. audit_publication is called with provider ado and the name <project>/<repository>.

Handler scm_pull_request (lines 430-476) processes GitLab merge requests and Bitbucket pull requests; p["provider"] says which.

  1. Cancellation check; HostConfig.from_env(provider); token from the environment or the tenant secret <provider>/token, else PermanentError.
  2. svc.sync. svc.fetch_pull_request is attempted and an IngestError from it is swallowed (merged = None), because Bitbucket Cloud has no pull-request refs and the source branch was already fetched by the sync.
  3. If the event's head_sha does not resolve in the clone, the head becomes merged, or failing that the resolved source branch p["host"]["source_ref"].
  4. refresh_landscape, resolve, set concrete["pr"], execute; same error handling and second cancellation check.
  5. scmhosts.publish writes the comment and commit status; ScmHostError is mapped by its permanent flag; audit_publication records the result.

Key functions and classes

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A caller can lower max_depth and max_results but never raise them above the tenant's values (lines 69-70).
  • job_key does not hash strip_prefixes, exclude_patterns or test_naming directly; they are covered only through the configuration identity.
  • Cancellation is cooperative. The pull-request handlers check the flag before starting and again before publishing; the analysis itself is not interrupted. JobQueue.is_latest is not used here.
  • In the error path of the pull-request handlers, refresh_landscape is called inside the except IngestError block; an exception raised by that second call would replace the original error.
  • execute defaults time_budget_s to 60.0 and test_naming to True when the request lacks them (older queued payloads).
  • reproduce reports identical and versions_match separately: a difference with mismatched versions is expected, a difference with matching versions is not.
  • rescan_landscape returns early with graph_id: None when no repository could be rescanned.

jobs.py 184 lines

A job queue stored in one PostgreSQL table, jobs. The API process adds and inspects jobs for its own tenant; worker processes claim, run and finish jobs across tenants.

How it works

A Job (line 31) is the in-memory copy of a claimed row: tenant_id, id, kind, key, group_key, group_seq, payload, attempts, max_attempts, cancel_requested and the worker_id that claimed it. COLS (line 27) lists the columns in the order Job.from_row expects.

StatusMeaning and how a job gets there
queuedWaiting. Set by enqueue, by a retryable fail, and by reap_expired.
runningClaimed by a worker that holds a lease (locked_by, locked_until).
succeededSet by succeed, with the handler's result.
failedPermanent failure, or attempts exhausted.
cancelledA queued job cancelled by cancel.
supersededA queued job replaced by a newer job with the same group_key.

The producer methods open self.db.tx(tenant_id), a transaction bound to one tenant. Several of their statements filter only by key, id or group_key with no tenant_id condition; they depend on that tenant binding (the module docstring names a row-level security policy) to see only the tenant's rows. The consumer methods use self.db.pool.connection() directly and always filter by tenant_id.

enqueue (lines 54-76), all in one transaction:

  1. Deduplicating insert. insert into jobs (...) values (..., now() + make_interval(secs => %s)) on conflict (tenant_id, key) do nothing returning id. A new key yields the new id. An existing (tenant_id, key) yields no row. delay_s sets run_after, the earliest time the job may be claimed.
  2. Existing job. When nothing was inserted, select id, status from jobs where key = %s reads the existing job. Unless force is true and the status is failed, cancelled or superseded, the method returns {"job_id", "deduplicated": True, "status"} and changes nothing.
  3. Forced re-run. Otherwise an update resets the row: status='queued', attempts=0, error and result cleared, cancel_requested=false, lease cleared, run_after=now(), new payload and group key, and group_seq=nextval('job_group_seq') so the row counts as the newest in its group.
  4. Supersede. If a group_key was given, two updates apply "newest wins": set status='superseded' where group_key=%s and status='queued' and id<>%s retires older waiting jobs, and set cancel_requested=true where group_key=%s and status='running' and id<>%s asks an older running job to stop.
  5. Returns {"job_id", "deduplicated": False, "status": "queued"}.

Other producer methods. get (line 78) selects one row by id and returns its fields with timestamps as ISO strings; the payload is included only with with_payload=True. cancel (line 91) tries update ... set status='cancelled' where id=%s and status='queued'; if that matches nothing it tries set cancel_requested=true where id=%s and status='running'; it returns "cancelled", "cancel_requested" or None. is_latest (line 102) reads the job's group_key and group_seq and returns true when no row satisfies group_key=%s and group_seq > %s and status <> 'cancelled'; a job without a group is always latest, an unknown job never is. beat (line 114) upserts a row in worker_heartbeats (on conflict (worker_id) do update set last_seen = now(), info = excluded.info) so readiness checks can tell whether any worker is alive.

claim (lines 121-151) selects and locks one job fairly, in one transaction.

  1. Candidate query. The inner select takes every job with status='queued' and run_after <= now(), optionally restricted to the given kinds, whose tenant passes the database function tia_tenant_active and, when this_region() returns a region, tia_tenant_in_region. It left-joins a subquery counting each tenant's running jobs (coalesce(r.n, 0) as running) and numbers each tenant's queued jobs oldest first (row_number() over (partition by j.tenant_id order by j.created_at) as rn). The outer query sorts by running, rn, created_at and keeps 30 rows. Sorting by running first favours the tenant with the fewest running jobs; sorting by rn next places every tenant's first job ahead of any tenant's second, which is round-robin.
  2. Per-tenant lock. For each candidate, select pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(%s, 1)) on the tenant id takes a transaction-scoped advisory lock without waiting. If another worker holds it the candidate is skipped. This serialises claims per tenant so the following count cannot be raced.
  3. Cap. select tia_tenant_job_cap(%s) returns the tenant's own limit. The effective cap is min(per_tenant_cap, tenant_cap) when the tenant has one, else per_tenant_cap (default 4): a tenant may lower the deployment's cap but never raise it. If select count(*) from jobs where tenant_id=%s and status='running' is at or above the cap, the candidate is skipped.
  4. Claim. A common table expression re-selects the row where tenant_id=%s and id=%s and status='queued' for update skip locked, and the update joined to it sets status='running', attempts=attempts+1, locked_by and locked_until=now()+make_interval(secs => lease_s) (the lease, default 300 seconds), returning the COLS columns. If the row was taken meanwhile the update returns nothing and the loop continues.
  5. The first successful claim is returned as a Job; with no claim the method returns None.

Finishing. Every consumer update includes locked_by=%s, so a worker that lost its lease cannot change a job another worker now owns.

  • heartbeat (line 153) extends locked_until for a job that is still running and still owned, and returns the cancel_requested column. The return value is false when the row no longer matches or cancellation was requested.
  • succeed (line 161) sets status='succeeded', stores the result and clears error and lease.
  • fail (line 167) first passes the message through scrub_text(error, generic=True) so a secret in an error cannot reach the table, and truncates it to 2000 characters. The update sets status = case when %s or attempts >= max_attempts then 'failed' else 'queued' end (the parameter is permanent) and run_after = now() + make_interval(secs => %s * power(2, greatest(attempts - 1, 0))). Because attempts was incremented at claim time, the delays with the default base_delay_s=5 are 5 seconds after the first failure and 10 after the second; with the default max_attempts=3 the third failure is final.
  • reap_expired (line 177) finds jobs where status='running' and locked_until < now(), meaning the worker died, and sets them back to queued with run_after=now() (or failed when attempts are exhausted) with the error 'lease expired (worker lost)'. It returns the number of rows changed.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
Job31Dataclass for a claimed job; from_row (45) builds it from a COLS row.
JobQueue.enqueue54Idempotent insert, optional forced re-queue, supersede of older group members.
JobQueue.get78Job state for the API.
JobQueue.cancel91Cancels a queued job or flags a running one.
JobQueue.is_latest102Whether a newer non-cancelled job exists in the same group.
JobQueue.beat114Worker liveness upsert.
JobQueue.claim121Fair, capped, leased claim of one job.
JobQueue.heartbeat153Lease extension and cancellation signal.
JobQueue.succeed / fail161 / 167Terminal or retry transitions with exponential backoff.
JobQueue.reap_expired177Recovers jobs whose lease expired.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Delivery is at-least-once: a job whose worker dies after doing the work but before succeed is run again, so handlers must tolerate repetition (the publishers update comments in place for this reason).
  • In enqueue, the follow-up select ... where key = %s is unpacked directly; if the conflicting row were not visible to the session, fetchone() would return None and the unpacking would raise TypeError.
  • In claim, advisory locks taken for tenants that turn out to be at their cap stay held until the transaction ends, and only the first 30 candidates are examined. If those 30 all belong to tenants that are locked or capped, the call returns None even when later rows are claimable; the worker's next poll tries again.
  • fail does not check status='running', only ownership through locked_by. It also sets run_after when the job becomes failed, which has no effect.
  • reap_expired does not look at cancel_requested; a re-queued job keeps the flag and the handler's first cancellation check will skip it.
  • The string prefix f on line 145 has no placeholders; it is harmless.

repos.py 463 lines

Registers repositories and keeps TIA's read-only copies of them current. It clones remotes as bare repositories, reads local repositories in place, fetches pull-request refs, resolves references to commit hashes and discovers the repositories of a GitHub organisation or Azure DevOps project.

How it works

The registry record is the Repo dataclass (line 38). Its fields fall in three groups:

FieldMeaning
name, url, provider, kind, pathIdentity. provider is github, ado, gitlab, bitbucket, git, local or upload. kind is remote (a bare clone under the workspace), local (a user's directory, read in place) or upload.
default_branch, last_synced, last_commit, last_error, branchesResult of the last fetch.
scan_status, scan_error, analysed_commit, last_successful_scan, scan_files_failedResult of the last extraction. scan_status is never, ok, partial (some files could not be parsed) or failed.

Running git with a token. _git (line 109) is the only place a network git command runs. It sets GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 and GCM_INTERACTIVE=never so git never waits for input, and merges in _auth_env (line 84), which passes the token as an HTTP header through environment variables instead of the command line:

return {"GIT_CONFIG_COUNT": "1", "GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0": "http.extraheader",
        "GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0": f"Authorization: Basic {b64}"}

b64 is the Base64 of user:token, where the user name depends on the provider: x-access-token for GitHub, an empty string for Azure DevOps, x-token-auth for Bitbucket and oauth2 for GitLab and everything else. The call runs inside a tracing span and increments the CONNECTOR and CONNECTOR_DURATION metrics with the operation (clone, fetch, ls-remote or other) and outcome. The default timeout is 900 seconds. A non-zero exit raises IngestError whose text is the stderr after _redact (which replaces the token and three Base64 forms of it with ***) and _friendly (line 133), which maps common git errors to one of four fixed messages: authentication failed, repository not found, cannot reach the host, TLS certificate failure.

Registering a remote (_register_remote, lines 213-244).

  1. The URL scheme must be https, http, ssh or file; file is refused unless allow_local is set.
  2. A user name or password embedded in a non-SSH URL is refused.
  3. _require_tls_for_token refuses to send a token over plain http unless the host is loopback.
  4. If allowed_hosts is configured, the host must be in it. For non-file URLs, _public_host calls netsafe.resolve to require that the host resolves only to public addresses (skipped when allow_local is set). These two checks prevent a tenant from aiming the server at internal network addresses.
  5. detect_provider classifies the URL, the name is cleaned by _clean, and a duplicate name is refused.
  6. Any leftover directory is removed, airgap.guard is consulted, and git clone --bare --no-tags runs.
  7. _fetch_heads is attempted (errors ignored) and _refresh_meta fills the default branch, last commit and branch list.

register (line 158) requires exactly one of url and path, stores the record with store.put_repo and writes a repository_registered audit event. _register_local (line 199) requires allow_local, an existing directory and a successful git rev-parse --git-dir.

Sync (sync, lines 261-280). For a remote, _fetch_heads runs git fetch --prune --no-tags origin +refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*, which mirrors every remote branch and deletes branches removed upstream. _follow_remote_default then runs git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD and repoints the clone's HEAD with git symbolic-ref, because a remote's default branch can change after registration; a failure here is ignored. _refresh_meta updates the record. On IngestError the record gets last_error, scan_status="failed" and scan_error, is saved, a repository_sync_failed audit event is written and the error is re-raised.

Scan state (record_scan, lines 282-298). Called after an extraction. If no error was passed but the repository still has a last_error from a failed fetch, that error is used: extracting the old revision again does not clear a failed fetch. Otherwise the status becomes partial when files_failed is non-zero, else ok. If nothing changed compared with the previous four values the method returns without writing; last_successful_scan is updated only when something changed and there is no error.

References. resolve (line 354) tries the given reference as written, then refs/heads/<ref>, then origin/<ref>; with no reference it resolves the default branch. merge_base (line 363) runs git merge-base and falls back to its first argument when the histories are unrelated. fetch_pull_request (line 315) fetches the host-specific ref into refs/tia/pr/<n>:

ProviderSource refs tried, in order
gitlabrefs/merge-requests/<n>/head
bitbucketrefs/pull-requests/<n>/from
any otherrefs/pull/<n>/head, then refs/pull/<n>/merge

pin_commits (line 335) writes refs/tia/pin/<sha> for each commit so it survives a force-push and garbage collection. It first reads the ref file directly (gitutil._read_ref) and skips the git process when the pin already exists; local repositories are never modified.

Discovery (discover, lines 399-436). For ado it calls <base>/<org>/<project>/_apis/git/repositories?api-version=7.1 with Basic authentication (empty user, token as password) and drops disabled repositories. For github it pages through /orgs/<org>/repos, falling back to /users/<org>/repos on a 404, 100 per page, and drops archived and disabled repositories. A custom api_base must be an http(s) URL on a public host.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Git is used only to read; nothing in this module pushes.
  • _redact covers the raw token and the Base64 forms for the users x-access-token, empty and oauth2, but not x-token-auth, which _auth_env uses for Bitbucket.
  • In _follow_remote_default (line 253) and fetch_pull_request (line 317) the string intended as the docstring comes after the first statement, so Python treats it as an unused expression and the functions have no docstring. Behaviour is unaffected.
  • commit_upload refuses to upload into a name already registered from a non-upload source.
  • fetch_pull_request refuses non-remote repositories and reports the last underlying error when every candidate ref fails.
  • _http_json (line 439) raises IngestError with the URL (query string removed) and HTTP code, or "cannot reach <host>"; its timeout is 30 seconds.
  • _clean raises "invalid repository name" when nothing remains after sanitising.

upload.py 258 lines

Accepts source code as a zip archive or as individual files of a folder, validates every path, stages the files on disk under size and count limits, and stores the staged tree as one commit on main of a bare repository that TIA owns.

How it works

Each upload is a full snapshot. Uploading the same repository name again adds another commit, so two uploads can be compared like the base and head of a pull request. UploadConfig (line 36) holds the limits, read from the environment by from_env:

FieldDefaultEnvironment variable
enabledTrueTIA_ALLOW_UPLOADS
max_bytes2 GiBTIA_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES
max_files200,000TIA_MAX_UPLOAD_FILES
session_ttl_s24 hoursTIA_UPLOAD_SESSION_TTL_S

Path validation (clean_member, lines 54-76). Backslashes are converted to / and then the checks run in this order:

ConditionResult
Contains any of < > : " | ? * or a control character (_BAD_CHARS)IngestError "unsafe file name". The colon rule also blocks Windows alternate data streams.
Starts with / or a drive letterIngestError "absolute path"
No segments left after dropping empty and . segmentsNone
Any segment is ..IngestError "path traversal"
A segment ends with a space or dot, or its part before the first dot is a Windows device name (con, prn, aux, nul, com1-com9, lpt1-lpt9)IngestError "file name not allowed"
Longer than 1024 charactersIngestError
Ends with / (a directory entry)None
Any segment is in SKIP_DIRS (.git, node_modules, __pycache__, .venv, venv, .tox, .mypy_cache, .pytest_cache, .idea)None (skipped, not an error)
OtherwiseThe cleaned relative path

Session. A Session (line 79) is a directory <root>/<sid> containing tree/ (the staged files), meta.json (tenant, name, byte and file counters, skipped count, source type, creation time) and paths.txt (one accepted path per line). Session.create first deletes any sibling session directory whose modification time is older than session_ttl_s, then creates a new one with a random 32-hex id. Session.open requires the id to match [0-9a-f]{32}, the metadata file to exist and its tenant to equal the caller's; all three failures raise the same "unknown upload". flush writes the metadata and touches the directory so activity postpones expiry. use_source prevents mixing a zip and a folder in one session.

Writing one file (add, lines 139-173).

  1. clean_member validates the name; a None result increments skipped and returns False.
  2. The lower-cased path is looked up in seen(), a dictionary loaded from paths.txt. A previous path that differs only in letter case is an error, because the two would collide on a case-insensitive file system.
  3. The destination is resolved and must still lie under the resolved staging directory (os.path.commonpath), a second defence against traversal.
  4. replaced is the size of an existing file at that path, so a retried batch overwrites without being counted twice.
  5. A new path that would exceed max_files raises UploadTooLarge.
  6. The chunks are written one at a time. Before each write, bytes - replaced + written is compared with max_bytes; exceeding it raises UploadTooLarge. The limit therefore applies to bytes actually written.
  7. Counters are updated and a new path is appended to paths.txt.

Zip archives (add_zip, lines 175-206). A file that is not a zip raises IngestError. Two early checks use the archive's own directory: more entries than max_files, or a declared total size above max_bytes. Each entry is then inspected: the encryption flag (flag_bits & 0x1) is refused; the Unix file type in external_attr >> 16 must be a regular file or directory, which rejects symbolic links and device files; directory entries are validated by name and dropped. _root_to_strip decides whether to remove a single top-level folder: it applies only when every file is inside the same top-level directory, and then either strip_root is "true" or it is "auto" and the folder's name equals the repository name ignoring case. Each member is streamed through add in 1 MiB chunks (CHUNK).

Committing (commit_tree, lines 228-258). Git commands run through the local _git (line 219), which passes -c core.autocrlf=false -c core.safecrlf=false -c core.fsmonitor=false so file content is stored without line-ending conversion.

  1. Both paths are made absolute; an upload with no files raises IngestError.
  2. If the bare repository does not exist it is created with git init --bare -q -b main.
  3. The environment points git at the bare repository (GIT_DIR), the staging tree (GIT_WORK_TREE) and a private index file upload-<uuid>.idx (GIT_INDEX_FILE), with the fixed identity TIA Upload <upload@tia.invalid>.
  4. git add -f -A . stages everything (-f overrides ignore rules), and git write-tree produces the tree hash.
  5. The current refs/heads/main commit is read. If its tree equals the new tree, the function returns (parent, False): identical content creates no commit.
  6. git commit-tree creates the commit, with the parent when there is one and the label as the message.
  7. git update-ref refs/heads/main <sha> <expected old value> moves the branch only if it still has the expected value (an empty string means "must not exist"). A failure means another upload committed concurrently and raises an error asking the caller to retry.
  8. The temporary index file is deleted in a finally block.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
UploadConfig36Limits and the enable switch.
UploadTooLarge50Subclass of IngestError for a size or count limit.
clean_member54Validates one member name; returns the safe path, None to skip, or raises.
Session.create / open93 / 109Start or reopen a tenant-bound staging session.
Session.flush / discard120 / 125Persist metadata; delete the session.
Session.add139Writes one file with path and quota checks.
Session.add_zip175Validates and extracts an archive through add.
Session._root_to_strip208Decides whether to strip a single wrapping folder.
commit_tree228Stores the staged tree as a commit; returns (commit, created).

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Nothing uploaded is ever executed; archive members are read as bytes only.
  • The archive's declared sizes are only an early rejection; the real limit is enforced chunk by chunk in add, which defeats an archive that understates its sizes.
  • When add raises UploadTooLarge part-way through a file, the partially written file stays in the staging tree and neither the counters nor paths.txt are updated. Whether the caller always discards the session after that error is decided outside this module.
  • The early entry-count check in add_zip counts directory entries as well as files, so an archive with many directories can be refused slightly below the real file limit.
  • NotADirectoryError, IsADirectoryError, FileExistsError and PermissionError during a write (for example a file where a directory is needed) become IngestError "conflicting path".

relationships.py 124 lines

Keeps a history of cross-repository dependencies. A relationship here is a consumer file in one repository that calls an endpoint provided by another repository. Each relationship is recorded as current or stale, with a reason when stale.

How it works

observed(graph) (line 34) reads the relationships present in a graph. It keeps only edges whose rel is CONSUMES_API and whose two nodes are in different repositories. The key is <consumer repo>|<consumer file>|<provider endpoint node id>, so several calls from one file to one endpoint are one relationship; the record keeps the lowest non-zero line number, plus both repositories and services, the endpoint's method and path, the edge's resolution strategy and its evidence status.

reconcile(store, graph, extracts, repos, graph_id) (line 68) compares what the graph shows now (seen) with what the store holds (prev) and writes only the rows that changed. scans maps each repository name to its Repo record.

CaseWhat is written
Seen, and neither repository's scan_status is failedstate: current, stale fields cleared, last_confirmed_at = now, both confirmed commits set to the extracts' commits, first_seen_at preserved. If the stored row is already current with the same commits, evidence status and strategy, nothing is written. A previously stale row counts as recovered, a missing one as new.
Seen, but the consumer's or provider's latest scan failedstate: stale, stale_reason: scan_failed. The graph still shows the relationship, but only at an older revision that is no longer known to be the latest. last_confirmed_at and the confirmed commits keep their old values; stale_since is kept if the row was already stale. Counted as became_stale unless it was already stale for this reason.
In the store but not seenstate: stale with the reason from _stale_reason. Skipped when the row is already stale with the same reason and detail. Counted as became_stale when it was current.

_stale_reason (line 54) decides why an unseen relationship disappeared, in this order:

  1. Either repository is no longer registered: evidence_removed ("repository ... was removed from TIA").
  2. Either repository's scan failed, or it has no extract: scan_failed.
  3. The consumer file is listed in the consumer extract's files_failed: parse_failed.
  4. Otherwise: evidence_removed ("no longer found in ... at <commit>").

After store.put_relationships(write) the function re-reads all rows, counts current and stale, and returns those counts with the sorted key lists became_stale, recovered and new. A relationships_reconciled audit event is written only when one of the three lists is non-empty.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
UNVERIFIABLE23("scan_failed", "parse_failed"): stale reasons under which the relationship may still be real.
observed34Cross-repository CONSUMES_API relationships of a graph, keyed.
_stale_reason54Reason and detail text for a relationship that is no longer seen.
reconcile68Updates the stored history and returns a summary.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The scan_failed detail on line 62 reads old.get('last_confirmed_commit', '?'), but the rows written by reconcile use the keys last_confirmed_consumer_commit and last_confirmed_provider_commit, and no other module in tia/ mentions last_confirmed_commit. The text therefore ends with "last confirmed at ?".
  • Line 79 indexes extracts[c] and extracts[p] directly. This is safe as long as the graph was built from the same extracts, which is how refresh_landscape calls it.
  • Only CONSUMES_API edges are tracked; event, schema and database relationships have no history here.
  • Unchanged rows are not rewritten, so last_confirmed_at of a stable relationship is the time its commits or evidence last changed, not the time of the latest reconcile.

incidents.py 96 lines

Given an incident record, lists the changes that are associated with it and states how each one is associated. It never names a cause: the result always has root_cause: None and a fixed explanatory note.

How it works

parse_incident (line 28) validates and normalises input. It requires id, started_at and either service or repo, otherwise it raises ValueError. It truncates the id to 80 characters and the title to 300, lower-cases the environment (default production), converts timestamps to UTC ISO strings through _ts (which accepts a trailing Z), and keeps at most 50 dictionary entries of changes. If the record has error or error_message, it stores the first 400 characters and a failure_signature so the same failure seen in a test run can be linked to the incident.

investigate (line 42) builds the candidate list:

  1. The time window is from window_hours (default 72) before the incident start up to the start.
  2. The incident's repository and service are completed from each other using extracts (a map from repository to its extract, which carries the service name).
  3. upstream, the set of services the incident's service depends on, is computed from the graph: for every node of type endpoint belonging to the service, testscope.dependencies(g, n) is walked and the services of the reached nodes are collected; the service itself is removed.
  4. Candidates are accumulated in a dictionary keyed by (repo, first 40 characters of revision). The inner function add appends an association type (once) and an evidence item to the candidate.
  5. Each association type is added from its source, as in the table below.
  6. When analyses_for is supplied, each candidate with a repository and revision gets analyses, the ids of stored analyses of that change.
  7. Each candidate's associations are put in the fixed order of ASSOCIATIONS, and candidates are sorted by their first association, then repository, then revision.
AssociationCondition
directly_recordedThe change appears in the incident's own changes list.
time_correlatedA deployment record inside the window, to the incident's environment, whose repository or service is the incident's.
dependency_derivedA deployment inside the window, to the same environment, of a service in upstream. The evidence adds because: "<service> depends on <upstream service>".
manually_validatedA manual link whose incident_id equals the incident's id; the evidence records who, when and the note.

Deployment evidence includes hours_before_incident, rounded to one decimal.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
ASSOCIATIONS, NOTE19, 20The four association types in display order; the "not a root cause" statement.
_ts24Parses an ISO timestamp and converts it to UTC.
parse_incident28Validates and normalises an incident record.
investigate42Returns {incident, window_hours, upstream_services, candidates, root_cause: None, note}.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A deployment whose deployed_at is missing or unparseable is skipped (lines 73-76). environment and revision are read with d[...] on lines 79-84 without the same protection, so a deployment record in the window lacking either would raise KeyError.
  • _ts calls astimezone on the parsed value; for a timestamp with no offset Python interprets it in the server's local time zone.
  • A change can carry several association types; they are merged into one candidate.
  • With no graph (g is None) the upstream set is empty and no dependency_derived candidates are produced.

temporal.py 72 lines

Compares the dependency graphs of two releases and answers two historical questions: who consumed a given contract in a given graph, and which revision of a service was deployed in an environment at a given time.

How it works

A release is a named set of repository revisions; the graph for it is built elsewhere and passed in. This module only compares.

_key(g, e) (line 16) gives an edge an identity that is stable between versions: (rel, source end, destination id). If the source node's type is callsite, listener, runtime_ref or contract_ref, the source end is <type>@<service or repo> instead of the node id. Those node ids include a file line, so without this a call site that moved to another line would be reported as one dependency removed and another introduced.

relationships(g, rels) (line 27) collects edges whose relationship type is in DEPENDENCY_RELS (CONSUMES_API, CONSUMES_EVENT, PUBLISHES, USES_SCHEMA, READS, WRITES, MAPS_TO, CALLS, TESTS) into a dictionary by key; setdefault keeps the first edge seen for each key.

compare(g_from, g_to, rels, cross_service_only) (line 38) returns:

  • introduced: keys in the newer graph only.
  • removed: keys in the older graph only.
  • modified: keys in both whose (status, strategy) pair differs, with before and after.
  • summary: the three counts.

With cross_service_only, an entry is kept only when its from_service differs from its to_service. Keys are sorted by their string form so the output order is deterministic.

consumers_of(g, node) (line 51) iterates g.impacted_by(node) and keeps the neighbours reached through CONSUMES_API, CONSUMES_EVENT or USES_SCHEMA, sorted by service and node id.

deployed_at(records, at, repo) (line 62) sorts deployment records by (deployed_at, id) and, for each record at or before at, overwrites the entry for its repository (or service) and environment. The entry left at the end is the latest deployment not later than at.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
DEPENDENCY_RELS13The nine relationship types that count as dependencies.
_key16Line-independent identity of an edge.
relationships27Dependencies of one graph by key.
compare38Introduced, removed and modified dependencies between two graphs.
consumers_of51Direct consumers of an endpoint, schema or topic.
deployed_at62Revision running per repository and environment at a time.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Only the source end is normalised in _key; the destination is always the raw node id.
  • Because call sites collapse to type@service, two call sites in one service to the same target are one relationship, and only the first one's status and strategy are compared.
  • deployed_at compares timestamps as strings (d["deployed_at"] > at). This is correct only when every timestamp uses the same ISO format and offset.
  • A change of evidence alone (for example a different line) with the same status and strategy is not reported as modified.

calibration.py 121 lines

Computes two descriptive reports from stored data: how often each rule's findings were confirmed by reviewers or incidents (precision per rule), and counts of past analyses per application or team and period (trends).

How it works

Verdicts. A reviewer records one of VERDICTS (confirmed, false_positive, irrelevant) on a finding. rules_of(report, f) (line 27) copies the finding's severity_rule, inference_rule, resolution_strategy, evidence_status, impact_type, severity, component and the report's configuration_version. That copy is stored with the verdict, so calibration never has to re-read or re-score a stored report.

precision(verdicts, incident_confirmations) (line 33).

  1. Verdicts are sorted by (at, evidence_id) and written into a dictionary keyed by (analysis_id, finding_id), so the latest verdict for a finding replaces earlier ones. Each is tagged source: "reviewer".
  2. Incident confirmations are added with setdefault, so they apply only to findings no reviewer judged. They carry verdict: "confirmed" and source: "incident".
  3. For each of the six DIMENSIONS (severity_rule, inference_rule, resolution_strategy, evidence_status, impact_type, configuration), the verdict is counted under the value its rules have for that dimension; a missing value is skipped. from_incidents counts those that came from incidents.
  4. finish adds judged (all three counts), precision = confirmed / (confirmed + false_positive) rounded to three decimals (None when the denominator is zero) and too_few_to_act_on, true when judged is below MIN_SAMPLE (10). irrelevant verdicts are reported but excluded from precision.

incident_confirmations (line 63) produces the second input. For each incident it gathers (repo, revision) pairs from the incident's changes and from manual links to it, calls analyses_for(repo, rev) to get the stored reports for that change, and emits one confirmation for every finding whose component equals the incident's service.

trends(reports, owners, incident_refs, group, period) (line 92). Failed reports and reports without generated_at are skipped. Each remaining report is placed in a row keyed by the application (the change's service, or its repository) or, with group="team", the owning team ("(no owner)" when unknown), and by period. _period (line 81) returns YYYY-MM for month, YYYY-Qn for quarter, and the ISO week YYYY-Wnn for any other value. Per row it accumulates:

Output fieldHow it is computed
change_volumeNumber of analyses.
breaking_changes, breaking_shareAnalyses in which any changed component has breaking set; share of the volume.
unresolved_dependency_shareShare of analyses with an unknown whose area is in UNRESOLVED_AREAS.
material_findingsTotal findings with material set.
missing_verification_shareMaterial findings with no tests, divided by material findings; None when there are none.
incident_associationsNumber of incident references matching the report's repository and the first 40 characters of its proposed revision.

The result carries TREND_NOTE, which states that the figures describe the past and are not predictions.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
rules_of27The rule identifiers of a finding, stored with a verdict.
precision33Per-dimension counts and precision from verdicts and incident confirmations.
incident_confirmations63Findings confirmed by incidents.
_period81Period label of a timestamp.
trends92Descriptive counts and shares per group and period.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • One finding contributes to up to six tables, once per dimension; judged_findings counts distinct findings.
  • incident_confirmations reads ln["repo"] and ln["revision"] by index (line 69); a manual link missing either key would raise KeyError, whereas incidents.investigate reads the same links with .get.
  • An unrecognised period value silently produces ISO weeks.
  • Divisions by the analysis count are safe because a row exists only after at least one analysis was counted.

Source-control and change-management connectors

This layer talks to systems outside TIA. webhooks.py, scmhosts.py and ado.py authenticate and parse incoming pull-request events; github.py, githubapp.py and publish.py obtain GitHub credentials and write the advisory comment and check run; itsm.py attaches a summary to a ServiceNow change record. access.py mirrors each host's list of readers so TIA can decide who may see which repository, and neo4jgraph.py copies the current graph into a Neo4j database for other tools.

webhooks.py 114 lines

Holds the server-side GitHub connector settings (ScmConfig) and the helpers the webhook endpoint needs: verifying GitHub's signature, extracting the facts from a pull_request event, and matching the event's repository to a registered one.

How it works

ScmConfig (line 24) is a frozen dataclass, so it is changed only by making a copy with dataclasses.replace.

FieldDefaultSource in from_env
github_tokenNoneTIA_GITHUB_TOKEN through env_secret (a literal or a secret reference)
github_webhook_secretNoneTIA_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET, encoded to bytes
github_api_urlhttps://api.github.comTIA_GITHUB_API_URL, validated by check_outbound
comment_audiencetargetTIA_COMMENT_AUDIENCE: target, readers or all
check_runTrueTIA_CHECK_RUN; false for 0, false, no, off
public_urlNoneTIA_PUBLIC_URL without a trailing slash; used for report links
github_app_id, github_app_keyNoneTIA_GITHUB_APP_ID, TIA_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY
github_app_client_id, github_app_client_secretNoneTIA_GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_ID, TIA_GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_SECRET
github_web_urlhttps://github.comTIA_GITHUB_WEB_URL, validated by check_outbound

Two functions layer per-tenant values over this deployment-wide object. with_stored_token (line 58) keeps an environment token if there is one; otherwise it reads the tenant's stored secret github/token. with_tenant_config (line 66) replaces comment_audience with the tenant's choice when the tenant made one.

Signature check. GitHub signs each delivery with an HMAC (a keyed hash) of the raw request body and sends it in the X-Hub-Signature-256 header. verify_signature recomputes it and compares in constant time, so the comparison's duration does not leak how many characters matched:

if not secret or not header or not header.startswith("sha256="):
    return False
expected = hmac.new(secret, body, hashlib.sha256).hexdigest()
return hmac.compare_digest(expected, header[len("sha256="):].strip().lower())

Event parsing. parse_pull_request (line 79) returns None for events that need no analysis. An event is relevant when its action is in PR_ACTIONS (opened, reopened, synchronize, ready_for_review), or is edited and the changes object contains base (the target branch changed). For a relevant event it returns action, pr (number), head_sha, base_ref, full_name, clone_url, draft and installation_id. A missing field raises ValueError("malformed pull_request event: ...").

Repository matching. repo_key (line 103) reduces a URL to <lower-case host><lower-case path without trailing slash or .git>, ignoring the scheme. match_repo returns the first registered repository whose URL has the same key as the event's clone_url. github_full_name (line 94) extracts owner/name from an https or SSH-style URL by taking the last two path segments, and returns None for file: URLs, absolute paths and strings that contain neither :// nor @.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
PR_ACTIONS20The pull-request actions that trigger an analysis.
ScmConfig / from_env24 / 40GitHub connector settings and their environment loader.
with_stored_token58Falls back to the tenant's stored token.
with_tenant_config66Applies the tenant's comment audience.
verify_signature71Constant-time check of X-Hub-Signature-256.
parse_pull_request79Facts from a pull_request payload, or None.
github_full_name94owner/name from a clone URL.
repo_key / match_repo103 / 111Normalised URL key; registered repository for an event.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • verify_signature returns False when no secret is configured, so an unconfigured server rejects every delivery instead of accepting unsigned ones.
  • parse_pull_request reports draft but does not filter on it; the decision about drafts is left to the caller.
  • repo_key uses urlsplit, which finds no host in an SSH shorthand such as git@github.com:owner/repo.git. A repository registered with that form produces a key that will not equal the key of the event's https clone_url, so match_repo will not find it.
  • The module docstring calls the single environment token an interim model; the GitHub App path in githubapp.py is its replacement.

github.py 178 lines

The low-level GitHub REST client. It makes authenticated calls, creates or updates TIA's single pull-request comment, builds and creates or updates the check run, and lists a repository's collaborators.

How it works

_call(method, url, token, payload) (line 36) is the one HTTP function. It sends Authorization: Bearer <token>, Accept: application/vnd.github+json, a JSON body when a payload is given, and tracing propagation headers. The request passes through airgap.checked and has a 20-second timeout. The response body is parsed as JSON (an empty body gives None). Errors are not translated: urllib.error.HTTPError and OSError propagate to the caller. In a finally block the call records the CONNECTOR metric with an operation derived from the URL by _operation (check_run if the URL contains check-runs, comment if it contains /issues/, else api) and an outcome of success, http_4xx-style, or unreachable.

Comment upsert (upsert_comment, line 82). "Upsert" means update if present, otherwise insert.

  1. Page through GET /repos/<repo>/issues/<pr>/comments?per_page=100&page=N.
  2. On each page, find_marker_comment returns the first comment whose body contains MARKER, a hidden marker string defined in report.py that every TIA comment carries.
  3. Stop when the marker is found or a page has fewer than 100 comments.
  4. If found, PATCH /repos/<repo>/issues/comments/<id> with the new body and return "updated"; otherwise POST a new comment and return "created".

Check run payload (check_run_payload, line 150). A check run is the status entry GitHub shows on a commit. The payload has the fixed name CHECK_NAME ("TIA impact analysis"), status: completed, the current UTC time, external_id set to the analysis id, and a conclusion computed by policy.check_conclusion from the report's policy and policy results. Its output has three parts:

  • title from _title (line 100), at most 255 characters: a failure message, a "no downstream impact identified ... (not proof of none)" message when there are no findings and none withheld, or counts such as 3 finding(s), 2 material (1 high, 2 medium) with a note about findings withheld by the visibility policy.
  • summary from _summary (line 113): change type and status; a warning line when the status is neither complete nor failed; up to five material findings; a count and list of unknowns that affect confidence; up to ten policy results; required verification steps (de-duplicated, at most 1500 characters); the report link; and a closing line that depends on whether the policy mode is enforcing.
  • text: the full rendered comment with at most 50 rows.

Summary and text are cut to MAX_TEXT (60,000 characters; GitHub's limit is 65,535). check_run_annotations (line 138) adds file and line annotations for changed components that have a file and line and whose type starts with api or db_; the level is warning for a breaking change, else notice; at most MAX_ANNOTATIONS (50) are sent.

Check run upsert (upsert_check_run, line 168). It lists the check runs of the commit filtered by check_name, and if one named CHECK_NAME exists it sends PATCH /check-runs/<id> with the payload minus head_sha; otherwise POST /check-runs.

Collaborators (list_collaborators, line 63). Pages through /collaborators?affiliation=all (direct collaborators, team members and organisation members). Each person's role is normalised to one of ROLE_ORDER (pull, triage, push, maintain, admin): role_name values read and write are mapped to pull and push; if the name is not a known role (a custom role), the highest true flag in the permissions object is used. Logins are lower-cased.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
find_marker_comment28First comment containing TIA's marker.
_call36Authenticated JSON request with tracing and metrics.
list_collaborators63(login, role) of everyone with access.
upsert_comment82Creates or updates the single TIA comment.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • _call sends a body only when payload is truthy, so an empty dictionary is sent as a request with no body.
  • The module docstring (line 5) says the conclusion is always neutral, while check_run_payload takes it from check_conclusion and its own docstring (lines 151-152) describes a policy mode in which the conclusion reports mandatory policy outcomes. The always-neutral statement holds only in advisory mode.
  • GitHub accepts check-run creation only from a GitHub App installation token; with a personal access token the call fails and publish.py reports the check run as skipped.
  • GitHub answers the collaborators list only for a token with push access to the repository.

githubapp.py 257 lines

Implements authentication as a GitHub App, the mechanism that replaces personal access tokens. It signs the App's identity token, exchanges it for short-lived installation tokens, checks who may link an installation to a tenant, and chooses the right credential for a tenant's repository.

How it works

A GitHub App is installed by a customer on an account, and each such installation has an id and a chosen set of repositories. Authentication is a two-step chain:

  1. App JWT. GitHubApp.jwt (line 67) builds a JSON Web Token: a header {"alg": "RS256", "typ": "JWT"} and a payload {"iat": now - 60, "exp": now + 540, "iss": app_id}, each Base64url-encoded, joined by a dot and signed with the App's RSA private key using PKCS#1 v1.5 padding and SHA-256. Issuing it 60 seconds in the past tolerates clock differences; the nine-minute expiry stays under GitHub's ten-minute maximum.
  2. Installation token. installation_token (line 106) calls POST /app/installations/<id>/access_tokens authenticated with the JWT. GitHub returns a token valid for about an hour and limited to that installation's repositories.

Token cache. self._tokens maps an installation id to (token, expiry) and is guarded by a threading.Lock. A cached token is reused while more than REFRESH_BEFORE (300 seconds) of life remain. The expiry comes from the response's expires_at; if that is missing or unparseable the code assumes 3000 seconds from now. forget clears one entry or all.

Error translation. _as_app (line 74) makes a call with the JWT and converts HTTP failures to AppError, which carries a code and a message that is safe to show:

ConditionAppError code
HTTP 401 or 403not_configured (the App id or key is wrong)
HTTP 404not_installed; installation_token rewrites this to installation_gone (uninstalled)
Other HTTP status, or OSErrorunreachable
Installation has suspended_atinstallation_gone (in installation, line 88)
Response has no string tokenno_access
Private key not loadable, not RSA-like, or under 2048 bitsbad_key (in load_key, line 43)

Linking an installation to a tenant. Knowing an installation id must not be enough to claim it. user_sees_installation (line 159) asks GitHub to prove the person's right:

  1. It requires the App's client id and secret, else AppError("not_configured").
  2. It exchanges the OAuth code (a one-time code GitHub gives after the person authorises the App) for a user token at <web>/login/oauth/access_token. No token means False.
  3. It pages through GET /user/installations with the user token, looking for the installation id. Not found means False.
  4. When found, it returns self._owns(...). _owns_account (line 185) requires an account name; for a User account the token's own login (GET /user) must equal it ignoring case; for an organisation, GET /user/memberships/orgs/<account> must return role == "admin" and state == "active". An HTTP error there means False.

The ownership step exists because an outside collaborator on one repository can see an installation without being entitled to administer it.

Choosing a credential (TokenSource.token_for, line 242).

  1. With no App configured, return fallback or the configured personal token.
  2. Load the installations linked to this tenant with my_installations. None raises AppError("not_installed").
  3. If the caller's hint (the installation id from the webhook) is one of the tenant's installations, use it.
  4. Otherwise ask GitHub which installation covers the repository (installation_for_repo, GET /repos/<full>/installation) and require that the answer is one of the tenant's; if not, not_installed.
  5. Return installation_token for that id.

The remaining module functions call database functions: my_installations (tia_my_installations, tenant-bound transaction), installation_tenant (tia_installation_tenant, which tenant owns an installation), and provider_mapped (tia_provider_mapped). scm_namespace returns the lower-cased first segment of a repository's full name, which is the unit a tenant owns on GitLab and Bitbucket.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
AppError35Exception with a code and a displayable message.
load_key43Loads and validates the PEM private key.
GitHubApp.jwt67Signs the App's nine-minute identity token.
GitHubApp._as_app74Call as the App with error translation.
GitHubApp.installation86Account, permissions and repository selection of an installation.
GitHubApp.installation_for_repo94Installation id covering a repository.
GitHubApp.installation_token106Cached installation access token.
GitHubApp.repositories136Repositories an installation was given, paged 100 at a time.
GitHubApp.user_sees_installation159Proof that a person may link an installation.

TokenSource230Selects installation token or personal token for a tenant's repository.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The expiry is parsed on line 122 as time.mktime(time.strptime(...)) - time.timezone. mktime interprets the value as local time and applies daylight saving when it is in effect, while time.timezone is the non-daylight offset. On a server whose local zone is observing daylight saving the computed expiry is one hour too early, so a one-hour token never appears to have 300 seconds left and a new token is requested on every call. On a UTC server the result is correct.
  • repositories forgets the cached token when GitHub refuses the list, so the next call obtains a fresh one.
  • user_sees_installation catches urllib.error.URLError, of which HTTPError is a subclass, so an HTTP error from the code exchange or the installations list is reported as unreachable.
  • A webhook's installation id is only a hint: it is used only if it belongs to the tenant, so a forged id cannot obtain another tenant's token.
  • The module never logs, audits or returns the private key or a token in an error.

publish.py 92 lines

Publishes one analysis to a GitHub pull request as one comment and one check run, and applies the rule that decides which repositories the comment may name. The webhook worker and the publish API endpoint both use it.

How it works

Audience rule. A pull-request comment is visible to everyone who can see the pull request, so it must not reveal repositories those people cannot read. audience_view(report, target_repo, scm, comment_repos) (line 26) returns the version of the report the comment may show. comment_repos is the proven-safe list from AccessStore.comment_repos, or None when it was not computed.

comment_audiencecomment_reposView
readers or alla listredact(report, comment_repos)
allNoneThe full report
target, or readers with Noneanyredact(report, [target_repo]): only the pull request's own repository is named

comment_scope (line 35) decides whether that list is computed: it calls access.comment_repos when the audience is readers, or when it is all and authentication is required; otherwise it returns None. With authentication required, all therefore behaves like readers.

publish_to_github (line 51).

  1. No token raises PublishError(..., permanent=True).
  2. The view and the report permalink (permalink(scm.public_url, analysis_id)) are computed.
  3. Comment. github.upsert_comment is called with the rendered view. An HTTP status from 400 to 499 other than 429 raises a permanent PublishError (the token lacks pull-request write access or the pull request is unknown). Any other HTTP status, or a network error, raises a non-permanent one. The PUBLISH metric records created, updated or failed.
  4. Check run. Attempted when the check_run argument is true, or when it is None and scm.check_run is true. Any HTTP or network error is caught and stored as {"skipped": reason} from _skip_reason; for 401, 403 and 404 the reason explains that check runs need a GitHub App installation token with checks:write.
  5. Returns {"comment", "check_run", "permalink"}.

audit_publication (line 85) writes pr_comment_published when a comment was written, and either check_run_skipped (when the check-run result is a dictionary) or check_run_published. The Azure DevOps and GitLab/Bitbucket handlers reuse it by passing their status result as check_run.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
PublishError18Comment refused; permanent says whether a retry can help.
audience_view26Redacts the report to what the comment may show.
comment_scope35Computes the provable reader set when the audience setting requires it.
_skip_reason42Explanation for a skipped check run.
publish_to_github51Comment (required) and check run (best effort).
audit_publication85Audit events for what was published.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The comment is the contract: its failure fails the publication. The check run never does.
  • The check run is built from the same redacted view as the comment, so it cannot reveal more than the comment.
  • HTTP 429 (rate limited) is treated as transient.
  • Error messages contain only the HTTP status, never a token or a response body.

scmhosts.py 273 lines

The GitLab and Bitbucket connector. It authenticates and parses merge-request and pull-request webhooks, writes one comment and one commit status, and lists who can read a repository. It supports gitlab.com, self-managed GitLab, Bitbucket Cloud and Bitbucket Data Center.

How it works

HostConfig (line 48) holds provider (gitlab or bitbucket), base_url, webhook_secret and token. from_env reads TIA_<PROVIDER>_URL (default https://gitlab.com or https://bitbucket.org), TIA_<PROVIDER>_WEBHOOK_SECRET and TIA_<PROVIDER>_TOKEN. The property cloud is true when the base URL is one of the two public hosts. The property api is <base>/api/v4 for GitLab, https://api.bitbucket.org/2.0 for Bitbucket Cloud and <base>/rest for Data Center.

Outbound restriction. Every REST call goes through _call (line 131), which first calls allowed(cfg, url) (line 73). The URL's host must be the configured host (plus api.bitbucket.org for Bitbucket Cloud) and the scheme must be https, or http only to localhost, 127.0.0.1 or ::1. Otherwise it raises ScmHostError("refusing to send the ... token to ..."). This also covers URLs that come back from the host, such as Bitbucket's next page links.

Webhook authentication (verify, line 82). With no secret configured the result is always False. GitLab sends the shared secret itself in X-Gitlab-Token, which is compared in constant time. Bitbucket sends an HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body in X-Hub-Signature (the code also accepts X-Hub-Signature-256) as sha256=<hex>, compared in constant time.

Event parsing (parse_event, line 92) returns pr, head_sha, base_ref, source_ref, remote_url, full_name, project_id and event, or None when no analysis is needed.

HostAccepted eventsIgnored when
GitLabobject_kind == "merge_request" with action open, reopen or updateState is not opened; draft or work in progress; an update without oldrev (a title or description edit with no new commits)
Bitbucket CloudX-Event-Key of pullrequest:created or pullrequest:updatedState is not OPEN, or draft
Bitbucket Data Centerpr:opened, pr:from_ref_updated, pr:modifiedState is not OPEN, or draft

For GitLab project_id is the numeric project id; for Bitbucket it is None. The Data Center full name is <project key>/<slug> and the clone URL is the link named http or https. A missing field raises ValueError("malformed <provider> event: ...").

Comment upsert (upsert_comment, line 182) finds TIA's earlier comment by MARKER and updates it, otherwise creates one:

HostListUpdateCreate
GitLabGET .../merge_requests/<iid>/notes?per_page=100&sort=ascPUT .../notes/<id> with {"body"}POST .../notes
Bitbucket CloudGET .../pullrequests/<id>/comments?pagelen=100PUT .../comments/<id> with {"content": {"raw"}}POST .../comments
Data CenterGET .../pull-requests/<id>/activities?limit=100, entries with action COMMENTEDPUT .../comments/<id> with {"text", "version"}POST .../comments

The Data Center update sends the comment's current version because that API uses it for optimistic locking. GitLab projects are addressed by project_id or the URL-encoded full name.

Commit status (post_status, line 210). _state (line 169) maps the policy conclusion from check_conclusion to the host's vocabulary:

ConclusionGitLab stateBitbucket state
failurefailedFAILED
action_requiredpendingINPROGRESS
anything else, including neutral and NonesuccessSUCCESSFUL

The description starts with "Advisory:" when the conclusion is neutral or None, else "Policy <outcome>:", and is cut to 250 characters. GitLab receives POST /projects/<id>/statuses/<sha> with the fields in the query string and the name tia/impact-analysis. Bitbucket receives a JSON body with the key tia-impact-analysis at .../commit/<sha>/statuses/build (Cloud) or /build-status/1.0/commits/<sha> (Data Center). publish (line 222) calls upsert_comment, whose failure propagates, then post_status, whose ScmHostError is caught and returned as {"skipped": ...}.

Permission mirror (list_readers, line 236) returns (login, permission) pairs in GitHub's vocabulary.

  • GitLab: pages through /projects/<id>/members/all (which includes inherited group members). Access level 20 (Reporter) and above can read code; the level is mapped down to the nearest entry of GITLAB_READ (20: pull, 30: push, 40: maintain, 50: admin). Members whose state is not active are dropped.
  • Bitbucket Cloud: follows the next links of /workspaces/<ws>/permissions/repositories/<slug>; the login is the user's nickname or account_id; read, write, admin map to pull, push, admin.
  • Data Center: reads repository-level and then project-level user permissions and keeps the stronger of the two for each login.
