Edge cases and failure handling

  • _call marks HTTP 4xx other than 429 as permanent, and 5xx, 429 and network errors as transient; 401 and 403 add "(check the token's scopes)". Its timeout is 20 seconds.
  • upsert_comment reads only the first page of existing comments (100 notes, comments or activities). On a merge request with more than that before TIA's comment, the marker would not be found and a second comment would be created.
  • The Data Center permission lists use limit=1000 with no paging.
  • Users who can read only through a group permission on Data Center are not listed, because only the permissions/users endpoints are read.
  • full_name returns None for a URL on any host other than the configured one.

ado.py 277 lines

The Azure DevOps connector. It authenticates and parses pull-request service hooks, writes one comment thread and one pull-request status, and computes who may read a repository from Azure DevOps access control lists.

How it works

AdoConfig (line 47) holds hook_username and hook_password (TIA_ADO_HOOK_USERNAME, TIA_ADO_HOOK_PASSWORD), token (TIA_ADO_TOKEN) and api_url (TIA_ADO_API_URL, for an on-premises Azure DevOps Server). The property receiving is true when both hook credentials are set.

Receiving. An Azure DevOps service hook cannot sign its body, so it is authenticated by HTTP Basic credentials set on the subscription. verify_basic (line 64) decodes the Authorization: Basic header strictly (validate=True), splits at the first colon and compares user name and password separately with hmac.compare_digest; both comparisons are always evaluated. parse_pull_request_event (line 84) accepts only the event types git.pullrequest.created and git.pullrequest.updated, ignores pull requests whose status is not active (or absent) and drafts, and requires the organisation base URL in resourceContainers.collection.baseUrl (or .account). It returns pr, head_sha (from lastMergeSourceCommit), base_ref and source_ref with refs/heads/ removed, repository_id, repository, project, remote_url, base_url, collection_id and event.

Outbound restriction. _call (line 107) first checks allowed_host (line 76): the host configured in api_url is accepted over https or http; otherwise the scheme must be https and the host must be dev.azure.com, vssps.dev.azure.com or end in .visualstudio.com. The call authenticates with Basic authentication using an empty user name and the personal access token as password, with a 20-second timeout. HTTP 4xx other than 429 is a permanent AdoError; 5xx, 429 and network errors are transient.

Publishing. upsert_thread (line 132) lists the pull request's threads, and if the first comment of any thread contains MARKER it sends PATCH .../threads/<id>/comments/<id> with the new content. Otherwise it creates a thread whose status is closed, so a branch policy that requires all comments to be resolved is not blocked by TIA's comment. post_status (line 144) posts to .../pullRequests/<id>/statuses?api-version=7.1-preview.1 with the context {"name": "impact-analysis", "genre": "tia"}, a state from policy.ado_state and a description that begins "Advisory:" when the state is notApplicable, else "Policy <outcome>:". publish (line 155) treats the thread as required and the status as best effort (AdoError becomes {"skipped": ...}).

Permission mirror. Azure DevOps stores permissions as access control lists (ACLs) in a security namespace; GIT_NAMESPACE is the id of "Git Repositories". Each ACL entry gives an identity descriptor an allow bitmask and a deny bitmask. The bits used are READ = 2, CONTRIBUTE = 4 and MANAGE = 8192.

  1. list_readers (line 223) fetches the repository to learn its id and project id; not found is a permanent AdoError.
  2. _effective (line 185) reads the ACL at three levels in order: token repoV2 (all repositories), repoV2/<project>, repoV2/<project>/<repo>. For each descriptor and bit, a later level's explicit deny or allow overwrites the inherited value; within one entry deny takes precedence over allow. An ACL with inheritPermissions false resets everything inherited.
  3. _identities (line 207) resolves the descriptors in chunks of 50 through the identities service with queryMembership=Expanded. identity_base (line 174) locates that service: vssps.dev.azure.com for the cloud, <org>.vssps.visualstudio.com for the older host form, the collection URL itself for Azure DevOps Server.
  4. For each resolved identity, a group (isContainer) contributes its descriptor to every member; a user contributes its own. Members not yet resolved are looked up in a second _identities call.
  5. For each user that is active and has a login (_login reads the Account property, falling back to Mail), a bit is granted when at least one applicable descriptor says allow and none says deny. Users without READ are dropped. The result is admin with MANAGE, push with CONTRIBUTE, otherwise pull.

parse_remote (line 265) splits a remote URL containing /_git/ into base_url, project and repository, handling both https://dev.azure.com/<org>/<project>/_git/<repo> and https://<org>.visualstudio.com/<project>/_git/<repo>.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does

AdoConfig47Hook credentials, token and optional server URL.
verify_basic64Constant-time check of the hook's Basic credentials.
allowed_host76Whether a URL may receive the token.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The module docstring (line 9) says the status state is always notApplicable, but post_status takes the state from ado_state and has a separate description for other states. The always-advisory statement matches only the case in which ado_state returns notApplicable.
  • verify_basic returns False when the hook credentials are not configured or the header is not valid Base64.
  • A deny on any identity that applies to a user (their own or any of their groups) removes that permission, even when another group allows it.
  • The CONNECTOR metric in _call always records the operation as publish, including for permission-mirror calls.
  • list_readers returns an empty list when no ACL entry exists at any of the three levels.

itsm.py 133 lines

The change-management integration, currently for ServiceNow. It attaches a plain-text summary of an analysis to one existing change record, and can create a task for a team notification. It never creates, approves or transitions a change record.

How it works

Client. The ServiceNow constructor (line 69) validates its inputs: the instance URL must use https (or http only when TIA_ALLOW_PRIVATE_ITSM_HOSTS is set), must have a host and no path beyond /, and there must be either a token (sent as Bearer) or a user name and password (sent as Basic). _call (line 80) sends requests through netsafe.request, which according to the module docstring allows only public addresses and follows no redirects. A netsafe.FetchError becomes a transient ItsmError; an HTTP status of 400 or more becomes an ItsmError that is permanent unless the status is 5xx or 429.

attach(number, report, link) (line 98) uses three calls:

  1. The number must match CHANGE_NUMBER, ^[A-Z]{2,6}\d{4,12}$ (for example CHG0031234). This check also guarantees the number cannot alter the query it is placed in.
  2. find_change queries GET /api/now/table/change_request with sysparm_query=number=<number> and a limit of 2. Exactly one row must come back; zero is "not found" and two is "ambiguous".
  3. It lists attachments of that record filtered by the file name tia-impact-<analysis_id>.txt. If one exists the method returns created: False and writes nothing, which makes attaching idempotent per analysis.
  4. Otherwise it posts the summary as text/plain to /api/now/attachment/file and returns created: True with the attachment's sys_id.

summary(report, link) (line 37) builds the attachment text. If the status is not complete the first line is a warning that names the status and what it means (complete_with_gaps, partial, failed, running). Then follow the analysis id, repository, base and proposed revisions (first 12 characters), change type and report link; unless the analysis failed, the finding counts and up to 15 material findings; the release policy mode, outcome and each policy result; the unknowns that affect confidence (up to 20); and a closing line stating that the attachment is advisory and does not approve or change the record.

Tasks. _create_task (line 113), attached to the class as ServiceNow.create_task on line 125, creates one task per analysis and assignment group. It builds a correlation id tia-<analysis_id>-<group> (at most 100 characters), looks for an existing task with that correlation_id, and returns it with created: False if found; otherwise it posts a task with a short description (160 characters), description (4000 characters), assignment group and the correlation id.

client_for(cfg_itsm, secrets) (line 128) requires itsm.provider == "servicenow" in the tenant configuration and reads the credentials itsm/servicenow/username, itsm/servicenow/password and itsm/servicenow/token from the tenant's secret store.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
CHANGE_NUMBER24Pattern a change-record number must match.
ItsmError27Exception with a permanent flag (default true).
summary37The attachment text.
ServiceNow.__init__69Validates the instance URL and credentials.
ServiceNow._call80Request through netsafe with error mapping.
ServiceNow.find_change91Looks up exactly one change record by number.
ServiceNow.attach98Attaches the summary once per analysis.
_create_task113Creates or finds the task for an analysis and group.
client_for128Builds a client from tenant configuration and secrets.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • In summary (lines 61-63) the heading Unknowns: is added whenever the report has any unknowns, but the list below it includes only those with affects_confidence; if none qualify the heading has no entries.
  • In _create_task the assignment group is placed in sysparm_query as part of the correlation id without a format check like the one applied to change numbers. The value comes from the tenant's own configuration.
  • HTTP 401 and 403 add a hint that the account needs read access to change_request and permission to add attachments.
  • Without a secret store (secrets is None) all credentials are None and the constructor raises "no ServiceNow credentials are stored".

access.py 225 lines

Decides which repositories a signed-in principal may read and which repositories a pull-request comment may name. For hosted repositories the decision is based on a mirror: a copy, stored in TIA's database, of the reader list each source-code host reports.

How it works

Each repository has an access mode, stored in the table repo_access or defaulted by default_mode (line 39):

ModeWho may readDefault for
scmPeople the host says can read it, matched by their login on that hostProviders github, gitlab, bitbucket, ado
tenantEvery member of the tenantupload and local
restrictedOnly users with an explicit row in repo_grantsAny other provider (a plain git server has no mirror)

Mirror tables. scm_permissions has one row per (tenant, host, repo, login) with a permission and synced_at. scm_sync_status has one row per repository with the last attempt, success flag, error text, collaborator count and whether the repository is private. mirror() (line 91) reads both and returns, per (host, repo), the logins with their permissions, a fresh flag and private. Freshness is computed in SQL as synced_at > now() - make_interval(secs => %s) with the maximum age, and a repository is fresh only if every one of its rows is. The maximum age comes from max_age(): TIA_ACCESS_MAX_AGE_S, default 3600 seconds, never below 60.

Refreshing (sync, lines 115-171). For each repository:

  1. The host and the repository's name on that host are derived: github_full_name for GitHub, scmhosts.full_name for GitLab and Bitbucket, ado.parse_remote for Azure DevOps. A repository with no such name is skipped.
  2. The token is obtained; token may be a function of the repository, because with a GitHub App it differs per installation.
  3. The reader list is fetched: ado.list_readers (after checking ado.allowed_host), scmhosts.list_readers, or for GitHub the lister (default github.list_collaborators) plus a describe call to GET /repos/<full> for the private flag. Only the GitHub path sets private; for the other hosts it is None.
  4. In one transaction the repository's old scm_permissions rows are deleted, the new ones inserted, and scm_sync_status upserted with ok = true.
  5. On any exception the permission rows are left untouched and only scm_sync_status is upserted with ok = false and an error message. The old rows therefore keep their old synced_at, become stale after the maximum age, and stop granting access. The loop continues with the next repository.

Read decision (readable, lines 174-204). The result is None for "every repository" or a frozen set of names.

  1. No principal (development mode) or a system principal: None.
  2. A service principal: None when its repo_scope is "*", otherwise the registered names that are in its scope.
  3. A user: the cache is checked under (user_id, sorted repository names); an entry younger than 5 seconds is returned.
  4. Otherwise modes, grants and the mirror are loaded and each repository is tested by its mode. For scm the user must have a login for that host in p.scm_logins, the mirror entry must exist and be fresh, and the login's permission must be in READ_PERMS (all five GitHub roles).
  5. The set is cached and returned.

Comment scope (comment_repos, lines 206-225). The result always contains the target. Other repositories are added only when all of the following hold: the target is hosted, in scm mode, with a fresh mirror that has at least one login and private is True; the other repository is hosted, in scm mode, with a fresh mirror; and the target's reader set is a subset of the other repository's reader set (readers <= {...}). In words: a comment may name another repository only if everyone who can see the pull request can provably read that repository too.

Configuration methods: modes, set_mode (validates against MODES, upserts), grants, set_grants (deletes and re-inserts the grants of one repository in one transaction) and status (the sync status rows with a freshness flag). Every change calls forget() to clear the decision cache.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
max_age32Mirror freshness limit in seconds.
default_mode39Access mode for a provider when none is stored.
AccessStore.modes / set_mode55 / 59Read and set per-repository modes.
AccessStore.grants / set_grants68 / 77Read and replace explicit grants.
AccessStore.mirror91The mirrored reader lists with freshness and privacy.
AccessStore.status106Sync status per repository for display.
AccessStore.sync115Refreshes the mirror from every host.
AccessStore.readable174Repositories a principal may read.
AccessStore.comment_repos206Repositories a pull-request comment may name.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The design fails closed: no mirror, a stale mirror, a user without a login on that host, or an unknown mode each mean the repository is not readable.
  • Because private is set only by the GitHub path of sync, the condition tm["private"] is not True means a comment on a GitLab, Bitbucket or Azure DevOps pull request never names another repository through comment_repos.
  • A successful sync that returns zero readers leaves no scm_permissions rows, so the mirror has no entry for the repository and nobody can read it.
  • The hint "(listing collaborators needs a token with push access to the repository)" is appended for HTTP 403 and 404 from any host, although the wording describes GitHub.
  • Error messages stored in scm_sync_status use the exception text only for AppError and RuntimeError; other exceptions are reduced to an HTTP status or "the host could not be reached", so response bodies and tokens are not stored.
  • The 5-second decision cache is per AccessStore object and is cleared by every configuration change and sync made through the same object.

neo4jgraph.py 262 lines

Copies one version of a tenant's graph from PostgreSQL into a Neo4j graph database so that other tools can query it with Cypher (Neo4j's query language). PostgreSQL remains the system of record; Neo4j holds only the current landscape of each tenant.

How it works

Connection. config_from_env (line 53) returns None when NEO4J_URI is unset and raises Neo4jUnavailable when the URI is set without NEO4J_PASSWORD; the user defaults to neo4j and NEO4J_DATABASE is optional. connect (line 65) imports the neo4j driver lazily (a missing package raises Neo4jUnavailable with an install hint), consults airgap.guard, creates the driver and calls verify_connectivity; a failure closes the driver and raises Neo4jUnavailable with the first 200 characters of the reason.

Projection (project, lines 101-153) is a pure function: it turns a graph into rows without contacting a server. Every key is <tenant>|<TIA node id>, which keeps tenants apart in one database.

  1. Nodes. Each node becomes {key, id, props}, grouped by label. label_of (line 83) converts the node type to a label by splitting on non-alphanumeric characters, capitalising each part and prefixing Tia: endpoint gives TiaEndpoint, event_schema gives TiaEventSchema; a result that fails the NAME pattern becomes TiaUnknown. Properties are filtered by _scalar (line 89), which keeps strings, numbers, booleans and flat lists of those and drops everything else; the names in SKIP_PROPS (key, tenant, graph_version, id) are reserved.
  2. Relationships. Each edge whose two ends exist and whose name matches NAME becomes a row grouped by relationship type, with properties status, strategy, rule, and the evidence file and line. Its key rkey is <tenant>|<src>|<rel>|<dst>|<strategy>.
  3. Impact edges. TIA's edges point from consumer to provider, but a change travels the other way for some relationship types. For each edge, if its type is in TRAVERSED_SRC_TO_DST an IMPACTS row is added in the same direction; if it is in TRAVERSED_DST_TO_SRC the row is reversed; other types get none. Duplicates are removed by a key that includes the original type, stored as the property via. This lets a single variable-length Cypher pattern answer "what does a change to X reach".
  4. Written test cases. When extracts is given, each entry of an extract's manual_tests becomes a node testcase:<repo>:<id> with its title, entry point, priority, steps, expected result, file and line. regression.targets_of returns the endpoints the case reaches, which of them were reached through a page, and the pages it names. A COVERS row is added to each named screen node that exists in the graph (strategy screen-named) and to each reached endpoint (strategy screen with via_page, or entry-point). The status is source_confirmed when the case has a non-empty entry_point, otherwise inferred.

Writing (sync, lines 184-213).

  1. ensure_schema creates, if absent, uniqueness constraints on TiaNode.key and TiaGraphVersion.key and an index on TiaNode.tenant.
  2. Nodes are written per label, then test cases with the label TiaTestCase, in batches of BATCH (2000) rows: 
    UNWIND $rows AS r MERGE (n:TiaNode {key: r.key}) SET n:<Label>
SET n += r.props, n.id = r.id, n.tenant = $tenant, n.graph_version = $version
    MERGE finds or creates the node by key, so re-running the sync does not duplicate anything.
  3. Relationships are written per type, then IMPACTS, then COVERS: both end nodes are matched by key and the relationship is merged on rkey, then stamped with tia = true, the tenant and the graph version.
  4. Cleanup removes what belongs to other graph versions. The inner function drain repeats a delete limited to 10,000 items (BATCH * 5) per transaction until it deletes nothing: first relationships with tia = true of this tenant whose graph_version differs, then TiaNode nodes of this tenant whose graph_version differs (DETACH DELETE).
  5. The remaining nodes and tia relationships are counted and a single TiaGraphVersion node keyed by the tenant is merged with the version, the time and the counts.

Ontology links (link_ontology, lines 216-241) connect TIA nodes to nodes of another model in the same database, only from an explicit mapping. The label, key property and relationship name given by the caller must match NAME, because they are inserted into the query text. For each mapping entry, the TIA node is found by exact node id, or as an endpoint whose method + ' ' + route (or path) equals the reference, or as a rule or screen with that name. Exactly one match is required; zero or two are reported in not_linked with a reason. The link is merged and marked kf_link = true; if the other node does not exist nothing is created and the entry is reported.

Reading (regression_tests, line 258) runs REGRESSION_CYPHER: from the changed node ids, follow IMPACTS for 0 to max_depth hops (clamped to between 0 and 20) to endpoints along paths that contain no TiaTest node, then return the test cases that COVERS those endpoints with the minimum hop count, coverage status and page.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
Neo4jUnavailable48Not configured, driver missing or server unreachable.
config_from_env53Connection settings from the environment.
connect65Returns a verified driver.
label_of83Node type to Neo4j label.
_scalar89Keeps only values Neo4j can store as a property.
project101Graph to rows: nodes, relationships, impact edges, test cases, coverage.
ensure_schema162Constraints and index.
_write_nodes / _write_rels168 / 176Batched idempotent writes.
sync184Writes a projection and removes other versions; returns counts.
link_ontology216Explicit links to another model's nodes.
regression_tests258Test cases reached by a change, read from Neo4j.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Labels and relationship types cannot be passed as query parameters in Cypher, so they are placed in the query text. _write_nodes and _write_rels assert the NAME pattern first, and project skips edges whose name fails it; this prevents query injection through a node type or edge name.
  • A relationship row whose end node is missing in Neo4j is dropped silently, because the two MATCH clauses return no row for it.
  • Cleanup touches only TiaNode nodes and tia = true relationships of the tenant. Ontology links are not deleted directly, but DETACH DELETE of a node that left the graph removes its links too.
  • Neo4j does not apply TIA's per-repository access control; the mirror contains every repository of the tenant, so access to the database must be limited to principals allowed to see the whole landscape.
  • The sync is not one transaction: a failure part-way leaves a mix of versions, which the next successful sync corrects.
  • max_depth is converted with int() and clamped before being formatted into the query.

Impact engine

This layer turns two revisions of one repository into an impact report. impact.py is the orchestrator: it compares the base and the proposed revision, walks the dependency graph from every changed element, and writes one finding (an affected component, with its evidence path and severity) per node it reaches. The other modules either feed it (configkeys.py finds configuration changes, evidence.py parses ingested CI, coverage, runtime and deployment records), decorate its output (enrich.py, findings_catalog.py, structure.py, communities.py, diagram.py), or present it (report.py renders and redacts, findingfilter.py filters).

impact.py 987 lines

The change-impact engine. Given a set of repositories, a target repository and two revisions of it, it produces the report dictionary that every other surface (API, pull-request comment, web page, policy check) reads. Its docstring states the constraints it works under: it reads only TIA's own extracts and graph, calls no external system during traversal, never writes the graph, and never invents a consumer.

How it works

The request. AnalysisRequest (lines 46-64) is a dataclass holding everything an analysis depends on.

FieldDefaultMeaning
reposrequiredRepository name to local path, for every repository in scope.
target, base, headrequiredThe changed repository and the two revisions compared.
revisions{}Revision to analyse for each other repository; a missing entry means "HEAD" (line 170).
pull_request, change_recordNoneCopied into the report; pull_request also changes the analysis id (line 869).
max_depth8Longest path, in edges, the traversal follows.
max_results200Maximum number of distinct reached nodes.
time_budget_s60.0Wall-clock budget, measured from the start of _analyze.
test_namingTruePassed to the graph builder (whether tests are linked by naming convention).
strip_prefixes, exclude_patterns()Path prefixes to strip when matching routes; path globs skipped during extraction. If exclude_patterns is empty the tenant configuration's patterns are used (line 145).
freshness{}Per repository, the scan state at analysis time.
configNoneThe tenant's TenantConfig; None means tenantconfig.DEFAULT.
stale_relationships[]Consumer relationships that were confirmed earlier and can no longer be verified.
evidence{}Ingested evidence records grouped by kind (see evidence.py).

Entry point. analyze (lines 130-137) opens a trace span named analysis.run, calls _analyze, copies the status, finding count and analysis id onto the span, and passes the report and the elapsed time to telemetry.record_analysis. All the work is in _analyze (lines 140-987), a single function whose sections share state through local variables. The walk below follows the order the code runs.

Setup (lines 142-148). t0 is the start time used by the time budget. rec is the store when persist is true and None otherwise: every write (audit rows, snapshot, analysis) goes through rec, while cache reads still use store. This is how a reproduction run reuses cached extracts without recording a new analysis. cfg is the tenant configuration and excl the exclusion globs. An analysis_requested audit row is written.

Section 1: resolve revisions and extract (lines 150-184).

  1. If req.target is not a key of req.repos the function returns _failed(...) immediately.
  2. Both revisions are resolved to commit ids (base_sha, head_sha) with gitutil.resolve_rev, the target is extracted at each (base_ex, head_ex), and changed_files (a list of (status, path) pairs from git) is read. Any gitutil.GitError here returns a failed report.
  3. extracts, the dictionary of repository name to RepoExtract used for the graph, starts as {target: base_ex}. The graph therefore describes the base revision of the target; the proposed revision is only diffed against it.
  4. Every other repository is extracted at its requested revision by the inner function one. With more than four other repositories this runs in a thread pool of at most 16 workers (lines 175-177); results are consumed in sorted name order so the report is identical to the sequential case. A repository that raises GitError does not fail the analysis: it is appended to repo_failures.

_extract (lines 71-84) tries the store's cache twice. If the revision already looks like a 40-character lower-case hex commit id it asks the cache before running git at all; otherwise it resolves the revision and asks again. Only on a miss does it call extract_commit and store the result. The cache key includes EXTRACTOR_VERSION, the strip prefixes and the exclusion patterns.

Section 2: change detection (lines 186-337). The output of this section is three lists: changed_components (what the report shows as changed), seeds (graph nodes the traversal starts from) and file_level (changed paths that could not be compared symbol by symbol), plus the string change_type.

  1. Unreadable files (lines 190-193). unparsed is the set of files that failed to parse at the proposed revision. _without_files (lines 116-127) builds cmp_base, a copy of the base extract with every endpoint, schema, function, test, entity, migration and type declared in those files removed, and also removes any schema that a removed endpoint returned or accepted. Without this, everything in a file with a syntax error would be reported as deleted. unevaluated counts what was left out per kind (endpoints, schemas, functions, entities, migrations) and later becomes the changes_not_evaluated unknown.
  2. Contract, event and data diffs (lines 194-196). contract_changes = diff_contracts(cmp_base, head_ex, cfg.compatibility), event_changes = diff_events(...) with the same rule overrides, and data_changes, changed_migrations = diff_data(cmp_base, head_ex).
  3. Function diff (lines 197-201). base_fn and head_fn map function id to function. fn_changes lists every base function that is missing at head ("removed") or whose hash differs ("modified"); added_fns lists ids only present at head.
  4. Covered files (lines 203-206). covered_files collects every file already explained by a symbol-level change: files of changed and added functions, of contract and event changes, and of every migration at either revision. test_files is every file holding an extracted test at either revision.
  5. Test-only check (lines 208-213). only_tests stays true only if every changed path is a test file. A path counts as a test when it is in test_files, contains a /tests/ directory, has a file name starting with test_, or satisfies is_testcase_file.
  6. Components and seeds. Each kind of change appends a component dictionary and zero or more seeds, as the table below shows.
  7. File-level fallback (lines 309-323). Any changed path not in covered_files, not a test and not ending in .md or .txt, becomes a component of type config (extensions .yaml, .yml, .toml, .cfg, .json, .ini) or file, with change: "file_level" and effect: "unknown". Every base function in that file is seeded with kind: "unclassified".
  8. Change type (lines 324-337). The first matching row of the second table below decides change_type.
Source of changeLinesComponent typeSeeds
REST contract change214-223api_schema if the change kind contains schema or field, else api_endpointEvery node id in the change's seeds; kind is breaking or nonbreaking from the change's breaking flag.
Event contract change224-231event_schema if the first seed id starts with <service>:eschema:, else event_topicAs above.
Schema migration change232-242db_column if the change names a column, else db_tableEach seed carries access (which side of the table it breaks) and a data dictionary with table, column, kind, database. The pair (component, change) is kept in data_components for section 6.
Stored procedure, view or trigger244-269db_procedureThe procedure node proc_id(proc_db, name), except for an added procedure. The change is also appended to data_changes.

Unexplained changed file309-323config or fileEvery base function in the file, kind: "unclassified".

Procedures are compared by name between cmp_base.procedures and head_ex.procedures (lines 244-261). Equal digest means unchanged. Otherwise: only at head gives procedure_added (not breaking); only at base gives procedure_removed (breaking); a different signature gives procedure_signature_changed (breaking, detail holds the first 120 characters of the new signature); anything else is procedure_body_changed (not breaking), with the detail listing tables it now writes or no longer writes.

The configuration step (lines 283-288) calls configkeys.analyse. If git fails there, cfg_change becomes an empty result and config_error holds the first 200 characters of the error, which later produces the configuration_not_analysed unknown. Files that were successfully parsed as configuration join covered_files, so they do not also appear as file-level changes.

OrderConditionchange_type

Section 3: graph and evidence snapshot (lines 339-361).

  1. get_or_build(store, extracts, req.strip_prefixes, req.test_naming) returns the graph g and its version id graph_version.
  2. ev is the ingested evidence. apply_runtime overlays runtime observations on g and returns runtime_labels; the comment on line 343 says this touches this analysis's copy of the graph only.
  3. smap is the merged service map of all extracts. Its criticality table is overlaid with cfg.criticality, so the tenant's registry wins over a repository's own file.
  4. stale_by_key indexes req.stale_relationships by key. Two closures use it: edge_key(e) builds "<repo>|<file>|<dst>" from the edge's source node, and is_stale(e) is true for a CONSUMES_API edge whose key is in stale_by_key.
  5. snapshot_id is "snap-" plus the first 12 hex characters of a SHA-256 over: each repository's commit and extractor version, the target's head commit, the graph version, the stale relationships, the freshness of the analysed repositories, the ids of the evidence records used, and the tenant configuration identity. Two analyses with the same snapshot id read exactly the same inputs.
  6. seeds is filtered to nodes that exist in g (line 361). A seed for something the graph has no node for is silently dropped.

Section 4: bounded traversal (lines 363-402). reached maps a node id to {"path": [node ids], "edges": [Edge], "seeds": [seed indexes]}. The inner function bfs(allow_inferred) runs one breadth-first search per seed using g.impacted_by(node), which yields (dependent node, edge) pairs.

  • A node whose path already has max_depth edges is not expanded. If it still has dependents, truncated is set with reason "traversal depth limit (N) reached".
  • A node of type test is never expanded: tests are leaves.
  • An edge with status inferred is skipped unless allow_inferred is true.
  • If the seed has an access value, READS and WRITES edges of the other kind are skipped (line 384). A new required column, for example, breaks only code that writes the table, so only WRITES edges are followed.
  • Before accepting each neighbour the time budget is checked; exceeding it sets truncated with reason "analysis time budget exceeded" and returns.
  • The first path that reaches a node is the one kept. Later seeds that reach the same node only append their index to its seeds list. Reaching a new node when reached already holds max_results entries sets truncated with reason "result limit (N) reached" and returns.

The search runs twice (lines 400-402): bfs(False) first, so every node reachable through confirmed edges gets a confirmed path, then bfs(True) to add nodes that need an inferred hop. The second pass is skipped entirely if the first was truncated for any reason, including the depth limit.

Section 5: findings (lines 404-655). This section turns reached into the findings list. It first defines helper closures:

  • tests_reached (line 406): the subset of reached whose node type is test.
  • tests_for(node) (lines 410-419): the tests whose path passes through node (anywhere but the last position) and whose last edge is TESTS. Each test records its association strategy, evidence status and, if present, inference rule.
  • mapped_columns(ent, table) (lines 421-426): the columns an entity maps on a table, including join tables.
  • fixed_in_head(seed, ent_id) (lines 428-441): whether the entity at the proposed revision already matches the migration. An entity that no longer exists counts as fixed. For a dropped or renamed table the entity must no longer map it; for a dropped or renamed column the entity must no longer map that column; for column_added_required the entity must now map the new column. Any other kind returns False.

entity_finding(node, r) (lines 443-512) builds the finding for an ORM entity (a class mapped to a database table). Its steps:

  1. sds is the list of seeds that reached the node; kind is breaking if any seed is, else nonbreaking if any is, else unclassified. status is _path_status of the path.
  2. writers_only is true when every seed has access == "write". accessors lists the functions that read or write the entity, taken from READS/WRITES edges into it (reads are left out when writers_only). If the change only breaks writers and nothing writes, the function returns None and no finding is produced (line 458).
  3. fixed is true when the entity is in the changed repository and every seed is fixed_in_head. structural is true when any seed drops or renames a table or column.
  4. Severity comes from compute_data(kind, status, same_repo, fixed, structural, crit, cfg).
  5. The reason sentence names the service, the entity and its location, the tables or columns involved, the database, and how many analysed functions read and write it, and appends " The entity mapping is updated in the same change." when fixed.
  6. Limitations are added when the entity is in another repository (it is linked only through the shared database name, and the text says whether that name was declared in tia-services.yaml or taken from spring.datasource.url), when a type or nullability change is involved (mapping compatibility is not evaluated), when the service has unanalysed data access, when no tests were found for a cross-repository entity, and when no criticality is configured.
  7. The returned finding has node_type: "entity", impact_type: "data" and a data block with the entity, table, database, the first 20 accessors, the total accessor count and the change labels.

The main loop (lines 513-616) visits every reached node. Entities go to entity_finding. For every other node the impact type is chosen by the first matching row; nodes that match nothing produce no finding.

Node typeExtra conditionimpact_type
listenernoneevent
callsite, contract_ref, runtime_refa seed that reached it is a schema or endpoint nodecontract
samea seed is a table or column nodetransitive
sameotherwisedirect
endpointin another repositorytransitive
functionin the changed repositorydirect
procedure, or functionfor a function: some edge on the path has strategy sqldata
anything else (tests, topics, schemas, tables, endpoints of the changed repository, functions in other repositories reached without SQL)no finding

For each finding the loop computes kind and status as above, looks up the component's criticality, calls compute(kind, status, cross, crit, cfg) for the severity and rule id, and finds cons, the first CONSUMES_API or CONSUMES_EVENT edge on the path. It then writes:

  • Limitations (lines 545-558, 603-604): the path is inferred (with the inference rule ids); the path is stale (with the stale reason and detail); the change is file-level; no tests were found for a cross-repository component; criticality is not configured; the caller is known only from runtime telemetry.
  • Reason (lines 562-595), by node type. For call sites the resolution strategy prefix selects the wording: R1 "through a generated client", R2 "through a fully resolved HTTP call", R3 "through a declared contract", R5 "through a unique path match", R6 "through calls observed at runtime", anything else "via a resolved call". For listeners the reason names the topic, says "declares in tia-services.yaml that it consumes" when the strategy is E2, and lists every topic on the path when an event was re-published through more than one. For data impacts it lists each SQL step in reverse path order with the SQL text of the last SQL edge.
  • Required verification (lines 596-602): run the associated tests if any; otherwise, for a cross-repository component, identify and run consumer tests; for any cross-repository component, verify compatibility with the deployed consumer version.
  • The finding (lines 605-616): component, repository, node, node_type, impact_type, severity, severity_rule, material, evidence_status, optional resolution_strategy and inference_rule, reason, change_nature, depth (number of edges), path (one entry per node with type, repository, file, line), path_edges, evidence, tests, limitations, required_verification.

_path_status (lines 87-99) reduces a path to one evidence status. The weakest hop decides, in this order: any stale edge gives stale; else any inferred; else any runtime_observed; else any corroborated; else any contract_confirmed; else source_confirmed.

Stale relationships (lines 617-655). A stale relationship whose consumer file no longer parses has no edge in the graph, so the traversal cannot reach it. The code therefore builds affected_eps (endpoints of the target that were reached or seeded) and, for each stale record whose provider is the target, whose consumer is another repository and whose provider_endpoint is affected, appends the record to stale_on_change. If the record's key is not in covered_keys (the keys of CONSUMES_API edges already on a reached path) it adds a synthetic finding: node type callsite, impact type contract, evidence status stale, depth 1, a two-hop path from the endpoint to the recorded consumer location, severity from compute(kind, STALE, True, ...), and the verification step "Re-scan <repo> and confirm whether it still calls ...".

Post-processing of findings (lines 656-682).

  1. For every finding, apply_override applies the tenant's severity override for its rule. When one applies, the finding gets severity_override (rule, default, configured) and a limitation saying which configuration set it. material is then recomputed with the tenant's thresholds; a breaking change counts toward materiality unless the rule is SEV-DATA-UPDATED.
  2. Findings are sorted by severity (highest first), then depth, component and node id, and numbered F1, F2, ... in that order (lines 664-666).
  3. scope is built by test_scope(...); scope["config_checks"] comes from configkeys.checks and scope["regression"] from regression.select.
  4. runtime_of attaches a runtime summary to findings whose path has runtime observations.
  5. builds and deploy are computed by build_view and deployment_view only when the corresponding evidence exists. With deployment evidence, each finding outside the target repository gets deployed and extra limitations from deployment_limitations.

Section 6: unknowns and status (lines 684-853). An unknown is a statement of something the analysis could not see. Each has an area, a reason, and affects_confidence, which decides whether it downgrades the status. The complete list, in the order the code can emit them:

procedures_not_found757g.sql_unresolved is non-empty and there are data changes or a seed whose id starts with proc:.true
dynamic_sql761There are no data changes and some extract has a data unknown of kind dynamic_sql.false
severity_overridden767Any finding carries severity_override; items count overrides per rule.false
configuration_not_analysed775config_error is set (git failed in configkeys.analyse).true
configuration_outside_repository780The change newly reads a key that no configuration file in the repository sets.false
file_level_analysis785file_level is non-empty.true
unsupported_language_files789Any extract has non_python_files.true
routes_not_resolved793Any extract has route_unknowns (first 100 listed).true
unsupported_http_clients798Any extract has unsupported_clients.true
inaccessible_repositories803repo_failures is non-empty.true
parse_failures809Any extract, or the proposed revision of the target, has files_failed.true
context_paths_not_applied820A configuration repository sets a servlet context path for an analysed service that has none of its own, and the path is not in strip_prefixes.true
changes_not_evaluated825unevaluated is non-empty (declarations in files that do not parse at head).true

The status (lines 848-853) is decided before the evidence unknowns (from ci_results_unavailable down) are appended; all of those have affects_confidence false, so they could not change it anyway.

if truncated or repo_failures or parse_failures or stale_scans:
    status = PARTIAL
elif any(u["affects_confidence"] for u in unknowns):
    status = COMPLETE_WITH_GAPS
else:
    status = COMPLETE

Report assembly (lines 855-897). summary counts findings, material findings, findings per severity and per evidence status. It gets a message when there are no findings ("No downstream impact was identified ... this is not proof that no impact exists."), which is replaced by a more specific sentence for a test-only or documentation-only change. The analysis id aid is tia-pr-<number>-<first 7 of head> for a pull request, otherwise tia- plus 12 hex characters of a SHA-256 over target, both commits and the snapshot id. The report dictionary holds analysis_id, status, summary, change (including changed_components and tests_in_changed_repository), evidence_snapshot_id, coverage (repositories analysed with their freshness, inaccessible repositories, the graph's resolution statistics, an optional data summary, traversal limits and whether they were hit, the tenant configuration id and changed settings when it is not the default, and whether criticality is configured), findings, test_scope, unknowns, an empty policy_results, generated_at, configuration_version and extractor_versions.

Derived overlays (lines 898-912). structure_mod.analyse and communities_mod.cluster are both run for the target repository only and stored as structural_risk and communities. For each finding in the target repository, limitations_for_file adds notes when the finding's evidence file is on a cycle, is a coupling hub, or is part of a layer violation. enrich_all adds the catalog's rule_id, explanation and remediation to every finding. build_architecture_diagram produces architecture_diagram.

Evidence enrichment (lines 913-955). A TestEvidence object is built from the test-run and coverage records; the revisions it treats as "analysed" are [head_sha, base_sha] for the target and the extracted commit for every other repository. enrich_tests adds evidence states to every test, coverage to changed functions, test_recommendations and test_gaps. The section then appends the evidence unknowns listed above, sets report["deployments"] and report["builds"] (without the internal _all key) when present, and sets report["failure_signatures"] when test runs contain grouped failures.

Policy (lines 956-961). owners maps service to owning team: each extract's service_map["owner"], overridden by cfg.ownership. report["owners"] keeps only the owners of the target service and of components with findings. evaluate_policy is called with the report, cfg.policies, the criticality table, the owners and the ingested approval records; its mode, outcome and blocking go to report["policy"] and its per-rule results to report["policy_results"].

Scrub (lines 962-972). scrub_json(report) returns a copy with credential-shaped values removed and in_report, the number removed. in_source counts the secrets each extract withheld (for the target, the larger of the base and head counts). If either is non-zero the secrets_withheld unknown is appended, advising rotation.

Persistence (lines 973-987). Only when rec is set: put_snapshot stores everything needed to reproduce the run (target, both commits, every repository's commit, extractor versions, configuration version, graph id, the full tenant settings with version and digest, strip prefixes, exclusion patterns, test_naming, traversal limits, freshness, stale relationships and evidence ids by kind); put_analysis stores the report; an analysis_completed audit row is written. The report is returned.
