Edge cases and failure handling

  • A failed report (lines 102-113) has no summary, coverage or test_scope keys. Its id is tia-failed- plus 10 hex characters of a SHA-1 over target, base, head and the reason, so the same failure produces the same id.
  • Only a problem with the target repository fails the analysis. An unreadable other repository gives status partial and the inaccessible_repositories unknown.
  • Any parse failure in any repository, at the analysed or the proposed revision, gives status partial, not complete_with_gaps (line 848).
  • If the confirmed-edge pass is truncated, the inferred pass does not run, so inferred-only consumers are absent from a truncated report.
  • Because the graph uses the target's base extract, a function added by the change is listed as a component but never seeds the traversal.
  • Line 611 takes the first rule id among the path's edges when the status is inferred. This assumes every inferred edge carries a rule; if none did, the indexing would raise IndexError. Whether the graph builder guarantees this is outside this file.
  • A finding for a runtime-only caller outside the analysed repositories has repository set to the text "(outside the analysed repositories)" (line 606).
  • The event_consumers unknown (Kafka-only coverage) is tied to REST involvement (contract_or_endpoint_involved), not to event changes, so a change that touches only an event schema does not emit it.
  • The classification on line 215 looks for the words schema or field in the change kind. A schema-level change whose kind has neither word (request_enum_value_removed, response_enum_value_added, request_constraint_tightened on a schema field) is therefore typed api_endpoint.

enrich.py 376 lines

Adds what ingested evidence says to an analysis: runtime observations on graph edges, CI results and coverage on tests, builds, deployments and recurring failure signatures. It reads records already loaded from TIA's evidence store and never queries an external system.

How it works

Module constants: ENVIRONMENTS = ("development", "testing", "staging", "production") is the fixed list of environments a deployment view is checked against; SKIP_NOTE is the caveat attached to test recommendations; NO_TEST is the fixed sentence "No relevant test evidence found in the analysed sources".

Runtime overlay, apply_runtime (lines 26-58). Each record's data describes calls observed from from_service to to_service with a method and path.

  1. The function indexes the graph's endpoint nodes by service and looks for an endpoint of to_service with the same method whose path matches (graph._path_matches). If none exists the record is skipped: an observed call to something TIA has not extracted is not turned into an endpoint.
  2. If CONSUMES_API edges from the calling service to that endpoint already exist, each one with status source_confirmed is upgraded to corroborated, and the observation is stored under the key ("edge", source node, endpoint).
  3. Otherwise a node runtime:<from_service>-><endpoint id> of type runtime_ref is added (once), with a CONSUMES_API edge of status runtime_observed and strategy R6. The node's repo is the repository of the calling service if it is analysed, else None.

The returned labels dictionary maps edge keys to lists of observations (environment, time window, count, caller and callee versions, source, evidence id). runtime_of (lines 61-72) walks consecutive node pairs of a finding's path, looks each pair up in both directions, and summarises: environments, the overall window (earliest start, latest end), total calls, versions and evidence ids. It returns None when the path has no observation.

Test evidence, class TestEvidence (lines 76-178). The constructor picks, per repository, one test run and one coverage report with _pick: the record whose revision matches an analysed revision (prefix match in either direction, so short and full commit ids compare equal), else the first record in the list. It then builds:

  • results[repo]: every test of the chosen run indexed under each identity from evidence.test_keys;
  • fn_span[function id]: (repo, file, first line, last line) for every extracted function;
  • handler_of[endpoint id]: the handler function of every endpoint.

states(g, t, component, contract) (lines 119-157) returns the separate evidence states of one test with respect to one component:

KeyValue
existsAlways True.
associatedThe test's association strategy.
runOnly when the repository has a run: build id, revision, of_analysed_revision, environment, time, report reference, evidence id.
resultThe CI result; not_in_run when a run exists but the test is not in it; no_ci_results when the repository has no run.
failure_signatureThe signature id, for a failed or errored test with a message.
executed_componentTrue/False from per-test coverage overlapping the component's line span; None when there is no per-test coverage for that repository (unknown, never "not executed").
validated_contractTrue only if the finding is a contract finding, the test passed in a run of the analysed revision, and the association is test-http-call.
statesA list summary: exists, associated, the result if it is one of passed/failed/error/skipped, executed, validated_contract.

span resolves a node to a line range: an endpoint is replaced by its handler; a known function gives its extracted span; any other node with a file and line gives a one-line span. component_covered checks that span against whole-run coverage; tests_executing lists the tests whose per-test coverage overlaps a line range.

enrich_tests (lines 181-236) applies the above to a report:

  1. Every test in every finding and every test-scope row is updated with its states; findings also get coverage when the component is covered by a coverage report.
  2. For each changed function that existed at base, tests whose per-test coverage executed it are recommended with priority 1, and the component gets a coverage block (covered or not, up to 50 executing tests).
  3. Tests on findings are recommended with priority 2, or 3 when their evidence status is inferred. Tests on test-scope rows are recommended with priority 2.
  4. The inner rec function merges recommendations by (repo, file, name), keeps the lowest priority number and accumulates distinct reasons in why.
  5. report["test_recommendations"] holds the sorted list, SKIP_NOTE and the meaning of each priority. report["test_gaps"] lists entry points with no tests and material findings with no tests, each with the NO_TEST text.
  6. The return value is the sorted list of repositories that have relevant tests but no CI run; impact.py turns it into the ci_results_unavailable unknown.

Builds, build_view (lines 249-270). Records are sorted by finished_at then id and grouped by repository (a record may name a repository or a service). For the target the view holds the latest build of the proposed and of the base revision; for other repositories, the latest build of the analysed revision. proposed_build_failed and proposed_revision_unbuilt are derived flags. The internal key _all carries every build for deployment_view and is removed by impact.py before the report is stored.

Deployments, deployment_view (lines 274-321). Records are sorted by deployment time. For each repository and environment, the last record wins and is compared with the analysed revision (is_analysed_revision), and for the target also with the proposed and base revisions. Rollbacks are detected by replaying the history: a record is a rollback if it carries rollback or if its revision differs from the previous one and was deployed earlier; the last five are kept. When a build record names the deployed artifact (matched through evidence.artifact_names) the build is attached, and artifact_revision_mismatch is set if the build's revision differs from the deployment's. incomplete lists repositories missing a record for any of the four ENVIRONMENTS. deployment_limitations turns these facts into sentences for a finding.

Failure signatures, failure_signatures (lines 339-376). Every failed or errored test in every run is grouped by evidence.failure_signature of its message. Each group collects tests, runs, revisions, repositories and first/last seen times, plus incidents carrying the same signature id. The assessment is "already failing on the base revision: not introduced by this change" when seen on both base and head of the target, "new on the proposed revision" when only on head, "recurring" when in more than one run, else "seen once". Groups on the proposed revision sort first; at most 100 are returned.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
apply_runtime26Corroborates or adds CONSUMES_API edges from runtime records; returns observation labels.
runtime_of61Summarises the runtime observations along a finding's path.
TestEvidence76Chooses the run and coverage report per repository and answers per-test questions.
TestEvidence._pick98The record of an analysed revision, else the first record.
TestEvidence.states119The distinct evidence states of one test for one component.
TestEvidence.component_covered159Whether whole-run coverage touches a node's lines.
TestEvidence.tests_executing170Tests whose per-test coverage overlaps a line range.
enrich_tests181Writes test states, coverage, recommendations and gaps into the report.
build_view249Latest build per repository and revision of interest.
deployment_view274What is deployed where, with rollbacks and the producing build.
deployment_limitations324Sentences for a finding whose repository is deployed at another revision, was rolled back, or has an artifact mismatch.
failure_signatures339Groups failures across runs and incidents by signature.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A runtime observation never removes an edge and never lowers a status; it can only upgrade source_confirmed to corroborated or add an R6 edge.
  • _pick falls back to the first record given. The store returns records newest first (see evidence.py), so the fallback is the most recent run, and of_analysed_revision is then false.
  • Revision comparisons are prefix matches (_same_rev, line 240), so a 7-character id matches the full commit.
  • Priority-1 recommendations (from coverage, line 215) are created without last_result or states. They only gain those fields if the same test is also recommended through a finding or test-scope row.
  • Deployment records for repositories that are not in the analysis are ignored (line 284).

evidence.py 433 lines

Parsers that turn pushed evidence (JUnit XML, test JSON, coverage reports, OpenTelemetry traces, deployment and build lists) into normalised dictionaries, and EvidenceStore, the per-tenant table access that stores and retrieves them.

How it works

Constants.

NameValueUse
KINDStest_run, coverage, runtime_edge, deployment, incident, verdict, approval, acknowledgement, notification, itsm_attachment, release, incident_link, buildThe only evidence kinds EvidenceStore.put accepts.
BUILD_STATUSESsucceeded, failed, cancelled, runningAllowed build statuses.
MAX_BYTES25 * 1024 * 1024Largest XML text accepted.
MAX_TESTS200,000Largest number of test cases in one upload.
RESULTSpassed, failed, error, skippedAllowed test results.

XML safety, _xml (lines 33-42). Text longer than MAX_BYTES is refused. So is any text with <!doctype in its first 4096 characters or <!entity anywhere, which blocks entity-expansion and external-entity attacks before the standard-library parser sees the input. A parse error becomes EvidenceError.

Test results. parse_junit (lines 61-85) iterates every testcase element. The result defaults to passed; a failure child gives failed, an error child gives error (the loop stops at the first of these), a skipped child gives skipped. Messages are cut to 300 characters. parse_test_json (lines 88-114) accepts either pytest-json-report (items with nodeid; outcomes xfailed map to skipped, xpassed to passed, unknown outcomes to error; the parametrisation suffix in square brackets is dropped from the name) or a plain list of result dictionaries, whose result must be in RESULTS.

Matching CI tests to extracted tests. A CI result and a test TIA extracted are matched when their key sets intersect. test_keys (lines 117-125) produces, lower-cased: classname.name; module.name derived from the file path; and LastClassSegment.name. tia_test_keys (lines 128-141) produces module.name from the repository file; for Java, ClassName.name and the fully qualified class under src/test/java/; and, given a dotted qualified name, module.qualname and qualname.

Coverage, parse_coverage (lines 160-204). Three formats, chosen by content unless fmt is given:

  • coverage.py JSON (text starts with {): per file, executed_lines become ranges; the contexts map (line to test contexts) fills per_test, using the part of each context before | as the test id.
  • JaCoCo (root tag report): path is package/sourcefile; a line is covered when its ci attribute is above zero.
  • Cobertura (root tag coverage): per class element; a line is covered when hits is above zero; several classes in one file are merged.

Covered lines are stored as inclusive ranges built by _ranges (consecutive line numbers merged into [first, last]). in_ranges(ranges, lo, hi) is an overlap test. match_file (lines 207-217) maps a repository-relative path to a coverage entry: an exact key first, else the longest coverage path that is a suffix of the repository path or has the repository path as a suffix (coverage tools often write absolute or package-relative paths).

Runtime, parse_otlp (lines 232-272). Reads OpenTelemetry traces in OTLP/JSON. All spans are indexed by span id with their resource's service name, environment and version. A span counts as one observed call when its kind is server (2, "SPAN_KIND_SERVER" or "SERVER"), its parent span is in the same payload, and the parent belongs to a different service. The route is taken from http.route, url.path or http.target and must start with /; the method from http.request.method or http.method. Calls are aggregated by (caller, callee, method, normalised path, environment) with a count, the overall time window and the versions seen. parse_edges (lines 275-287) accepts the same aggregate already computed by an exporter.

Deployments and builds. parse_deployments (lines 291-304) requires a repository or service, an environment and a revision of 7 to 40 hex characters; a missing deployed_at becomes the current time. parse_builds (lines 308-332) requires a repository or service, a revision and a build_id; status defaults to succeeded; artifacts may be plain names or objects with name, version, digest (each cut to 300 characters, at most 100 per build). artifact_names lists the forms under which a deployment may name an artifact: name, name:version, name@version, the digest, name@digest.

Failure signatures, failure_signature (lines 351-360). Takes the first line of a failure message (at most 400 characters) and applies the substitutions in _SIG_RULES in order: UUIDs to <uuid>, 0x... literals and hex runs of 12 or more to <hex>, timestamps to <time>, quoted strings to <value>, file paths to <path>, numbers to <n>, runs of whitespace to one space. The pattern is cut to 200 characters; the id is fs- plus 12 hex characters of its SHA-1.

The store, EvidenceStore (lines 368-433). Every method runs inside self.db.tx(self.tenant_id).

  • put scrubs the data with scrub_json, computes a SHA-256 digest over the kind, source, repository, service, environment, revision and data, and inserts with on conflict (tenant_id, kind, digest) do nothing. Pushing the same evidence twice therefore returns the existing id with created: False.
  • _rows orders by observed_to desc nulls last, id desc, so lists are newest first.
  • for_analysis loads what one analysis uses: up to 50 test runs and 10 coverage reports per repository, up to 5000 runtime edges and 5000 deployments for the tenant, up to 50 builds per repository (including builds with no repository), up to 500 incidents, and the approvals recorded for exactly this target and head revision.
  • pinned reloads the records an earlier analysis used, by id; a record deleted since is simply missing, which makes the reproduction differ.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
EvidenceError29Raised for any evidence that cannot be accepted.
_xml33Size and DOCTYPE/entity checks, then XML parsing.
_iso49Normalises a timestamp (ISO text, epoch seconds, or epoch nanoseconds when above 1e14) to UTC ISO text.
parse_junit61JUnit XML to a list of test results.
parse_test_json88pytest-json-report or a plain list to test results.
test_keys, tia_test_keys117, 128Comparable identities of a CI result and of an extracted test.
_ranges, in_ranges145, 156Build line ranges; test a range for overlap.
parse_coverage160Cobertura, JaCoCo or coverage.py JSON to {format, files, per_test}.
match_file207Finds the coverage entry for a repository file.
parse_otlp232OTLP/JSON traces to aggregated service-to-service calls.
parse_edges275Validates pre-aggregated runtime edges.
parse_deployments, parse_builds291, 308Validate and normalise deployment and build lists.
artifact_names335All names an artifact can be referred to by.
failure_signature351Normalised first line of a failure message and its id.
digest_of364SHA-256 of kind and data, used for de-duplication.
EvidenceStore368put, list, get_many, delete, for_analysis, approvals, pinned.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The two test parsers treat the size limit differently: parse_junit raises once there are more than MAX_TESTS cases (line 81), while parse_test_json silently reads only the first MAX_TESTS items (line 98).
  • Empty uploads are errors: no testcase elements, no tests, no files, no spans, or an empty list each raise EvidenceError.
  • A JUnit time attribute that is not a number gives duration_s: None instead of failing.
  • In parse_otlp, a span without startTimeUnixNano is read as 0, which _iso converts to the 1970 epoch, not to "unknown".
  • A server span whose parent is not in the same payload is not counted, so traces exported one service at a time yield no edges.
  • put rejects an unknown kind and scrubs secret-shaped values before computing the digest, so the stored data and the digest both describe the scrubbed form.

report.py 386 lines

Presents a finished report: it removes everything an audience is not allowed to see (redact and its helpers), builds the reviewer's evidence view, compares two runs, and renders the Markdown comment posted on a pull request.

How it works

Redaction, redact(report, readable_repos) (lines 16-113). readable_repos is the set of repositories the reader may see; None means no restriction. The function works on a deep copy r and applies these steps in order:

  1. Findings. A finding in an unreadable repository is dropped and counted in hidden. In a kept finding, path hops through unreadable repositories are replaced by a hop with node "[redacted]" and type redacted, and tests in unreadable repositories are removed.
  2. Test scope. testscope.redact_scope filters the rows; regression.redact removes hidden test cases and their count is recorded in regression.withheld; configkeys.redact_checks filters configuration checks, and the number removed is added to test_scope.redacted.items.
  3. Policy results go through redact_policy (below).
  4. Owners are kept only for visible_services: the components of kept findings plus the changed service.
  5. Diagram is redrawn with visible services only (diagram.redact_diagram).
  6. Structural overlays. If the reader cannot read the changed repository, structural_risk and communities are removed, and limitations containing "(structural overlay)" or "(folder convention overlay)" are stripped from kept findings.
  7. Evidence sections. Test recommendations, test gaps, deployments and builds are filtered by repository. A failure signature is removed whole unless every repository it was seen in is readable. tests_executing on changed components is emptied when the changed repository is unreadable.
  8. Unknowns. In every unknown with items, items whose repo is not readable are removed and counted in redacted_items. For repository_freshness and stale_relationships, whose free-text reason names repositories, the reason is replaced by a generic sentence and count removed. files_by_repo is filtered.
  9. Data components. mapped_by entries in unreadable repositories are removed and counted in mapped_by_redacted.
  10. Coverage. coverage.data.databases is removed (private databases are named after services), and the repository lists are filtered.
  11. Summary. r["redacted"] records the hidden count and a note; the summary's counts are recomputed from the kept findings.

redact_policy (lines 116-131) leaves each rule's outcome untouched, because outcomes are computed on the whole report and a reader must not be able to make a rule pass by not seeing something. It removes evidence items that belong to unreadable repositories (decided by the item's repo or by the repository of the finding it names), records withheld_evidence, and replaces the reason with its text before the first colon plus a withheld note. redact_snapshot (lines 134-143) filters a snapshot's revisions, freshness and stale_relationships; a stale relationship is kept only if both its consumer and provider repositories are readable.

Audit helpers. evidence_view (lines 146-160) returns, for each material finding, its hops with the exact revision of each hop's repository taken from the snapshot, plus the snapshot itself. differences (lines 163-166) lists the top-level report keys whose values differ between two runs, ignoring generated_at; it is the check behind reproducing a result. permalink builds <public_url>/analyses/<id> or returns None when no public URL is configured.

The pull-request comment, render_comment(report, max_rows=12, link=None) (lines 182-274). The first line is always MARKER (<!-- tia-analysis -->), an HTML comment that lets the poster find and update its earlier comment. The body is assembled in this order:

  1. Heading with the status. A failed report prints only the failure reason, "No impact conclusions can be drawn from this result." and the footer.
  2. Repository, short revisions and change type.
  3. For any status other than complete, a warning line explaining why this "is not a clean impact assessment".
  4. Counts by severity. With no findings: the summary message, or, if findings were withheld by redaction, an explicit statement that this is not a clean result.
  5. The tester's section from _test_scope_md: regression test cases first (_regression_md), then the "What to test" table of entry points, rule gaps, consistency checks, configuration checks, and the evidence lines from _evidence_md (CI result counts, up to 15 recommended tests, runtime confirmation, deployed consumers that differ from the analysed revision, at most 6 listed).
  6. The Mermaid diagram block.
  7. A table of material findings, at most max_rows, showing severity, component, evidence status with strategy, and the reason up to ". Change:". A collapsed "Required verification" list follows, de-duplicated per component.
  8. Structural risk counts, only for items on changed files.
  9. A count of collapsed non-material findings.
  10. Contract changes (component types starting with api) and data changes (tables and columns, excluding the harmless kinds table_added, column_added, column_made_nullable, column_type_widened).
  11. Release policy mode and outcome with one line per rule.
  12. Coverage: repositories analysed and the outbound-call resolution rate.
  13. Every unknown with affects_confidence true.
  14. The footer: optional link, the analysis id, snapshot id and configuration version.

Helper tables: SEV_ICON upper-cases severities; EFFECT_TEXT maps change effects to display text (breaking to "BREAKING", unknown to "unknown (file-level)"); RESULT_TEXT maps CI results to suffixes, and _result rewrites the suffix to end ", other revision)" when the run was not of the analysed revision. _md escapes pipes and removes line breaks so that source text cannot break a table.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
redact16Returns a copy of the report limited to readable repositories.
redact_policy116Withholds policy evidence from unreadable repositories; outcomes unchanged.
redact_snapshot134Filters a snapshot to readable repositories.
evidence_view146Audit view: snapshot plus the evidence path of each material finding.
differences163Top-level sections that differ between two reports.
to_json169Indented JSON, key order preserved, non-ASCII kept.
permalink177Stable URL of an analysis page, or None.
render_comment182Markdown comment for a pull request.
_evidence_md296CI, recommended tests, runtime and deployment lines.
_test_scope_md323The tester's section of the comment.
_regression_md358Regression test cases to run again.
_footer383Link and identifying ids.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • redact returns the original object, not a copy, when readable_repos is None or the report failed (line 21).
  • The unknowns filter on line 83 keeps an item only if its repo is readable. Items that have no repo key at all (for example the {"topic": ...} items of event_consumers_unknown, the {"rule", "count"} items of severity_overridden, the {"key": ...} items of configuration_outside_repository) are therefore removed for every restricted reader and counted as redacted, even though they name no repository.
  • The footer text is fixed: "Advisory only - does not block merge." It is printed even when the policy section above it reports enforcing mode with a failed mandatory rule.
  • The warning text lookup on lines 194-196 covers complete_with_gaps, partial and running; any other non-complete status would raise KeyError.
  • The rule id is deliberately not printed beside a remediation (comment on line 226), because an id such as SEV-BREAKING-CRITICAL would contradict a severity the tenant has overridden.

findingfilter.py 98 lines

Filters the findings of a report for the API endpoint GET /api/v1/impact-analyses/{aid}/findings and for the web interface. Values inside one filter are alternatives (OR); different filters must all hold (AND).

How it works

apply (lines 68-98) normalises each of its eight optional arguments with _vals, which accepts a list, tuple, set or comma-separated string, trims and lower-cases each value, and returns None for an empty input (meaning "filter not used"). It validates reach, kind and issue against the fixed tuples REACH, KINDS and ISSUES and raises ValueError with all problems joined by semicolons. It then keeps each finding that passes every active filter and returns {"findings", "total", "matched", "filters"}.

FilterA finding matches when
severityits severity is one of the values.
serviceits lower-cased component or repository is one of the values.
reachreach_of is one of the values: transitive if impact_type is transitive or depth is greater than 3, else direct.
kindkinds_of intersects the values. A finding is contract if its impact type is contract or event; data if its impact type is data or it has a data block; test if it has tests; runtime if it has a runtime block or its evidence status is runtime_observed or corroborated; operational if the report has a deployment record for its repository.
evidenceits evidence_status is one of the values.
environmentthe values intersect the environments its repository is deployed to plus those of its runtime observations.
policypolicy_of intersects the values: the outcomes of rules whose evidence names this finding, plus the outcomes of rules whose evidence names no finding at all (such a rule applies to the whole change).
issueissues_of intersects the values: stale for stale evidence; inferred and unresolved for inferred evidence; unresolved also when a limitation contains "not analysed", "unresolved" (any case) or "No relevant test evidence".

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
reach_of13Classifies a finding as direct or transitive.
kinds_of19Set of impact kinds a finding belongs to.
environments_of34Environments relevant to a finding.
policy_of39Policy outcomes that concern a finding.
issues_of49Evidence problems on a finding.
_vals61Normalises a filter argument to a lower-cased set or None.
apply68Validates the filters and returns the matching findings.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • severity, evidence, environment and policy values are not validated: an unknown value simply matches nothing.
  • reach_of uses a fixed depth threshold of 3 that is not configurable.
  • Filter values are lower-cased; finding fields compared with them (severity, evidence_status) are already lower-case in reports, and service and environment are lower-cased explicitly.

findings_catalog.py 98 lines

A fixed catalog that gives each rule id a one-sentence explanation and a remediation, and attaches them to findings. It changes no severity and no evidence status.

How it works

CATALOG (lines 11-72) is a dictionary of rule id to {"explanation", "remediation"}. It has 15 entries in three groups:

Rule idExplanation (as written in the code)
SEV-BREAKING-CRITICALA breaking change reaches a critical service through confirmed evidence.
SEV-BREAKING-CONFIRMEDA breaking change reaches a downstream component through confirmed evidence.
SEV-NONBREAKING-CRITICALA non-breaking change still reaches a critical service and needs coordinated verification.
SEV-NONBREAKING-CONFIRMEDA confirmed downstream dependency is affected by a non-breaking change.
SEV-LOCALImpact is confined to the changed repository under the analysed scope.
SEV-UNCLASSIFIEDThe change could not be classified at symbol level, so severity stays unknown.
SEV-INFERRED-CAPAt least one hop on the path is inferred, so severity is capped.
SEV-STALE-CAPA previously confirmed relationship could not be re-verified and is treated as stale.
SEV-DATA-MAPPING-BROKENA migration removes or renames mapped data that this repository still maps.
SEV-DATA-UPDATEDThe data mapping was updated in the same change as the migration.
INF-CALL-AMBIGUOUSAn intra-repository call matched more than one function by simple name.
INF-INHERITS-AMBIGUOUSA superclass name matched more than one type in the repository.
STRUCT-CYCLEChanged code sits on a circular dependency among analysed call edges.
STRUCT-HIGH-COUPLINGA changed file is a high-degree hub on the call graph.
STRUCT-LAYER-VIOLATIONA dependency crosses architectural layers upward (for example utils importing services).

The ten SEV- ids are exactly severity.RULE_IDS. The STRUCT- ids are used by structure.py, which calls lookup directly.

enrich (lines 84-92) picks the first non-empty of severity_rule, inference_rule, rule_id on the finding, looks it up, and uses setdefault to add rule_id, explanation and remediation. Because every finding from impact.py has a severity_rule, the severity rule always wins and the INF- entries are reached only for objects that have no severity rule.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
CATALOG11Rule id to explanation and remediation.
lookup75Returns {rule_id, explanation, remediation} or None for an empty or unknown id.
enrich84Adds catalog fields to one finding; safe to call twice.
enrich_all95Applies enrich to a list in place and returns it.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An unknown rule id leaves the finding unchanged; nothing is raised.
  • setdefault means an existing explanation or remediation on a finding is never overwritten.
  • The catalog text describes the default rule. When a tenant overrides a severity, the explanation still describes the default (the comment in report.py line 226 is the reason the rule id is not printed in comments).

structure.py 175 lines

Computes three structural-risk overlays on the call graph at file level: circular dependencies, highly coupled files and layer-direction violations. The module docstring states these are triage context, not confirmed impact, and that layer names are folder conventions.

How it works

The file graph. _call_file_edges(g, repo) (lines 40-56) scans g.edges for CALLS and INHERITS edges, keeps those whose two ends have files that differ (and, when repo is given, both lie in that repository), and returns tuples (definition file, user file, edge). For Edge(caller, CALLS, callee) the definition file is the callee's. File paths are repository-relative, so restricting to one repository prevents two repositories' app.py from merging into one node.

Cycles, find_cycles (lines 62-89).

  1. Build dadj: user file to the set of files it depends on.
  2. For each start file in sorted order, run a depth-first search with an explicit stack of (node, path).
  3. When a neighbour equals the start and the path has at least two files, a cycle is found. Its key is the sorted tuple of its files, so the same set of files is reported once regardless of where the search started.
  4. Otherwise the neighbour is pushed if it is not already on the path and the path is shorter than 8 files.
  5. Every stack pop counts one step. When steps exceeds budget (default CYCLE_SEARCH_STEPS = 50_000) the search stops and, if a state dictionary was passed, sets state["truncated"] = True. The search also stops at limit cycles (default 25).
  6. Cycles are sorted longest first, then by file list.

Coupling, high_coupling (lines 92-98). Each file edge adds 1 to the degree of both files. Files with degree at least COUPLING_MIN_DEGREE = 4 are ranked by degree descending and the top COUPLING_TOP = 10 returned.

Layers, detect_layer_violations (lines 101-119). detect_layer finds the first path segment that is a key of LAYER_ORDER (case-insensitive) and returns it; a path with no such segment is treated as utils.

OrderFolder names
0ui
1controllers, controller, api, web
2services, service, application, app
3domain, model, models, entities
4data, repository, repositories, dao, persistence
5utils, util, lib, libs, common, shared, helpers
9tests, test, __tests__

A CALLS edge is a violation when the user file's order number is greater than the definition file's, that is, a lower layer depends on a higher one (for example utils calling services). INHERITS edges and any edge touching a test layer are skipped. Each violation records both files, both layers, a suggestion sentence, the relationship and the edge's evidence status. At most 50 are collected.

analyse (lines 122-160) runs all three, marks each item with on_changed_path (a cycle or violation that includes a changed file; a hub that is a changed file), merges in the catalog entry for STRUCT-CYCLE, STRUCT-HIGH-COUPLING or STRUCT-LAYER-VIOLATION, and returns the lists with a summary of six counts, cycle_search_truncated and a note. limitations_for_file (lines 163-175) returns up to three fixed sentences for a file that is on a cycle, is a hub, or is part of a violation; these are the sentences report.redact recognises by their "(structural overlay)" and "(folder convention overlay)" suffixes.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
detect_layer28Layer name of a path by folder convention; default utils.
_call_file_edges40File-to-file dependency edges from CALLS/INHERITS.
find_cycles62Bounded search for simple cycles among files.
high_coupling92Top files by number of cross-file edges.
detect_layer_violations101Calls from a lower layer into a higher one.
analyse122The full overlay with changed-file marking.
limitations_for_file163Limitation sentences for one file.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Cycles longer than 8 files are never found, because paths stop growing at length 8.
  • Degree counts edges, not distinct neighbours: ten calls between the same two files add ten to each file's degree.
  • A file in a folder that matches no layer name (a root-level main.py, for example) is classed as utils, the lowest non-test layer. A call from it into any recognised higher layer is reported as a violation with the suggestion "utils should not import from ...".
  • The limit of 50 violations is applied in graph edge order before sorting (lines 116-118), so which 50 are kept depends on edge order when there are more.
  • The first sentence of limitations_for_file says "Changed code sits on a circular dependency", although the function is called with a finding's evidence file, which is not necessarily a changed file.

communities.py 99 lines

Groups the files of one repository into communities (sets of files that call each other more than they call outside) and lists the most connected files, which the code calls god nodes. The result is labelled a derived overlay, not evidence.

How it works

_weighted_edges (lines 18-31) collects CALLS and INHERITS edges between different files of the repository as (file a, file b, weight). The weight is 1.0 when the edge's status is in models.CONFIRMED and 0.5 otherwise, so inferred and ambiguous edges count half.

cluster(g, seed=42, rounds=20, top_god=8, repo=None) (lines 34-99) is a label-propagation algorithm written without external libraries:

  1. Files are sorted and numbered. adj is an undirected weighted adjacency list; degree[i] is the sum of the weights of file i's edges.
  2. Each file starts with its own label (labels[i] = i).
  3. In each round the visiting order is shuffled with a Fisher-Yates shuffle driven by a linear congruential generator, rng = (1103515245 * rng + 12345) & 0x7FFFFFFF, started from seed. Using its own generator makes the result identical on every run and every Python version.
  4. Each file, in that order, adopts the label with the largest total edge weight among its neighbours. Ties go to the smaller label number (the key is (weight, -label)). Updates take effect immediately, so later files in the same round see them.
  5. The loop ends after a round with no change, or after rounds rounds.
  6. Files are grouped by final label. Groups are ordered by size descending, then by their first file name, and numbered from 0. A community's label is the most common top-level folder among its files, or root for files with no folder. files holds the first 50 members; size and file_count both hold the full member count.
  7. god_nodes are the top_god files by weighted degree. file_community maps every file to its community id.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_file_of14The file of a graph node, or None.
_weighted_edges18Cross-file edges with weight 1.0 (confirmed) or 0.5.
cluster34Deterministic label propagation; returns communities, god nodes and the file-to-community map.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • With no cross-file edges the function returns empty lists and the note "No CALLS/INHERITS file edges; communities not computed."
  • Files with no cross-file edge are not in the graph at all, so they belong to no community.
  • Label propagation can oscillate; the fixed limit of 20 rounds guarantees termination, and whatever labels exist then are used.
  • The label is only the dominant folder name; two communities can have the same label.

diagram.py 99 lines

Builds a small service-level flowchart in Mermaid syntax (a text format that renders as a diagram on GitHub and similar tools) showing the changed service, the affected services and the evidence edges between them.

How it works

build_architecture_diagram(g, report, max_nodes=24) (lines 19-57):

  1. target is the changed service (or the repository name if the report has no service). services starts with the target, every finding's component, and the service of every node on every finding's path.
  2. Every graph edge with relationship CONSUMES_API, CONSUMES_EVENT, PUBLISHES, CALLS, INHERITS, READS or WRITES is mapped to a pair of services with _svc. Edges inside one service are skipped, as are edges where neither end is in services. Each distinct (from, to, relationship) becomes one link carrying the status of the first such edge seen.
  3. Both ends of every kept link are added to services, so direct neighbours of the affected services appear too.
  4. The services are sorted by name and cut to max_nodes. affected is the sorted list of finding components other than the target.

_svc (lines 14-16) returns a node's service, else its repo, else the text before the first colon of the node id.

_render (lines 60-81) drops links whose ends were cut, keeps at most 40 links, and writes the diagram: flowchart LR, one node line per service named S<index> with its label (quotes replaced, cut to 80 characters by _safe), the target marked :::changed, and one arrow per link labelled with the relationship. An inferred link is drawn dashed (-.->), any other solid (-->). The returned dictionary holds the text in diagram and also the raw services and edges lists, which is what allows redact_diagram (lines 84-92) to redraw the diagram with only the visible services and report how many were removed in redacted_services. mermaid_markdown wraps the text in a fenced mermaid block with a heading and the note.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_safe10Makes a label safe for Mermaid: no double quotes or line breaks, at most 80 characters.
_svc14The service a node belongs to, with fallbacks.
build_architecture_diagram19Selects services and links from findings and graph edges.
_render60Writes the Mermaid text and the result dictionary.
redact_diagram84Redraws the diagram for a restricted audience.
mermaid_markdown95Markdown lines for the comment; empty list when there is no diagram.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The cut to 24 services is alphabetical (line 55), not by relevance. With more than 24 services the changed service itself can be cut; in that case no node is marked and the classDef changed line is omitted (line 70).
  • For a node with neither service nor repo, _svc falls back to the node id's prefix, so such a prefix can appear in the diagram as if it were a service.
  • Only one status is kept per (from, to, relationship): the first edge seen decides whether the arrow is dashed.
  • redact_diagram returns its input unchanged when there is no diagram text.

configkeys.py 413 lines

Finds configuration and environment-variable changes between two revisions of a repository: keys that a configuration file adds, removes or changes, and keys that the code starts or stops reading. It links each to the functions that read the key and never reports a value.

How it works

Limits and file classification. MAX_CONFIG_BYTES = 1_000_000 per file, MAX_CONFIG_FILES = 300 when indexing the whole tree, MAX_HITS = 20_000 lines from one search. is_config_file (lines 73-80) decides by name:

  • rejected first if it matches NOT_CONFIG: tia-services.yml, OpenAPI and Swagger files, lock files, tool configuration (pyproject.toml, setup.cfg, tox.ini, .flake8, mypy.ini, pytest.ini, mkdocs.yml, .pre-commit-config.yaml, cargo.toml), or anything under .github, .gitlab, .circleci, node_modules, contracts or a test-case folder;
  • accepted if it is a .env file (including .env.<name> and names ending in .env), has one of CONFIG_EXT (.properties, .yml, .yaml, .ini, .cfg, .toml), or is JSON named appsettings*, config*, settings* or env*.

Key comparison. norm_key removes every non-alphanumeric character and upper-cases the rest. payments.base-url, PAYMENTS_BASEURL and payments:base_url all become PAYMENTSBASEURL, which mirrors Spring's relaxed binding and lets a property file key match an environment-variable read.

Parsing configuration, parse_config (lines 117-150). Returns key (as written) to a comparable value string. YAML (all documents), JSON and TOML are flattened by _flatten: nested mappings become dotted keys, lists containing mappings become key[0], key[1], and scalars or lists of scalars are stored as their JSON text. A key named environment or env holding a mapping or list is treated by _env_block as a declaration of environment variables (docker-compose environment: as a mapping or ["K=V"] list; Kubernetes env: as [{name, value}]), and those variable names are stored without any prefix. INI and CFG files give section.key. Anything else (.properties, .env) is read line by line as KEY=VALUE or key: value, skipping blank lines and lines starting with #, ! or ;, and stripping a leading export. config_diff (lines 153-171) parses both sides and emits added, removed or value_changed per key; the values themselves are compared and discarded.

Finding reads in code. READS (lines 49-60) is a list of ten regular expressions, each capturing the key in group 1:

Pattern matchesLanguage or framework
getenv("K")Python os.getenv, Java System.getenv
environ["K"], environ.get("K")Python
@Value("${key") (up to } or :)Spring
getProperty("key")Java
process.env.KEY, process.env["KEY"]Node
Getenv("K"), LookupEnv("K")Go
GetEnvironmentVariable("K"), Configuration["K"], GetValue<T>("K").NET

_grep (lines 179-205) runs git grep -n -I -z -E <GREP> <rev>, optionally limited to given files, and parses the NUL-separated output into (path, line, text). GREP is one coarse alternation that pre-selects candidate lines; the precise READS expressions then run on each line. Nothing is checked out or executed.

reads_at (lines 224-260) turns hits into read records {key, norm, function, file, line, via}:

  1. Hits are kept only in code files (CODE_EXT: Python, Java, Kotlin, JavaScript, TypeScript, Go, C#, Scala, Groovy) that are not excluded.
  2. _Spans.at(file, line) finds the innermost extracted function containing the line (the one with the latest start line). A read inside a function is recorded against it.
  3. A read outside any function (a module constant or a Java field) is followed one step. ASSIGNED extracts the name on the left of the assignment; for a Java @Value field, FIELD looks for the field name after the annotation on the same line or on the next line. The file is then read at that revision and every function in it that mentions that name as a whole word becomes a reader, with via set to the name.
  4. If no function uses the name, one record with function: None is kept. Uses in other files are not followed (stated in the module docstring).

One change, analyse (lines 283-336).

  1. For every changed configuration file, read it at base (unless added) and head (unless deleted) and diff. A file that cannot be read or parsed goes to unparsed; otherwise to parsed and its changes to key_changes.
  2. If there are key changes or changed code files, compute base_reads for the whole repository at base, and attach to each key change its readers: base reads with the same normalised key.
  3. If code changed, compute head_reads for the changed, non-deleted code files only. before and after index reads by (function or file, normalised key). Reads in after but not before are newly_read; the reverse are no_longer_read.
  4. For each new read: new_to_repository says whether any base code read that key; set_in lists the configuration files at head that define it (from config_keys_at, which indexes up to 300 configuration files of the tree); other_readers lists other functions already reading the same key.

The result has key_changes, read_changes, parsed_files, unparsed_files and config_index_complete. impact.py uses the first four; it does not read config_index_complete.

Tester checks, checks (lines 348-391). Produces one dictionary per key change or read change with a plain-language check sentence and the entry points whose test-scope path passes through a reading function. The three kinds are config_key_changed, config_newly_read and config_no_longer_read. The wording covers the cases a tester needs: a removed key ("the code now falls back to its default or fails: check what it does"), a changed key nobody reads ("it may be read by a framework, another service or a deployment script"), and a newly read key that no file sets ("make sure it is set in Dev ... before testing"). CHANGE_TEXT supplies the verb phrase for added, removed and value_changed.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
norm_key68Comparable form of a key: alphanumerics only, upper case.
is_config_file, is_code_file73, 83Classify a path by name.
_env_block, _flatten88, 102Flatten nested configuration into keys; recognise environment-variable blocks.
parse_config117Parses one configuration file into key to value text; raises ValueError on failure.
config_diff153Key-level difference of one file, or None if either side does not parse.
keys_in_line175All keys read on one source line.
_grep179Runs git grep at a revision.
_Spans208Maps a file and line to the innermost function.
reads_at224Every configuration read at a revision, attributed to functions.
config_keys_at263Normalised key to the configuration files that set it, and whether the index is complete.
analyse283The configuration side of one change.
checks348Tester-facing checks with entry points.
redact_checks394Removes checks and entry points in unreadable repositories; returns the number withheld.
_unique406De-duplicates reads per function and file.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • On Python 3.10 tomllib is unavailable; a TOML file then raises ValueError in parse_config, lands in unparsed_files, and is handled by file-level analysis in impact.py.
  • git grep exit code 1 means "no match" and returns an empty list; any other non-zero code raises gitutil.GitError, which impact.py converts into the configuration_not_analysed unknown.
  • A value change is detected by comparing serialised values, but only the fact of the change leaves the module.
  • The names String, int, var, let and const are rejected as assignment targets (line 247) because the ASSIGNED pattern can capture a type or keyword instead of the variable.
  • Following a module-level read is a textual whole-word match within the same file, so an unrelated identifier with the same name counts as a use.
  • The check on lines 328-330 (still) can never exclude anything: dropped already contains only keys absent from after, and still is built from the same head_reads.
  • redact_checks edits the entry_points of the check dictionaries it keeps in place.

Rules (deterministic, versioned)

These modules hold the decisions that turn facts into judgements: how severe a finding is (severity.py), whether a contract or event change is breaking (contracts.py, events.py), whether a change passes the tenant's release policy (policy.py), and which teams must be told (coordination.py). Each is a plain function over its inputs with a fixed default rule table that a tenant can override through configuration; nothing here reads the database or the graph.

severity.py 79 lines

Gives every finding a severity and the id of the rule that produced it. It is pure: the same inputs always give the same answer.

How it works

compute(change_kind, status, cross_repo, criticality, policy) takes four facts about a finding. change_kind is the seed kind from change detection (breaking, nonbreaking or unclassified); status is the evidence status of the whole path (its weakest edge); cross_repo says whether the affected node is in another repository; criticality is the affected service's criticality from the tenant registry or tia-services.yaml. The checks run in this order and the first that applies decides:

#ConditionSeverityRule id
1change_kind == "unclassified"unknownSEV-UNCLASSIFIED
2not cross_repolowSEV-LOCAL
3breaking and criticality is "critical"criticalSEV-BREAKING-CRITICAL
4breakinghighSEV-BREAKING-CONFIRMED
5not breaking and criticality is "critical"highSEV-NONBREAKING-CRITICAL
6not breakingmediumSEV-NONBREAKING-CONFIRMED

After rows 3–6, if status is not in CONFIRMED (that is, it is inferred, stale or unknown), a cap applies. The cap is read from the tenant configuration: stale_cap for a stale path, inferred_cap otherwise, both "medium" by default.

  • Cap "none": the severity from the table stands, with its original rule id.
  • Severity above the cap: it is lowered to the cap and the rule id becomes SEV-STALE-CAP or SEV-INFERRED-CAP.
  • Severity at or below the cap: the severity stands, but the rule id still becomes the cap rule, so the finding records that its path was unconfirmed.

Severities are compared by their index in SEVERITY_ORDER = ["unknown", "low", "medium", "high", "critical"] (_rank).

compute_data rates an ORM entity reached by a database change:

SituationSeverityRule id
fixed_in_change: the head revision already stopped mapping what the migration removeslowSEV-DATA-UPDATED
Same repository, breaking, and structural (table or column dropped or renamed)highSEV-DATA-MAPPING-BROKEN
Same repository, anything else (type or nullability change)lowSEV-LOCAL
Another repository sharing the databasedelegates to compute with cross_repo=True

apply_override looks the rule id up in the tenant's severity_overrides. If a different severity is configured, it returns that severity and a record {"rule", "default", "configured"} which the engine stores on the finding. is_material returns true when the severity is in the tenant's material_severities (default critical, high, medium) or when there is a breaking change on the path and breaking_is_material is set (default true).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
RULE_IDS18The ten rule ids this module can produce; tenant overrides are validated against it.
_rank22Position of a severity in SEVERITY_ORDER.
compute26The main severity table plus the inferred/stale cap.
compute_data49Severity for an ORM entity affected by a schema change.
apply_override67Tenant severity override for a rule, with a record of what it replaced.
is_material75Whether a finding is worth attention under the tenant's thresholds.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Criticality alone never produces critical: row 3 also needs a breaking change.
  • Same-repository findings are low before the cap is considered, so the cap never changes them.
  • policy=None means the defaults; every setting is read with getattr(..., default), so a configuration object without a field behaves like the default.
  • Only an exact "critical" criticality matters; "high" criticality has no effect on severity.

contracts.py 148 lines

Compares the REST endpoints and schemas of one repository at two revisions and lists every contract change, each marked breaking or not.

How it works

diff_contracts(base, head, rules) works on two RepoExtracts. Each change it reports is a dictionary with kind, breaking, rule (a configurable rule name, or None for the always-breaking basics), element (the node id), seeds (graph nodes the traversal starts from), file, line and detail. For a configurable rule, breaking is rules[rule] == "breaking", where rules is RULES merged with the tenant's compatibility.rules.

  1. Endpoints. Head endpoints are indexed by (method, norm_path) and by handler name. For each base endpoint:
    • No head endpoint with the same key, but the same handler now serves another route: endpoint_method_or_path_changed, always breaking.
    • No match at all: endpoint_removed, always breaking.
    • Matched: compare response_shape (object vs list) and success_status when both sides declare them; compare the sorted auth markers (none → some is auth_added, some → none is auth_removed with security_review: True, otherwise auth_changed); and for every non-path parameter at head: newly required (request_param_required), allowed values removed (request_enum_value_removed) or constraints tightened (request_constraint_tightened).
  2. Schemas. For each base schema the code first finds the base endpoints that return it (resp_eps) and accept it (req_eps); used is true if either exists.
    • Schema gone at head: schema_removed, breaking if used.
    • Field gone: schema_field_removed, breaking only if the schema is returned by an endpoint (consumers read responses).
    • Field type text changed: schema_field_type_changed, breaking if used.
    • Field became required and the schema is a request body: request_field_became_required, breaking.
    • Allowed values narrowed on a request schema: request_enum_value_removed; widened on a response schema: response_enum_value_added.
    • Constraints tightened on a request schema: request_constraint_tightened.
    • Field added: request_field_became_required (breaking) if it is required and the schema is a request body; otherwise schema_field_added, not breaking.
  3. The list is sorted by (element, kind, detail) so output order is stable.

_allowed finds a field's allowed values from its constraints.allowed, or by stripping Optional[/List[ from its type and looking the type name up in the extract's enums. _tightened reports each constraint that accepts less: a lower max_length, max_items, maximum or exclusive_maximum; a higher min_length, min_items, minimum or exclusive_minimum; a constraint that did not exist before; or a new or different pattern or format.

The configurable rules and their defaults (RULES):

RuleDefaultMeaning
request_enum_value_removedbreakingA request field or parameter no longer accepts a value it used to.
response_enum_value_addedbreakingA response field may carry a value consumers have not seen.
request_constraint_tightenedbreakingA request field or parameter accepts less than before.
request_param_requiredbreakingA query or header parameter becomes required, or a required one is added.
response_shape_changedbreakingA response switches between one object and a list.
success_status_changedbreakingThe declared success status changes, for example 201 to 200.
auth_addedbreakingA route with no authentication requirement gets one.
auth_changedbreakingThe authentication requirement of a route changes.
auth_removedcompatibleA route loses its requirement; flagged for security review.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
NOT_EVALUATED22Aspects never compared (headers, error response bodies); the engine lists them under unknowns.
RULES23Default classification of each configurable rule.
_allowed28Allowed values of a field, from constraints or an enum type.
_tightened36The ways a field's constraints became stricter.
diff_contracts51Endpoint and schema comparison; returns the sorted change list.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Path parameters are skipped in the parameter comparison: a changed path is already an endpoint change.
  • A removed response field on a schema used only as a request body is reported but not breaking.
  • An unknown rule name in the tenant configuration is treated as breaking (rules.get(rule, "breaking")).
  • Headers and error response bodies are never reported as compatible; they are named in NOT_EVALUATED and surface as an unknown.
  • Field types are compared as text, so Optional[str] → str | None counts as a type change.

events.py 162 lines

Reads message schemas (Avro and JSON Schema), ties each to its Kafka topic, and compares a repository's event contracts at two revisions from the consumers' point of view.

How it works

  1. Which files are schemas. is_event_schema_file accepts .avsc, .schema.json, or any .json under a schemas/ or events/ folder, never inside node_modules.
  2. Parsing. parse_event_schema loads the JSON.
    • An Avro record ("type": "record" with a fields list): each field's type text comes from _avro_type, which unwraps unions with null (marking the field nullable), and renders enums, arrays, maps, nested records and logical types. A field is required when it has no default and is not nullable. Enum symbols become allowed. The name is namespace.name; the topic comes from x-topic or topic.
    • A JSON Schema (has properties or $schema): each property's type is its type or the last segment of its $ref; required comes from the top-level required list; enum becomes allowed; the topic comes from x-topic.
    • Anything else, or invalid JSON, returns None.
  3. Declarations. declared_events reads events: {produces: [...], consumes: [...]} from tia-services.yaml. Items may be a bare topic string or {topic, schema}; topic names must match TOPIC (letters, digits, ., _, -, 1–249 characters).
  4. Tying schemas to topics. assign_topics uses, in order: the declaration that names the schema file (topic_source = "declared"); a topic inside the schema ("schema"); or a known topic whose name equals the file's stem after treating ., _ and - alike ("file_name", which the graph marks inferred). It then appends each declared producer or consumer to the extract's events with declared: True.
  5. Comparison. diff_events matches schemas by id and reports, per field: removed, type changed, enum values added (only if the base had a value list), enum values removed (only if the head has one), field added (required or optional). It also reports every topic the base produced that the head no longer produces, seeding the topic node topic:<name>. Each change seeds the schema node so the traversal reaches every consumer of the topic.

The default rules (EVENT_RULES), judged from the consumer's side because consumers read with the schema they were built against:

RuleDefaultWhy
event_field_removedbreakingConsumers that read the field lose it.
event_field_type_changedbreakingConsumers deserialise the old type.
event_enum_value_addedbreakingConsumers may receive a value they do not handle.
event_enum_value_removedcompatibleConsumers simply stop seeing a value.
event_field_addedcompatibleOld consumers ignore it.
event_required_field_addedcompatibleA producer-side obligation; consumers are unaffected.
event_schema_removedbreakingThe contract is gone.
event_topic_no_longer_producedbreakingConsumers stop receiving messages.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
EVENT_RULES21Default breaking/compatible classification per event rule.
is_event_schema_file27Whether a path holds a message schema.
_avro_type34Type text, nullability and enum symbols of an Avro type.
parse_event_schema54Avro or JSON Schema text to a field dictionary.
declared_events86Producers, consumers and schema-to-topic links from tia-services.yaml.
assign_topics103Ties schemas to topics and adds declared events to the extract.
diff_events125Event contract changes between two revisions.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A union of several non-null types is rendered as "a | b" and treated as not nullable unless null is also in the union.
  • Unlike contracts.py, an unknown rule name raises KeyError (rules[rule]); in practice only the eight built-in names are emitted.
  • A schema whose topic cannot be found keeps no topic; it is still compared, but no consumer can be reached through it.
  • Only Kafka is modelled; the engine reports other messaging systems as an unknown.

policy.py 278 lines

Evaluates the tenant's release-policy rules against a finished report and says whether the change passes, and what to report to the pull request's protection check.

How it works

The tenant setting looks like {"mode": "advisory" | "enforcing", "rules": [{"id", "type", "mandatory", "applies_to", "params", "description"}]}. validate checks it and appends every problem found: unknown keys, a mode outside MODES, a missing or repeated or over-80-character id, a type outside RULE_TYPES, a non-boolean mandatory (default true), an applies_to that is not lists of strings under criticality and services, tests_passed.params.max_priority outside 1–3, a non-string production_evidence.params.environment, and more than 100 rules.

evaluate(report, policies, criticality, owners, approvals, coordination):

  1. For each rule, _applies decides whether it applies. With no applies_to it always does. With services, the changed service must be listed. With criticality, the changed service or any materially affected component must have one of the listed criticalities. A rule that does not apply passes with "The rule does not apply to this change".
  2. Otherwise the rule's function in RULES runs. Each returns {id, type, mandatory, outcome, reason, evidence} with outcome pass, fail, not_evaluated or requires_review.
  3. The overall outcome is the worst, in the order fail > not_evaluated > requires_review > pass.
  4. blocking is true only in enforcing mode when a mandatory rule failed.
Rule typePass whenOther outcomes
no_breaking_changeNo material finding with change_nature == "breaking" (restricted to the listed criticalities if applies_to.criticality is set)fail if any; requires_review if none but the report is partial; not_evaluated if the analysis failed
tests_passedEvery recommended test with priority ≤ max_priority (default 2) passed in CI on the analysed revisionrequires_review if no relevant tests, if some have no passing result, or if they passed only on another revision; not_evaluated if no CI result at all; fail if any failed or errored
contract_validatedEvery material contract or event finding has a test with validated_contractnot_evaluated if none of their tests has a CI result; fail if any consumer lacks a validating test
owner_approvalEvery team owning a materially affected component, other than the changing team, approved (the latest decision per owner wins)fail if any rejected; requires_review if any pending, or if owners are unknown
no_unresolved_dependenciesNo unknown in UNRESOLVED_AREAS (unresolved outbound calls, stale relationships, event consumers unknown, inaccessible repositories)requires_review, or fail when params.strict is set
analysis_completeStatus completerequires_review for complete_with_gaps (listing the confidence-affecting areas); fail for partial or failed
production_evidenceThe proposed revision is deployed in params.environment (default staging) and tests_passed with priority 2 passesnot_evaluated without deployment records; fail if another revision runs there; otherwise the tests rule's outcome
teams_acknowledgedEvery team to notify has acknowledged, or there are nonerequires_review at analysis time (no coordination status yet) or while teams are waiting; not_evaluated if a notification failed

Two adapters turn the result into what source-control hosts expect. check_conclusion gives the GitHub check-run conclusion: neutral unless the mode is enforcing; then failure if a mandatory rule failed, action_required if one is not evaluated or needs review, else success. ado_state maps those to Azure DevOps states (notApplicable, failed, pending, succeeded).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
RULE_TYPES25The eight rule types a tenant may configure.
validate32Validates and normalises the policies setting.
_applies81Whether a rule's applies_to matches this change.
evaluate99Runs every rule; returns mode, worst outcome, blocking flag and results.
r_no_breaking117The no_breaking_change rule.
r_tests_passed132The tests_passed rule, reading report["test_recommendations"] (filled by enrich.py).
_same_revision155Whether a recommended test's run was on the analysed revision.
r_contract_validated163The contract_validated rule.
r_teams_acknowledged177The teams_acknowledged rule, from the coordination status.
