Edge cases and failure handling

  • A missing data source never yields pass: without CI results the test rules are not_evaluated.
  • Advisory mode (the default) never blocks, whatever the rules say; TIA never changes the host's branch-protection settings.
  • The module docstring lists seven rule types; teams_acknowledged is the eighth, documented in coordination.py.
  • At analysis time impact.py calls evaluate without a coordination status, so a teams_acknowledged rule always starts as requires_review when any team must acknowledge.
  • Evidence lists are cut to 20 entries.

coordination.py 197 lines

Decides which teams must be told about a change, sends each one notification through its configured channel, and reports who has acknowledged.

How it works

  1. Configuration. validate checks the tenant setting {"auto_notify", "channels": {team: channel}, "default": channel}. A channel type must be one of slack, teams, webhook, email (needs a to address matching EMAIL) or servicenow_task (needs an assignment_group, cut to 120 characters). auto_notify defaults to false.
  2. Who to tell. teams_to_notify groups the report's material findings by the owning team of each component, leaving out the team that owns the changed service.
  3. What to say. message builds a subject ([TIA] repo PR #n affects k component(s) owned by team) and a plain-text body listing up to 20 findings (severity, component, reason cut to 300 characters) and the acknowledgement endpoint POST /api/v1/impact-analyses/<id>/acknowledgements.
  4. Which channel. channel_for picks the team's channel, else the default.
  5. Delivery. send returns {"channel", "status": "sent" | "failed", "detail"} and never raises.
    • Slack, Teams, webhook: the URL is the tenant secret coordination/<team>/webhook, else coordination/default/webhook. The payload is {"text"} for Slack, {"title", "text"} for Teams, and a structured JSON body for a generic webhook. _post_json checks the air-gap guard, posts through netsafe.request (private addresses only with TIA_ALLOW_PRIVATE_NOTIFY_HOSTS) and treats any status of 300 or more as failure.
    • E-mail: _email needs TIA_SMTP_HOST (port TIA_SMTP_PORT, default 587). It uses STARTTLS when offered and refuses to send in clear text to any host other than localhost; it logs in when TIA_SMTP_USER is set.
    • ServiceNow: creates a task through the ITSM client, assigned to the channel's group.
  6. Status. status combines the notification and acknowledgement evidence records per team. A team is acknowledged if any acknowledgement exists, else notification_failed if its latest notification failed, else notified, else pending. The result has per-team detail, counts per state, and complete when every team acknowledged.

The sending loop itself, including "deliver once, retry only failed teams", is in analysis_service.notify_owners.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
CHANNEL_TYPES28The five supported channel types.
NotifyError32A delivery failure with a readable reason.
validate36Validates the coordination setting.
teams_to_notify82Team to material findings, excluding the changing team.
message93Subject and body of one team's notification.
channel_for108A team's channel, or the default.
_post_json112Guarded webhook POST.
_email124SMTP delivery with STARTTLS required off this machine.
send150Delivers one notification; never raises.
status185Per-team coordination status.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • One failing channel never affects another: every exception inside send becomes a failed result with its detail.
  • A missing webhook secret is a failure with the secret's name in the detail, which the demo shows as "no channel configured".
  • A team with no owner mapping is never notified; policy.r_owner_approval reports such components as unknown owners.
  • An acknowledgement counts even if the latest notification failed, because the status checks acknowledgements first.

Tester views

This layer turns the dependency graph and the per-repository extracts into answers a software tester can use without reading code: what to retest after a change, which written test cases to re-run, how well the tests cover each entry point, what values to send, and a ready-to-review browser script. testscope.py produces the list of reached entry points that regression.py and testerpack.py consume; automation.py produces the input inventory that coverage.py, testerpack.py and scripts.py build on; flows.py, graphquery.py and graphview.py read the graph directly. An "entry point" in this layer always means an HTTP API endpoint node of the graph.

testscope.py 396 lines

Builds the "test scope" of one analysis: the endpoints a change reaches, why, how strong the change is, which tests are linked, plus consistency checks and "rule gaps" (a data rule applied by some consumers of a shared endpoint and not by others). It also classifies the effect of a change to a single function.

How it works

Change effect of one function (classify_function, line 68). The inputs are two maps of function id to Function record: the base revision and the proposed ("head") revision of one repository. Callers are found by simple name: every other head function whose calls list contains the last dotted part of the qualified name. The decision runs in this order:

  1. The function is missing in head. If callers exist and no head function has that simple name any more, the effect is breaking; otherwise compatible_change.
  2. body_hash (the hash computed without the docstring) is equal in both revisions: no_functional_change.
  3. signature_diff reports incompatible changes. If any caller exists in both revisions with an unchanged hash, the effect is breaking (callers were not updated); otherwise behaviour_change.
  4. Only compatible signature changes, or none at all: behaviour_change.

signature_diff (line 38) has two modes. If any parameter carries a type key (the Java form), it compares the ordered list of parameter types only. Otherwise (the Python form) it compares by name: a removed parameter, a parameter that lost its default, a new parameter without a default, and a changed order of positional parameters that exist on both sides are incompatible; a new parameter with a default is compatible. A changed returns value is always incompatible.

Effects are ranked by EFFECT_RANK (line 23): breaking 4, behaviour_change 3, unknown 2, compatible_change 1, no_functional_change 0. strongest returns the highest-ranked effect of a list, or unknown for an empty one.

Graph walks. _walk (line 96) is a breadth-first search that refuses any edge whose status is inferred and any node whose type is not in WALK = function, endpoint, callsite, contract_ref, runtime_ref, listener, topic. dependencies(g, n) walks g.depends_on (what n uses); dependents(g, n) walks g.impacted_by (what uses n). Both include the start node.

How entry points are derived (test_scope, line 281). The caller passes reached, a map from node id to {"path", "edges", "seeds"} produced by the impact traversal, and seeds, the changed components, each with an effect and a label. The function looks at every reached node plus every seed that is itself an endpoint:

  1. An endpoint node becomes a row in entry_points. A seed endpoint that the traversal did not reach gets a stand-in record with a one-node path and no edges.
  2. A reached function, contract_ref or runtime_ref becomes a row in code_without_entry_point only if nothing of type function, endpoint or callsite is impacted by it (lines 292-293), that is, it is the top of a chain with no analysed endpoint above.
  3. Every other node is skipped.
  4. For a kept node: the seeds are those recorded on the path plus any seed that is the node itself; change_effect is the strongest of their effects; via lists the functions and endpoints strictly between the two ends of the path; depth is the number of edges; evidence_status comes from the injected path_status function, or source_confirmed when there are no edges; cross_repository is true when the node's repository is not target.
  5. A why sentence is composed. For an endpoint it says "is changed directly" (no edges), "depends on the change through A -> B" (the via names reversed), or "is served by the changed code". Runtime and contract references get their own wording and a rewritten entry_point name.
  6. Both lists are sorted by: effect rank descending, cross-repository rows first, shallower depth first, node id.

Linked tests come from _lane_tests (line 260): it calls the injected tests_for on the entry point and on each path node in the entry point's own repository, and keeps only tests of that repository, deduplicated by (repo, file, name).

Consistency checks (lines 336-344). For each reached or seeded endpoint s, the entry points in a different repository whose path contains s are collected. Two or more produce one check telling the tester to call them with the same input and compare the records.

Rule gaps (rule_gaps, line 166). A "rule" here is an item filter recorded on a function by the extractors (Function.filters, each with line, expr, fields). The algorithm, for each endpoint S that has a declared response schema:

  1. above = dependents(g, S); entries are the other endpoints in above. Fewer than 2 entries: skip.
  2. A rule's key is the sorted tuple of its field names, normalised by norm_field (lower-case, everything except a-z0-9 removed, so on_notice equals onNotice), intersected with the normalised field names of S's response schema. A filter with an empty intersection is not a rule on S's data and is ignored (_rules, line 154).
  3. own = rules in dependencies(g, S): rules S applies itself. These are never gaps.
  4. For each entry e, its "lane" is dependencies(g, e) & above: the nodes between e and S. The lane's rules are collected the same way.
  5. For each rule key found in any lane and not in own: if at least one entry has it and at least one lacks it, a gap is emitted with applied_by, missing_in, the expression, evidence_status fixed to source_confirmed, and a reason sentence naming up to 5 lacking entry points.

Inside test_scope the gaps are limited to reached or seeded endpoints, and each gets resolved_by_this_change: true when the gap is in the target repository and resolved_in_head (line 213) finds, by walking the head revision's functions from the shared endpoint's handler (calls followed only when the simple name is unique), a filter whose normalised fields are a superset of the gap's fields.

Common components (shared_components, line 239). For every endpoint, every function or endpoint in its dependencies is credited with that endpoint. Components used by at least min_entry_points (default 2) endpoints are ranked by entry-point count, then service count, and cut to limit (default 100); each row lists at most 20 users.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
strongest27Highest-ranked effect of a list; unknown if empty.
norm_field32Normalises a field name for cross-language comparison.
signature_diff38Returns (incompatible, compatible) differences between two signatures.
classify_function68Returns (effect, reason) for one changed function.
_walk, dependencies, dependents96, 108, 113Confirmed-only breadth-first walks down and up the graph.
entry_name118METHOD route for an endpoint (falls back to the normalised path), otherwise the node name.
labels_of125Reads plain-language names from entry_points: in each repository's service map; keys are service:METHOD normalised-path, labels cut to 120 characters.
rule_gaps166Finds rules applied by some consumers of a shared endpoint and not others.
resolved_in_head213True when the proposed revision moves a gap's rule into the shared endpoint itself.
shared_components239Ranks functions and endpoints by how many entry points depend on them.
test_scope281Assembles entry points, code without entry point, consistency checks, rule gaps and a summary.
summary_of360Counts rows, effects (zero counts dropped) and distinct linked tests.
redact_scope368Removes or masks everything in repositories the reader may not see.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • signature_diff returns two empty lists when either signature is missing, so a change with no signature data is reported as "logic changed (same signature)".
  • Caller detection is by simple name, so a caller of a different function with the same name is counted; the reason text states the count rather than hiding this.
  • Inferred edges never contribute to rule gaps, common components or lanes, because _walk rejects them.
  • redact_scope drops whole rows in unreadable repositories and counts them; masks unreadable via and path steps as [redacted] and rewrites why; withholds a consistency check or rule gap entirely if any participant is unreadable; and adds a redacted note with the count.
  • A runtime_ref with no repository shows (outside the analysed repositories) as its repository.

regression.py 156 lines

Selects, from the written (manual) test cases stored in the analysed repositories, the ones a change makes worth re-running. Every case lands in exactly one of three buckets: selected, not_selected or unmapped.

How it works

The suite is manual_tests of every extract (the changed repository at its base revision), each tagged with its repository. Matching reuses the text rules of coverage._entry_matches and coverage._page_matches.

  1. _endpoints (line 39) lists every endpoint with its label; _pages (line 45) lists every UI page with calls, the endpoint node ids matched to the page's API calls by (method, normalised path).
  2. targets_of (line 58) finds what one case tests. on_page is every page the case names. An endpoint is matched directly only if the case's entry_point column starts with an HTTP method (regular expression METHOD, line 28) or the case names no page; this prevents a page route such as /orders from also matching an API with the same path. The result is the direct endpoints plus the endpoints called by the named pages.
  3. Bucket decision in select (lines 75-105):
    • No endpoint nodes but pages were named: not_selected, reason "tests screen X, which calls no API".
    • No endpoint nodes and no pages: unmapped, reason "review it by hand".
    • Nodes found but none is in the scope's entry_points: not_selected, naming up to 3 endpoints it tests.
    • Otherwise selected, with one reason per reached node (entry point, service, change effect, the scope's why, and via_page when the link came through a page).
  4. Reasons are sorted by effect rank descending; the first sets the case's change_effect and headline reason.
  5. Selected cases are sorted by effect rank descending, then priority, repository, id. PRIORITY_RANK (line 24) maps p0/blocker/critical to 0, p1/high to 1, p2/medium/normal to 2, p3/low to 3, p4 to 4; anything else is 5.
  6. If head_cases is given, cases of the target repository at the proposed revision are compared by id with the base ones: a new id is added; a difference in title, steps, expected or entry_point is edited. These go to changed_by_this_change, separately from the regression buckets.

summary_of (line 123) builds the headline, for example "Regression-test 4 of your 10 test case(s) (5 not affected by this change; 1 could not be mapped - review by hand)."

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_priority31Numeric rank of a case's priority text; 5 when unknown.
_case35Keeps id, title, entry_point, priority, type, file, line, repo.
targets_of58Returns (endpoint nodes, (page, node) pairs, pages named) for a case.
select68Buckets every case and returns the full result with basis and summary.
summary_of123Counts and headline.
redact137Removes cases and reasons in unreadable repositories; returns the number withheld.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A case TIA cannot map is never treated as unaffected; it is listed under unmapped.
  • With no written cases at all the headline says nothing was found to select from.
  • In redact, a selected case whose reasons are all in unreadable repositories stays selected with the reason "through code in repositories you cannot access". The summary is recomputed without a total argument, so the total becomes the sum of the three remaining buckets.
  • "Selected" is a static statement that the change reaches what the case names; the constant BASIS says it does not mean the case will fail.

coverage.py 343 lines

Answers "are my tests enough?" on the technical side. For each endpoint and UI page it derives a list of "technical aspects" (things a test suite should exercise) and decides, by static text matching, which automated tests and written test cases address each one.

How it works

How aspects are generated for an endpoint (api_aspects, line 49). The input is one item of automation.api_inventory.

KindId suffixGenerated when
call#callAlways, once per endpoint.
missing_input#missing:NAMEThe input is required and is not a path parameter.
invalid_value#invalid:NAMEThe input has a non-empty rule description, or has allowed values (then the title says "a value outside A, B is rejected").
allowed_values#values:NAMEThe input has allowed values; the aspect carries them as strings.
error_status#status:CODEOne per error status found in the handler and the functions it calls.
rule#rule:f1,f2One per distinct set of normalised filter fields among the functions in testscope.dependencies(g, endpoint).

For a page (page_aspects, line 81): one call aspect "page X is opened", then for each input, select or textarea element: missing_input if mandatory, invalid_value if it has rules and is not a select, allowed_values if option_values returns any option. The element is named by its label, then name, then CSS locator, then tag; the name attribute is kept as also so either spelling matches.

Which tests are considered for an endpoint (coverage_report, lines 231-261). An automated test is considered if it is "linked" (a TESTS edge points from it to any node in the endpoint's dependencies that is in the endpoint's own repository) or one of its http_calls has the same method and a matching path. A written case is considered if _entry_matches is true, except that a case that names a page is skipped unless its entry_point column starts with an HTTP method.

Automated test matching (_auto_covers, line 102). REJECT is {400, 422}; lits is the lower-cased set of the test's literals; sent is the test's request bodies to this endpoint (each [method, path, keys, {key: value}]).

Aspect kindCovered when
callThe test calls the endpoint ("calls it") or is linked ("linked to its code").
invalid_value, body field, bodies visibleA sent value for the field fails automation.violates against the field's rules and the test asserts 400 or 422. A visible body with only valid values does not count.
allowed_values, body field, bodies visibleFor each allowed value actually sent; the values hit are recorded.
missing_input, bodyThe test asserts 400/422 and sends a body whose keys do not include the field.
missing_input, query/headerThe test asserts 400/422 and the field name is not among its literals.
invalid_value, value not visibleThe test asserts 400/422 and the field name is among its literals.
allowed_values, value not visibleFor each allowed value that appears among the literals.
error_statusThe status is in the test's asserted statuses.
ruleA rule field appears among the literals, exactly or after norm_field normalisation.

Except for call, and for rule when the test is linked, the test must actually call the endpoint (line 121).

Written test case matching (_written_covers, line 146). The text is title + steps + expected. A field is "mentioned" (_mentions, line 43) when its lower-cased name or its spaced form (accountId and account_id both become account id) appears not adjacent to another letter or digit.

Aspect kindCovered when
callAlways (the case already named the entry point).
missing_inputThe field (or its also name) is mentioned and MISSING_WORDS matches anywhere: missing, without, empty, blank, omit..., leave... out, left out, not filled/entered/provided/given, no value.
invalid_valueThe field is mentioned and INVALID_WORDS matches: invalid, exceed..., too long/short/big/small/large, more than, less than, longer, shorter, wrong, letters, negative, format, over, under, above, below, beyond, not allowed, non-numeric, special character(s).
allowed_valuesFor each allowed value that appears as a whole token, case-insensitively.
error_statusThe status number appears as a word, or one of its phrases in REASONS appears (for example 404: "not found", "unknown", "does not exist", "doesn't exist"; 409: "conflict", "duplicate", "already exists").
ruleAny of the rule's fields is mentioned.

Naming an entry point (_entry_matches, line 167). If the case has an entry_point column: METHOD /path must match method and path; a value starting with / must match the path; anything else must equal the endpoint's label, case-insensitively. Without the column, the text is scanned for /path tokens (optionally preceded by a method) and, failing that, for the label as a substring. Paths are compared with graph._path_matches after normalize_path. _page_matches (line 185) compares against the page name, route and component name; in free text only names longer than 3 characters count.

Status and percentage. _apply (line 194) sets each aspect to covered or missing; an allowed_values aspect is covered when no value is missing, partial when some are hit, else missing. The percentage is 100 * (covered + 0.5 * partial) / total, rounded to one decimal, and 100.0 when there are no aspects.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_words, _mentions37, 43Prose forms of a field name and the whole-token search for them.
api_aspects49Aspects of one endpoint.
page_aspects81Aspects of one UI page.
_auto_covers102Why an automated test addresses an aspect, or None.
_written_covers146Why a written case addresses an aspect, or None.
_entry_matches, _page_matches167, 185Whether a written case names an endpoint or a page.
_apply, _record194, 207Store hits on aspects and compute their status.
coverage_report214The full report: entries, unmatched written tests, summary.
assess_test_case308Runs the report for one ad-hoc case and lists what it covers and misses per matched entry point.
redact_coverage325Drops unreadable entry points and recomputes coverage from readable tests only.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • "Covered" means a test addresses the aspect, never that it passes; the report's basis string says so.
  • Written cases are not matched to automated-test logic and vice versa; pages are matched by written cases only (the page loop at line 262 never looks at automated tests).
  • With only_written set (the single-case query), automated tests are ignored entirely (line 221).
  • Tests linked through TESTS edges count only when the linked code is in the endpoint's own repository, so a provider's tests do not cover a consumer's endpoint.
  • At line 246 the same linked flag is passed for both the linked and ep_reached parameters of _auto_covers.
  • The query/header missing_input rule is loose: any test that calls the endpoint, asserts 400 or 422 and does not contain the field name as a standalone literal is taken to leave the field out.
  • Aspect ids are built from the input name alone, so a path parameter and a body field with the same name on one endpoint share an id (lines 55-63, 233).

automation.py 401 lines

Builds the automation inventory: for every endpoint its inputs, rules, generated valid and invalid values and a test matrix; for every UI page its elements with locators, rules and values. Values come only from declared rules; when a value cannot be generated the module says so instead of guessing.

How it works

Normalising a field's rules (rules_of, line 38). base_type extracts the innermost named type by removing wrappers such as Optional, List, Set, Annotated, Union, Field, Query, Path and a trailing []. Allowed values are constraints["allowed"] or, failing that, the enum declared under that type name. The kind is then chosen in this order:

KindChosen when
enumThere are allowed values.
booleanType in BOOL (bool, boolean, Boolean) or the HTML hint is checkbox.
integerType in INT (int, Integer, long, Long, short, Short, byte, BigInteger, conint, PositiveInt, NegativeInt).
numberType in NUM (float, double, Decimal, BigDecimal, ...) or hint number/range.
date / datetimeType in DATE / DATETIME, or hint date / datetime-local.
stringType in STR, or any other hint is present.
objectEverything else.

A format of email or an email hint forces string with format = "email".

Generating values (values, line 130) returns two lists of {"value", "why"}. A required field always gets the invalid marker <missing> first.

Kind / constraintValid valuesInvalid values
enumEvery allowed value.999999 if all allowed values are integers, else NOT_A_<NAME> (name upper-cased, non-alphanumerics to _).
booleanTrue, False."maybe".
integer / numberThe lowest and highest allowed, where declared. An exclusive bound is moved inward by the step (1 for integers, 0.01 for numbers). With no bounds: 1 or 1.5.One step below the minimum, one step above the maximum, 1.5 for an integer ("not a whole number"), and "abc".
date2026-01-312026-13-45
datetime2026-01-31T10:15:00Zyesterday
string, format: emailtester@example.comnot-an-email
string, patternA sample from sample_for_pattern, or None with "TIA cannot generate one"; plus a sample of exactly max_length when the first is shorter.The first of "!@#", "abc", "123", 40 As, "" that does not match.
string, lengths only"a" * min_length, "a" * max_length; with neither, "sample text".(see next row)
any string with min_length / max_length"a" * (min - 1) ("empty" when min is 1); max + 1 characters (A when a pattern exists, else a).
objectNone with "an object of type T (see its schema)".None generated.
multi (list) fieldsA one-item list is put first; the "typical value" entry is dropped.A list one shorter than min_items, one longer than max_items, and the bare single value ("not a list").

Sampling a pattern (sample_for_pattern, line 58). Anchors ^ and $ are stripped, the pattern is split on top-level |, and each alternative is read unit by unit. _unit maps known character classes through _CLASSES (for example \d to 0, [A-Z] to A, \w and . to a), takes the first character of any other non-negated class, accepts escaped punctuation and plain literals, and gives up on groups and bare metacharacters. _quant repeats the unit the minimum number of times: 0 for * and ?, 1 for +, m for {m} or {m,n}. A result shorter than min_len is padded by repeating its last character. The candidate is returned only if re.fullmatch accepts it and it fits the length limits.

The API test matrix (endpoint_inventory, line 279):

  1. Inputs are the endpoint's params (path, query, header) followed by the fields of the first request schema found, marked in: "body".
  2. Each input gets valid, invalid and a prose rules string from _describe. Path parameters lose the <missing> case, because a path without the parameter is a different route.
  3. The baseline (case C1, "valid baseline", expect accepted) holds the first non-None valid value of every input that is required or has one, grouped by location.
  4. One case per invalid value: the baseline with that field replaced or removed, kind invalid. The expected outcome is rejected (400) when the endpoint's file ends in .java, otherwise rejected (422); for a path parameter it is rejected (400 or 404).
  5. One case per further valid value (valid[1:], skipping None), kind boundary or allowed value, expect accepted.
  6. The list is cut to MAX_CASES = 200; case_count keeps the real number and cases_truncated flags the cut. valid_combinations is the product of the valid-value counts per input (at least 1 each).

_handler_statuses (line 256) collects error statuses from the handler and every function reachable through calls, following a call only when exactly one function in the repository has that simple name; results are deduplicated by (status, detail).

UI inventory (ui_inventory, line 358). Each element's rules are computed with the HTML input type (or the tag for non-input elements) as the hint, defaulting to text. A select with options becomes an enum of option_values; if all options are placeholders it falls back to string. Only input, select and textarea get rules, valid and invalid. Each API call of the page is matched to endpoints by (method, normalised path) across all extracts; an unmatched call gets an explanatory note. The page summary counts elements, mandatory ones, dropdowns and elements whose locator strategy is position (fragile).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
base_type29Innermost named type of a type expression.
rules_of38Normalised rules: type, kind, required, multi, nullable, allowed, constraints, optional default.
sample_for_pattern58A string matching a simple regular expression, or None.
_split_alt, _unit, _quant84, 101, 119The small pattern reader behind it.

violates203Why a concrete value breaks a field's rules, or None; used by coverage.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An invalid regular expression makes sample_for_pattern return None and makes _matches return True, so no "does not match" value is claimed for a pattern the module cannot evaluate.
  • violates checks a multi field only for being a list; the items are not checked against the rules.
  • For a multi field, the valid values after the first stay scalar (line 194), while the bare scalar is also listed as invalid ("a single value, not a list"). The matrix therefore can contain an "accepted" case and a "rejected" case with the same scalar value for that field.
  • The baseline's why says "every mandatory input with a valid value", but line 298 also includes optional inputs that have a generated value.
  • A required input with no generatable value appears in the baseline as None.

testerpack.py 101 lines

Assembles the "tester pack" for one finished analysis: for the entry points the change reaches, and only those, a retest list, a coverage gate, the API test matrix and the locator pack of the pages that call them.

How it works

build_pack (line 30) receives a finished and already redacted report, the extracts and graph of the repositories the reader may read, and optionally the revisions used and a list of repositories that could not be read. It never starts an analysis.

  1. The touched entry points are test_scope.entry_points of the report. An inventory or coverage row is a "hit" when its node id is among them or its (repository, entry point name) pair is.
  2. coverage_report is run on the whole landscape; api_inventory is filtered to hits; ui_inventory pages are kept when any of their API calls resolves to a hit endpoint. Coverage rows are kept for hits and for those pages.
  3. gate_row (line 47) gives each entry point an outcome:
    • not_evaluated: no coverage row. The reason is that the repository could not be read at the analysed revision, or that the entry point is outside readable or analysed repositories, or the generic "no analysed entry point to measure coverage against".
    • gaps: the row has any missing or partial aspect.
    • covered: every aspect is addressed by a test.
    If any linked test has result failed or error, the reason gains "N linked test(s) FAILED in the last CI run"; the outcome itself is not changed by this.
  4. Rows are produced for each entry point, for each code_without_entry_point item (always not_evaluated) and for each kept page.
  5. The overall gate outcome is gaps if any row has gaps, else not_evaluated if any row is not evaluated, else covered. With no rows the headline is "This change reaches no entry point: nothing to retest."

The returned dictionary has the keys analysis_id, report_status, change, basis (revisions, unavailable repositories and the constant NOTE), gate, retest (slimmed entry points, rule gaps, consistency checks), coverage, api, ui and unknowns.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_slim23Keeps the tester-facing fields of a test-scope row and of its tests.
build_pack30Builds the whole pack.
gate_row (nested)47One gate row with outcome, reason, counts and the titles of aspects not fully covered.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • With no extracts or no graph, coverage, API and UI sections are empty and every entry point is not_evaluated.
  • The coverage summary counts the distinct tests referenced by the kept rows (line 83), not all tests in the repositories, and reports 0 unmatched written tests.
  • not_evaluated is explicitly not "safe": NOTE states that it means TIA has nothing to measure.

scripts.py 156 lines

Generates a Playwright test in JavaScript for a user flow from one screen to another, using the screen navigation stored in the graph and the element inventory. Anything the code does not state becomes a TODO comment and an entry in unknowns.

How it works

Finding the screen path (route_between, line 30). screens_of maps each normalised route of one repository to its screen node id. The start and end routes are normalised and looked up; if either is unknown the result is None. Otherwise a breadth-first search follows NAVIGATES_TO edges (neighbours visited in sorted order, so the result is deterministic) and returns the shortest chain of screen ids, or None if the screens are not connected.

Assembling the script (generate, line 74):

  1. If there is no chain, return {"error", "screens_known", "note"} listing the routes that exist.
  2. Load the repository's pages from ui_inventory, keyed by repo:screen:page; index endpoint nodes, RETURNS edges (endpoint to its response node) and CALLS_API edges (screen to endpoints).
  3. Write the header: two comment lines (repository, first 12 characters of the commit, UTC date, start and end route), the require('@playwright/test') line, BASE_URL (environment variable or the base_url argument, default http://localhost:3000) and the opening of one test(...).
  4. For each screen in the chain:
    • The first screen gets await page.goto(BASE_URL + route) with colons removed from the route; later screens get await expect(page).toHaveURL(new RegExp(...)), where the expression is the normalised route with each parameter replaced by [^/]+ and $ appended. A route with a parameter is recorded as an unknown.
    • Each input, select or textarea (except types hidden, submit, button) is filled. _value takes the first non-None valid value from the inventory, else the first non-placeholder option. A select uses .selectOption(...), a checkbox or radio uses .check(), anything else .fill(...). With no value, a mandatory element or any select gets a TODO and a "no value" unknown; an optional input is silently skipped.
    • The locator is page. plus the element's playwright form, or page.locator("css or xpath"). A locator with strategy position (fragile) is recorded as an unknown.
    • The action that leaves the screen is chosen in this order: an a element whose href or to attribute equals the next route; the first button of type submit; the first button.
    • If the screen calls an API and the action is not the link, the click is wrapped in Promise.all with page.waitForResponse for the first called endpoint (method and path expression), followed by expect(response.status()).toBe(N) when the response node has a success_status, else expect(response.ok()).toBeTruthy(). Otherwise it is a plain await ... .click().
    • With no action and a next screen, a "next step" unknown and a TODO are written.
  5. The last blank line is replaced by });. The file name is flow-START-to-END.spec.js, with slashes turned into hyphens, colons removed and an empty route written as root.

The result contains script, file_name, screens, steps (per screen: fields filled, action name, APIs called), unknowns and a note with the run command.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
screens_of25Normalised route to screen node id for one repository.
route_between30Shortest NAVIGATES_TO chain between two routes.
_js56Encodes a value as a JavaScript literal with json.dumps.
_locator60The Playwright locator expression for an element.
_value65A valid value for an input, or None.
generate74Builds the script and its metadata.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A screen in the chain with no inventory page is treated as having no elements, so only the URL check is written for it.
  • Only the first API the screen calls is awaited; other calls are listed in steps but not asserted.
  • The URL and response path expressions are built from normalize_path, which lower-cases every path segment, while the generated RegExp is case-sensitive; a route written with upper-case letters would not match (lines 106 and 139).
  • Fragile-locator warnings are issued only for input elements, not for the button or link that is clicked.

flows.py 234 lines

Shows the execution flow of an endpoint as a tree (handler, the code it runs, the services it calls, the data it reads and writes, topics, rules) and a per-service design summary (controllers, models, entities, how the code splits over roles).

How it works

How roles are assigned (role_of, line 37). The first rule that applies wins:

  1. The function is the source of a HANDLES edge: controller.
  2. A folder name in the file path matches FOLDER_ROLE (matched case-insensitively as a whole path segment):
    FoldersRole
    controllers, controller, api, web, routes, viewscontroller
    services, service, applicationservice
    domain, model, models, entities, schemasmodel
    data, repository, repositories, dao, persistencedata access
    clients, client, integrationsclient
  3. The qualified name has a class part whose name ends with a suffix in NAME_ROLE: Repository/Repo/Dao/DAO/Store give data access; Controller/Resource/Handler/View give controller; Service/Manager/UseCase give service; Client/Gateway/Adapter give client.
  4. Outgoing edges: READS or WRITES gives data access; PUBLISHES gives events; ISSUES to a callsite gives client.
  5. Otherwise service.

The execution tree (flow, line 63). The roots are the sources of HANDLES edges into the endpoint, at depth 1. The nested step function expands a handler or function by its outgoing edges, sorted by relationship name and then target name:

EdgeChild step
CALLS to a functionRecursive function step.
CALLS to a procedureLeaf of kind stored procedure.
ISSUES to a callsiteOne calls service leaf per CONSUMES_API target of the call site. With no target: an outbound call leaf noting the service could not be resolved, unless the call site has a client package and the function also publishes to a topic (then it is skipped, because the topic already shows it).
READS / WRITESLeaf of kind reads / writes with data_type.
PUBLISHESLeaf of kind publishes.
APPLIES_RULELeaf of kind rule, named by the rule expression.

A step reached through an inferred edge carries inferred (the edge's rule name). A function already on the current path is marked recursive and not expanded. Limits: MAX_DEPTH = 8 (a function at that depth is marked truncated) and MAX_STEPS = 300 function steps per tree. The result has declared_only: true when the endpoint has no handler, which is the case for endpoints declared only in a specification.

The design view (design, line 157). For each service node: controllers are handler functions with the endpoints they serve; roles counts every function by role; models are schemas reached by USES_SCHEMA from an endpoint; entities are entity or table nodes read or written by any function in _below the endpoint (the handler plus everything reachable through CALLS), each with the entry points that use it and whether they read or write. Entities are sorted by number of users, descending.

render (line 218) prints a flow as numbered, indented text lines such as 1.2 name [role] (inferred) file:line.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_index30Edges grouped by source node.
role_of37Design role of a function.
flow63Execution tree of one endpoint node.
flows144Trees for all endpoints, one repository's, or one named as METHOD /route or by node id.
design157Per-service design summary.
_below205Functions an endpoint runs.
render218Text form of a flow.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The order of calls inside a function is not recorded; siblings are listed by name and the result's note says so.
  • The step count covers functions only; leaves (data, topics, rules, service calls) are not counted towards MAX_STEPS.
  • The docstring of role_of lists a role function that the code never returns, and does not list events, which it does return.
  • Edges to nodes missing from the graph are skipped.

graphquery.py 157 lines

Answers four direct questions about a named component without any change being analysed: consumers, dependencies, tests and covers (what one test exercises).

How it works

Naming a component (find, line 44). Matching is by equality only, tried in this order:

  1. The text is a node id.
  2. The text matches METHOD path: endpoints with that method whose route equals the path as written or whose path equals its normalised form.
  3. The text equals a service name.
  4. The text equals a node's name or its last dotted part, excluding call sites, services and the structure node types (STRUCTURE_TYPES from graphschema). If both code and tests match, only the code is returned unless tests were asked for.

An optional repository filter applies throughout; for the covers question only test nodes are accepted.

Reaching (_reach, line 67). A breadth-first search runs twice: first over confirmed edges only, then again from everything reached so far with inferred edges allowed. A node therefore keeps the path that needs no inferred edge if one exists. Each reached node records its depth, parent, the relationship used and an evidence status equal to the weakest edge on its path, using _RANK: stale 0, inferred/unknown 1, runtime_observed 2, corroborated 3, contract_confirmed 4, source_confirmed 5. A test met on the way is recorded but not walked through.

Answering (query, line 100). limit is clamped to 1..1000 (default 200). A service stands for its endpoints (_starts, line 93). dependencies and covers walk g.depends_on; consumers and tests walk g.impacted_by. The rows are split into code and tests and grouped per question:

QuestionResult keys
consumersconsumers, entry_points (endpoints and listeners), services (other than the component's own), tests
dependenciesdependencies, endpoints_of_other_services, services, data (entity, table, column, procedure), topics
teststests, direct, through_callers
coversattached_to (depth 1), also_exercises (deeper), entry_points, services

Each test row says whether it covers the component directly or through a named parent. A limits list states what the answer cannot show: consumers outside the analysed repositories, the number of unresolved outbound calls, the static nature of test association, the presence of inferred rows, and truncation.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_label, _row27, 38Display name and output row of a node.
find44Node ids a name may mean.
_reach67Two-pass breadth-first search with weakest-link status.
_starts93A service's endpoints, or the node itself.
query100The public entry point.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An unknown question raises ValueError.
  • No match returns status: "not_found" with a hint; several matches return status: "ambiguous" with up to 25 candidates and the total, never a guess.
  • When the limit is reached the search stops adding nodes and the answer is flagged truncated.
  • A service with no EXPOSES edges is searched from its own node, which has no traversed edges, so the answer is empty.

graphview.py 265 lines

Produces node-and-edge lists for the web UI's graph display at three levels of detail, plus an overlay showing exactly what one analysis found. It is read-only.

How it works

build_view (line 51) uses the graph passed in or builds one. One service node per repository is always added, with counts (endpoints, functions, schemas, call sites, tests, unresolved calls, failed files), the commit, and criticality and owner from the merged service map.

LevelNodesEdges
serviceServices only.One CONSUMES_API edge per (consumer service, provider service) pair, aggregated from all resolved calls between different services.
endpointServices, endpoints, schemas, and one unresolved node per unresolved call.EXPOSES, USES_SCHEMA; one aggregated CONSUMES_API edge per (consumer service, endpoint); UNRESOLVED_CALL from a service to each of its unresolved calls.
functionEvery graph node, except that columns and structure types (screens, elements, locators, requests, responses, fields, examples, rules) appear only when a search or focus is given.Every graph edge between shown nodes; CONSUMES_API edges individually with their evidence.

An aggregated edge carries count, the sorted strategies, up to 20 evidence strings, stale_count, inferred_only, and a status chosen by _best: the first of corroborated, contract_confirmed, source_confirmed, runtime_observed, inferred, stale present among its members.

Stale relationships. The optional stale argument maps keys of the form consumer_repo|consumer_file|endpoint_id to history records. An edge whose key is present is shown as stale whatever the current graph says. Records whose key is no longer in the graph, and whose two repositories are both in the extracts, are still added to the aggregated edges as stale evidence.

Filters, in order:

  1. repos: keep nodes of those repositories.
  2. focus: keep the node and its neighbours within max(1, depth) hops, ignoring edge direction.
  3. Otherwise q: keep nodes whose label or id contains the text (case-insensitive) and their direct neighbours.
  4. If more than limit (default 600) nodes remain, keep the ones with the highest degree and set truncated.

impact_overlay (line 236) reads the findings of a report. Each finding has a path; its first node gets the role changed, its last affected (with the highest severity among findings ending there and the finding id), the rest via. Edges are deduplicated by (source, target, relationship).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_best25Strongest status in a set, by the order of STRENGTH; unknown if none.

build_view51Builds the view with filters and statistics.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An unknown level raises ValueError; a focus node that is not in the view raises KeyError.
  • A search with no hit returns an empty view.
  • A failed report gives an empty overlay.
  • Calls within one service are not drawn at service and endpoint level.
  • The endpoint label here uses the normalised path (line 37), whereas graphquery and testscope prefer the route as written.

Graph and identity resolution

This layer turns per-repository extracts into one typed dependency graph and decides which component in one repository is the same thing a call in another repository refers to. graphschema.py fixes the allowed node and relationship types and the direction a change travels; graph.py builds the graph, calling resolve.py for cross-repository links, data.py for tables and SQL, and graphstructure.py for screens, API fields and rules; graphstore.py gives each built graph a content-derived version id and caches it.

graph.py 208 lines

Defines the in-memory Graph class and build_graph, which turns a set of repository extracts into nodes and typed edges, each edge carrying an evidence status.

How it works

The Graph class (line 26) holds nodes (id to attribute dictionary), edges (a list of Edge records with src, rel, dst, status, strategy, rule, evidence) and two adjacency indexes. add_edge fills them according to the schema:

if e.rel in _IMPACT_SRC:
    self._impact[e.src].append((e.dst, e))
    self._deps[e.dst].append((e.src, e))
elif e.rel in _IMPACT_DST:
    self._impact[e.dst].append((e.src, e))
    self._deps[e.src].append((e.dst, e))

impacted_by(n) returns who is affected when n changes; depends_on(n) returns the reverse. Both return (neighbour, edge) pairs sorted by neighbour id and relationship name, which keeps every traversal deterministic. Relationships with no impact direction are stored in edges but appear in neither index.

Construction order in build_graph (line 103):

  1. Nodes, per repository (lines 107-127): the service node svc:<service>; one node per function, type declaration, schema, endpoint (with method, path = normalised path and route = path as written), call site and test. Functions are also indexed by simple name per repository.
  2. EXPOSES, HANDLES, USES_SCHEMA (lines 136-143). HANDLES is added only when the endpoint's source is code. USES_SCHEMA links an endpoint to its response and request schemas when a schema of that name exists in the same repository.
  3. ISSUES (line 144): from the enclosing function to each call site that has one.
  4. CALLS (line 147): each called name is looked up among the same repository's functions (excluding the caller itself). Exactly one candidate gives a source_confirmed edge. Several candidates give one inferred edge to each, with rule INF-CALL-AMBIGUOUS, and ambiguous_calls grows by the number of candidates. No candidate gives no edge.
  5. INHERITS (line 157): the same one-or-many logic over type names, with rule INF-INHERITS-AMBIGUOUS.
  6. TESTS (line 166), three strategies:
    • test-http-call, confirmed: the test's HTTP call has the same method as an endpoint of its own repository and _path_matches its normalised path.
    • test-import-call, confirmed: the test imports a module (exactly, or as a dotted suffix) and calls a simple name defined in it.
    • test-naming, inferred with rule INF-TEST-NAMING: only when test_naming is true and neither strategy above linked the test. The file stem is derived (test_x.py or x_test.py gives x; XTests.java, XTest.java, XIT.java give X) and the test is linked to every function of a module whose last name part equals the stem.
  7. Events: add_events (line 73). One global topic:<name> node per topic. A producer gets PUBLISHES from its function, or from the service node when no function is known. A consumer gets a listener node, CONSUMES_EVENT to the topic and ISSUES from its function. Status is contract_confirmed with strategy E2 when the event is declared, else source_confirmed with E1. An event schema is linked by USES_SCHEMA from its topic; when the topic was derived from the file name the edge is inferred with rule INF-SCHEMA-FILE-NAME.
  8. Cross-repository calls: resolve_consumers returns the CONSUMES_API edges, the unresolved list, statistics and synthetic contract_ref nodes, all added to the graph (lines 199-204).
  9. Data: add_data_layer, then add_sql_lineage.
  10. Use-case structure: add_structure.

_path_matches (line 20) compares an endpoint template with a concrete or templated path: both are split on /, the segment counts must be equal, and each pair must be equal or have {} on either side.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_path_matches20Segment-wise path comparison with {} as a wildcard.
Graph26Nodes, edges, impact indexes, unresolved calls, statistics.
Graph.add_edge40Stores an edge and indexes it by impact direction.
Graph.impacted_by / depends_on51, 54Sorted neighbours in the impact direction and against it.
Graph.to_dict / from_dict58, 63Serialisation; from_dict re-adds every edge so the indexes are rebuilt.
add_events73Topics, producers, listeners and event schemas.
build_graph103Builds the whole graph from extracts.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Calls and inheritance are linked by simple name inside one repository only; nothing here links code across repositories.
  • An ambiguous call is kept as several inferred edges rather than dropped, so its impact is visible but marked.
  • A USES_SCHEMA edge is silently omitted when the named schema is not among the repository's schemas.
  • Every edge's evidence records repository, file, line and commit.
  • How module_name (imported from extract_python) names Java files was not read for this section, so the exact behaviour of the Java branch of test-naming is not confirmed here.

resolve.py 163 lines

The only place where relationships between repositories are created: it decides which provider endpoint each outbound HTTP call or declared contract refers to, using five enumerated strategies. A name match alone never creates a relationship.

How it works

StrategyWhat it usesResulting status
R1A generated or shared client package mapped to a provider service.source_confirmed
R2A call whose base (configuration key or host) and path are both known.source_confirmed
R3A declared consumer contract (pact-like file).contract_confirmed, or corroborated when it confirms an R1/R2/R5 edge
R4The service map. It creates no edge itself; it is the lookup R1 and R2 use.(input only)
R5The path matches exactly one endpoint in another service.inferred, rule INF-PATH-UNIQUE

merged_service_map (line 24) merges the config_keys, hosts and packages dictionaries of every repository's service map (later repositories overwrite earlier keys) and collects criticality and owner per service.

Per call site (resolve_consumers, lines 62-123). Call sites whose client package's first part is in MESSAGING_PACKAGES (kafka, confluent_kafka, aiokafka, faust) are skipped: they are events, not HTTP calls.

  1. R1, when the call site has a client_pkg. The package is looked up in packages. If the provider is analysed, the candidates are its endpoints whose handler's simple name equals the client method (client_attr). Exactly one gives an edge; otherwise the reason records how many matched, or that the package is not mapped.
  2. R2, otherwise, when the path is known and base_kind is set. A config_key base is looked up in config_keys; a host base in hosts, or accepted directly when it equals an analysed service name. Candidates are the provider's endpoints with the same normalised path and, if the call's method is known, the same method. Exactly one gives an edge. Otherwise the reason is "call matches N endpoints", "no endpoint M P found in X" or "provider X not in analysed scope".
  3. R5, when there is still no edge, the path is known and there is no client package. Candidates are endpoints of other services with the same path and method (GET assumed in the first lookup; when the method is unknown, any method). Exactly one gives an inferred edge, except when R2 had identified the provider and found no such endpoint there (line 111): in that case no guess is made.
  4. No edge: the call site goes to unresolved with its reason (default "unresolved outbound call").

R3, contracts (lines 126-152). For each interaction of each contract, the method (default GET) and the normalised path must match exactly one endpoint of the named provider; otherwise the interaction is recorded as unresolved. On a match:

  • If a call site of the same consumer repository already resolved to that endpoint, that edge is upgraded in place: its strategy becomes for example R2+R3, its status corroborated, and the contract file is added to its evidence.
  • Otherwise a synthetic node repo:file#index of type contract_ref is created with a CONSUMES_API edge of status contract_confirmed.

Statistics. outbound_call_sites is the number of non-messaging call sites plus synthetic contract references; outbound_call_sites_resolved is resolved call sites plus synthetic references; resolution_rate is their ratio rounded to 3 decimals (None when the total is 0); resolution_by_strategy counts R1, R2, R5 per code edge and R3 per synthetic reference.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
merged_service_map24One service map for the whole landscape.
resolve_consumers37Returns (edges, unresolved, stats, synthetic nodes).

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Every strategy requires exactly one candidate. Zero or several candidates never produce an edge; the call is listed as unresolved with the count.
  • A call with a client package never falls through to R5.
  • A call with base kind literal_url gets no provider from R2 and can only resolve through R5.
  • R5 is suppressed only when the R2 reason contains "no endpoint". When R2 found "provider X not in analysed scope", R5 may still infer an edge to a different analysed service that happens to be the only one with that path; the edge is marked inferred.
  • A contract interaction that fails to match is added to unresolved but not to the outbound_call_sites total, so the unresolved list can be longer than total minus resolved.
  • The unresolved list is sorted by repository, file and line.

graphstore.py 49 lines

Gives each graph a version id computed from its inputs and loads or saves graphs through the Store, so the same inputs never build the same graph twice.

How it works

  1. graph_inputs (line 19) collects everything that determines a graph: each repository's commit (sorted by repository name), EXTRACTOR_VERSION, CONFIG_VERSION, the path prefixes to strip, the test_naming flag and GRAPH_SCHEMA_VERSION.
  2. graph_id (line 24) serialises that dictionary as JSON with sorted keys, hashes it with SHA-256 and returns "g-" plus the first 16 hexadecimal characters.
  3. get_or_build (line 37) asks the store for that id unless rebuild is set; a hit is turned back into a Graph with Graph.from_dict and returned with built = False.
  4. Otherwise it calls build_graph, saves the result with its inputs and statistics (overwrite=rebuild) and returns built = True.

_stats (line 28) records node and edge counts, nodes by type, the number of CONSUMES_API edges as cross_repo_relationships, and the number of unresolved calls.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
graph_inputs19The identity of a graph version.
graph_id24Content-derived id of the form g- + 16 hex characters.
get_or_build37Returns (graph, graph id, built).

Edge cases and failure handling

  • With store set to None the graph is always built and nothing is saved.
  • A change to any input, including the schema version, yields a new id; earlier versions stay in the store.
  • The constant LANDSCAPE = "landscape" is defined at line 16 and not used inside this file.

data.py 377 lines

Adds the data layer to the graph (entities, tables, columns, migrations, stored procedures and SQL lineage) and detects data changes: the structural operations a proposed revision adds to a repository's migrations, each classified as breaking or not.

How it works

Table identity. A table belongs to a database. database_of (line 25) returns the lower-cased database value of the repository's service map, or private:<service> when none is declared. Node ids are table:<db>:<table>, col:<db>:<table>.<column> and proc:<db>:<name>. Two services therefore share a table node only when both declare the same database; equal table names alone do not link them.

Replaying migrations. order_migrations (line 52) returns migrations in the order they apply: Flyway then Django sorted by the numbers in the version (a version R, the repeatable kind, sorts last), then plain SQL and Liquibase by file name, then Alembic along its revision chain (each migration follows its parent; roots first, orphans appended at the end). apply_op (line 77) applies one operation to a state of the form {table: {column: spec}}; the operations are create_table, drop_table, rename_table, add_column, drop_column, rename_column and alter_column (which merges type and nullable). schema_state replays all migrations into a final state.

Building the data layer (add_data_layer, line 112):

  1. Ownership: a table is owned by the first repository whose migrations create it; otherwise by the first entity that maps it (including join tables). The owner supplies the repo, file and line of the table and column nodes.
  2. For each entity: an entity node (with table, database, orm), MAPS_TO to its table, MAPS_COLUMN to each column; the same for each many-to-many join table.
  3. For each data access record: READS or WRITES from the function to the entity, only when both nodes exist.
  4. If the repository has migrations: a table and column node for everything in the replayed schema state, then one migration node mig:<id> per migration with one MIGRATES edge per distinct table it touches (including the new name of a rename).

All edges here are source_confirmed; the strategy is orm or the migration tool name.

SQL lineage (add_sql_lineage, line 182). Each procedure, view or trigger becomes a procedure node. Its reads and writes become READS/WRITES edges to tables, and to the known columns it names (all known columns when it names *); a trigger's on_table counts as a read. SQL embedded in functions (sql_access) is linked the same way. A call to a procedure is resolved by the nested resolve (line 202) in this order: exact name in the caller's database (confirmed); the only procedure with that short name in the caller's database (confirmed); the only procedure with that short name in the whole landscape (inferred, rule INF-PROC-NAME); otherwise the call is recorded in g.sql_unresolved.

Detecting data changes (diff_data, line 353):

  1. Every operation of every base migration is turned into a key (its JSON without the line) and counted per migration id.
  2. Head migrations are walked in apply order. An operation that matches an unused base key is consumed; an operation with no match is new and is passed to classify_op together with the schema state built so far. Every operation, new or not, is then applied to the state.
  3. A migration is listed as changed when it is new, has a new operation, or has base operations that no longer appear.

Classification (classify_op, line 310). A "seed" is the graph node the impact walk starts from; access narrows it to writers when set to write.

OperationChange kindBreakingSeed
create_tabletable_addednonone
drop_tabletable_droppedyesthe table
rename_tabletable_renamedyesthe table
drop_columncolumn_droppedyesthe column
rename_columncolumn_renamedyesthe column
add_column, NOT NULL and no defaultcolumn_added_requiredyesthe table, writers only
add_column, otherwisecolumn_addednonone
alter_column, type widenedcolumn_type_widenednonone
alter_column, other type changecolumn_type_changedyesthe column
alter_column, made nullablecolumn_made_nullablenonone
alter_column, made NOT NULLcolumn_made_requiredyesthe column, writers only

type_widened (line 278) accepts only provable widenings: an integer family of equal or higher rank (smallint 1, int/integer 2, bigint 3 and their aliases); the same character type with an equal or longer length; any character type to text; text to text; and numeric/decimal where precision, scale and the number of integer digits all do not shrink. Anything else is a breaking type change.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
database_of, database_declared25, 30The database a repository's tables belong to, and whether it was declared.
table_id, col_id, proc_id34, 38, 178Node id builders.
order_migrations52Apply order across migration tools.
apply_op, schema_state77, 101Schema replay.
add_data_layer112Entity, table, column, migration nodes and their edges.
add_sql_lineage182Procedure nodes, SQL reads/writes, procedure calls.
data_summary259Counts for the report; empty when there are no entities and no migrations.
type_widened278Whether every value of the old column type fits the new one.
classify_op310Changes one operation makes, judged against the schema before it.
diff_data353Returns (changes, changed migrations) between base and head.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Column operations on a table the replay does not know are ignored by apply_op (line 87).
  • An alter_column whose previous type is unknown is reported as a breaking column_type_changed from unknown.
  • An add_column with no nullable key is treated as nullable, so it is not breaking.
  • Edited migrations are detected by counting operation keys, so reordering operations inside a migration is not reported as a change, while a removed operation marks the migration as changed without producing a classified change.
  • diff_data takes the database from the base extract only.
  • The constant ADDITIVE (line 22) lists the four non-breaking kinds; it is not used inside this file.
  • Table nodes created by add_sql_lineage for tables not seen before use the referencing file as their location rather than an owner.

graphstructure.py 170 lines

Stores facts the extractors already produced as graph nodes so that they can be walked: screens, elements and locators for UI automation; requests, responses, fields and examples for API testing; business rules found in code. None of these relationships carries impact, so adding them changes no analysis result.

How it works

add_structure (line 60) indexes all endpoints by (method, normalised path) and then, for each repository in sorted order, calls three builders. Every collection is walked in a fixed sorted order so that the same inputs always build the same graph.

_api (line 72), per endpoint:

  1. A request node <endpoint id>#request with body_schema, linked by ACCEPTS.
  2. One field node per parameter (location path, query or header; default query) and per field of the first request schema found (location body), each linked by HAS_FIELD. The field id is <owner>:<place>:<name>.
  3. A response node <endpoint id>#response with schema, success_status, shape and error_statuses, linked by RETURNS; one field node per response schema field.
  4. One example node per API example with the same method and normalised path, numbered from 1, with the body stored as JSON text in body_json, linked by HAS_EXAMPLE with strategy <source>-example.

A field node carries field_type, required, multi, nullable, in, optional allowed_values and the limits. _limits (line 17) keeps only keys listed in graphschema.LIMITS and turns any non-scalar value into JSON text, because a graph store keeps no nested maps. _statuses (line 40) collects numeric error statuses from the handler and the functions it calls by unique simple name; it is used only for endpoints whose source is code.

_ui (line 123), only for repositories with UI pages:

  1. An application node <repo>:app listing the frameworks, linked from the service by HAS_APPLICATION.
  2. One screen node <repo>:screen:<page> per page, linked by HAS_SCREEN.
  3. One element node <screen>#el<i> per element. Its name is the label, else name, text, placeholder, tag (cut to 120 characters). A select also stores options and options_source.
  4. One locator node <element>#loc<rank> per locator, rank 1 first, with css, xpath, playwright, unique and best (true when its strategy and XPath equal those of the element's recommended locator).
  5. NAVIGATES_TO for each navigation whose target route is a known screen of the same repository and is not the screen itself; the strategy is ui- plus how the navigation was found.
  6. CALLS_API to every endpoint matching a page API call by method and normalised path: source_confirmed when exactly one endpoint matches, otherwise inferred with rule INF-UI-CALL-AMBIGUOUS for each.

_rules (line 164): for each function, each filter sorted by line and expression becomes a rule node <function id>#rule<n> with expr, fields and function, linked by APPLIES_RULE.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_limits17Constraint values as flat scalar properties.
_options28A dropdown's real values as strings, skipping empty-value placeholders.
_statuses40Sorted error statuses of a handler and what it calls.
add_structure60Entry point called by build_graph.
_api72Request, response, field and example nodes.
_ui123Application, screen, element and locator nodes; navigation and API calls.
_rules164Rule nodes.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A page API call that matches no analysed endpoint produces no edge.
  • A navigation to a route that is not a known screen, or to the same screen, produces no edge.
  • Screens without a route are still created; they sort last and cannot be navigation targets.
  • A field's allowed_values is read from the named enum of its type, or from constraints["enum"] (line 85). automation.rules_of reads constraints["allowed"] instead, and that is the key the extractors found in this repository write (for example extract_python.py line 132), so allowed values declared inline rather than as a named enum do not reach the field node.
  • _options and _statuses repeat logic that automation.option_values and automation._handler_statuses also implement.

Extraction: core and Python

This layer turns the files of one repository at one commit into a RepoExtract: a flat record of facts (endpoints, schemas, functions, outbound HTTP call sites, tests, ORM entities and more) with a file and line for each. models.py defines the record types; extraction.py drives a commit through a per-file cache; extract_python.py parses Python with the standard-library ast module, dispatches every other file type to its extractor, and joins the per-file results in assemble. extract_django.py, extract_orm.py, extract_openapi.py, testcases.py, registry.py, adapters.py and exclude.py each supply one kind of fact or one decision to that pipeline. Nothing in this layer executes repository code and nothing here creates graph edges.

models.py 268 lines

Defines the data classes that every extractor fills, the evidence and analysis status constants, and normalize_path, the one function that makes a route declared by a server comparable to a URL called by a client.

How it works

The module has no logic apart from normalize_path and the RepoExtract serialisation pair. Everything else is a @dataclass with plain fields, so a record can be turned into JSON with dataclasses.asdict and rebuilt from it.

Constants. Lines 8-14 name the evidence statuses (how well a fact or link is supported): source_confirmed, contract_confirmed, runtime_observed, corroborated, inferred, unknown, stale. CONFIRMED (line 17) is the set of the first four. Lines 20-21 name the analysis statuses running, complete, complete_with_gaps, partial, failed. SEVERITY_ORDER (line 23) is ["unknown", "low", "medium", "high", "critical"], lowest first.

RepoExtract (line 179) is both the per-file partial result and the assembled repository result. Its fields fall into groups:
