normalize_path (line 254) applies these steps in order:

  1. Drop everything from the first ?.
  2. Replace every ${...}, {...} and <...> with {} (line 258). This covers FastAPI {id}, Flask and Django <int:id> and shell-style ${id}.
  3. Replace :name directly after a / with {} (Express-style parameters, line 259).
  4. Lower-case every segment that is not {}.
  5. Remove the first entry of strip_prefixes that the path starts with (a gateway prefix); only one is removed.
  6. Remove a trailing / unless the path is a single character; return / if nothing is left.
Inputstrip_prefixesOutput
/Orders/{order_id}/Items?x=1()/orders/{}/items
/users/<int:id>/()/users/{}
/api/v1/orders/:id("/api/v1",)/orders/{}
/a/${x}/B()/a/{}/b
empty string()/

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
Endpoint, Schema, CallSite, Function, TestCase27, 48, 60, 77, 93The five core fact types of an API-serving repository.
Entity, DataAccess, Migration108, 130, 141Data-layer facts: mapped tables, functions that read or write them, schema migrations.
TypeDecl, Edge155, 168Class declarations with bases; a graph edge with its evidence.
RepoExtract179Container for all facts of one file or one repository.
RepoExtract.to_dict221asdict(self): recursive conversion to plain dicts and lists.
RepoExtract.from_dict225Rebuilds the typed records (Endpoint(**e) and so on) and copies the dict-valued fields.
normalize_path254Path-template normalisation described above.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • from_dict reads the older fields with d[...] and the newer ones with d.get(..., default), so a cached record written before a field existed still loads. A record with an unknown extra key inside an endpoint or schema would fail in Endpoint(**e).
  • The strip-prefix test is a plain startswith on the already lower-cased path: a prefix must be given in lower case, and "/api" would also be cut from /apiary/x, leaving ary/x.
  • A path that does not start with / is not given one: orders/{id} becomes orders/{}.
  • Parameter names are discarded, so /orders/{id} and /orders/{order_id} are equal after normalisation; this is the intended basis for matching callers to routes.

__init__.py 11 lines

Declares the package version and the version strings that identify the extraction logic, which are part of every cache key.

How it works

  • __version__ is "1.0.0" (line 3).
  • EXTRACTOR_VERSIONS (line 9) is a dict: python is py-8, java is java-7, data is data-2, followed by one entry per registered language adapter from adapters.versions(). With only the built-in adapters these are go-1, cs-2, kt-1, ts-1.
  • EXTRACTOR_VERSION (line 10) joins the values with +, giving py-8+java-7+data-2+go-1+cs-2+kt-1+ts-1. This string is stored in every RepoExtract and is part of the per-file cache key in extraction.py, so bumping any one extractor's version, or installing or upgrading a third-party adapter, makes every cached file fact miss and be re-derived.
  • CONFIG_VERSION (line 11) is "cfg-2;sev-1;inf-2". It is not used by the modules in this layer.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
EXTRACTOR_VERSIONS9Per-language extractor version strings.
EXTRACTOR_VERSION10The single cache-identity string built from them.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Importing tia runs adapter discovery (importlib.metadata.entry_points) once, at import time. Discovery failures are swallowed inside adapters.py, so the import cannot fail because of a broken third-party adapter.
  • The version is one string for all languages: a change to the Java extractor also invalidates cached Python file facts.

extraction.py 68 lines

Extracts one repository at one commit, reusing cached per-file facts so that only files whose content changed are parsed again.

How it works

extract_commit (line 20) wraps _extract_commit in a tracing span named extraction.run and records timing through telemetry.record_extraction. The work is in _extract_commit (line 33):

  1. gitutil.list_tree(path, commit) returns a mapping of file path to git blob id (the hash of the file's content).
  2. The small configuration files are read in full (lines 36-39): root-level tia-services.yaml/.yml and catalog-info.yaml/.yml, any Spring configuration file (is_service_config), any shared configuration file (is_shared_config), and contracts/*.json. They are decoded as UTF-8 with replacement of invalid bytes.
  3. read_service_map turns them into the service map; the service name is smap["service"] or, failing that, the repository name (line 41).
  4. Extractable paths are split into excluded (match a tenant exclude pattern) and py (to analyse). Despite its name, py holds every extractable file type, not only Python.
  5. A cache key is built per file by _file_key (line 16): 
    f"{repo}|{service}|{','.join(strip)}|{','.join(exclude)}|{EXTRACTOR_VERSION}|{blob}|{path}"
    The key contains the blob id, so an unchanged file at a new commit has the same key.
  6. store.get_file_extracts returns the cached parts. With no store, or with force=True, nothing is looked up and every file is "missing".
  7. Only the blobs of missing files are read. Each is decoded as strict UTF-8 and passed to extract_source. A UnicodeDecodeError yields a part whose only content is a files_failed entry "not valid UTF-8".
  8. Fresh parts are written to the store (overwrite=force).
  9. assemble is called with the tree minus excluded paths, the service map, the per-file parts and the small files. Because a full rescan and an incremental run both end in the same assemble call over the same per-file parts, their results are equal by construction.
  10. ex.exclude_patterns is set, and stats is returned: files, reused, parsed, and, when patterns were given, excluded (count) and excluded_paths (first 50).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_file_key16Builds the cache key of one file's facts.
extract_commit20Public entry point; returns (RepoExtract, stats) and records tracing and telemetry.
_extract_commit33Lists the tree, resolves the service, reuses or derives per-file facts, assembles.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The service name is part of the key. Renaming the service in tia-services.yaml therefore re-extracts every file, which is necessary because ids such as orders:schema:Order embed it.
  • Only UnicodeDecodeError is caught around extract_source (line 54). extract_file catches SyntaxError and adapters.run contains adapter failures, but any other exception raised by an extractor propagates and aborts the whole extraction.
  • A failed part (invalid UTF-8, syntax error) is cached like a successful one, so the failure is reported again on later runs without re-reading the file.
  • Excluded files are removed from the path list given to assemble (line 63); if they were left in, assemble would look them up in per_file and fail.

adapters.py 130 lines

Keeps the registry of language adapters (objects that turn one source file into a partial RepoExtract) for languages other than Python and Java, including third-party adapters installed as Python packages.

How it works

An Adapter (line 22) is a frozen dataclass:

FieldMeaning
nameShort id such as go.
versionFor example go-1; becomes part of EXTRACTOR_VERSION.
extensionsTuple of lower-case file suffixes.
extract_fileCallable (repo, service, path, text, strip) -> RepoExtract.
acceptsOptional (path) -> bool filter.
frameworksDocumentation only.

Adapter.handles (line 30) lower-cases the path, tests it against extensions, then calls accepts if present.

The built-in adapters (BUILTIN, line 63):

NameVersionExtensionsImplementation
gogo-1.gotia.lang.golang
csharpcs-2.cstia.lang.csharp
kotlinkt-1.kt, .ktstia.lang.kotlin
typescriptts-1.ts, .tsx, .js, .jsx, .mjs, .cjstia.lang.typescript

Each built-in is wired through a small wrapper (_go, _cs, _kt, _ts, lines 43-60) that imports the language module only when first called. All four use _not_generated (line 38) as their accepts filter: it rejects a path that contains one of the directories in GENERATED (/node_modules/, /vendor/, /bin/, /obj/, /dist/, /build/, /.next/, /coverage/, /target/), contains .min. or .bundle., or ends with .d.ts, .designer.cs, .g.cs, _pb.go or .pb.go.

registry() (line 95) builds the list once and caches it in the module variable _registry:

  1. _discover (line 75) loads every entry point in the group tia.adapters and keeps the objects that are Adapter instances.
  2. Candidates are processed in name order. One is rejected, with a log warning, if its name equals a built-in name or one of python, java, data, or if any of its extensions is already taken. .py and .java (RESERVED, line 71) are always taken.
  3. The result is BUILTIN followed by the accepted third-party adapters.

adapter_for(path) returns the first adapter that handles the path, versions() returns the name-to-version map used by __init__.py, and run (line 122) calls the adapter and converts any exception into a files_failed entry.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
Adapter22Description of one language adapter.
Adapter.handles30Suffix test plus optional accepts filter.
_not_generated38Rejects generated, vendored, minified and bundled files.
_discover75Loads third-party adapters from entry points.
registry95Built-ins plus non-clashing third-party adapters; cached, refresh=True rebuilds.
adapter_for114The adapter for a path, or None.
versions118Adapter name to version.
run122Runs an adapter on one file with failure containment.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An adapter that raises produces an otherwise empty part with {"file": path, "error": "<name> adapter: <ExceptionType>: <first 160 characters>"}; the rest of the repository is still analysed.
  • If entry-point discovery itself raises, _discover returns an empty list. An entry point that fails to load or is not an Adapter is logged and skipped.
  • Because the path is lower-cased before the suffix test, a third-party adapter that declares an upper-case extension never matches.
  • A file rejected by _not_generated (for example anything under vendor/) has no adapter, so adapter_for returns None and the file is not extractable through this route.

extract_python.py 1174 lines

Parses Python source with the standard-library ast module (an abstract syntax tree: the parsed structure of the code, without comments or formatting) and emits endpoints, schemas, functions, outbound call sites, events and tests. The same module also holds the per-file dispatcher for all file types (extract_source), the service-map and Spring configuration readers, and assemble, which joins per-file facts into the repository extract.

How it works

1. Entry: extract_file (line 1032).

  1. Create an empty RepoExtract with commit="".
  2. ast.parse(text). A SyntaxError adds {"file": path, "error": "SyntaxError line N"} to files_failed and returns.
  3. migration_tool(tree) (line 1057) returns "alembic" when the module has from alembic import op, any from alembic.op ... import, or any plain import alembic...; "django" when it has from django.db import migrations or imports from django.db.migrations; otherwise None.
  4. A _FileVisitor is created, is_migration is set, and run() performs the pre-pass and the main walk.
  5. For a non-migration file, django_file_facts adds Django facts, the functions that stand for view-class configuration, and serializer schemas (a serializer is skipped if a schema of that name already exists in the file).
  6. For a migration file, alembic_migration or django_migration (from extract_data.py) produces a Migration.

2. The pre-pass: constants, imports, router prefixes, client variables (prepass, line 330). It fills four dictionaries that the later steps consult.

DictionarySource patternEntry produced
importsimport os.pathimports["os"] = "os.path" (key is the first dotted part unless as is used)
importsimport numpy as npimports["np"] = "numpy"
importsfrom orders_client.api import OrdersApiimports["OrdersApi"] = "orders_client.api.OrdersApi"
import_modulesevery import abovethe module name as written (os.path, orders_client.api); later copied into every test's imports
constsBASE = "https://pay.example.com/v2"consts["BASE"] = ("literal", "https://pay.example.com/v2")
constsBASE = os.getenv("ORDERS_URL") (also os.environ.get, environ.get, getenv)("config_key", "ORDERS_URL")
constsBASE = os.environ["ORDERS_URL"] (also environ[...])("config_key", "ORDERS_URL")
constsBASE = settings.ORDERS_URL (also config.X, cfg.X)("config_key", "ORDERS_URL")
router_prefixrouter = APIRouter(prefix="/orders")router_prefix["router"] = "/orders"
client_varsapi = OrdersApi(cfg) where OrdersApi was imported with from pkg... importclient_vars["api"] = ("orders_client", "OrdersApi")
  • Imports are collected from the whole tree (ast.walk), including imports inside functions. A relative import with no module (from . import x) is skipped; from .models import X is recorded as models.X, with the leading dots lost.
  • Constants and router prefixes are read only from top-level assignments with exactly one plain-name target (line 340). The classification is done by _const_ref (line 363). The default argument of os.getenv("K", "default") is ignored: the constant is the configuration key K.
  • A router prefix is recognised only when the call is the bare name APIRouter with a literal prefix= keyword.
  • Client variables are collected from any assignment anywhere in the file whose value is a call to an imported name. The key is the unparsed target text, so self.api = OrdersApi() is stored as "self.api". The import must contain a dot (so it came from from ... import) and its top-level package must not be in NON_CLIENT_PACKAGES (line 40: fastapi, flask, starlette, pydantic, django, requests, httpx, sqlalchemy, typing, dataclasses, collections, pathlib, os).

3. The main walk (visit_body, line 386). For each direct statement of a module or class body: a class goes to visit_class, a function to visit_function, and any other statement is searched for calls, each of which is offered to maybe_callsite with no owning function.

4. Classes (visit_class, line 398). A class produces up to four kinds of fact:

  • ORM entity. Outside test and migration files, model_entity (in extract_orm.py) is asked whether the class is a mapped model.
  • Type declaration. Outside test and migration files, every class yields a TypeDecl whose bases are the last dotted part of each base, without the names in skip_bases (line 405: object, Exception, Enum, BaseModel, ABC, Protocol, TypedDict, Generic and a few more).
  • Enum. If a base is named Enum, IntEnum or StrEnum, the literal values of the class-level assignments are stored in out.enums[ClassName]. class Status(str, Enum): PENDING = "PENDING"; PAID = "PAID" gives enums["Status"] = ["PENDING", "PAID"].
  • Schema. If a base is named BaseModel (last dotted part), every annotated class attribute becomes a field through py_field, and a Schema with id <service>:schema:<ClassName> is emitted.

The class body, without its annotated assignments, is then walked with the class name appended to the scope, so a method get of class OrderView gets the qualified name OrderView.get.

5. Schema fields and their constraints (py_field, line 149, and _ann_info, line 98). py_field(annotation, default_value) is used for both schema fields and handler parameters. The type is the annotation's source text. _ann_info walks the annotation:

Annotation formEffect
X | None, Optional[X], Union[X, None]nullable is true; X is walked further
List[X], list[X], Set, set, Sequence, Tuple, tuple, FrozenSet, conlist[...]multi is true; the first type argument is walked
Literal["a", "b"]constraints["allowed"] = ["a", "b"] (only if every value is a literal)
Annotated[X, Field(...)]X is walked; constraint keywords of any Field/Query/Path/Body/Header/Cookie/Form call in the metadata are read
constr(...), conint(...), confloat(...), condecimal(...), conlist(...)constraint keywords are read; conlist also sets multi
EmailStrconstraints["format"] = "email"

Constraint keywords are renamed by CONSTRAINT_KW (line 70) and kept only when the value is a literal (string, number, boolean, or a negated number):

Keyword in sourceConstraint name
min_length, max_lengthmin_length, max_length
ge, leminimum, maximum
gt, ltexclusive_minimum, exclusive_maximum
pattern, regexpattern
min_items, max_items, multiple_ofsame names

The default value decides required:

SourceResult
name: str{"type": "str", "required": True}
name: str = Field(..., min_length=1, max_length=50){"type": "str", "required": True, "constraints": {"min_length": 1, "max_length": 50}}
qty: int = Field(1, ge=1){"type": "int", "required": False, "constraints": {"minimum": 1}, "default": 1}
note: Optional[str] = None{"type": "Optional[str]", "required": False, "nullable": True}
tags: List[str] = Field(default_factory=list){"type": "List[str]", "required": False, "multi": True}
status: Literal["NEW", "PAID"] = "NEW"{"type": "Literal['NEW', 'PAID']", "required": False, "constraints": {"allowed": ["NEW", "PAID"]}, "default": "NEW"}

For a Field(...)-style call, the first positional argument or the default= keyword is the default. The field is required when there is neither a default nor a default_factory, or when the default is the literal ... (line 157). A default of None also sets nullable.

6. Functions (visit_function, line 427). The id is <repo>:<path>::<qualname>. For every function the extractor first computes:

  • calls: for every call node anywhere in the function (including its decorators, default values and nested functions), the called name if it is a plain name (validate(x) gives validate) or the attribute name if it is an attribute (repo.save(x) gives save). The list is de-duplicated and sorted. No receiver type is recorded.
  • hash: the first 16 hex characters of the SHA-1 of ast.dump(node) (line 438). ast.dump does not include line numbers, comments or formatting, so moving or reformatting a function leaves the hash unchanged, while any change to its name, decorators, parameters, docstring or statements changes it.

Then one of two paths is taken. If the file is a test file (_is_test_file, line 53: basename starts with test_, ends with _test.py, or the path has a tests/ directory) and the function name starts with test, only add_test runs and no Function is recorded. Otherwise:

  1. A Function is appended with hash, calls, signature, body_hash, filters, statuses and end_line.
  2. maybe_endpoint checks the decorators.
  3. Outside migration files, function_refs (in extract_orm.py) records model references into out.data_refs.
  4. _local_consts computes the function's own resolvable names and stores them in self.local.
  5. Every call in the function body (not the decorators) is offered to maybe_callsite and maybe_event.

_signature (line 239) lists positional parameters (kind: "positional", default true for the trailing ones that have defaults), *args (var_positional), keyword-only parameters (keyword) and **kwargs (var_keyword), plus the return annotation as text. def f(a, b=1, *, c, **kw) -> int gives:

{"params": [{"name": "a", "kind": "positional", "default": False},
            {"name": "b", "kind": "positional", "default": True},
            {"name": "c", "kind": "keyword", "default": False},
            {"name": "kw", "kind": "var_keyword", "default": True}],
 "returns": "int"}

_body_hash (line 253) drops the first statement if it is a string expression (the docstring), then hashes the function name, the dumped arguments, the dumped return annotation, the dumped decorators and the dumped remaining statements, joined with |. Editing only the docstring changes hash but not body_hash.

7. Endpoints (maybe_endpoint, line 481). A function is an endpoint when one of its decorators is a call on an attribute, in one of two forms:

DecoratorFramework styleMethods
@app.get("/x"), @router.post("/x"); attribute is one of get, post, put, delete, patchFastAPI, Starlette, newer Flaskthe attribute, upper-cased
@app.route("/x"), @bp.route("/x", methods=["GET", "POST"])FlaskGET unless a literal list or tuple methods= is given; one endpoint per method

The object before the dot is not checked, so any <something>.get(...) decorator qualifies. The remaining values are derived as follows:

ValueHow it is derived
PathThe first positional argument, which must be a string literal; otherwise the decorator is skipped. If the decorator's owner is a plain name found in router_prefix, the prefix is put in front.
norm_path, idnormalize_path(path, strip_prefixes); id is <service>:<METHOD> <norm_path>.
response_schemaSource text of the response_model= keyword, for example List[OrderOut]. Assembly later reduces it to the schema name.
response_shape"list" when that text starts with List[, list[, Sequence[, Set[, set[, Tuple[ or tuple[ (optionally prefixed typing.); otherwise "object"; None without response_model.
success_statusThe status_code= keyword through _status_of (line 178): an integer literal from 100 to 599, or an attribute whose text contains HTTP_<3 digits>_, such as status.HTTP_201_CREATED.
authSource text of each authentication dependency found in the decorator's dependencies=[...] list or in the function's parameter defaults. _is_auth_dependency (line 232) accepts every Security(...) call, and a Depends(x) call only when the text of x matches AUTH_NAME (line 229: auth, user, token, admin, role, permission, scope, login, principal, current, security, api_key, apikey, jwt, oauth, case-insensitive). Depends(get_db) is not an auth marker; Depends(get_current_user) is.
request_schemasThe annotation text of every annotated positional parameter. Assembly keeps only those that equal a schema name.
paramsFrom _params, described next.

Parameters (_params, line 529). Every positional and keyword-only parameter is considered, except: self and cls; parameters without an annotation; parameters whose annotation's last dotted part is in SKIP_PARAM_TYPES (Request, Response, BackgroundTasks, Session, AsyncSession, WebSocket, HTTPConnection); and parameters whose default is a Depends, Security, Body or Form call. The location is header for a Header(...) default, cookie for Cookie(...), path when the path contains {name} or {name:converter}, and query otherwise. The rest of the record comes from py_field; a path parameter is always required.

Example:

router = APIRouter(prefix="/orders")

@router.get("/{order_id}", response_model=OrderOut)
def get_order(order_id: int, expand: bool = Query(False),
              user=Depends(get_current_user)): ...

This yields an Endpoint with method="GET", path="/orders/{order_id}", norm_path="/orders/{}", id="<service>:GET /orders/{}", handler="get_order", response_schema="OrderOut", response_shape="object", auth=["Depends(get_current_user)"], request_schemas=["int", "bool"] (emptied at assembly) and params holding order_id (in path, required) and expand (in query, not required, default False). user has no annotation and is skipped as a parameter.

8. Outbound call sites (maybe_callsite, line 572). A call is examined only if it is a method call (x.y(...)). The text before the last dot is the owner.

  1. Imported client object. If the owner text is a key of client_vars, a CallSite is emitted with client_pkg (top-level package) and client_attr (the method name), and with method, norm_path, base_kind and base_value all None. api = OrdersApi(); api.get_order(7) gives client_pkg="orders_client", client_attr="get_order". Which endpoint that reaches is decided later, not here.
  2. HTTP verb call. Otherwise the method name must be one of get, post, put, delete, patch or request (line 586).
  3. Client-like owner. The first name of the owner must be in CLIENT_ROOTS (line 37: requests, httpx, session, client, http, api), or the owner text must end with client, session or http (for example self.http_client, self._session). This is a name test, not a type test: there is no check that requests or httpx is imported. Other libraries (aiohttp, urllib) are recognised only if the variable happens to be named this way.
  4. Method. The upper-cased verb. For .request(m, url) the first argument must be a string literal (else the method is None) and the URL is the second argument.
  5. URL. The first remaining positional argument goes to parse_url. A call with no positional argument (for example requests.get(url=...)) is not recorded.

9. Splitting the URL expression (parse_url, line 613, and _from_parts, line 647). The result is (path, base_kind, base_value, reason). A bare name is first resolved through _name_const (line 473), which looks in the current function's local names and then in the module consts; if it is a literal the name is replaced by that string.

  • String literal starting with http:// or https://: split with urlsplit; base is ("host", hostname), path is the URL path or /.
  • Any other string literal: the string itself is the path, with no base and the reason base URL not present in call.
  • f-string or + concatenation: flattened into a list of parts, each either a literal string or an expression, then handled by _from_parts:
    1. If the first part is an expression it must resolve as a base through _base_of (line 605): a resolvable name, settings.X/config.X/cfg.X, or an inline os.getenv("K")/os.environ["K"]. A literal that starts with http becomes ("host", hostname) and contributes its path; anything else resolvable is used as returned (("config_key", K) or ("literal", value)). If it does not resolve, the result is (None, None, None, "base expression not resolvable: ...").
    2. If the first part is a literal starting with http, the host is taken from it and the remainder of the literal starts the path.
    3. Every later literal is appended to the path and every later expression becomes {}.
    4. If the resulting path does not start with /, the path is None with the reason path does not start with a literal segment, but the base is kept.
  • Anything else (a variable that is not a known constant, a function call, urljoin(...), "...".format(...)): (None, None, None, "URL expression not statically resolvable: <first 60 characters>").

The following results were produced by running extract_file on a file with BASE = os.getenv("ORDERS_URL"), PREFIX = "/api" and HOST = "https://pay.example.com/v2" at module level:

Callmethodnorm_pathbase_kind / base_valuereason
requests.get(f"{BASE}/orders/{oid}")GET/orders/{}config_key / ORDERS_URLnone
requests.post(HOST + "/charges")POST/v2/chargeshost / pay.example.comnone
requests.get("https://x.example.com/a/b?q=1")GET/a/bhost / x.example.comnone
requests.get(f"{PREFIX}/orders")GET/ordersliteral / /apinone
requests.request("put", url)PUTNoneNoneURL expression not statically resolvable: url
requests.get(f"{self.base}/x")GETNoneNonebase expression not resolvable: self.base
requests.get(f"{BASE}{path}")GETNoneconfig_key / ORDERS_URLpath does not start with a literal segment
session.get("user_id")GETuser_idNonebase URL not present in call

Function-local names (_local_consts, line 458). Before a function's calls are examined, every assignment in it with a single plain-name target is classified with _const_ref (or copied from a module constant when the value is that constant's name). A name is usable only if all its assignments in the function resolve to the same single value; a name assigned two different values, or any unresolvable value, is left out. A local name takes precedence over a module constant of the same name.

10. Events (maybe_event, line 555). An event is a message published to or read from a named topic. Three call shapes are recognised, only for calls inside function bodies:

Call shapeConditionRecorded
producer.send("orders.created", ...); method is send, produce or send_and_waitowner text matches produc, kafka or publisher (case-insensitive); topic is the first positional argumentkind: "produces"
KafkaConsumer("orders.created", "orders.paid"), AIOKafkaConsumer(...)every positional argument is a candidate topickind: "consumes"
consumer.subscribe(["orders.created"]) or subscribe(topics=[...])owner text matches consum or kafka; the argument may be a list, tuple, set or single valuekind: "consumes"

A topic must be a string literal or a module-level constant holding a literal (_topic, line 550), and must match the pattern TOPIC imported from events.py (letters, digits, ., _, -; 1 to 249 characters). The record is {"kind", "topic", "function_id", "file", "line", "via"}, where via is owner.method, for example producer.send.

11. Item filters, the business rules (item_filters, line 280). An item filter is a condition that decides which items of a collection a function keeps. Only conditions that read a field of the loop item count; the fields are found by _item_fields (line 263), which recognises item["x"], item.get("x") and item.x.

Source patternRecorded expr and fields
[o for o in orders if o["status"] == "PAID"] (list, set, dict comprehensions and generator expressions)o['status'] == 'PAID', ["status"]
filter(lambda o: o.total > 100, orders) (lambda with exactly one parameter)o.total > 100, ["total"]
for o in orders: followed by if o.get("deleted"): continuenot (o.get('deleted')), ["deleted"]: the rule is stated as what is kept
for o in orders: followed by if o.total > 100: out.append(o) (append or add with the item as an argument)o.total > 100, ["total"]
for o in orders: followed by if o.active: yield oo.active, ["active"]

For for loops only if statements that are direct children of the loop body are examined, and the loop target must be a single name. Each record is {"line", "expr", "fields"}; the list is sorted by line and expression.

12. Raised statuses (raised_statuses, line 186). Every raise X(...) and return X(...) in the function is examined when the called name ends with HTTPException or Response (which includes JSONResponse). The status is the status_code= keyword, or for HTTPException the first positional argument, read by _status_of. Only statuses of 400 and above are kept. A literal detail= keyword is stored, cut to 120 characters. raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Order not found") on line 20 gives {"status": 404, "line": 20, "detail": "Order not found"}; return JSONResponse(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, content=...) gives status 409.

13. Tests (add_test, line 676). For a test function the extractor records a TestCase whose id has the same format as a function id. Its contents:

  • http_calls: every call <owner>.<verb>(...) where the verb is in HTTP_VERBS and the owner text is client, test_client or tc, or ends with client. The first argument must be a string literal, or an f-string resolved through parse_url, and must start with /. client.get(f"/orders/{oid}") gives ["GET", "/orders/{}"].
  • bodies: when such a call has a json= or data= keyword whose value is a dict literal, one entry [method, norm_path, sorted string keys, {key: value}] is added; the value map holds only keys whose value is a literal. client.post("/orders", json={"sku": "A1", "qty": 2, "meta": extra}) gives ["POST", "/orders", ["meta", "qty", "sku"], {"sku": "A1", "qty": 2}].
  • statuses (asserted_statuses, line 202): for every comparison whose left side's text ends with status_code, each comparator (or each element of a tuple, list or set comparator) that is a status. assert r.status_code == 404 gives [404]; assert r.status_code in (400, 422) gives [400, 422].
  • literals (literal_values, line 215): string and number literals of at most 60 characters and boolean literals, as strings, without duplicates, stopping at 60 values. Dict keys are included because they are ordinary string literals.
  • imports: all modules the file imports. called: the same simple-name list as a function's calls.

Expected responses (asserted_responses, line 701). The test is scanned for response variables: r = client.post("/orders", ...) with a literal path starting with /. Aliases of the form body = r.json() are tracked. Then every assert left == right is examined:

  • assert r.status_code == 201 sets the example's status.
  • assert r.json() == {...} or assert body == {...}, with a right side that ast.literal_eval can evaluate to a dict, sets the full body.
  • assert r.json()["status"] == "PENDING" or assert body["status"] == "PENDING" adds one field to a partial body.

One example per response variable is appended to out.api_examples: {"method", "norm_path", "status", "body", "partial", "source": "test", "test", "file", "line"}. partial is true when only individual fields were asserted. Examples whose JSON form exceeds 4000 characters are dropped.

14. Dispatch for all file types: is_extractable (line 934) and extract_source (line 942). extract_source picks the first matching branch:

OrderConditionExtractor
1ends with .javaextract_java.extract_file
2ends with .pyextract_file (this module)
3is_connector_path or ends with .scalaconnector_facts for connector configurations; a Scala file gets an empty part here and is read only by stream_facts below
4is_openapi_fileextract_openapi
5is_event_schema_fileparse_event_schema; the result is stored with id <service>:eschema:<name>
6is_testcase_fileparse_testcases; a ValueError becomes a files_failed entry
7adapter_for(path) is not Nonethe language adapter through adapters.run; if the file is also a front-end file, extract_ui_file adds ui_facts
8is_ui_fileextract_ui_file; a script with no page in it is counted in non_python_files
9is_sql_source and not is_data_filean empty part, filled by _sql_lineage below
10otherwiseextract_data.extract_file (migrations and other data files)

Three steps then run for every file:

  1. _sql_lineage (line 1001). For a SQL source file it adds the definitions returned by parse_definitions to procedures. For a source file with functions and no migration it calls embedded_sql to find SQL text inside functions and adds the results to sql_access. Where a function's SQL was read, the generic raw_sql unknown recorded for a line inside that function is removed (the owner of a line is the smallest function span containing it). If no dynamic statement remained in the file, data_access_library unknowns for the SQL-text libraries JDBC, Spring JDBC, JPA EntityManager and Spring Data JDBC are removed as well.
  2. For .py, .java, .kt, .kts and .scala files, stream_facts adds stream-processing events, SQL access, job functions and unknowns.
  3. scrub_extract(part) removes secrets and stores the count in secrets_withheld.

15. How secrets are withheld. The scrubbing itself is in scrub.py; this module's part is the single call on line 997, placed after all extraction so that nothing unscrubbed reaches the cache. scrub_extract works in place on the fields that quote source text: call-site reason, norm_path and base_value; data_unknowns reasons; unparsed migration statements; files_failed errors; function filters and statuses; test literals and bodies; schema fields; endpoint params; enums; ui_facts; manual_tests; api_examples. Recognised token formats (GitHub, AWS, Slack, Stripe, private keys, JWTs, a password in a URL) are replaced with [REDACTED:<kind>] everywhere; generic password=...-style assignments are replaced only in free-text fields. In call-site and unknown reasons the contents of string literals longer than three characters are also masked. Only the count is kept. Ids, paths and names are not rewritten by the generic tier, so scrubbing cannot break matching.

16. Service identity: read_service_map (line 896). The input is a dict of path to text for the small configuration files. The steps:

  1. Parse tia-services.yaml, then tia-services.yml, with yaml.safe_load. If both exist the second replaces the first. A YAML error or a non-mapping document gives an empty map.
  2. catalog_service_map (in registry.py) reads a Backstage catalog-info.yaml. Its service, owner and criticality fill only keys the map does not already have.
  3. If there is still no service, spring_application_name supplies it.
  4. spring_properties flattens the Spring configuration. A declared database: is lower-cased and marked database_source: "declared". Otherwise, if spring.datasource.url is present and jdbc_database_id can read it, that identity is used with database_source: "datasource_url".
  5. If any property whose key starts with spring.jpa.hibernate.naming has a value that mentions none of SpringPhysicalNamingStrategy, CamelCaseToUnderscoresNamingStrategy, SpringImplicitNamingStrategy, ImplicitNamingStrategyJpaCompliantImpl, the map gets jpa_custom_naming: True (table names derived by the default strategy would then be wrong).

17. Spring and configuration helpers.

  • is_service_config (line 771): the basename is one of application.yml, application.yaml, bootstrap.yml, bootstrap.yaml, application.properties, bootstrap.properties, and the path has no /src/test/ directory.
  • spring_application_name (line 776): tries the service configuration files shortest path first. In YAML (all documents of the file) it reads the nested key spring.application.name or a flat key with that exact name; in a properties file it uses the regular expression _SPRING_NAME. A value containing ${ (a placeholder) is rejected and the next file is tried.
  • spring_properties (line 859): flattens all service configuration files into one dict of dotted keys (_flatten, line 800). Because files are visited shortest path first and setdefault is used, the first value seen for a key wins. Properties files are parsed line by line with key = value or key: value; lines starting with # or ! are comments.
  • context_path (line 825): the first of server.servlet.context-path, server.context-path, server.servlet.contextPath whose value starts with /, uses only URL-safe characters and is not just /; a trailing / is removed (_context_of, line 817).
  • is_shared_config (line 812) and external_context_paths (line 830): a file shared/<service>.yml, .yaml or .properties (or under config-repo/) is a Spring Cloud Config file for another service. Its context path is recorded under the file's base name, for example shared/billing.yml with server.servlet.context-path: /billing-api gives {"billing": "/billing-api"}.
  • jdbc_database_id (line 882): returns driver://host[:port]/name in lower case, or None for a URL with a ${ placeholder, an embedded database (h2, hsqldb, derby, sqlite), or a URL the pattern cannot read. The name is taken from databaseName= if present, else from the text after the host up to the first ?, ; or &.
JDBC URLResult
jdbc:postgresql://db.internal:5432/orders?ssl=truepostgresql://db.internal:5432/orders
jdbc:sqlserver://host:1433;databaseName=Orderssqlserver://host:1433/orders
jdbc:oracle:thin:@host:1521:ORCLoracle://host:1521/orcl
jdbc:h2:mem:testNone
jdbc:mysql://${DB_HOST}/xNone

18. Joining per-file facts: assemble (line 1071).

  1. Create the output with the commit, service map and strip prefixes.
  2. For every path in sorted order: an extractable path has its part's lists concatenated into the output (lines 1084-1111; enums are merged with dict.update, counters are added). A contracts/*.json file is parsed and appended to contracts with a _file key; invalid JSON becomes a files_failed entry invalid JSON contract. Any other path that is not a known text or binary extension (TEXT_EXT, BINARY_EXT, lines 760-763) is counted in non_python_files.
  3. resolve_django turns the Django facts of all files into endpoints, because a URL module points at views in other files.
  4. Code wins over specification: an endpoint with source="openapi" is dropped when a code endpoint has the same id; a schema from a .yaml, .yml or .json file is dropped when a code schema has the same name (lines 1123-1126).
  5. resolve_data (ORM mappings, repositories, migrations), resolve_clients (Java declared clients) and keep_reachable_schemas run; they live in other modules.
  6. Each endpoint is cleaned against the set of schema names (lines 1131-1142): request_schemas keeps only exact schema names; a parameter whose innermost type name is a schema is removed from params because it is the body; response_schema is reduced to the schema name when it equals one or contains it in brackets (List[OrderOut] becomes OrderOut), and is set to None otherwise.
  7. If the service has a Spring context path, every endpoint from a .java file gets it prepended, with norm_path and id recomputed, and the HTTP calls of Java tests are put under the same prefix.
  8. external_context_paths, assemble_pages (front-end pages) and assign_topics (event schemas to topics) fill their fields.
  9. Endpoints, schemas, call sites, functions, tests and types are sorted by id, so the output order does not depend on processing order.

extract_repo (line 1167) is the full-rescan entry that works from an in-memory dict of path to text: it reads the service map, runs extract_source on every extractable file, and calls assemble.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Unparseable files. Only SyntaxError is caught (line 1037). The file is listed in files_failed and contributes nothing else.
  • Functions that are not extracted. visit_body looks only at direct children. A function or class defined inside if, try or with at module level is not recorded as a function or class; only the calls in it are checked for call sites. Nested functions and classes defined inside a function are not recorded separately; their calls, filters and statuses are attributed to the enclosing function.
  • Test functions. In a test file a function named test... gets no Function record, no endpoint check, no call sites and no events. Helper functions and fixtures in a test file are treated as ordinary functions.
  • Schemas. Only a class that lists BaseModel directly is a schema. A class that inherits from another project model (class OrderOut(OrderBase)) is not recognised here, and extends is never set for Python. Dataclasses and TypedDict are not schemas. Schema ids contain only the service and class name, so two classes with the same name in one service collide.
  • Endpoint decorators. The object before the dot is not checked and the path is not required to start with /. A decorator such as @mock.patch("app.svc.fn") on a non-test function is therefore recorded as the endpoint PATCH app.svc.fn. A path given as a keyword or as a non-literal is silently skipped. In methods=[...] a non-literal element produces an endpoint with an empty method.
  • Prefixes that are not seen. Only APIRouter(prefix=...) is read. A prefix given in app.include_router(router, prefix="/api") or a Flask Blueprint(url_prefix=...) is not applied.
  • Flask path parameters. _params looks for {name} in the path, so a Flask parameter declared as <int:id> is classified as query, not path.
  • Response shape. The list test is anchored at the start of the text: Optional[List[X]] is reported as object.
  • Call-site false positives. Recognition is by variable name. session.get("user_id") on a dict-like session, or cache_client.get(key), is recorded as an outbound HTTP call; for a plain literal the path is kept even when it does not start with /.
  • Relative literal base. When the first part of an f-string is a constant holding a non-URL string (PREFIX = "/api"), the result has base_kind="literal", base_value="/api", and the path does not include that prefix. The value literal is not among those listed in the CallSite.base_kind comment.
  • Imported-client call sites are broad. Any variable assigned from a call to a class imported with from pkg import Cls, outside the excluded packages, is a client variable, so repo = OrderRepository(); repo.save(x) also produces a CallSite with client_pkg and client_attr. Whether it links to anything is decided at a later stage.
  • Shadowing. _local_consts does not consider parameters: a function parameter with the same name as a module constant still resolves to the module constant.
  • Events. Only calls inside function bodies are checked, so a module-level consumer = KafkaConsumer("orders") is not recorded by this function. Topics held in function-local variables are not resolved.
  • Statuses. raised_statuses does not recognise Flask's abort(404) or a return body, 404 tuple. asserted_statuses ignores the comparison operator, so assert r.status_code != 500 records 500, and it does not match a reversed comparison (404 == r.status_code).
  • Literals. A negative number contributes two entries (-5 and 5) because the inner constant is visited as well.
  • Migration files. A file identified as an Alembic or Django migration gets no entities, type declarations, data references or Django facts. Any plain import alembic is enough to be classified as Alembic.
  • Configuration parsing. A YAML error in a service configuration file skips that file. _props_of (used for shared files) accepts only key=value lines in properties files, while spring_properties also accepts key: value.
  • Context path. In assemble it is applied only to endpoints and tests from .java files.

extract_django.py 502 lines

Extracts Django and Django REST framework (DRF) routes. Django declares routes in URL modules that point at views in other files, so the work is split into a per-file step that records what each file declares and an assembly step that walks the URL tree and emits endpoints.

How it works

Step 1, per file: file_facts (line 200). It returns three lists: facts (plain dicts), functions that stand for view-class configuration, and serializer schemas. It returns three empty lists for a test file or a file that imports nothing from django or rest_framework (_uses_django, line 61).

Names are resolved to dotted module paths by _Names (line 70), built from the file's imports. Relative imports are resolved against the file's package. With from . import views in shop/urls.py, views.order_detail resolves to shop.views.order_detail. A name that was not imported is assumed to be defined in the file itself (<this module>.<name>). An expression that is not a plain dotted name resolves to None.

The facts recorded per file:

Fact kindSource patternContents
serializera top-level class with a base in SERIALIZER_BASES or ending in Serializername, project base serializer, file, line; a Schema is produced alongside
settingsROOT_URLCONF = "shop.urls"root_urlconf
urlconfa module with urlpatterns, a router, or another top-level list of URL patternsmodule, patterns, lists (other named pattern lists), routers
view_fna top-level function decorated with @api_view, or whose first parameter is named requestref, function_id, methods, inferred, auth
view_classa top-level class that looks like a view (see below)ref, bases, methods, extra_actions, auth, serializer, lookup, class_function_id

URL patterns (_pattern, line 120). Each call to path, re_path or url with at least two positional arguments inside urlpatterns = [...], urlpatterns += [...] or another top-level list becomes a pattern dict with route, regex (true unless the function is path) and line. The second argument decides the rest:

Second argumentPattern key
include("shop.urls") or include(("shop.urls", "shop"))include: "shop.urls"
include(router.urls)include_router: "router"
include(extra_patterns) where extra_patterns is a top-level list in the fileinclude_list: "extra_patterns"
any other include(...)unresolved: "include(...) cannot be followed"
OrderView.as_view()view: the resolved class reference
OrderViewSet.as_view({"get": "list", "post": "create"})view plus actions: {"GET": "list", "POST": "create"}
views.order_detailview: the resolved function reference

A non-literal route gives unresolved: "the route of path() is not a string literal". urlpatterns = router.urls and urlpatterns += router.urls become a pattern with an empty route and include_router.

Routers. An assignment from DefaultRouter(), SimpleRouter(), ExtendedSimpleRouter() or NestedSimpleRouter() declares a router; a literal trailing_slash= keyword is read, default true. Every router.register("orders", OrderViewSet) call anywhere in the file is recorded with its prefix, the resolved view reference, its line and the expression text.

View functions.

  • With @api_view(["GET", "POST"]): methods are the listed strings, or GET when the decorator has no argument. @permission_classes([IsAuthenticated]) supplies auth; AllowAny is left out. inferred is false.
  • Without it, when the first parameter is named request: @require_http_methods([...]), @require_GET, @require_POST or @require_safe restricts the methods. Otherwise the methods are GET plus every method the body compares request.method with (_methods_checked, line 101), and inferred is true. HEAD is dropped. auth lists the decorators login_required, permission_required, staff_member_required if present.

View classes. A class counts as a view when it defines a method named after an HTTP verb (get, post, put, patch, delete) or a viewset action (list, create, retrieve, update, partial_update, destroy), or when a base is a known framework class or ends with View, ViewSet, APIView or Mixin (lines 269-273). For such a class the fact records:

  • methods: every method name with its function id <repo>:<path>::<Class>.<method> and line.
  • extra_actions: methods decorated with @action(detail=..., methods=[...], url_path=...); methods default to GET, and url_path defaults to the method name.
  • auth: the names in permission_classes = [...] without AllowAny.
  • serializer: the name in serializer_class = ....
  • lookup: lookup_url_kwarg if set, else lookup_field, else pk.

Each view class also yields a Function with id <repo>:<path>::<Class> (line 299). Its hash and body_hash are the same value: a SHA-1 over the base names and the dumped non-method statements of the class. This function stands for inherited actions such as ModelViewSet.list, which have no function in the repository: changing queryset, serializer_class or permission_classes changes this hash.

Serializers as schemas (_serializer, line 163). Each class-level assignment whose value is a call to a known field type (FIELD_TYPES, line 154) or to a name ending in Serializer (a nested serializer) becomes a field:

SourceField
name = serializers.CharField(max_length=80){"type": "str", "required": True, "constraints": {"max_length": 80}}
total = serializers.DecimalField(read_only=True, ...){"type": "Decimal", "required": False, "read_only": True}
items = ItemSerializer(many=True){"type": "ItemSerializer", "required": True, "multi": True}
note = serializers.CharField(required=False, allow_null=True){"type": "str", "required": False, "nullable": True}
class Meta: fields = ["id", "status"]each listed name not declared explicitly: {"type": "model", "required": False}

A field is required when required=True is written, or, with no required keyword, when it is neither read_only=True nor given a default. Recognised constraints are max_length, min_length, max_value, min_value. Schema.extends is the first base that ends in Serializer and is not a framework base.

Step 2, at assembly: resolve (line 322).

  1. Serializer inheritance. A serializer with a project base gets the base's fields merged in (its own fields win, except that a Meta.fields placeholder of type model does not override a field the base declares).
  2. Indexes. URL modules by module name, views by dotted reference, view classes by simple name. find_module and find_view accept an exact match, or the single entry whose dotted path ends with the target. This covers an import that omits a source root such as src..
  3. Roots. The URL modules named by ROOT_URLCONF. If none resolves, every URL module that no other module includes is a root.
  4. Walk (walk, line 458). For each pattern, the route is converted by _route and joined to the prefix by _join (which inserts a / only when the prefix is non-empty and does not already end with one). Then: include recurses into the target module; include_router calls expand_router; include_list recurses into the named list; otherwise the view is looked up and emit_view runs.
  5. Output. Endpoints whose id is not already present in out.endpoints are appended, and the collected unknowns are added to out.route_unknowns.

Regular-expression routes (_route, line 308). A path() route is used as written, converters included (orders/<int:pk>/; normalize_path later turns <int:pk> into {}). For re_path and url, the leading ^ and trailing $ are removed, each named group (?P<name>...) becomes {name}, and escaped ., - and / are unescaped. If any of \ ( ) [ ] * + ? | remains the route is not a plain path and becomes an unknown. ^orders/(?P<pk>\d+)/$ becomes orders/{pk}/.

Methods of a routed view (emit_view, line 412).

ViewEndpoints emitted
view functionone per entry of its methods
class with an as_view({...}) action mapone per mapped method; the handler is the named action if the class (or a project base) defines it, else the class-configuration function; shape is list for the list action, else object; status 201 for create
other classthe union of the methods implied by its generic bases (GENERIC_METHODS, line 36: for example ListCreateAPIView gives GET and POST) and the HTTP-verb methods it defines; status 201 for POST when a base name contains CreateAPIView

Framework bases are found through the repository's own classes by lineage (line 361), so class Base(ModelViewSet), class OrderViewSet(Base) still resolves to ModelViewSet. inherited (line 373) merges methods and extra actions from project base classes (the subclass wins) and takes auth, serializer and lookup from the nearest class that sets them.

Router expansion (expand_router, line 434). For each registration the actions are those implied by the viewset's framework bases (VIEWSET_ACTIONS, line 32) plus the standard actions the class defines itself. Each maps to a method and a collection or detail route through ACTION_ROUTE (line 30). router.register("orders", OrderViewSet) with class OrderViewSet(ModelViewSet), included under api/, yields:

ActionMethod and path
list, createGET /api/orders/, POST /api/orders/ (status 201)
retrieve, update, partial_update, destroyGET, PUT, PATCH, DELETE /api/orders/{pk}/
@action(detail=True, methods=["post"]) def cancelPOST /api/orders/{pk}/cancel/

The trailing slash is omitted when the router was created with trailing_slash=False. In every case emit (line 402) builds the Endpoint with id <service>:<METHOD> <norm_path>, response_schema set to the view's serializer name, and auth from the view or its bases. The first endpoint emitted for an id is kept.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
module_name44File path to dotted module name.
_uses_django61Whether the file imports django or rest_framework.
_Names, _Names.ref70, 90Resolve a name in a file to a dotted path through absolute and relative imports.
_methods_checked101HTTP methods a plain view compares request.method with.
_pattern120One path/re_path/url call as a pattern dict.
_serializer163A DRF serializer class as a Schema plus a fact.
file_facts200All Django facts of one file.
_route308A route as a path template, or None for a regular expression that is not a plain path.
_join318Joins a prefix and a route.
resolve322Walks the URL tree across files and emits endpoints and route unknowns.
lineage, inherited (inside resolve)361, 373Framework bases through project classes; inherited methods, actions, auth, serializer, lookup.
emit, emit_view, expand_router, walk (inside resolve)402, 412, 434, 458Build one endpoint; expand a view; expand a router; traverse patterns.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Every unresolvable route becomes a route_unknowns entry {"file", "line", "reason"} instead of being dropped. The reasons are: non-literal route; include(...) that cannot be followed; a view expression that cannot be resolved; a regular expression that is not a plain path; an included module not in the repository; a router not declared in the same module; a view not in the repository; a registration that does not resolve to a viewset; a class that declares no HTTP method or exposes no action.
  • A URL call with fewer than two positional arguments (for example path("x/", view=v)) is ignored without an unknown (line 122).
  • Include cycles are stopped by the seen set of visited modules, and recursion stops silently beyond depth 12.
  • When two project classes share a simple name, lineage and inherited do not follow that base (they require exactly one class of that name), so inherited actions may be missed.
  • A suffix match that finds more than one module or view is treated as not found.
  • A router included from another module (include(other.router.urls)) is not followed: only a plain name declared in the same module is accepted.
  • Meta.fields = "__all__" adds no fields, because only a list or tuple of strings is read.
  • Any top-level function whose first parameter is named request in a Django-importing file is recorded as a view function; it becomes an endpoint only if a URL pattern refers to it.

extract_orm.py 360 lines

Recognises Python ORM models (classes mapped to database tables by Django, SQLAlchemy, Flask-SQLAlchemy or SQLModel) as Entity records, and records which functions read or write which model.

How it works

Part 1: model_entity (line 136) is called for every class outside test and migration files. It first looks at the file's imports as one string: the Django branch runs if it contains django.db, the SQLAlchemy branch if it contains sqlalchemy, sqlmodel or flask_sqlalchemy. If neither, the class is not an entity.

Django branch.

  1. Each class-level assignment whose value is a call to a field from django.db.models is read by _django_attr (line 107). _is_django_field (line 127) checks the import, so a DRF serializer field named CharField is not mistaken for a model field.
  2. The inner class Meta is read for proxy, abstract and db_table.
  3. The class is a model if a base is named Model or it has at least one model field. A proxy model returns None.
  4. The table is the literal db_table (table_source="declared") or <app>_<classname> in lower case (table_source="django_default", with a note that a custom AppConfig label would change it). The app label is the directory holding the file, or the directory above a models/ package (app_label, line 76).
Field declarationColumn produced
name = models.CharField(max_length=80)name: {"field": "name", "type": "charfield(80)", "nullable": False, "pk": False}
id = models.AutoField(primary_key=True)id: pk true (also for BigAutoField, SmallAutoField without the keyword)
note = models.TextField(null=True, db_column="NOTE_TXT")note_txt: nullable true
customer = models.ForeignKey(Customer, ...) (also OneToOneField)customer_id: type fk
tags = models.ManyToManyField(Tag) in model Order, app shopno column; a join table shop_order_tags whose columns come from m2m_through in extract_data.py

SQLAlchemy, Flask-SQLAlchemy and SQLModel branch. Each class-level statement is classified:

StatementEffect
__tablename__ = "orders"the table name; a non-literal value marks the name dynamic
def __tablename__(cls)dynamic table name
__abstract__ = Trueabstract flag
__table__ = ...a data_unknowns entry of kind sqlalchemy_table_object
id = Column(Integer, primary_key=True) or id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(primary_key=True)a column through _sa_attr (line 87)
annotated attribute in a SQLModel class (table=True or direct base SQLModel), except Relationship(...) and ClassVara column; Field(primary_key=True), Field(nullable=...) and Field(sa_column=Column(...)) are honoured

_sa_attr reads the positional arguments of Column/mapped_column: the first string literal is the column name (else the attribute name), and the first non-literal argument that is not ForeignKey, Sequence or Identity is the type; if none, the type comes from the inside of Mapped[...]. Nullability is the explicit nullable= keyword; else false for a primary key; else, for a Mapped[...] annotation, whether it is optional; else true. The outcome is then decided in this order:

  1. If the class has a table name, a dynamic table name, table=True, or is a Flask model (a base equal to or ending in Model) with at least one column: a dynamic name gives an entity with an empty table, table_source="unresolved" and a dynamic_tablename unknown. With no declared name, the table is the lower-cased class name for SQLModel or snake_flask(ClassName) for Flask-SQLAlchemy, with table_source="naming_strategy". Otherwise the declared name is used.
  2. Else, if the class has columns: an abstract entity (a mixin or abstract base) that contributes columns to its subclasses at assembly.
  3. Else None.

Part 2: function_refs (line 290) returns, for one function, a list of {"function_id", "model", "op", "file", "line", "via"}. It first builds a map of local variable to model name: self and cls map to the enclosing class; an annotated parameter maps to its annotation if that names a capitalised type; a variable assigned from X.objects..., session.query(X)..., select(X) or a constructor call X(...) maps to X. Then every call in the body is matched:

Callopvia
Order.objects.filter(...), .get(...), .all() and any other manager or queryset methodreadOrder.objects.filter
Order.objects.create(...) and the other names in DJANGO_WRITE (line 17), including a chained Order.objects.filter(...).delete()writeOrder.objects.create
order.save(), order.delete() on a variable of known model, or Order(...).save()writeOrder.save
order.refresh_from_db()readOrder.refresh_from_db
session.query(Order) (first argument), select(Order, Item) (every argument)readquery or select
insert(Order), update(Order), delete(Order) as a bare function or on sa., sqlalchemy., sql.writeinsert, update, delete
session.query(Order).filter(...).update({...}) or .delete()writequery.update, query.delete
session.add(order), add_all([...]), merge, delete, bulk_* where the argument is a variable of known model or a constructor callwritesession.add
session.get(Order, order_id) (capitalised first argument, at least two arguments)readsession.get

A reference names the model only by its simple name. Whether that name is a mapped model is checked at assembly, where references to other names are dropped. Raw SQL is reported separately: a call to execute, executemany, exec_driver_sql, raw or executescript whose first argument is a string literal, f-string, concatenation or text(...) call adds a data_unknowns entry of kind raw_sql (line 358), which _sql_lineage in extract_python.py may later remove if the SQL was read.

NameLineWhat it does
