Edge cases and failure handling

  • What cannot be mapped is recorded in out.data_unknowns rather than ignored: an explicit __table__ object, a dynamic __tablename__, raw SQL.
  • A Django model with no fields of its own that inherits from a project base (not directly from Model) is not recognised by the Django branch.
  • The Django branch does not add the implicit id primary-key column; whether a later step does is outside this module.
  • In a file that imports both Django and SQLAlchemy, a class with __tablename__ is handled by the SQLAlchemy branch (line 165).
  • __abstract__ = True is honoured only in the second decision step. A Flask-SQLAlchemy base class with __abstract__ = True and columns satisfies the first step (flask_model and has_col, line 224) and is returned as a concrete entity with a derived table name.
  • The local-variable map treats any assignment from a bare-name call as a constructor (x = len(y) maps x to len). This is harmless because references to non-models are dropped at assembly.
  • cursor.execute(sql) with the SQL held in a variable matches none of the raw-SQL argument forms and produces no unknown in this function.
  • session.query(X).update({...}) with arguments matches two rules (lines 341 and 352) and yields two identical write references.

extract_openapi.py 177 lines

Reads an OpenAPI 3 or Swagger 2 specification file kept in a repository and emits the endpoints, schemas, enums and example responses it declares, in the same record types as code-declared ones.

How it works

is_openapi_file (line 23) accepts a file whose base name starts with openapi or swagger (any case) and ends in .yaml, .yml or .json, outside node_modules. extract_openapi (line 101) then:

  1. Rejects text longer than 10 MB with a files_failed entry.
  2. Parses with json.loads for .json, else yaml.safe_load. A parse error becomes a files_failed entry.
  3. Requires a mapping with an openapi or swagger key and a mapping paths; otherwise the file is counted in non_python_files.
  4. Schemas. Every entry of components.schemas (OpenAPI 3) or definitions (Swagger 2) becomes a Schema with id <service>:schema:<name>; each property becomes a field through json_field, required when listed in the schema's required array. A string or integer schema with an enum list is also stored in out.enums.
  5. Base path. Swagger 2 basePath is prefixed to every path; OpenAPI 3 has none (the servers list is not read).
  6. Operations. For each path starting with / and each of get, post, put, patch, delete, one Endpoint is emitted with source="openapi", handler="openapi:<operationId>" (or openapi:<METHOD> <path> without an operation id) and the same id format as a code endpoint, which is what lets assemble drop it when the code declares the same route.

How each endpoint value is read:

ValueSource in the specification
paramsPath-level parameters followed by operation-level ones, with in equal to path, query, header or cookie. The rules come from the parameter's schema (OpenAPI 3) or from its own type, enum, minimum, maximum, pattern, maxLength, minLength, items keys (Swagger 2). A path parameter is always required.
request_schemasSwagger 2: the $ref of the in: body parameter. OpenAPI 3: the schema of the first requestBody.content entry whose media type contains json. A $ref gives its last path segment; an inline schema with properties is registered as a new schema named <operationId>Request (spaces replaced by _).
success_status, response_schema, response_shapeThe first response code, in string order, that starts with 2. Its JSON schema (OpenAPI 3 content, or Swagger 2 schema) gives the shape: list for type: array, else object; and the schema name from the $ref of the schema or of its items.
authThe operation's security, or the root security when the operation has none. Each requirement becomes security:<scheme> or security:<scheme>[scope1,scope2].

json_field (line 31) maps JSON-schema keywords to the same constraint names the Python extractor uses:

JSON schemaField
type: array with itemsmulti: True, type List[<item type>]; minItems/maxItems are read from the array, everything else from the item
$ref: '#/components/schemas/Order'type Order
minLength, maxLength, minimum, maximum, pattern, multipleOfmin_length, max_length, minimum, maximum, pattern, multiple_of
exclusiveMinimum: true with minimum: 0 (OpenAPI 3.0 form)exclusive_minimum: 0, and minimum is removed
exclusiveMinimum: 0 (numeric form)exclusive_minimum: 0
enum: [...]allowed: [...]
format: email, nullable: trueformat: "email", nullable: True

Example responses. For every response of every operation, success and error alike, _examples (line 73) collects content.<json type>.example, each content.<json type>.examples.*.value, the schema's own example when nothing else was found, and Swagger 2 examples.<json type>. Each becomes an api_examples entry with partial: False and source: "openapi". Examples over 4000 characters of JSON are dropped.

Line numbers are found by _line_of (line 96), a text search for <name>: or <path>:, because the parsers do not report positions.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
is_openapi_file23File-name test for specification files.
json_field31A JSON-schema property as a TIA field record.
_schema63A named JSON schema as a Schema.
_examples73Example bodies a response declares, bounded in size.
_line_of96Line of the first occurrence of a text, or 1.
extract_openapi101The whole specification as a partial RepoExtract.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Nothing is fetched or followed: a parameter given as $ref is skipped (line 136), and $ref values are reduced to their last path segment whether they point inside the file or outside it.
  • Schema composition (allOf, oneOf, anyOf) is not read: only properties and required are, so a schema built only from allOf has no fields.
  • A response code such as 2XX is accepted as the success response but gives success_status=None; default is never treated as success.
  • A Swagger 2 body parameter with an inline schema (no $ref) gives no request schema.
  • _line_of finds the first textual occurrence, which can be an earlier key that ends the same way (searching Order: also matches PurchaseOrder:). In a JSON file keys are followed by ":, not :, so the search fails and every line is 1.
  • A file named like a specification but without the expected keys is not an error; it is counted as not analysed.

testcases.py 79 lines

Reads written (manual) test cases kept in a repository as CSV, YAML or JSON and returns them as uniform rows. Nothing is executed; the text is matched against code elsewhere.

How it works

is_testcase_file (line 31) accepts a path, compared in lower case, that ends in .csv, .yaml, .yml or .json and has a directory named testcases, test-cases or test_cases anywhere in it.

parse_testcases (line 52) reads the file:

  • CSV. The delimiter is detected from the first 4096 characters with csv.Sniffer among , ; tab and |, falling back to the standard comma dialect. The first row is the header. Rows whose cells are all blank are skipped. The row's line is reader.line_num.
  • YAML and JSON. The document must be a list of objects, or a mapping with the list under test_cases, testcases or tests. Entries that are not objects are skipped. The row's line is its position in the list, not a file line.

Each raw row goes through _row (line 40). Keys are normalised by _norm_key: runs of whitespace and underscores become one space, and the result is lower-cased, so Test_Case ID becomes test case id. Each output field takes the first non-empty value among its accepted column names (COLUMNS, line 19):

Output fieldAccepted column names (after normalisation)
idid, test case id, testcase id, case id, key, tc id, test id
titletitle, summary, name, test case, test case name
stepssteps, step, description, procedure, test steps, action, actions
expectedexpected, expected result, expected results, expected outcome
entry_pointentry point, endpoint, api, screen, page, feature
prioritypriority
typetype, test type, category

A list value (YAML steps written as a list) is joined with newlines. Missing fields are empty strings. A row without an id gets <file>#<n>, where n is its 1-based position. Every row also carries file and line.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
is_testcase_file31Path test for written test-case files.
_norm_key36Normalises a column name for loose matching.
_row40Maps one raw row to the seven output fields plus file and line.
parse_testcases52Parses a CSV, YAML or JSON file into rows; raises ValueError if unreadable.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Invalid JSON or YAML, or a document that is not a list, raises ValueError; extract_source turns it into a files_failed entry test cases: ....
  • At most 5000 cases per file are read (MAX_CASES); the rest are ignored without a message.
  • A mapping document without any of the three list keys yields no rows and no error.
  • The entry_point tuple also lists entry_point with an underscore, which can never match because normalised keys contain no underscore; the spaced form covers that column name.
  • A file under testcases/ named like a specification (openapi.json) is handled as a specification, because extract_source tests is_openapi_file first.

registry.py 109 lines

Imports an organisation's service catalog (who owns each service and how critical it is) from Backstage descriptors, CSV or JSON. Extraction uses one function of it, catalog_service_map, to read a repository's own catalog-info.yaml as a fallback for the service map.

How it works

Backstage descriptors (from_backstage, line 42). The text may hold several YAML documents. A document is used when its kind is Component, API or System and it has metadata.name. It yields {"service", "owner", "criticality"}:

  • service is metadata.name.
  • owner is spec.owner with a leading Backstage reference prefix removed (_owner, line 29): group:default/team-payments becomes team-payments.
  • criticality (_crit, line 34) is the annotation tia/criticality when it is one of critical, high, medium, low, none; otherwise spec.tier or the label tier, mapped through TIERS (line 19): tier-1, tier1 or 1 is critical; 2 is high; 3 is medium; 4 is low.

General import (parse_registry, line 58). The format is backstage, csv (columns named exactly service, owner, criticality) or json (a list of objects with those keys). Rows without a service are dropped, then the result is validated: at least one row, at most 5000, and every criticality given must be one of the five levels.

Applying to configuration (merged_config, line 86). Returns a deep copy of the current tenant configuration in which criticality[service] and ownership[service] are set for each row that states them. The registry wins for the services it names; other entries are untouched.

Use during extraction (catalog_service_map, line 99). Takes the dict of small files, reads catalog-info.yaml or catalog-info.yml, and returns the first usable descriptor as a dict with only its non-empty keys. read_service_map uses it to fill service, owner and criticality where tia-services.yaml is silent.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
RegistryError25The error raised for unreadable or invalid registry input; a ValueError.
_owner29Strips group:/user: and default/ from an owner reference.
_crit34Criticality from annotation or tier.
from_backstage42Backstage YAML documents to rows.
parse_registry58Parses and validates a registry in one of three formats.
merged_config86Configuration with owners and criticalities applied.
catalog_service_map99A repository's own catalog file as {service, owner, criticality}.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • catalog_service_map never raises: invalid YAML is caught and gives an empty dict, so a broken catalog file cannot stop extraction.
  • When the catalog file holds several descriptors, only the first usable one is taken, whatever its kind.
  • An unknown tier value gives no criticality rather than an error.
  • The module docstring names only Component; the code also accepts API and System (line 49).
  • CSV column names are matched exactly and case-sensitively, unlike the loose matching in testcases.py. JSON owners are not passed through _owner, so a Backstage-style prefix stays.

exclude.py 57 lines

Decides whether a file path matches one of a tenant's exclude patterns (path globs), so that extraction.py can skip it.

How it works

_compile_one (line 18) translates one glob into an anchored regular expression, after replacing backslashes with / and trimming spaces. The result is cached for up to 512 patterns.

Glob tokenRegular expressionMeaning
**/(?:.*/)?zero or more whole directories
**.*anything, across directories
*[^/]*anything within one path segment
?[^/]one character within a segment
any other characterescapeditself; regular-expression metacharacters are literal

matches (line 45) tests each compiled pattern against the full path and against the file's base name; either match excludes the file. should_exclude (line 54) returns false for an empty pattern list and otherwise compiles and matches.

PatternPathExcluded
vendor/**vendor/x.pyyes
vendor/**a/vendor/x.pyno (the pattern is anchored at the start)
**/gen/**a/gen/x.py, gen/x.pyyes
*.min.jsa/b/x.min.jsyes (matches the base name)
vendorvendor/x.pyno

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
compile_patterns13Compiles a list of globs, skipping empty strings.
_compile_one18Glob to anchored regular expression, cached.
matches45Whether any pattern matches the path or its base name.
should_exclude54The one function extraction calls.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A pattern that is only whitespace compiles to (?!), which never matches.
  • A bare directory name does not exclude the directory's contents; vendor/** or **/vendor/** is needed.
  • Because the base name is also tested, a pattern without a / such as *.sql applies at any depth.
  • Matching is case-sensitive, and no pattern can raise: every character other than * and ? is escaped.
  • The patterns are part of the per-file cache key and are stored in RepoExtract.exclude_patterns, so changing them re-extracts and the analysis records what was skipped.

Extraction: Java, data, UI and other languages

This layer turns non-Python source files into the same normalised facts the Python extractor produces: endpoints, schemas, outbound call sites, functions, tests, database entities, migrations, SQL access, event topics and UI pages. Each module exposes a per-file function that depends only on the file's path and text and returns a partial RepoExtract (the container data class in tia/models.py); anything that needs more than one file (an OpenFeign interface and its callers, a Spring Data repository and its entity, an Angular component and its template) is stored as a raw per-file fact and joined later by an assembly function (resolve_clients, keep_reachable_schemas, resolve_data, assemble_pages). No module in this layer creates graph edges, and none executes or compiles repository code.

extract_java.py 1,151 lines

The Java adapter for Spring Boot / Spring Web code. From one .java file it extracts HTTP endpoints, DTO schemas with validation constraints, outbound HTTP call sites (RestTemplate, WebClient, RestClient, OpenFeign), functions, JUnit tests, JPA entities, Spring Data repositories, Kafka topics and class inheritance.

How it works

Parser. The file is parsed with tree-sitter (a parser library that builds a concrete syntax tree without compiling) using the tree_sitter_java grammar (line 33). All work is done by one _JavaVisitor object per file (line 208). Helper _t returns a node's text, and _walk yields every node under a node in source order.

Entry point extract_file (line 1079) runs in this order:

  1. package-info.java and module-info.java are counted as analysed and return an empty extract (_is_stub, line 204).
  2. The text is parsed. If the tree contains any error, the first ERROR or missing node gives a line number, {"file", "error": "SyntaxError line N"} is appended to files_failed, and nothing else is extracted from the file.
  3. Otherwise _JavaVisitor.run() runs a file-level pre-pass (prepass) and then visits every top-level type declaration (visit_type).

The pre-pass (prepass, line 225) builds lookup tables that the rest of the visitor uses. It is flat over the whole file: a variable name maps to one type and one constant for the entire file, not per method.

TableFilled fromExample
package, imports, import_listpackage_declaration and import_declaration nodes. Only non-static, non-wildcard imports go into imports (simple name to fully-qualified name).import com.acme.client.BillingClient; gives imports["BillingClient"] = "com.acme.client.BillingClient"
out.unsupported_clientsAny import under a prefix in UNSUPPORTED_HTTP (OkHttp, Apache HttpClient, java.net.http, Retrofit, Vert.x, Unirest, JAX-RS, Jersey; lines 59-62).import okhttp3.Request; gives {"file": path, "library": "OkHttp"}
out.data_unknowns (kind data_access_library)In non-test files, any import under UNANALYSED_DATA_LIBS (EntityManager, Spring JDBC, jOOQ, Hibernate Session, MyBatis, JDBC, MongoDB, Redis, Spring Data JDBC; lines 64-67).import org.springframework.jdbc.core.JdbcTemplate; records "Spring JDBC is used here; the data it reads or writes is not analysed (FR-041)"
types (variable to simple type name)Fields, method and constructor parameters, local variables. A generic type keeps its base name; var x = new Foo() takes the constructed type; builder types are mapped to what they build by BUILDER_TYPES (line 57).private final WebClient.Builder b; gives types["b"] = "WebClient"
consts (name to ("literal", value) or ("config_key", key))@Value("${key}") on a field or parameter gives a config key (a default after : is dropped by _PLACEHOLDER, line 71). A field initialised with a string literal is a literal constant if it is static final or the string starts with http://, https:// or /. A local variable becomes a constant only if every assignment of that name in the file evaluates to the same string and it starts with one of those three prefixes (lines 268-273).@Value("${billing.url:http://x}") String billingUrl; gives consts["billingUrl"] = ("config_key", "billing.url")
method_returnsFor each method with a body and no parameters: the expression of its single return. If the method has several returns, or the name is declared twice, the entry is None (ambiguous).String base() { return baseUrl; }
bases (HTTP client variable to base)_bind_base (line 329): in any variable initialiser or assignment, the first call named baseUrl or rootUri, or WebClient.create(...) / RestClient.create(...), whose first argument _base_of can resolve.this.client = builder.baseUrl(billingUrl).build(); gives bases["client"] = ("config_key", "billing.url")
type_names_usedEvery type_identifier in the file; used for test imports.

Annotations are read by annotations (line 346) into a dict: simple annotation name to a list of (key, value_node) pairs, where an unnamed single argument has key None. _arg(items, "value", "path") returns the node for any of the given keys and treats the unnamed argument as value. _values expands an array initialiser {a, b} into its elements.

Type declarations (visit_type, line 550) handle class, interface, record and enum nodes, recursing into nested types with a qualified scope:

  1. An enum's constant names are stored in out.enums[name].
  2. The class-level path prefixes are read from @RequestMapping (_mapping_paths); the default is one empty prefix.
  3. Class-level authorisation markers are collected from the annotations in AUTH_ANNOTATIONS (PreAuthorize, PostAuthorize, Secured, RolesAllowed, PermitAll, DenyAll) as strings such as @PreAuthorize("hasRole('ADMIN')").
  4. In non-test files: a class with @Entity, @MappedSuperclass or @Embeddable goes to _add_entity; otherwise an interface without @FeignClient goes to _add_repository.
  5. An interface or class with @FeignClient goes to _add_client. Otherwise a class or record that carries none of the STEREOTYPES annotations (line 46: RestController, Service, Component, ...) is a schema candidate (_add_schema). Note that Entity is not a stereotype, so an entity class is also a schema candidate.
  6. In non-test files a TypeDecl is recorded with the simple names of the superclass and implemented or extended interfaces (generic arguments stripped; Object, Record, Enum ignored). These feed inheritance edges elsewhere.
  7. Each method and constructor in the body goes to visit_method, except inside a Feign interface, whose methods are declarations of calls and not implementations.

Methods and functions (visit_method, line 884). The function id is repo:path::Outer.Inner.method; a constructor is named <init>. A second method with the same qualified name (an overload) gets the suffix ~{parameter count}_{occurrence}. The hash is the SHA-1 (first 16 hex digits) of all leaf tokens of the method except comments, so reformatting and comment edits do not change it; hash and body_hash hold the same value. calls is the sorted set of every invoked method name in the body. An interface method with no mapping annotation is skipped. In a test file a method annotated with one of TEST_ANNOTATIONS (Test, ParameterizedTest, RepeatedTest, TestFactory, TestTemplate) becomes a TestCase and nothing else. Every other method becomes a Function that also carries:

  • signature: parameter names and declared types, and the return type text.
  • filters (_stream_filters, line 134): each .filter(x -> cond) or .removeIf(x -> cond) with a single-parameter lambda whose condition reads fields of the item through getters or field access. owners.stream().filter(o -> o.isActive()) gives {"expr": "o.isActive()", "fields": ["active"]}; for removeIf the expression is wrapped as !(...).
  • statuses (_java_statuses, line 104): error statuses (400 and above) from new ResponseStatusException(HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND), ResponseEntity.notFound() / badRequest() / unprocessableEntity() / internalServerError(), and ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.X) or status(404).

Endpoints (_mapped line 867, _add_endpoints line 926). A method is an endpoint when it carries @GetMapping, @PostMapping, @PutMapping, @DeleteMapping, @PatchMapping or @RequestMapping. The code does not check that the class is a @RestController; the mapping annotation alone is enough, which is how controller interfaces are supported. For @RequestMapping the verbs come from method = RequestMethod.X (or an array of them), defaulting to GET. Paths come from value or path (single string, array, or a constant that _const_str can evaluate). One Endpoint is emitted per verb, per method path, per class prefix.

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/owners")
class OwnerController {
  @GetMapping("/{ownerId}")
  public ResponseEntity<Owner> find(@PathVariable int ownerId,
                                    @RequestParam(defaultValue = "10") int size) { ... }
}

produces Endpoint(id="svc:GET /owners/{}", method="GET", path="/owners/{ownerId}", norm_path="/owners/{}", handler="OwnerController.find", response_schema="Owner", response_shape="object", params=[{"name": "ownerId", "in": "path", "type": "int", "required": True}, {"name": "size", "in": "query", "type": "int", "required": False, "default": "10"}]). The pieces are derived as follows:

  • Full path: class prefix with trailing slash removed, plus /, plus the method path; a leading / is added if missing; then normalize_path (in models.py) replaces path variables with {}, lower-cases literal segments and strips configured gateway prefixes.
  • Response schema (unwrap, line 523): wrappers in WRAPPERS (ResponseEntity, Mono, Optional, CompletableFuture, ...) are removed; collections in COLLECTIONS (List, Set, Flux, Page, ...) and arrays become List[T]; void and primitives give None.
  • Response shape: "list" if the return type text is a collection (optionally inside ResponseEntity<, Mono<), an array or a Flux; "object" for any other non-void type; otherwise None (regex at line 929).
  • Success status: @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.CREATED) or a three-digit number, mapped through HTTP_STATUS.
  • Request schemas: the unwrapped type of each parameter annotated @RequestBody or @ModelAttribute; a List[T] body is recorded as T.
  • Parameters: @PathVariable (in path, always required), @RequestParam (in query), @RequestHeader (in header). The name is the annotation's value/name or the Java parameter name. Query and header parameters are required unless required = false or a defaultValue is present. Validation constraints on the parameter are read by the same _field function used for DTO fields.
  • Auth: the method's authorisation annotations merged with the enclosing class's.

DTO schemas and validation (_add_schema line 662, _field line 617). For a record the fields are its header parameters; for a class they are the non-static field declarations. The wire name is the Java name unless @JsonProperty("x") renames it; @JsonIgnore removes the field (_json_name). A schema is recorded only if it has at least one field or a superclass. Each field is a dict {"type", "required", optional "multi", optional "constraints"}:

SourceResult
@NotNull, @NotBlank, @NotEmpty, or a primitive type (int, long, double, boolean, ...)required: True
@Size(min=, max=)min_length/max_length, or min_items/max_items when the type is List, Set, Collection, Iterable or an array
@Min, @DecimalMin / @Max, @DecimalMaxminimum / maximum (numeric suffixes L, D, F stripped by _num)
@Positive / @PositiveOrZero / @Negativeexclusive_minimum: 0 / minimum: 0 / exclusive_maximum: 0
@Pattern(regexp = "...")pattern
@Emailformat: "email"
@NotBlank / @NotEmpty on a non-collection with no explicit minimummin_length: 1

Example: record NewOwner(@NotBlank @Size(max = 30) String firstName, List<@Valid Pet> pets) gives {"firstName": {"type": "String", "required": True, "constraints": {"max_length": 30, "min_length": 1}}, "pets": {"type": "List<@Valid Pet>", "required": False, "multi": True}}. Every non-stereotype class is a candidate at this stage; keep_reachable_schemas (line 1126) prunes them at assembly: it first merges superclass fields into subclasses (Schema.extends), then keeps only Java schemas named by an endpoint's request or response, plus, transitively, any schema whose name appears as an identifier in a kept schema's field types.

Outbound HTTP calls (_scan_body, line 965). Every method invocation in a method body is classified by the declared type of the receiver variable (looked up in types):

PatternConditionHTTP methodURL argument
restTemplate.getForObject(url, ...) and the other names in TEMPLATE_METHODS (line 37)receiver type is RestTemplate (a RestTemplateBuilder variable also counts, including builder.build().getForObject(...))From the method name; for exchange / execute the last dotted segment of the second argument (HttpMethod.POST gives POST)first argument
client.get().uri("/x/{id}", id)the call is named uri and _chain (line 1001) walks the receiver chain back to a root variable of type WebClient or RestClientThe verb call found in the chain (get(), post(), ...) or the argument of .method(HttpMethod.X)first argument of uri
kafkaTemplate.send("topic", ...)receiver type starts with KafkaTemplate or the variable name matches kafkaTemplate|producernot an HTTP call: see Kafka below
any other var.method(...)the variable has a known type that is not in BUILTIN_TYPES and was not imported from a package in FRAMEWORK_PREFIXESrecorded as a typed call {"type", "fqn", "method", "function_id", "file", "line"} in out.typed_calls, resolved at assembly

How the URL is resolved (parse_url, line 450). It returns (path_template, base_kind, base_value, reason). The base is the part of the URL that identifies the target service: a host name (base_kind = "host") or a configuration key ("config_key") to be looked up later. Supporting functions: _const_str (line 380) evaluates a string literal, a literal constant identifier, a + concatenation of those, or a parenthesised one; _base_of (line 404) resolves an expression that can only be a base: a constant, a ${key} placeholder, a field in consts, environment.getProperty("k") / env.getProperty("k") / System.getenv("K") (config key), or a call to a no-argument method of this file, resolved through _returned_base (line 429). That last function also recognises service discovery: a return expression containing getInstances("customers-service"), choose(...) or getInstance(...) with a literal service name yields the base http://customers-service.

URL argumentResult (path, base_kind, base_value, reason)
"http://vets-service/vets/{id}"("/vets/{id}", "host", "vets-service", None)
"/vets"("/vets", None, None, "base URL not present in call"); if the client variable has an entry in bases, _emit fills the base in and clears the reason
billingUrl + "/invoices/" + id (with billingUrl bound by @Value("${billing.url}"))("/invoices/{}", "config_key", "billing.url", None): the leading variable is the base, later non-literal parts become {} (_from_parts, line 497)
String.format("%s/owners/%d", base, id)the format string is split on format specifiers (_FORMAT_SPEC, line 72); each specifier consumes one argument as a variable part, then _from_parts applies
URI.create(x), new URI(x)recurses into x
uri(b -> b.path("/x").build(id)) (a lambda)the first literal argument of a path(...) call inside the lambda, if it starts with /; otherwise reason "uri builder not resolvable" (_emit, line 1016)
anything else(None, None, None, "URL expression not statically resolvable: ...")

_emit (line 1015) writes a CallSite with id repo:path:line even when the path is unknown, so an unresolved call is visible as a gap and is not silently dropped.

OpenFeign (_add_client, line 832). An interface annotated @FeignClient is stored in out.clients. Its base is the url attribute (a ${key} placeholder gives a config key, an http... URL gives its host, any other string is used as a host), or else the name/value attribute (the service name, used as a host, or a config key if it is a placeholder). Each method's mapping annotation gives [verb, normalised path], with the client's path attribute prefixed.

@FeignClient(name = "billing-service", path = "/api")
interface BillingClient {
  @GetMapping("/invoices/{id}") Invoice get(@PathVariable long id);
}
// elsewhere, in another file:  billingClient.get(42);

The interface file yields {"type": "BillingClient", "base_kind": "host", "base_value": "billing-service", "methods": {"get": ["GET", "/api/invoices/{}"]}}; the caller file yields a typed call {"type": "BillingClient", "method": "get", ...}. At assembly, resolve_clients (line 1098) joins them:

  1. For each typed call, sorted by file, line and method, look up a Feign client by the simple type name. If found and the method is declared there, append a CallSite with that verb, path and base. If the client exists but the method is not declared, the call is dropped.
  2. Otherwise take the package of the call's fully-qualified type and find the longest key of the service map's packages section that equals or prefixes it. If one matches, append a CallSite with no method or path but client_pkg set to that key and client_attr set to the method name (a call through a shared client library).
  3. All other typed calls are discarded, and out.typed_calls is cleared.

Tests (_add_test, line 1035). A file is a test file if its path contains /src/test/ or its name ends in Test.java, Tests.java, IT.java or ITCase.java. For each test method the HTTP calls it makes are collected as [verb, normalised path] pairs, but only when the path resolves to a string starting with /:

  • MockMvc: get("/owners/1"), post(...) and so on, called unqualified or on MockMvcRequestBuilders, when the file imports something containing MockMvcRequestBuilders.
  • Fluent clients: .uri("/x") on a chain rooted at a variable of type TestRestTemplate, WebTestClient, MockMvc or RestTemplate, when a verb is found in the chain.
  • Template methods (getForObject, postForEntity, ...) on a variable of one of those types; exchange and execute are skipped here because their verb is not taken from the name.

The test also records asserted statuses (MockMvc matcher names in MOCKMVC_STATUS such as isNotFound, and is(...) / isEqualTo(...) calls whose text contains status), up to 60 distinct literals of at most 60 characters, the invoked method names, and imports: the file's import list plus the fully-qualified name of every type identifier used anywhere in the file.

Kafka. @KafkaListener(topics = "orders.created") (also arrays and constants) on a method records {"kind": "consumes", "topic", "function_id", "via": "@KafkaListener"} in out.events; kafkaTemplate.send("orders.created", payload) records {"kind": "produces", ..., "via": "KafkaTemplate.send"}. In both cases the topic must evaluate to a constant string and match the TOPIC pattern imported from tia/events.py (a module outside this section).

JPA entities and Spring Data repositories are also extracted here (_add_entity line 712, _add_repository line 801) but are described under extract_data.py, which resolves them.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A file with any syntax error is reported in files_failed and contributes no facts at all; there is no partial extraction for Java.
  • types and consts are file-wide maps keyed by bare variable name. Two methods that use the same variable name for different types see whichever declaration was visited last.
  • If a mapping annotation has a value/path that is not a constant string (for example a constant imported from another class), _mapping_paths returns an empty list (line 865) and no endpoint is emitted for that mapping; no unknown is recorded.
  • In a mixed concatenation, only the first variable can be a base; a constant identifier in a later position is treated as a path parameter. host + OWNERS_PATH + id therefore produces the path {}{}, which fails the "must start with /" check and is reported as "path does not start with a literal segment" (lines 463 and 518).
  • When a client's default base is a URL with a path (baseUrl("http://billing/api")), _emit keeps only the host name (lines 1025-1027); the /api prefix is not added to the call's path. If the default base has no host (for example baseUrl("/api")), base kind and value become None and the reason is still cleared.
  • A Kafka send whose topic is not a constant, or does not match the topic pattern, is skipped silently (the continue at line 982 also prevents it being treated as a typed call).
  • Statuses set by an exception class's own @ResponseStatus are not followed (stated in the docstring at line 105).
  • Methods declared inside an enum body are not visited: visit_type iterates the direct children of the body, and an enum's methods sit one level deeper in the tree-sitter grammar. (This is inferred from the loop at lines 595-601; the code has no explicit enum-method handling.)
  • An abstract class method (no body) still becomes a Function with empty calls.

extract_data.py 1,149 lines

The data-layer adapter. It parses schema migrations (Flyway and plain SQL, Liquibase, Alembic, Django) into a list of structural operations, and at assembly time it resolves ORM facts that span several files: inherited columns, join columns, table names, and which function reads or writes which entity.

How it works

Naming helpers. norm_ident (line 39) reduces a possibly quoted and schema-qualified name to its last part, unquoted and lower-cased: "public"."Owner" gives owner. snake (line 45) reproduces Spring Boot's default physical naming strategy: an underscore is inserted before an upper-case letter that follows a lower-case letter and is followed by a lower-case letter, then everything is lower-cased, so userId gives user_id but userID gives userid. snake_flask (line 57) is Flask-SQLAlchemy's rule (OrderLine gives order_line); it is defined here for use by another module.

JPA entities are extracted by _add_entity in extract_java.py (line 712) and finished here. Per file, for a class annotated @Entity, @MappedSuperclass or @Embeddable:

SourceFact
@Table(name = "OWNERS")table = "owners", table_source = "declared"
no @Table nametable = snake(class name), table_source = "naming_strategy", plus a note that the name was derived
plain field private String firstName;column first_name (or the @Column(name=) value), pk if @Id, nullable unless it is the key or a primitive, overridden by @Column(nullable=)
static, transient, @Transient fieldskipped
@ManyToOne / @OneToOne without mappedBya pending join {"field", "target", "name" (from @JoinColumn), "nullable", "pk"}; the column name is decided at assembly because it depends on the target's key
@OneToManyskipped: the foreign key is in the other table
@ManyToMany with @JoinTable(name=, joinColumns=, inverseJoinColumns=)a join_tables entry with the declared columns; without a declared table and without mappedBy, an unknown of kind default_join_table
@ElementCollection with @CollectionTable(name=)a join_tables entry; without a table name, an unknown of kind default_collection_table
@Embedded / @EmbeddedIdthe embedded type name, merged at assembly
@Inheritance, @DiscriminatorColumn, @DiscriminatorValuean unknown of kind entity_inheritance
class has no @Entity (mapped superclass or embeddable)abstract = True: it contributes columns but maps no table

Example: @Entity class OrderLine { @Id Long id; @ManyToOne Order order; } yields an Entity with table order_line, column id (key) and a pending join on order. At assembly, merge_entity_columns finds the target entity Order, takes its key column (default id), and adds the column order_id with type fk(Order).

Spring Data repositories are recorded by _add_repository in extract_java.py (line 801): an interface in a file that imports something from org.springframework.data and extends a generic type whose base name is in SPRING_DATA_BASES (line 931: Repository, CrudRepository, JpaRepository, PagingAndSortingRepository, reactive and list variants). The first type argument is the managed entity. Each declared method is stored with its @Query text (if constant), whether nativeQuery = true, and whether @Modifying is present. A @Query whose text is not a constant produces an unknown of kind query_not_constant.

SQL migrations. is_sql_migration (line 66) accepts a .sql file outside /src/test/ whose path contains /db/migration, /migrations/ or /flyway/, or whose name starts with r__, matches v<digits>__, or is schema.sql / schema-*.sql. Files whose name starts with u (Flyway undo scripts) are rejected. The SQL is parsed by hand-written code, not a SQL library:

  1. split_statements (line 92) is a character scanner that returns (statement, first_line) pairs. It drops -- and /* */ comments; copies quoted strings, back-quoted and bracketed identifiers and PostgreSQL dollar-quoted bodies ($$...$$, $tag$...$tag$) without looking inside; ends a statement at ;, at a line containing only / (Oracle) or GO (SQL Server). A statement is marked procedural when its first 80 characters match _PROC_HEAD (CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION|PROCEDURE|TRIGGER|PACKAGE|TYPE BODY, DO, DECLARE) or it starts with BEGIN followed by something other than a transaction keyword. Inside a procedural statement the scanner counts BEGIN/CASE against END (ignoring END IF, END LOOP and similar) and only ends the statement at a ; when the depth is back to zero.
  2. parse_statement (line 376) collapses whitespace and matches the statement against regular expressions in a fixed order (table below), returning a list of operation dicts and, when it could not read the statement, one unparsed entry {"line", "reason", "text"}.
  3. parse_sql (line 443) concatenates the results for a whole script.
StatementResult
BEGIN, BEGIN TRANSACTIONignored
procedural head, or anything starting with BEGINunparsed: "procedural SQL ... is not analysed"
CALL / EXEC / EXECUTEunparsed: "stored-procedure call is not analysed"
CREATE TABLE [IF NOT EXISTS] t (...){"op": "create_table", "table", "columns": {name: {"type", "nullable", "pk"}}}; table-level PRIMARY KEY (a, b) marks those columns; CREATE TABLE t AS ... (no parenthesis) gives columns: None; temporary and external tables are ignored, unlogged tables are kept
ALTER TABLE t <actions>actions split on top-level commas and each passed to _ops_alter_action (line 278)
DROP TABLE [IF EXISTS] a, b [CASCADE]one drop_table per name
RENAME TABLE a TO b, c TO d (MySQL)rename_table operations
anything matching _IGNORE_STATEMENT (line 363): indexes, sequences, views, grants, INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE, SET, COMMENT ON, ...ignored: no structural effect on mapped tables
anything elseunparsed: "statement not analysed: ..."

_ops_alter_action recognises these action forms across PostgreSQL, MySQL, Oracle and SQL Server:

ActionOperation
ADD [COLUMN] [IF NOT EXISTS] c type ..., ADD (c1 type, c2 type)add_column with type, nullable, default, pk
DROP [COLUMN] [IF EXISTS] c; a bare identifier directly after a drop (T-SQL DROP COLUMN a, b)drop_column
ALTER [COLUMN] c [SET DATA] TYPE talter_column with type
ALTER [COLUMN] c SET|DROP NOT NULLalter_column with nullable
ALTER COLUMN c type [NOT] NULL (T-SQL), MODIFY [COLUMN] c type (MySQL, Oracle)alter_column with type and, when stated, nullable
CHANGE [COLUMN] old new type (MySQL)rename_column (if the names differ) and alter_column
RENAME COLUMN a TO b / RENAME TO t2rename_column / rename_table
ADD CONSTRAINT, DROP CONSTRAINT, OWNER TO, ENABLE, SET, engine and charset options, partitions, ...ignored
anything elseunparsed: "ALTER TABLE action not analysed: ..."

parse_column_def (line 261) reads one column definition. The name is the first identifier; the type is the text up to the first constraint keyword in _TYPE_END (NOT NULL, DEFAULT, PRIMARY KEY, REFERENCES, ...). The column is non-nullable if it has NOT NULL, is a primary key, or is a serial type; it has a default if it has DEFAULT, auto-increment, identity, GENERATED, or is serial. Example: ALTER TABLE owners ADD COLUMN email VARCHAR(120) NOT NULL DEFAULT '' gives {"op": "add_column", "table": "owners", "column": "email", "type": "varchar(120)", "nullable": False, "default": True, "pk": False, "line": N}.

Liquibase. is_liquibase_path (line 75) accepts .xml, .yaml, .yml or .json files whose path contains changelog or /liquibase/. XML is parsed with xml.etree.ElementTree (_lb_xml, line 542), which first refuses any document containing <!DOCTYPE or <!ENTITY; YAML with yaml.safe_load_all and JSON with json.loads (_lb_data, line 579). Both walk each changeSet and hand every change to _lb_change (line 484):

Change typeOperation
createTablecreate_table with columns from column children and their constraints (primaryKey, nullable)
addColumnadd_column per column; default is true if any defaultValue* or autoIncrement attribute is present
dropColumn, renameColumn, modifyDataTypedrop_column, rename_column, alter_column (type)
addNotNullConstraint / dropNotNullConstraintalter_column with nullable false / true
dropTable, renameTabledrop_table, rename_table
sqlthe embedded text goes through parse_sql
createProcedure, customChange, sqlFile, ... (_LB_PROC)unparsed: "Liquibase X is not analysed"
indexes, keys, sequences, data changes, include, ... (_LB_IGNORED)ignored
any other tagunparsed: "Liquibase change type not analysed: X"

Line numbers for Liquibase operations are found by searching the raw text for the next occurrence of the tag or key after a moving cursor (line_of), because neither parser reports positions. <rollback> blocks are skipped.

Alembic (alembic_migration, line 674) takes an already-parsed Python ast.Module. It requires a module-level revision = "..." and an upgrade function; down_revision (string, or first string of a tuple) becomes parent. It then walks every call in upgrade whose receiver is the name op, or a name bound by with op.batch_alter_table("t") as batch_op: (in which case the table comes from the with). Recognised methods: create_table, add_column, drop_column, drop_table, rename_table, alter_column (new_column_name gives a rename, type_ and nullable give an alter_column), and op.execute (a string literal or text("...") goes through parse_sql; anything else is unparsed as "dynamic SQL in op.execute"). Columns are read from sa.Column("name", sa.String(50), nullable=False) by _sa_column, and sa_type (line 639) maps SQLAlchemy type names to SQL families: sa.String(50) gives varchar(50), sa.Integer() gives integer. downgrade is never read.

Django (django_migration, line 792) requires a class named Migration with an operations list. The app label is the name of the directory two levels above the file (orders/migrations/0002_x.py gives orders); the version is the file name without extension; the parent is the dependency on the same app. Default table names are <app>_<model lower-case>.

OperationResult
CreateModelcreate_table with columns from fields; an id autofield key is added if no field is a key and the model is not proxy or abstract; options={"db_table": ...} overrides the name and sets declared; each ManyToManyField adds a create_table for its link table
AddField / AlterFieldadd_column / alter_column; an added many-to-many field creates the link table
RemoveField, RenameFielddrop_column (with orm_field holding the attribute name), rename_column
DeleteModel, RenameModel, AlterModelTabledrop_table, rename_table
RunSQLstring literals go through parse_sql; a non-literal gives unparsed "dynamic RunSQL is not analysed"
RunPythonunparsed: "RunPython: its schema or data effect is not analysed"

_dj_field (line 771) derives a column from a field call: ForeignKey and OneToOneField use <name>_id with type fk; other fields use the attribute name (or db_column) and a type such as charfield(100); nullable comes from null=True. Example: migrations.AddField("order", "note", models.CharField(max_length=100, null=True)) in app shop gives {"op": "add_column", "table": "shop_order", "column": "note", "type": "charfield(100)", "nullable": True, "pk": False, "default": True, "model": "order", "app": "shop"}. (A nullable field counts as having a default, because adding it does not break existing rows.)

alembic_migration and django_migration are not called from this module; they are written to be called by whichever module parses Python files.

Per-file entry point extract_file (line 902) handles SQL and Liquibase files only. A .sql file becomes a Migration with tool flyway if its base name starts with v or r (any case), otherwise sql; the version comes from _flyway_version (V1_2__init.sql gives 1.2, a repeatable migration gives R, anything else the file name). A changelog-named file that does not contain a databaseChangeLog root returns an empty extract (so a release-notes CHANGELOG.yaml is not an error).

Assembly: resolve_data (line 1033) runs once per repository, before resolve_clients because it reads the same typed_calls:

  1. merge_entity_columns (line 982) copies columns from abstract bases, mixins and embedded types into each entity (a base is used only when exactly one entity of that name and ORM kind exists), then converts each pending join into a column named @JoinColumn(name) or snake(field)_<target key>. If the target entity was not found, a note records the assumed name. For Django models it adds the columns and link tables of AbstractBaseUser, AbstractUser and PermissionsMixin (_auth_base), records an unresolved_base_model unknown for any other base not defined in the repository, and adds an implicit id key when none exists.
  2. Django table overrides: a table named by Meta.db_table on the model or in its CreateModel replaces the default name in every Django migration operation for that model and on the entity.
  3. Entity Framework: a C# class recorded with table_source = "unresolved" gets its table from the DbSet that exposes it (ToTable() name, else the DbSet property name with source ef_convention); classes that remain unresolved are dropped as plain classes.
  4. Entities that share a name get the suffix ~file on their id and are excluded from name-based lookup. An entity with no resolvable table yields an entity_table_unresolved unknown and is removed.
  5. If the service map sets jpa_custom_naming and JPA entities exist, a custom_naming_strategy unknown warns that derived names may be wrong.
  6. Spring Data access. For each typed call whose type is a recorded repository: if the repository's entity is not a known entity, record repository_entity_unresolved. If the called method has a constant @Query, the operation is write when @Modifying is present or the query starts with update, delete or insert, else read; other entities or tables named after FROM, JOIN, UPDATE, INTO in the query are added too (_query_targets), and a native query naming a table no entity maps produces native_query_table_unmapped. Without a query, the method name prefix decides (_op_of_method): WRITE_PREFIXES (save, delete, update, ...) are checked before READ_PREFIXES (find, get, count, exists, ...); a name matching neither gives repository_method_unknown.
  7. Model references from Python and C# (out.data_refs): each is matched to an entity by model name (a C# dbset reference is first translated to its entity). A name matching several entities gives model_name_ambiguous and no access.
  8. Results are de-duplicated per (function, entity, op) keeping the lowest line, sorted, and stored in out.data_access; unknowns are de-duplicated and sorted; repositories and data_refs are cleared.

Example: ownerRepository.findByLastName(name) in OwnerService.search, where OwnerRepository extends JpaRepository<Owner, Long>, gives DataAccess(function_id=".../OwnerService.java::OwnerService.search", entity="svc:entity:Owner", op="read", via="OwnerRepository.findByLastName").

Unknowns recorded (all are dicts {"file", "line", "kind", "reason"} in out.data_unknowns): data_access_library, entity_inheritance, default_join_table, default_collection_table, query_not_constant (from the Java adapter); unresolved_base_model, entity_table_unresolved, custom_naming_strategy, repository_entity_unresolved, native_query_table_unmapped, repository_method_unknown, model_name_ambiguous (from this module). Unreadable migration statements go to Migration.unparsed instead.

Key functions and classes

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A parse failure in a Liquibase file (ElementTree.ParseError, ValueError, yaml.YAMLError, json.JSONDecodeError) is caught and reported in files_failed as "cannot parse migration: ..."; SQL never fails as a whole, it only yields unparsed entries.
  • is_sql_migration rejects every file whose name starts with u (line 69), which excludes Flyway undo scripts but also files such as users.sql in a migrations directory.
  • The tool label is flyway for any SQL file whose name begins with v or r (line 908), so views.sql is labelled Flyway with its file name as version.
  • For a Liquibase <sql> change with several unreadable statements, only the first unparsed entry is kept (bad[0], line 530).
  • Django RemoveField uses the attribute name as the column (line 862), so removing a foreign key named owner yields column owner, not owner_id; the extra orm_field key is presumably used by a consumer to compensate, but that code is outside this module.
  • When two repositories share a simple name, the first recorded one is used (cands[0], line 1102).
  • Lines 416-417 of parse_statement replace a one-element list with itself and have no effect.

extract_ui.py 647 lines

The front-end adapter. It reads Angular, React, Vue and plain HTML source and produces pages: each page's interactive elements, several ways to locate each element in a browser test with a uniqueness check, whether an input is mandatory, its input rules, the options of each dropdown, the API calls the page makes and where it navigates.

How it works

Parser. There is no HTML or JavaScript parser library; the module uses only the standard library. Markup is tokenised by a hand-written scanner (scan, line 62) that works for both HTML and JSX (JSX is the HTML-like syntax embedded in React JavaScript). Script-side facts (form validators, arrays, API calls, routes) are found with regular expressions plus small bracket-matching helpers (_balanced, _split_top).

File selection (is_ui_file, line 32). Accepted extensions are .html, .htm, .vue, .jsx, .tsx, .ts, .js, .mjs. Files under generated or vendored directories (node_modules, dist, build, .angular, lib, webjars, ...), minified or bundled files, type declarations, spec and test files and *.config.js|ts are rejected. extract_ui_file additionally ignores any file over 1,000,000 characters.

The scanner (scan) emits three token kinds: {"open": tag, "attrs", "line", "self_closing"}, {"close": tag, "line"} and {"text", "line"} (whitespace-collapsed). HTML comments are skipped, and the content of <script> and <style> is skipped. Each attribute value is stored as a pair whose first element says how it was written:

SourceStored asMeaning
id="email", id=email("s", "email")static string
value={row.id} (JSX)("e", "row.id")expression; braces are matched by _skip_braces, which skips strings and template literals
required("b", True)bare flag
{...props}skippedJSX spread

Two accessors read attributes: static (line 138) returns a literal value only (a string, a bare flag as "true", or a JSX expression that is itself a quoted string, number or boolean); bound (line 154) returns the expression of an Angular binding [x]="expr" or [(x)]="expr", or of a JSX x={expr}.

Elements (template_elements, line 178). The token stream is walked once with a stack of open tags. Tags that are neither in HTML_TAGS nor contain a hyphen are ignored (React components, TypeScript generics). An element is recorded when it is a control (input, select, textarea, button, or an a that has a static href, (click), onClick or routerLink), or carries a test id attribute (data-testid, data-test-id, data-test, data-cy, data-qa), or has a role attribute. For each element:

FieldHow it is derived
attrsall static and flag attributes, excluding JSX expressions and Angular (event), *directive and [binding] attributes
typetype attribute; "text" for an input without one
namefirst available of: formControlName, bound [formControl], name, the last segment of the [(ngModel)] expression (_model_name: owner.email gives email), v-model, id
labeltext of a <label> that wraps the element; else the text of a label whose for / htmlFor equals the element's id; else the aria-label
textfor button and a: the text tokens between the open and close tags
mandatorya static required attribute (not "false") or a binding whose expression is exactly true; mandatory_source is then "required attribute"
constraintsmaxlength to max_length, minlength to min_length, max to maximum, min to minimum, pattern, step; type="email" adds format: "email"
actionsAngular event names ((click) gives click) and JSX handlers (onClick)
dynamic_idtrue when id is bound ([id], [attr.id], id={...})
repeatedtrue when the element or an open ancestor has *ngFor or v-for

Example: <label for="email">Email</label><input id="email" formControlName="email" maxlength="80" required> gives {"tag": "input", "type": "text", "name": "email", "label": "Email", "mandatory": True, "mandatory_source": "required attribute", "constraints": {"max_length": 80}, ...}.

Dropdown options. Inside an open <select>, each <option> with a static value is appended to the select's options as {"value", "text", "line"}. An option that is looped (*ngFor, v-for) or has a bound value is instead recorded in _dynamic_options with its source expression: the loop expression, or the preceding text token if it contains .map( (the JSX case), or the bound value expression.

Script-side facts.

  • form_controls (line 395): Angular reactive-form rules. It finds .group({ ... }) and new FormGroup({ ... }) object literals, splits them into top-level key: value parts, and scans each value for Validators.required|requiredTrue|email|minLength(n)|maxLength(n)|min(n)|max(n)|pattern(...). It also finds name = new FormControl(...). email: ['', [Validators.required, Validators.maxLength(80)]] gives {"email": {"mandatory": True, "constraints": {"max_length": 80}}}.
  • arrays (line 382): literal arrays assigned to a name. Items must be literals, or object literals with a value, code, id or key property; any other item makes the whole array dynamic and it is not recorded. currencies = ['EUR', 'USD'] gives {"currencies": ["EUR", "USD"]}.
  • api_calls (line 439): http|httpClient|api|axios (optionally this.) followed by .get|post|put|delete|patch( and a quoted or template string, and fetch("...", { method: "X" }) (default GET). _api_path replaces ${...} with {}, removes a scheme and host and the query string, and keeps the result only if it starts with /. this.http.get<Owner>(`/api/owners/${id}`) gives {"method": "GET", "path": "/api/owners/{}", "line": N}.
  • navigations (line 457): links in markup (to=, href=, routerLink=, [routerLink]="['/x'...") and calls in code (navigate, navigateByUrl, router.push, history.replace, $router.push), only for paths starting with /; de-duplicated per (target, via).
  • routes (line 476): Angular and object-style React routes matching path: 'x', component: Name or path: 'x', element: <Name, and JSX <Route path="x" element={<Name />}> tokens.

Per-file facts (extract_ui_file, line 490). The function returns a list of fact dicts of three kinds:

FileFacts
.html, .htmone template fact; framework is angular if the text contains formControlName, *ngFor, (click), [(ngModel)] or routerLink, else html
.vueone template fact (framework vue) from the content of <template>...</template>
script with @Component({...})a component fact (framework angular) with class name, selector, template_file (resolved from templateUrl relative to the file, or the file itself for an inline template: `...`), controls, arrays, api_calls, code navigations; plus a template fact for an inline template
.jsx, .tsx, or a .js file containing return (< or => (<Tagif any elements are found: a template fact and a component fact (framework react) whose class is the default export or the first capitalised function or const, with controls empty
any scriptone route fact per route found

For scripts, strip_js_comments (line 166) first blanks // and /* */ comments while preserving newlines, so commented-out markup is ignored and line numbers stay correct.

Assembly (assemble_pages line 589, _page line 610).

  1. Templates are indexed by file. Each component is paired with the template at its template_file; its route is the first route fact naming its class. Templates not claimed by any component become pages on their own with no route.
  2. For each element, a reactive-form rule with the same name is merged in: it can set mandatory (source "form validator (Validators.required)") and adds constraints, with template attributes taking precedence on conflict.
  3. For each select with dynamic options, the source expression is searched for of X, in X or X.map(. If the last segment of X names a literal array of the component, its values become the options and options_source is "literal list 'X' in the component"; otherwise options_source says "dynamic: X (filled at run time; values not in the source)". A select without dynamic options gets "static options in the template".
  4. Locators are computed (below) and the best one chosen.
  5. The page dict holds page (route, else component class, else file name), route, component, framework, files, elements, api_calls (each with a norm_path from normalize_path) and navigations. Pages are sorted by route, then name.

Locator candidates and ranking (_locators, line 540). A locator is a way for a test tool to find an element. Candidates are generated in a fixed order, which is the ranking from most to least robust. Each carries a CSS selector, an XPath and a Playwright expression, and a unique flag computed by counting the page's recorded elements that would match the same value.

RankStrategyConditionExample CSS / Playwright
1test idany of the five test id attributes[data-testid="save"] / getByTestId('save')
2idstatic id, not dynamic#email
3namename attribute; unique among elements of the same taginput[name="email"]
4form controlformControlName[formcontrolname="email"]
5aria labelaria-labelgetByLabel('Close')
6labellabel text, for input, select, textareaXPath //label[normalize-space()='Email']/following::input[1]
7textvisible text, for button and agetByRole('button', { name: 'Save' })
8placeholderplaceholder attributegetByPlaceholder('Search')
9position (fragile)always added; index among recorded elements of the same taginput:nth-of-type(2); always marked unique

The element's chosen locator is the first candidate that is unique and is not the position strategy; if none qualifies, the position locator is used. A repeated element also gets locator_note: "repeated for each item of a list: unique only within its row".

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
is_ui_file32Decides whether a path is front-end source worth reading.
scan62Tokenises HTML or JSX into open, close and text tokens with line numbers.
static / bound138 / 154Literal value of an attribute / expression of a bound attribute.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Nothing is rendered or executed. Values loaded from an API, elements built in data loops and computed ids are labelled dynamic or repeated; they are not guessed.
  • Uniqueness is checked only against the elements this module recorded for the page (controls and elements with a test id or role), not against the full document, and not against child components rendered inside the page.
  • _page removes aria_label from each element after computing its locators (line 642) while later elements are still being processed, so the aria-label uniqueness count for a later element (line 569) no longer sees earlier elements. With two elements sharing an aria-label, the first is marked not unique and the second is marked unique.
  • Vue support is limited to the template: a .vue file produces no component fact, so its API calls, arrays and navigation calls in the script are not extracted; Vue bindings written as :required or @click are not recognised by bound or the actions rule; and the regex at line 503 requires a bare <template> tag, otherwise the whole file is scanned.
  • A route is recognised only when component: or element: immediately follows path: in that order (line 478); lazy-loaded routes (loadChildren, loadComponent) are not matched.
  • Only one component per file is read: the first @Component and the first exported class after it.
  • An unterminated tag (no closing >) is skipped by advancing one character; stray closing tags are ignored.
  • The comment at line 635 announces a lookup of JSX .map lists in the component source, but no code follows it; the .map case is handled earlier through the preceding text token (line 234).

sqllineage.py 296 lines

SQL data lineage: for stored procedures, functions, triggers and views defined in SQL files, and for SQL statements embedded as strings in application code, it works out which tables are read, which are written and which procedures are called.

How it works

Parser. Everything is done with regular expressions over text; there is no SQL grammar. The module reads statements only for their table references.

One statement (statement_refs, line 54).

  1. _strip removes -- and /* */ comments and replaces every single-quoted string value with '', so text inside values can never be mistaken for a table name.
  2. Dynamic SQL is detected first: EXECUTE IMMEDIATE, sp_executesql, EXEC(@sql), EXECUTE format(, EXECUTE '...' or EXECUTE x || set the dynamic reason.
  3. Common table expression names (WITH name AS (, and , name AS () are collected and excluded from tables.
  4. Table references are matched by keyword:
PatternClassified as
INSERT [ALL] INTO t, REPLACE INTO t, UPSERT INTO t, MERGE INTO t, TRUNCATE [TABLE] twrite
UPDATE [ONLY] t [alias] SETwrite
DELETE [FROM] [ONLY] twrite
FROM t, JOIN t, USING t, and comma-joined tables after them (FROM a, b)read, unless followed by ( (a table function) or preceded by DELETE
CALL p, EXEC[UTE] [PROCEDURE] p, PERFORM p, JDBC escape {call p} / {? = call p}call (not sp_executesql, not a name starting with @)
  1. Each raw name passes through _name (line 44): temporary tables and table variables (#t, @t) are dropped; the name is normalised with norm_ident; pseudo-tables in _NOT_TABLES (dual, unnest, generate_series, ...) and SQL keywords are dropped. A name containing the marker __tia_dynamic__ (inserted where application code interpolates a value) sets the dynamic reason "a table or procedure name is built at run time".
  2. A table that is both written and read stays in reads only if a FROM or JOIN actually names it (line 93).

Example: INSERT INTO audit_log (id) SELECT o.id FROM orders o JOIN customers c ON c.id = o.customer_id gives {"reads": ["customers", "orders"], "writes": ["audit_log"], "calls": [], "dynamic": None}.

columns_named (line 97) returns every identifier-like word in the statement that is not a keyword (at most 600, sorted), plus "*" for SELECT * or an INSERT without a column list. These are candidate column names used elsewhere to link a statement to a specific column.

Definitions in SQL files (parse_definitions, line 138). is_sql_source accepts .sql, .pls, .plsql, .pks, .pkb, .prc, .fnc, .trg, .vw and .db2. The regex _DEF finds each CREATE [OR REPLACE|ALTER] PROCEDURE|PROC|FUNCTION|TRIGGER|VIEW|MATERIALIZED VIEW|PACKAGE BODY name. The body extends to the position returned by _body_end (line 124): the closing dollar-quote tag if one opens within 4,000 characters with no ; before it (PostgreSQL); otherwise the earliest of a GO line (SQL Server), a / line (Oracle) or the next CREATE of a definition; otherwise end of file. _definition (line 166) then splits the body on ;, runs statement_refs on each piece and unions the results. The output dict has name, kind, reads, writes, calls (minus the procedure itself), line, end_line, file, signature (the head before IS/AS/BEGIN/RETURNS, at most 300 characters), columns and digest (SHA-256 of the comment-free, whitespace-collapsed, lower-cased body, 16 hex digits, used to detect a changed body). Special cases: an Oracle package body is split into its member procedures and functions, named package.member; a trigger also records on_table.

SQL embedded in code (embedded, line 254).

  1. _literals (line 199) lexically finds string literals in any language: ordinary single and double quotes with backslash escapes (ending at a newline), triple-quoted strings, C# verbatim and interpolated strings (@", $", $@"), and back-quoted strings (JavaScript templates, Go raw strings). Interpolations (${x} in templates, {x} in C# $"..." and Python f-strings) are replaced by the dynamic marker.
  2. _joined (line 233) merges adjacent literals and + concatenations into one string; a non-literal operand between or after literals becomes the dynamic marker.
  3. Bind placeholders (%s, %(name)s, ?, :name, $1, @p) are replaced by 0 when the string has no dynamic part; {} / {0} format placeholders become the dynamic marker.
  4. is_sql keeps strings that start with a SQL verb (select, insert, update, delete, merge, with, call, exec, truncate, ...), are longer than 8 characters and are not a bare keyword.
  5. The statement is attributed to the smallest function whose line span contains it (the caller passes the file's Function objects); a statement outside every function is skipped.
  6. statement_refs produces one access record per table or procedure: {"function_id", "op": "read"|"write"|"call", "target", "file", "line", "sql" (120-character snippet), "columns"}. A dynamic statement adds an unknown of kind dynamic_sql.
  7. Procedure calls made through an API are matched by the regexes in _API_CALLS (line 188): Python cursor.callproc("p"), Spring withProcedureName("p") / withFunctionName("p"), @Procedure("p"), procedureName = "p", createStoredProcedureQuery("p"); and by _CS_PROC for C# new SqlCommand("p", ...) followed within 400 characters by CommandType.StoredProcedure.

Example: in Python, cur.execute("SELECT id FROM orders WHERE status = %s", (s,)) inside list_open gives {"function_id": "...::list_open", "op": "read", "target": "orders", "sql": "SELECT id FROM orders WHERE status = %s", "columns": ["id", "orders", "status"]}. The function also returns covered, the set of (function_id, line) pairs it read, so the caller can drop a generic "raw SQL not analysed" entry for the same line.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_strip37Removes comments and empties string values.
_name44Raw reference to a normalised table name, None, or the dynamic marker.
statement_refs54Reads, writes, calls and dynamic reason of one statement.
columns_named97Candidate column names in a statement.
is_sql107Decides whether a string literal is a SQL statement.
is_sql_source119Path test for SQL definition files.
_body_end124Finds where a definition's body ends.
parse_definitions138All procedure, function, trigger, view and package-member definitions in a file.
_definition166Builds one definition record from its body.
_literals / _joined199 / 233String literals in source text; concatenations merged.
embedded254SQL access and procedure calls found in application code.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Dynamic SQL is never treated as "no access": it produces a dynamic_sql unknown, both for definitions and embedded statements.
  • Sub-queries are not separated from the outer statement; their tables are simply added to the same read set.
  • Because _definition splits on every ;, control-flow keywords do not matter, but a ; inside a nested dollar-quoted string would split mid-statement (string values are only emptied later, per piece).
  • The expression at line 179, reads - writes | (reads & writes), is equal to reads by operator precedence, so a definition's read list is simply the union of its statements' reads.
  • C# stored-procedure calls found by _CS_PROC are added to the access list but, unlike the other API calls, not to covered (line 294 compared with line 287).
  • String detection is lexical and language-agnostic, so an apostrophe in a comment starts a "literal" that runs to the next apostrophe or end of line; such text is discarded later unless it happens to start with a SQL verb.

streams.py 189 lines

Event-stream and data-pipeline lineage for pipelines declared in source control: Kafka Streams topologies, Spark jobs and Kafka Connect connector configurations. It reports which topics each consumes and produces and which tables each reads and writes.

How it works

Parser. Regular expressions over the file text for Kafka Streams and Spark; json.loads or a line-by-line key=value reader for connector configurations.

Source files (stream_facts, line 65). The function returns immediately with empty lists unless the text mentions Kafka Streams (StreamsBuilder, KStream, KTable, org.apache.kafka.streams) or Spark (readStream, writeStream, SparkSession, pyspark, org.apache.spark, spark.read|table|sql). _constants (line 32) collects string constants declared in the same file (Java, Kotlin, Scala, JavaScript-style declarations and Python upper-case module assignments). Then:

PatternFact
Kafka Streams .stream(x), .table(x), .globalTable(x)event consumes, via KafkaStreams.stream etc.
Kafka Streams .to(x), .through(x)event produces
Spark .option("subscribe", "a,b")one consumes event per comma-separated topic, via Spark.readStream(kafka)
Spark .option("subscribePattern", ...), .option("assign", ...)unknown event_topic_unresolved: "the topics are chosen at run time"
Spark .option("topic", x)event produces, via Spark.write(kafka)
spark.table(x), read.table(x)SQL access read on the table, columns ["*"]
.saveAsTable(x), .insertInto(x), .toTable(x)SQL access write on the table

The argument x is resolved by _args (line 41): a string literal, an identifier (or dotted name whose last segment is) in the constants map, or a list wrapper such as Arrays.asList(...), List.of(...), listOf(...), Seq(...) of those. Pattern.compile(...) or any other expression is returned as unresolved, and for topics an event_topic_unresolved unknown is recorded.

Each fact is attributed to the smallest enclosing function (_owner). A statement outside any function, as in a Spark job script, is attributed to a synthetic job function with id repo:path::<job> and qualified name <job file.py>; its hash is the SHA-256 of the whole file, so any change to the script counts as a change to the job. Example: builder.stream("orders").mapValues(...).to(OUT_TOPIC) with static final String OUT_TOPIC = "orders.enriched"; gives a consumes event for orders and a produces event for orders.enriched.

Kafka Connect (connector_facts, line 148). is_connector_path accepts .json and .properties files whose path contains connect, outside node_modules, and whose file name does not contain package. _connector_config flattens a JSON {"name", "config": {...}} wrapper and requires a connector.class key; without it the file is not a connector and four empty lists are returned. The connector becomes a pipeline function with id repo:path::connector:<name> and a hash of its sorted configuration.

  • Sink (the class name contains sink): each topic in topics is consumed, and a table is written whose name is table.name.format with ${topic} substituted (default: the topic name). topics.regex produces an unknown.
  • Source (anything else): tables come from the first present of table.include.list, table.whitelist, table.includelist, tables. Each table is read, and a topic is produced: for a Debezium class with a prefix, <prefix>.<table as written>; otherwise <prefix><last segment of the table>, where the prefix is topic.prefix or database.server.name. A custom query produces a dynamic_sql unknown; no table list and no query produces an event_topic_unresolved unknown.

Example: {"name": "orders-sink", "config": {"connector.class": "io.confluent.connect.jdbc.JdbcSinkConnector", "topics": "orders", "table.name.format": "dw_${topic}"}} gives a consumes event on orders and a write on table dw_orders, both owned by connector orders-sink.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
_constants32String constants declared in the file.
_args41Topic or table names in an argument expression, or the unresolved expression.
_owner60Id of the smallest function containing a line.
stream_facts65Kafka Streams and Spark facts: (events, sql_access, job_functions, unknowns).

_connector_config131Parses JSON or properties into a flat config dict, or None.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Invalid JSON, or a file without connector.class, is treated as "not a connector" and yields nothing; no failure is reported.
  • The Kafka Streams patterns match any .stream(, .table(, .to( or .through( call in a file that mentions Kafka Streams. A Java collection call such as list.stream() matches with an empty argument, which _args returns as unresolved, so an event_topic_unresolved unknown is recorded for it (lines 94 and 88).
  • An unresolved table argument to spark.table, saveAsTable, insertInto or toTable is dropped without an unknown (lines 108 and 113 use only the resolved names), although the module docstring says run-time names are reported.
  • SQL passed to spark.sql(...) is not handled here; the docstring assigns it to sqllineage.py.
  • All connector facts use line 1.
