Edge cases and failure handling

  • A limit of 0 is valid and refuses every addition; null or an absent key means unlimited.
  • check meters and compares in a separate transaction from the action it guards, so two concurrent requests can both pass at the boundary; the code takes no lock.
  • An operator's change to a plan or region is seen by a running process after at most 10 seconds because of the settings cache.
  • month_bounds does not validate its argument; a malformed month string raises ValueError from int() or datetime().
  • check with an unknown what raises KeyError.
  • The module docstring names the commands plan-set and usage for setting limits and exporting billing data; the commands defined in tenants.py are limits-set and meter.

retention.py 143 lines

Applies each tenant's data-retention settings: deletes cached extraction data, reports, ingested evidence and old audit entries past their configured age, while keeping the audit chain verifiable. It also builds the audit summary report.

How it works

Retention is a section of the tenant configuration. Each key is a number of days, or null (the default) to keep forever. validate (line 32) enforces a minimum per key and a maximum of 36500 days for all:

SettingMinimum daysWhat enforce_tenant deletes when older than the limit
graph_days1Rows of file_extracts and extractions by created_at, and rows of graph_versions except those any graph_heads row points at.
analysis_days30Rows of analyses, then evidence_snapshots older than the limit that no remaining analysis references through data->>'evidence_snapshot_id'.
runtime_days1evidence_records of kinds runtime_edge, deployment, test_run, coverage, by ingested_at.
incident_days30evidence_records of kinds incident, incident_link, verdict, by ingested_at.
audit_days365Audit entries, with an anchor (below).

Purging audit entries without breaking the chain (lines 71-80):

  1. Computes the cutoff as an ISO timestamp with second precision and finds the last entry whose ts is before it (highest seq).
  2. If there is one, upserts the tenant's single audit_anchors row with that entry's seq and hash and the cutoff as purged_before.
  3. Deletes every entry with seq <= that sequence number.

The anchor is the hash the next remaining entry names as its prev_hash, so Store.verify_audit starts from the anchor and the remaining chain still verifies. If every entry was purged, Store.audit continues numbering from the anchor.

enforce(admin_dsn, tenant, now, audit_key) (line 84) is the scheduled job:

  1. Connects with the admin DSN, because the runtime role cannot delete audit entries or write anchors.
  2. Selects tenants with status active (optionally one, by id or name).
  3. For each: sets tia.tenant_id for the transaction, loads the newest configuration, calls enforce_tenant if any retention value is set, and commits. Each tenant is its own transaction.
  4. Afterwards, for every tenant where something was removed, writes a retention_enforced audit entry (with the removed counts and the settings) through a Store on a small Database pool. This is the first entry written after the purge.

The command line is python -m tia.retention enforce [--tenant X]. It reads TIA_ADMIN_DATABASE_URL and TIA_AUDIT_KEY through env_secret; the audit key must be the same one the server uses, or the new entry would not verify. It prints a JSON list of {tenant, tenant_id, retention, removed}.

audit_report(c, tenant_id, start, end) (line 111) counts audit entries in a period by event and by actor (the actor field of the payload, or system when absent or unparsable) and returns both tallies sorted by name.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
KEYS, MINIMUM26 / 27Setting names and their minimum days.
RUNTIME_KINDS, INCIDENT_KINDS28 / 29Evidence kinds covered by runtime_days and incident_days.
validate32Checks the retention section, appending to the caller's problem list.
enforce_tenant47Applies one tenant's retention in the caller's transaction; returns rows removed per category.
enforce84Runs every active tenant and audits each purge.
audit_report111Who did what in a period.
main126The command-line interface.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The graph a scope currently points at is never removed, whatever its age; only graph_id is protected, not previous_id.
  • Evidence kinds outside the two lists (approval, acknowledgement, notification, itsm_attachment, release, build) have no retention setting and are kept.
  • Suspended tenants are skipped, since only active tenants are selected.
  • The audit cutoff is compared as text (ts < %s), which works because ts is stored as a fixed-format ISO string in UTC.
  • A second purge replaces the anchor row (on conflict (tenant_id) do update); only the most recent anchor is kept.
  • When no entry is older than the limit, removed["audit"] is 0 and no anchor is written.
  • A value of 0 cannot be configured (it is below every minimum), and enforce_tenant also skips falsy values when building its cutoffs.

backup.py 194 lines

Exports all of one tenant's rows into a single encrypted file, restores such a file into a database at the same schema version, and verifies that a restored tenant matches the file. It complements whole-database point-in-time recovery, which is operator infrastructure outside this code.

How it works

Export (export, line 54):

  1. Loads the key: TIA_BACKUP_KEY (or TIA_BACKUP_KEY_FILE, or a reference) must decode from base64 to exactly 32 bytes. Without a key the export is refused unless unencrypted=True.
  2. Reads the tenant's registry row as JSON (row_to_json), found by id or name, and the list of applied migration versions.
  3. For every table with a tenant_id column except tenants (discovered from information_schema), reads all of the tenant's rows as JSON, ordered by their JSON text so the order is deterministic.
  4. Builds a manifest entry per table: the row count and the SHA-256 of json.dumps(rows, sort_keys=True, default=str).
  5. Serialises {format: 1, created_at, tenant, schema, manifest, tables} as key-sorted JSON and compresses it with gzip.
  6. Writes the file in one of two layouts.
LayoutBytes
UnencryptedTIABAK1\n, plain\n, gzip body.
EncryptedTIABAK1\n, aesgcm\n, tenant id, \n, 12-byte nonce, AES-256-GCM ciphertext of the gzip body. The AAD is TIABAK1\n plus the tenant id, so changing the clear-text tenant id (or any byte) makes decryption fail.

Secrets inside the backup remain encrypted exactly as in the database (rows of tenant_secrets and wrapped keys in tenant_keys); the key-encryption key is not in the file.

Read (read, line 77) checks the magic bytes, refuses an unencrypted file unless unencrypted=True, decrypts, decompresses, and recomputes every table's digest against the manifest; a mismatch raises "the backup is damaged".

Restore (restore, line 102):

  1. Reads and checks the file.
  2. Requires the target's list of applied migrations to equal the backup's list exactly; otherwise it tells the operator to migrate the target first.
  3. Refuses if the target already has a tenant with this id or this name (or the as_name override): a restore never merges.
  4. Inserts the registry row with json_populate_record(null::tenants, ...), keeping only keys that are columns of the target's tenants table, with the name replaced when as_name is given.
  5. Orders the tables with _insert_order (parents before children by foreign keys between the backed-up tables; on a cycle it takes the first remaining name alphabetically) and inserts each row with json_populate_record. Tables with an identity column get overriding system value so the original ids are kept.
  6. Commits once at the end, so a failure leaves nothing behind.

Verify (verify, line 149) re-reads each table of the restored tenant in the same order, and compares the row count and digest with the manifest. It returns {tables_checked, mismatched, ok}. The docstring points to GET /api/v1/audit/verify for checking the audit chain itself.

Command line: python -m tia.backup export <tenant> --out FILE [--unencrypted], restore FILE [--as-name NAME] [--unencrypted], verify <tenant> FILE [--unencrypted]. Exit code 2 means no DSN, 1 means an error or a failed verification.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
MAGIC28b"TIABAK1\n", the file signature.
BackupError31Any refusal or failure, with an operator-readable message.
tenant_tables35Tables with a tenant_id column, excluding tenants.
_key41Loads and checks the 32-byte backup key.
export54Produces the backup bytes.
read77Decrypts, decompresses and integrity-checks a backup.
restore102Writes a backup into a database that does not have the tenant.
_insert_order130Topological order by foreign keys.
verify149Compares a restored tenant with the backup.
main164The command-line interface.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Any decryption failure is reported as "cannot be decrypted with TIA_BACKUP_KEY or was altered", with the exception type but no detail.
  • The table list is discovered from the catalog, so a table added by a later migration is included without a code change. Unlike tenants.tenant_tables, this query does not restrict to base tables.
  • tenant_events has no tenant_id column and is therefore not part of a backup.
  • None of the functions set tia.tenant_id on the admin connection. Because the tables force RLS, reading and inserting rows here relies on the admin role bypassing RLS (a superuser); the project's admin guide describes the admin DSN as "an owner or superuser".
  • restore keeps the original values of identity columns but does not advance the identity sequence afterwards (lines 122-125).
  • The whole backup is built and held in memory as one JSON document.

Foundations

These modules have no knowledge of impact analysis; they are the small utilities everything else stands on. gitutil.py reads repositories without running their code; scrub.py removes credentials from any text before it is stored or logged; credentials.py, transport.py, netsafe.py and airgap.py control where secrets come from and which network connections are allowed; jwtverify.py verifies sign-in tokens; and metrics.py, telemetry.py, tracing.py and obs.py provide metrics, traces and structured logs, all of which pass through the scrubber.

gitutil.py 136 lines

Read-only access to a git repository on disk: resolve a revision to a commit, list files and blob ids, read file contents, and list the files changed between two revisions. It never executes code from the repository.

How it works

Everything goes through _git (line 16), which runs git -C <repo_path> <args...> with subprocess.run as an argument list (no shell), captures output, and raises GitError with git's standard error text on a non-zero exit, or if git is not installed. Only read commands are used: rev-parse, ls-tree, cat-file, show, diff.

Fast revision lookup. resolve_rev (line 57) avoids starting a git process for the common cases, because (as its docstring says) a process costs tens of milliseconds and an analysis may touch hundreds of repositories:

  1. If the revision is HEAD, or looks like a plain name ([A-Za-z0-9._/-]+) and is not a 40-hex commit id, it locates the git directory with _git_dir: <path>/.git if that is a directory, or the path itself if it has a HEAD file and an objects directory (a bare repository). A worktree, where .git is a file, returns None.
  2. _read_ref (line 35) reads the ref file directly: HEAD, refs/heads/<name>, or the name itself if it starts with refs/. A ref: ... line is followed recursively up to depth 5. If the loose file does not exist it scans packed-refs for a line ending in the ref name. The result is accepted only if it is 40 hex characters.
  3. If any of that fails, it falls back to git rev-parse --verify <rev>^{commit}, which handles tags, abbreviated ids and expressions.

Listing and reading. list_tree (line 74) runs git ls-tree -r -z and returns path -> blob id for regular files only (type blob with mode 100644 or 100755), which skips symbolic links (mode 120000) and submodules. The blob id is git's content hash, which the extraction cache uses as the identity of a file's content. read_blobs (line 88) fetches many blobs in one process by writing the ids to git cat-file --batch and parsing its output: each record is a header line <id> <type> <size>, then exactly size bytes, then a newline. changed_files (line 128) runs git diff --name-status --no-renames base head and returns (status letter, path) pairs; with renames disabled a rename appears as a delete and an add.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
GitError12Raised for any git failure.
_git16Runs one git command; returns text (UTF-8 with replacement) or bytes.
_git_dir26Finds the git directory of a normal or bare repository.
_read_ref35Resolves a ref from loose files or packed-refs.
resolve_rev57Revision to commit id, with the file-based fast path.
list_files69All paths at a revision.
list_tree74Regular files at a revision with their blob ids.
read_blobs88Contents of many blobs in one process.
read_file112git show rev:path as text.
read_tree116All files that decode as UTF-8, one git show per file.
changed_files128Changed paths between two revisions.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • _read_ref refuses a ref containing .., so a crafted name cannot read a file outside the git directory, and it returns None on any read or decoding error so that the caller falls back to git.
  • In read_blobs, a missing object produces the header <id> missing (two fields), which raises GitError("blob not found: ...").
  • list_tree decodes paths with surrogateescape, so a path that is not valid UTF-8 is preserved rather than dropped.
  • read_tree silently skips binary or undecodable files; read_file instead decodes with replacement characters.
  • The fast path tries a plain name as a branch first. For a name that exists only as a tag it finds nothing and falls back to git; for a name that is both a branch and a tag, the fast path returns the branch.

scrub.py 181 lines

Removes credentials from text before it reaches the database, a report, a pull-request comment, a log line or the audit chain. Source code under analysis may contain secrets, and fragments of it (an unresolved call, an unparsed SQL statement, an error message) end up in TIA's records.

How it works

There are two tiers. The high-confidence tier matches formats that cannot be an ordinary identifier, so it is applied to every string. The generic tier matches assignments such as password=..., which could damage real identifiers or paths, so it is applied only to free-text fields. Every replacement is counted; the value itself is never kept.

High-confidence patterns (HIGH, line 20). Each match is replaced by [REDACTED:<kind>]:

KindWhat it matches
github_tokenghp_, gho_, ghu_, ghs_ or ghr_ followed by 30 to 255 letters or digits; or github_pat_ followed by 40 to 255 letters, digits or underscores.
aws_access_keyAKIA, ASIA, AGPA, AIDA, AROA, ANPA or ANVA followed by 16 upper-case letters or digits.
private_keyFrom -----BEGIN ... PRIVATE KEY----- (optionally PRIVATE KEY BLOCK) to the matching END line, or to the end of the text if the block was cut off.
slack_tokenxox then one of a b p r s o, a hyphen, and at least 10 letters, digits or hyphens.
stripe_keysk_ or rk_, then live_ or test_, then at least 16 letters or digits.
jwtThree dot-separated base64url parts of at least 8 characters, the first two starting with eyJ.

Password in a URL (USERINFO, line 28): scheme://user:password@. The scheme and user are kept and the password becomes [REDACTED:url_password], unless the password is a placeholder. This is also applied to every string.

Generic patterns (GENERIC, line 29), applied only when generic=True. The value is replaced by [REDACTED:secret], keeping the key, the separator and any quotes:

#What it matches
1A key named pass, passwd, password, secret, token, api_key/api-key/apikey, access_key, private_key, client_secret, auth or authorization, an optional closing quote, : or =, an optional opening quote, and a value of at least 6 characters with no whitespace, quote, ;, ,, ), } or ].
2SQL: password (optionally =) followed by a single-quoted value of at least 3 characters.
3SQL: identified by followed by a single-quoted value of at least 3 characters.
4bearer followed by at least 20 token characters.

Two filters prevent false positives in the generic tier (and the placeholder filter also applies to URL passwords):

  • PLACEHOLDER (line 37): the value starts with $, {, <, %, ? or [REDACTED; or consists only of *, only of x, or only of dots; or is exactly none, null, true, false, changeme or example (case-insensitive). Template variables and dummy values are left alone.
  • CODE_EXPR (line 36): an unquoted value that looks like a dotted name (settings.AUTH) or a function call (get_password(...)) is code, not a credential, and is left alone.

scrub_text(s, generic=False) (line 76) applies the tiers in that order and returns (clean text, count). scrub_json(obj, generic_everywhere=False) (line 104) walks dictionaries, lists and tuples. Strings get the high-confidence tier always; the generic tier is added when generic_everywhere is set, or for everything underneath a key in FREE_TEXT (line 38): reason, text, detail, error, message, limitations, required_verification, summary, failure_reason, note, notes, sql, statement. Dictionary keys themselves are not scrubbed.

mask_literals(s) (line 42) is a separate, stricter tool for code and SQL snippets quoted in reasons: it replaces the contents of every string literal longer than three characters with ..., keeping the quotes. It understands backslash escapes and the SQL doubled quote '', and it masks a literal that was cut off by truncation. The reasoning in its docstring: the snippet only needs to show what kind of expression could not be analysed, and a literal could be a credential no pattern recognises.

scrub_extract(part) (line 125) applies these to one file's extraction result in place and returns the count:

FieldTreatment
Call site reason; data unknown reason; unparsed migration text and reasonGeneric tier, then mask_literals.
Call site norm_path and base_valueHigh-confidence tier only.
Failed file errorGeneric tier.
Function filters and statuses, test literals and bodies, schema fields, endpoint params, enums, ui_factsscrub_json with the default tiers.
manual_tests, api_examplesscrub_json with the generic tier everywhere.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
HIGH, USERINFO, GENERIC20 / 28 / 29The pattern tables.
CODE_EXPR, PLACEHOLDER, FREE_TEXT36 / 37 / 38False-positive filters and the free-text key names.
mask_literals42Blanks the contents of string literals in a snippet.
mark72Builds the [REDACTED:kind] marker.
scrub_text76Scrubs one string; returns the text and the count withheld.
scrub_json104Scrubbed copy of a nested structure and the count.
scrub_extract125Scrubs a per-file extraction result in place.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A string shorter than 6 characters is returned unchanged without matching anything.
  • Non-string leaves (numbers, booleans, None) pass through scrub_json untouched; a tuple comes back as a list.
  • Once a key in FREE_TEXT is seen, the generic tier applies to the whole subtree below it.
  • The generic bearer pattern has no quote group, so the CODE_EXPR filter applies to it: a bearer value made only of dotted identifier-like segments is treated as a code expression and kept. A JWT is still caught earlier by the high-confidence tier.
  • An unterminated private-key block is redacted to the end of the string.
  • The count of withheld values is what later surfaces in a report as the secrets_withheld unknown (see telemetry.py, which turns it into a metric).

credentials.py 248 lines

Resolves where TIA's own secrets come from (a literal, a file, another environment variable, or HashiCorp Vault) and provides the key-encryption-key wrappers used by the tenant secret store.

How it works

Secret references. resolve(value, vault) (line 127) interprets a configuration value by its prefix:

FormExampleResolution
NoneStays None.
file:<path>file:/run/secrets/github_tokenread_secret_file: the file is read as bytes, decoded as strict UTF-8, trailing \r and \n removed; an unreadable or empty file raises SecretError.
env:<NAME>env:OTHER_VARIABLEThe value of that variable; unset raises SecretError.
vault:<mount>/<path>#<key>vault:secret/tia/github#tokenSplit at # and at the first /; all three parts are required. Read with VaultClient.kv_get (KV version 2) and converted to a string.
anything elseA literal, returned as is (development only, per the docstring).

env_secret(name, default, vault) (line 148) is how the rest of the code reads a secret setting. It looks at NAME and NAME_FILE: if both are set it raises SecretError ("set only one") rather than guessing; NAME_FILE is read as a file; NAME goes through resolve; otherwise the default is returned.

Vault client. VaultClient (line 69) is a minimal HTTP client. from_env reads VAULT_ADDR (required), VAULT_TOKEN or VAULT_TOKEN_FILE (one is required), VAULT_NAMESPACE and VAULT_CACERT. The address must pass require_secure_url. Each call (_call, line 86) re-reads the token file when no literal token was given, so a rotated token is picked up; sends X-Vault-Token and, if set, X-Vault-Namespace; passes the request through airgap.checked; and uses a 10-second timeout. It offers kv_get, kv_put, transit_encrypt and transit_decrypt.

Secure URLs. require_secure_url(url, what, allow_insecure) (line 46) returns the URL if its scheme is https, or the caller allows insecure transport, or it is http to this machine. is_loopback (line 35) decides "this machine" by parsing: the name localhost, or a host that parses as an IP address and is loopback. Its docstring gives the reason for parsing instead of pattern matching: 127.example.com is a public hostname.

Key wrappers. KeyWrapper (line 161) is the interface: an id naming the key-encryption key, wrap(dek, aad) and unwrap(wrapped, aad).

  • LocalKeyWrapper (line 172) holds one or more 32-byte master keys by id and wraps with AES-256-GCM under the current one. The wrapped format is L1, one length byte, the key id, a 12-byte nonce, then the ciphertext. Because the key id is stored in the blob, unwrap can open a key wrapped under a previous master key as long as it is still loaded. Its id is local:<current id>.
  • VaultTransitWrapper (line 208) asks Vault's Transit engine to encrypt and decrypt, so the key never leaves Vault. The wrapped format is V1 followed by Vault's ciphertext string; the AAD is sent as Transit's context. Its id is vault-transit:<key name>.

keywrapper_from_env (line 225) chooses between them:

  1. If TIA_KEK_PROVIDER is vault-transit, returns a VaultTransitWrapper for the key named by TIA_KEK_TRANSIT_KEY (default tia).
  2. Otherwise reads TIA_MASTER_KEY and TIA_MASTER_KEY_PREVIOUS through env_secret. Each may be a comma-separated list of id:base64 items; an item without an id is named k1 (for TIA_MASTER_KEY) or prev<n>.
  3. If no key was found, returns None (the secret store is unavailable), or raises if the provider was explicitly local.
  4. The current key is TIA_MASTER_KEY_ID if set, else the first key loaded.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
SecretError31A secret could not be obtained or used; its message never contains secret material.
is_loopback35Whether a host is this machine.
require_secure_url46Enforces https except to loopback or with explicit permission.
read_secret_file56Reads a mounted secret file.
VaultClient69KV v2 and Transit calls to Vault.
resolve127Literal or file:, env:, vault: reference to a value.
env_secret148Reads NAME or NAME_FILE.
KeyWrapper161Interface for wrapping data keys.
LocalKeyWrapper172AES-GCM wrapping with locally loaded master keys; generate() makes a new base64 key.
VaultTransitWrapper208Wrapping by Vault Transit.
keywrapper_from_env225Builds the platform's wrapper from the environment.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Vault errors name the method, the path and the HTTP status, never the response body; network errors name only the exception type.
  • TIA_ALLOW_INSECURE_OUTBOUND set to 1, true or yes lets the Vault address be plain http to a non-loopback host.
  • LocalKeyWrapper refuses a master key that is not exactly 32 bytes and a current id that is not among the loaded keys. Unwrapping with a key id that is not loaded gives a message telling the operator to add it as a previous key; a failed integrity check gives a generic message.
  • Master keys must be valid base64 (validate=True).
  • If TIA_MASTER_KEY lists several items without ids, each is named k1 and the later one replaces the earlier in the dictionary.
  • An empty string in NAME is treated as unset by env_secret (the checks use truthiness).

transport.py 68 lines

Start-up checks that refuse unencrypted transport: the database connection must use TLS unless it is local, outbound calls that carry credentials must use https, and the server's own TLS files must be consistent.

How it works

check_db_tls(dsn, allow) (line 30) parses the DSN with psycopg.conninfo.conninfo_to_dict and returns it unchanged if any of the following holds; otherwise it raises TransportError with advice on how to fix the DSN:

  • The connection is local: there is no host, or every host in the comma-separated list is a unix-socket path (starts with /) or passes is_loopback.
  • The operator accepted the risk: TIA_ALLOW_INSECURE_DB is 1, true or yes (or the allow argument is true).
  • sslmode is one of SECURE_SSLMODES: require, verify-ca, verify-full. A missing sslmode counts as prefer, which is not secure because it silently falls back to no TLS.

check_outbound(url, what) (line 43) runs two checks in order: airgap.guard (is the host allowed at all in air-gapped mode) and require_secure_url with TIA_ALLOW_INSECURE_OUTBOUND as the override. A SecretError from the second is re-raised as TransportError.

tls_options() (line 51) builds the keyword arguments for uvicorn (the web server) to terminate TLS in the process:

Variableuvicorn optionRule
TIA_TLS_CERTFILEssl_certfileMust be set together with the key file; the file must exist.
TIA_TLS_KEYFILEssl_keyfileSame.
TIA_TLS_KEYFILE_PASSWORDssl_keyfile_passwordOptional; read through env_secret, so a reference or _FILE works.
TIA_TLS_CA_CERTSssl_ca_certsOptional.

With neither file set it returns an empty dictionary, meaning TLS is terminated upstream.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
SECURE_SSLMODES19The sslmode values that guarantee TLS.
TransportError22Subclass of SecretError for transport refusals.
allow_insecure26Reads an opt-out variable as a boolean.
check_db_tls30Refuses a remote database connection without TLS.
check_outbound43Air-gap and https check for an outbound URL.
tls_options51Server TLS settings from the environment.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • With several hosts in a DSN, all must be local for the exemption to apply.
  • Setting only one of the certificate and key variables is an error, not a silent fallback to plain http.
  • check_outbound catches SecretError only. airgap.AirGapError is a plain Exception, so an air-gap refusal propagates as AirGapError, not TransportError.
  • The docstring mentions that the server sends HSTS on https requests; that is implemented elsewhere, not in this module.

netsafe.py 154 lines

A hardened HTTP client for fetching URLs that a tenant administrator controls (identity-provider discovery documents, key sets, token endpoints). It prevents server-side request forgery (SSRF): using the service to reach addresses inside the platform's own network.

How it works

A tenant can point its identity provider at any address. request(method, url, ...) (line 101) makes one request with these protections, in order:

  1. airgap.guard(url, "outbound request"): in air-gapped mode the host must be allowed.
  2. check_url (line 70): the scheme must be https or http, there must be a host, and the URL must not carry a user name or password. http is accepted only when private hosts are allowed.
  3. resolve(host, port, private_ok) (line 54): looks the name up with socket.getaddrinfo and requires every returned address to be public. _public (line 47) converts an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address to IPv4 first, then requires is_global and not is_multicast. That rejects loopback, private ranges, link-local addresses (which includes the cloud metadata address 169.254.169.254) and reserved ranges.
  4. The connection is made by _Conn (line 81), a subclass of http.client.HTTPConnection whose connect dials the already-validated IP addresses directly instead of resolving the name again. This closes the DNS-rebinding gap, where a name answers with a public address when checked and a private one when used.
  5. For https the socket is wrapped with a default ssl context and server_hostname set to the original host name, so the certificate is still verified against the name although the connection went to an IP address. An extra CA file can come from the ca_file argument or TIA_IDP_CA_FILE.
  6. The request is sent with Host set to the URL's network location and Accept: application/json. http.client does not follow redirects, so a 3xx response is simply returned to the caller.
  7. The body is read with r.read(MAX_BODY + 1); more than MAX_BODY = 1,000,000 bytes raises "response is too large".

Time limits: TIMEOUT = 5 seconds applies to connecting and to each socket operation. Because a slow peer could send one byte at a time and never trip a per-operation timeout, a threading.Timer set to DEADLINE = 10 seconds calls _kill (line 27), which shuts down and closes the socket so the blocked read wakes up.

The only relaxation is TIA_ALLOW_PRIVATE_IDP_HOSTS (1, true or yes), read by allow_private(); it permits non-public addresses and plain http and is meant for development and tests.

Two helpers sit on top: get_json (line 134) requires status 200 and a JSON object, and post_form (line 147) posts a URL-encoded form and returns the status with the parsed JSON object, or an empty dictionary when the body is not a JSON object.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
MAX_BODY, TIMEOUT, DEADLINE22-241,000,000 bytes; 5 seconds; 10 seconds.
_kill27Shuts down and closes a connection's socket from another thread.
FetchError39Any refusal or failure of a fetch.
allow_private43Reads TIA_ALLOW_PRIVATE_IDP_HOSTS.
_public47Whether an IP address is globally routable and not multicast.
resolve54Resolves a host and rejects it if any address is not public.
check_url70Validates scheme, host and absence of credentials, then resolves.
_Conn81HTTP connection pinned to pre-validated addresses.
request101One guarded request; returns (status, body).
get_json / post_form134 / 147JSON GET and form POST built on request.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A host with one public and one private address is rejected, because all addresses must be acceptable; the error names the first bad address.
  • _Conn.connect tries the validated addresses in sorted order and raises FetchError with the last error if none connects.
  • request resolves the name twice (once inside check_url, once directly); the connection uses the second, validated result.
  • Network, HTTP protocol and TLS errors are all converted to FetchError("request to <host> failed: ..."). When the deadline timer kills the socket mid-read, the resulting OSError takes this path, so the "did not answer within 10 seconds" message (line 128) appears only if the read returned without raising.
  • post_form does not raise on a non-200 status; the caller receives the status and decides.
  • Caller-supplied headers are merged after the defaults and can therefore override Host and Accept.

airgap.py 62 lines

Enforces air-gapped operation: when enabled, every outbound connection must go to this machine or to a host on an explicit allow-list, and anything else fails loudly.

How it works

Two environment variables control it. TIA_AIRGAPPED set to 1, true or yes turns the mode on (enabled(), line 20). TIA_AIRGAP_ALLOWED_HOSTS is a comma-separated list of host names or *.suffix patterns, lower-cased and trimmed (allowed_hosts(), line 24): typically the customer's internal Git server, Vault and identity provider.

guard(url, what) (line 48) is the check every caller uses:

  1. If the mode is off, the URL is returned unchanged.
  2. host_of(url) (line 40) extracts the host: for anything with a scheme:// it uses urlsplit(...).hostname; for git's scp-like form user@host:path it takes the part between @ and :; otherwise it returns an empty string, meaning a local path.
  3. A local path is always allowed. A host is allowed if _host_ok accepts it; otherwise AirGapError is raised, naming the purpose (what), the host and the variable to change.

_host_ok (line 28) lower-cases the host and strips a trailing dot, then accepts localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1, any name ending in .localhost, an exact match of an allow-list entry, or a *.suffix pattern, which matches both subdomains and the bare suffix itself:

for pat in allowed_hosts():
    if pat.startswith("*.") and (host.endswith(pat[1:]) or host == pat[2:]):
        return True
    if host == pat:
        return True

checked(req, what) (line 59) applies guard to a urllib.request.Request and returns it, so callers can write urlopen(airgap.checked(req, "vault")). Within the modules of this section, the check is used by kms._http (purpose kms), VaultClient._call (vault), netsafe.request, transport.check_outbound and the trace exporter (tracing). The module docstring lists the full set of connections covered: GitHub and Azure DevOps APIs, GitHub App tokens, git fetches, identity-provider discovery, ServiceNow, cloud KMS and trace export.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
AirGapError16An outbound call left the allowed network.
enabled20Whether TIA_AIRGAPPED is on.
allowed_hosts24The parsed allow-list.
_host_ok28Loopback or allow-list match.
host_of40Host of an http(s) or ssh URL, or empty for a local path.
guard48Returns the URL or raises in air-gapped mode.
checked59guard for a urllib request object.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • The environment is read on every call, so a change takes effect without a restart of the check logic (the process must still see the new environment).
  • A refused call raises; it is never skipped silently, so a misconfigured integration is visible.
  • Loopback is recognised by the three literal forms above, not by parsing; another loopback address such as 127.0.0.2 would need to be listed.
  • The scp-like pattern requires a user@ part. A remote written as host:path without a user is not recognised as a host and is treated as a local path.
  • AirGapError derives from Exception directly, not from SecretError, so handlers that catch only SecretError do not catch it.

jwtverify.py 186 lines

Verifies JSON Web Tokens (JWTs: signed tokens issued by an OpenID Connect identity provider) using only the cryptography package. It checks the signature against the provider's published keys and then the standard claims.

How it works

Accepted algorithms. ALGS (line 23) lists nine, all asymmetric:

AlgorithmKey typeSchemeHash
RS256, RS384, RS512RSAPKCS#1 v1.5SHA-256, SHA-384, SHA-512
PS256, PS384, PS512RSAPSS, MGF1 with the same hash, salt length = digest sizeSHA-256, SHA-384, SHA-512
ES256, ES384, ES512ECECDSA on P-256, P-384, P-521SHA-256, SHA-384, SHA-512

HMAC algorithms (HS256 and so on) and none are absent, so a token cannot be "verified" by using a public key as an HMAC secret.

Verification steps (verify, line 150):

  1. split (line 64): the token must be a string of at most MAX_TOKEN = 16,384 characters with exactly three dot-separated parts; header and payload must be base64url-encoded JSON objects. It returns the header, the claims, the signing input (the first two parts joined by a dot) and the signature bytes.
  2. Any non-empty crit header rejects the token: this verifier understands no critical extensions.
  3. The algorithm is checked against ALGS before any key lookup, so an attacker-chosen algorithm cannot cause a key-set fetch.
  4. jwks.find(header) selects the key from the provider's key set by kid and key type. Keys embedded in or referenced by the token (jwk, jku, x5u) are never read.
  5. verify_signature (line 84): the key's kty must match the algorithm family; the key's alg, if present, must equal the token's; its use, if present, must be sig. RSA keys must be at least 2048 bits. For ECDSA the key's curve must match the algorithm, the signature must be exactly twice the coordinate length, and the raw r || s form is converted to DER before verification.
  6. Claims, in this order: iss equals the expected issuer; the expected audience is in aud (a string or a list); with more than one audience, azp must equal the audience; with require_azp, a present azp must equal it; exp must be a number and not more than leeway seconds in the past; nbf, if present, must be a number not more than leeway in the future; iat must not be more than leeway in the future; with max_age, the token must not be older than that by iat; with nonce, the claim must match; sub must be a non-empty string.

The clock tolerance LEEWAY is 60 seconds. On success the result is a Verified dataclass with header and claims.

Key set cache. Jwks (line 114) wraps a fetch function that returns the provider's key-set document. It refetches when the cache is older than ttl = 600 seconds or empty. find makes two passes: first with the cached keys, then, if no key matched, with a forced refresh, which handles a provider that rotated its keys. A forced refresh is honoured at most once per min_refresh = 30 seconds, so tokens with random kid values cannot make the service hammer the provider. A token with no kid matches only when the set holds exactly one key.

peek(token) (line 79) returns the claims without verifying anything. It exists only so the caller can read the issuer and audience to decide which tenant's identity provider to ask; its docstring warns never to trust the result.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
ALGS, CURVES23 / 28Accepted algorithms and elliptic curves.
MAX_TOKEN, LEEWAY29 / 3016,384 characters; 60 seconds.
TokenError33The token is not acceptable; the message is for the audit log, not the caller.
b64d / b64e37 / 44Base64url decode (adding padding) and encode (stripping it).
public_key52Builds an RSA or EC public key object from a JWK.
split64Parses a compact token into header, claims, signing input and signature.
peek79Unverified claims, for routing only.
verify_signature84Checks the signature with one JWK.
Jwks114Cached key set with rate-limited refresh.
Verified145Result holder.
verify150Full verification of signature and claims.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A key set with no keys, or one that is not a list, raises "the identity provider published no keys"; non-object entries are dropped.
  • An unsupported key type or a malformed JWK raises TokenError rather than a library exception.
  • exp given as a boolean is rejected explicitly (a boolean is a number in Python).
  • verify always requires the standard sub claim, even when the identity provider is configured with a different subject_claim (that setting is applied later, in identity.identity_from_claims).
  • verify has no parameter for an identity provider's allowed_algs setting; any restriction below the nine algorithms must be applied by the caller, outside this module.
  • The cache lock is held during the fetch, so concurrent verifications wait for one fetch instead of each starting their own.

metrics.py 227 lines

A small metrics registry with no external dependency that renders the Prometheus text exposition format, plus the definition of every metric the service exports.

How it works

A Metric (line 70) has a kind (counter, gauge or histogram), a name, help text, label names and a dictionary of values keyed by the tuple of label values. labels(...) returns a _Child bound to one label combination; it accepts positional or keyword labels but not both, and raises ValueError if the labels do not match the declared names. A counter can only increase; only a gauge can be set; only a histogram can observe. A histogram stores cumulative bucket counts: observe(v) increments every bucket whose bound is at least v, and adds to sum and count. The default bounds are DEFAULT_BUCKETS: 0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10, 30, 60, 120, 300 seconds.

Registry (line 112) creates metrics idempotently (registering the same name again returns the existing metric, or raises if the kind or labels differ) and also holds named collectors: functions called at scrape time that yield computed families such as queue depth. render() (line 141) writes each metric sorted by name with # HELP and # TYPE lines, then the collector output. Histograms render as _bucket series with an le label (ending in +Inf), _sum and _count. The single shared instance is REGISTRY.

The docstring states two rules: label values come only from small fixed sets (a route template, never a raw path; a status, never an id), and nothing records a tenant id, repository name, path or user content. The metrics are split into two families because "a healthy service can still produce an incomplete report".

Platform metrics (is the service working?):

timed(hist, **labels) (line 217) is a context manager that observes the elapsed time.perf_counter() seconds into a histogram when the block exits, including when it exits with an exception.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A collector that raises is skipped and counted in tia_metrics_collector_errors_total; it cannot break the scrape.
  • Label values are escaped for backslash, newline and double quote, and help text for backslash and newline.
  • Values that are whole numbers below 1e15 are printed as integers; infinities and NaN use the Prometheus spellings.
  • Each metric has its own lock; samples() copies the items under the lock and formats outside it.
  • dec on a counter raises, because it calls inc with a negative number.
  • The registry holds state per process, so each API or worker process exposes its own numbers.
  • The docstring of collector describes the series as {labels: value}; render actually iterates pairs of (labels dictionary, value).

telemetry.py 53 lines

Translates finished work (an analysis report, an extraction, a built graph) into updates of the metrics defined in metrics.py. It is separate from the engine so that a metrics bug can never change a report.

How it works

There are three functions, each wrapped in try ... except Exception: pass so that telemetry can never break the work it describes.

record_analysis(report, seconds) (line 8) reads the report dictionary:

  1. Increments tia_analysis_runs_total with the report's status and change.change_type (or unknown), and observes the duration.
  2. If the status is failed it stops here.
  3. Adds each non-zero count in summary.by_severity to tia_analysis_findings_total.
  4. For each entry in unknowns, increments tia_analysis_unknowns_total by area; if the area is secrets_withheld, adds the entry's count to tia_secrets_withheld_total.
  5. From coverage.traversal: observes reached into tia_analysis_traversal_nodes and increments tia_analysis_truncated_total when truncated is set.
  6. From coverage.outbound_call_sites and outbound_call_sites_resolved: adds the resolved count and the difference (never below zero) to tia_resolution_calls_total under resolved and unresolved.
  7. Adds each entry of coverage.resolution_by_strategy to tia_resolution_by_strategy_total.

record_extraction(stats, failed_files, seconds) (line 37) adds stats["parsed"] and stats["reused"] to tia_extraction_files_total, adds failed_files under failed when non-zero, and observes the duration. record_graph(graph) (line 48) sets the two graph gauges from len(graph.nodes) and len(graph.edges).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
record_analysis8Metrics from one finished analysis report.
record_extraction37Metrics from one repository extraction.
record_graph48Gauges for the latest graph's size.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • Any exception is swallowed. Because the whole body of record_analysis is one try block, a malformed report (for example one without status) leaves the metrics partly updated, up to the point of failure.
  • Label values such as severity, area and strategy come from the report as they are; the bounded-cardinality rule depends on the engine producing only fixed values for them.
  • The graph gauges describe the most recently built graph in this process, whichever tenant it belonged to; no tenant is recorded.

tracing.py 207 lines

Distributed tracing: it creates spans (timed records of one piece of work) linked into traces, reads and writes the W3C Trace Context header so a trace continues across processes, and optionally exports spans to an OpenTelemetry collector.

How it works

Trace context. The W3C traceparent header has the form 00-<32 hex trace id>-<16 hex span id>-<2 hex flags>. parse_traceparent (line 36) lower-cases and trims the header and matches it against TRACEPARENT (line 27); only version 00 is accepted, and an all-zero trace id or span id is invalid. It returns {trace_id, span_id, sampled}, where sampled is bit 0 of the flags, or None. In the other direction, Span.traceparent (line 54) formats the current span, and propagation_headers() (line 204) returns {"traceparent": ...} for an outbound call, or an empty dictionary when no span is active.

Spans. The current span lives in the context variable _current (line 29), so it follows the work across thread-pool hops without being passed around. Tracer.span(name, kind, parent, **attrs) (line 142) is a context manager:

  1. It decides the trace: an explicit parent (a parsed traceparent) is continued; otherwise the current span becomes the parent; otherwise a new trace starts with a random 32-hex id, and is sampled if random.random() < ratio. Children always inherit the sampling decision.
  2. It creates a Span with a random 16-hex id and the start time in nanoseconds, and makes it current.
  3. If the block raises (any BaseException), the span's status becomes error and error is set to "<ExceptionType>: <message>"; the exception is re-raised.
  4. In all cases it records the end time, restores the previous current span and calls _finish.

_finish (line 163) converts the span to a dictionary, appends it to recent (the last 200 spans, for tests and diagnostics), logs it as a message span with the dictionary under the extra field span if a logger is configured, and hands it to the exporter only if the span is sampled. Span.to_dict (line 57) passes every string attribute and the error text through scrub_text(..., True), so a secret in an exception message does not reach a log or a collector. Span kinds are internal, server, client, producer, consumer (mapped to OTLP codes 1 to 5 by KIND).

Export. OtlpExporter (line 64) posts OTLP/HTTP JSON to <endpoint>/v1/traces. The endpoint is checked once with transport.check_outbound (air-gap and https). export(span) puts the span dictionary on a bounded queue (max_queue = 2048) without blocking; if the queue is full the span is dropped and tia_trace_export_spans_total{outcome="dropped"} is incremented. A daemon thread named tia-otlp wakes every interval_s = 2 seconds and flush() sends batches of up to 100 spans with a 5-second timeout. A successful post counts the spans as exported; a network error counts them as failed and they are not retried, so tracing never builds a backlog. The payload carries resource attributes service.name and service.namespace = "tia", and status code 2 for an error span, 1 otherwise.

Configuration. configure_from_env (line 182) reads:

VariableDefaultMeaning
TIA_OTLP_ENDPOINTunsetCollector base URL; without it there is no exporter and spans go only to the log.
TIA_OTLP_HEADERSunsetComma-separated Name=value pairs sent with each export; read through env_secret, so it may be a secret reference.
TIA_SERVICE_NAMEtiaThe service.name resource attribute.
TIA_TRACE_SAMPLE1.0Fraction of new traces sampled; clamped to the range 0 to 1.

The module-level TRACER is replaced by configure; the convenience function span(name, kind, **attrs) (line 195) always uses the current TRACER.

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
TRACEPARENT, KIND, _current27-29Header pattern, span-kind codes, the current-span context variable.
_hex32Random hex string of a given length.
parse_traceparent36Parses an incoming traceparent header.
Span44One span; set adds attributes, traceparent formats the header, to_dict gives the scrubbed record.
OtlpExporter64Background batch exporter with a bounded queue.
OtlpExporter.payload95Builds the OTLP JSON body.
OtlpExporter.flush / shutdown104 / 127Sends everything queued; stops the thread and flushes once more.
Tracer132Creates spans and finishes them.
configure / configure_from_env176 / 182Replace the global tracer.
span, current_traceparent, propagation_headers195 / 199 / 204Module-level helpers for callers.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • A malformed or missing traceparent is ignored and a new trace starts; the request is not rejected.
  • Every span is logged regardless of sampling; sampling only decides export.
  • Only string attributes are scrubbed in to_dict; numbers and booleans pass through, and other objects pass through unconverted until the exporter turns them into strings.
  • Ids come from the random module, not a cryptographic source; they are identifiers, not secrets.
  • flush catches URLError and OSError only. The endpoint was already checked against the air gap when the exporter was constructed, so airgap.checked inside flush would raise only if the environment changed afterwards.
  • Tracer.recent is a plain list modified without a lock.

obs.py 92 lines

Structured logging: every log line is one JSON object that carries the request id and trace ids it belongs to, with every string passed through the secret scrubber.

How it works

The JSON line. JsonFormatter.format (line 49) builds a dictionary and serialises it compactly (separators=(",", ":"), default=str):

FieldSource
tsThe record's creation time in UTC as YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.mmmZ.
levelrecord.levelname.
loggerrecord.name, for example tia.http.
msgThe formatted message, scrubbed with the generic tier.
request_idThe context variable request_id_var, when set.
trace_id, span_idThe current span from tracing._current, when there is one.
any other keyEach attribute passed through extra= on the logging call (anything on the record that is not a standard attribute and does not start with _), cleaned by _clean.
exceptionThe full traceback text, scrubbed, when the record carries exception information.

The example in the module docstring shows the request log shape: method, route (the template, not the raw path), status and duration_ms arrive as extra fields on a tia.http record. RESERVED (line 28) is computed from a blank LogRecord plus message, asctime and taskName, which is how the formatter tells standard attributes from extras.

_clean (line 36) scrubs recursively: strings go through scrub_text(v, True); dictionaries and lists are walked (dictionary keys are converted to strings); numbers, booleans and None pass through; any other object is converted with str() and scrubbed. A token in an exception message or in a structured field therefore cannot reach a log.

Text format. TextFormatter (line 66) is for local use: HH:MM:SS LEVEL logger: message in local time, followed by [request id] and the dictionary of extras when present. It scrubs the same way.

Request ids. new_request_id(incoming) (line 31) accepts a caller's X-Request-ID only if it matches ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$ (short, no spaces or control characters, safe to log and echo); otherwise it generates uuid.uuid4().hex.

Setup. setup_logging(level, fmt, stream) (line 75) reads TIA_LOG_LEVEL (default INFO) and TIA_LOG_FORMAT (default json; any other value selects the text formatter). It creates one stream handler on standard error, marks it with _tia = True, and installs it on the loggers tia, uvicorn and uvicorn.error, first removing any handler carrying that mark; this makes the call idempotent. Propagation is switched off on those loggers, and uvicorn.access is disabled because the tia.http request log replaces it (it logs the route template and no query string). The module exports two ready loggers: http_log (tia.http) and log (tia).

Key functions and classes

NameLineWhat it does
request_id_var26Context variable holding the current request id.
REQUEST_ID / RESERVED27 / 28Accepted request-id pattern; standard record attributes to exclude from extras.
new_request_id31Validates an incoming id or generates one.
_clean36Recursive scrubbing of extra fields.
JsonFormatter48One JSON object per line.
TextFormatter66Human-readable single line.
setup_logging75Installs the handler on the tia and uvicorn loggers.
http_log, log91-92The request logger and the general logger.

Edge cases and failure handling

  • An incoming request id that fails the pattern is replaced, not sanitised, so a caller cannot inject characters into log lines or the echoed header.
  • The generic scrubbing tier is applied to every string, so text such as token=abcdef123 in a message appears as token=[REDACTED:secret]; this can also redact harmless values that happen to match.
  • Values that the JSON encoder cannot serialise are written with str() through default=str, so formatting does not raise on unusual extras.
  • The span record that tracing.py logs arrives as the extra field span and is cleaned again here.
  • The formatter reads tracing._current directly, a private name of the tracing module.
